**9,2,3b** And such people, having gone astray and not having grasped clearly every part of the faith and the exactness of our life, were ignorant of the resurrection of the dead and disbelieve it, and do not accept the Holy Spirit; for indeed they were ignorant of it.

**9,2,4** And this sect, while rejecting the resurrection of the dead and repudiating idolatry, practises idolatry within itself in ignorance, because the idols of the four nations were hidden in the mountain falsely called among them Gerizim.

**9,2,5** For whoever wishes to inquire exactly about Mount Gerizim must know that the two mountains lie near Jericho, that of Gerizim and that of Ebal, beyond the Jordan, on Jericho’s eastern side, as stated in Deuteronomy and in the book of Joshua, son of Nun.

**9,2,6a** So they practise idolatry unawares, by turning towards the mountain from every side when they pray [...], forsooth because it has been sanctified.

**9,2,6b** For it is impossible for the Scripture to lie, which says, “They continued doing the law and worshipping their idols until this day”, as is recorded in the fourth book of the Kingdoms.

**9,3,1a** These are refuted on every side regarding the resurrection of the dead — in the first place from Abel, because after his death his blood converses with the Master.

**9,3,1b** Now blood is not soul, but the soul is in the blood, and he did not say, “The soul cries to me”, but “The blood cries to me”, showing that there is a hope of resurrection of the bodies;

**9,3,2** but Enoch too was removed so that he should not see death, and he could not be found; and Sarah too, after her womb had become dead and the customary fountain had dried up, was quickened again for the conceiving of seed, and the old woman conceived a child according to promise, because of the hope of the resurrection;

**9,3,3** nay more, Jacob too, taking care for his own bones, gave command as about things that do not perish; and not only so, but Joseph too, giving command in the same sequence, gave a glimpse of the form of the resurrection.

**9,3,4** Nay more, the rod of Aaron too, which budded though it was dry, again bore fruit for the hope of life, signifying that our dead bodies will rise and leading towards resurrection; and the rod of Moses likewise, the wooden one, receiving life by God’s will and becoming a serpent, made the resurrection plain.

**9,3,5a** But Moses too, in blessing Reuben and his people, says, “May Reuben live, and may he not die”, of one who had died long before [...], so as to show that life follows death, and a judgement of a second death unto condemnation.

**9,3,5b** Therefore he lays two blessings on him, saying “May he live” with reference to the resurrection and “May he not die” with reference to the judgement, not speaking of the death that comes through release from the body, but of that which comes through condemnation.

**9,3,6a** These few points, then, will suffice to answer them.

**9,3,6b** And they have certain other practices too, full of folly: they rinse themselves with urine when they come from abroad, [as] though forsooth defiled; they baptize themselves in water with their clothes on when they touch another person of the foreign nations.

**9,3,6c** For they consider it defilement to touch or handle any other person from another doctrine.

**9,3,6d** Great is the madness of these people.

**9,4,1a** How readily their folly is refuted, apply your mind, most admirable one, and you will know.

**9,4,1b** For they loathe a dead body at once, though they themselves are dead in their works.

**9,4,1c** For not one testimony but many bear witness that the dead body is not abominable, but that the law was speaking in a riddle.

**9,4,2** For it is no longer two or three testimonies that bear witness to us of this, but the sixty-two myriads counted in the desert [...], and another such number and beyond, and yet many more besides, who followed the coffin of Joseph, which was carried for forty years throughout the whole camp and was not loathed and did not defile.

**9,4,3a** But the law spoke the truth, that “if anyone touches the dead man, he remains unclean until evening, and shall wash with water and shall be clean”.

**9,4,3b** But it was hinting at this in a riddle with reference to the putting to death of our Lord Jesus Christ in his passion in the flesh.

**9,4,4a** For by the article “the” one can signify the noun’s distinction.

**9,4,4b** For where the article stands, the affirmation made through the article applies to one particular thing, defined and most evident; but without the article it is to be taken indefinitely of any one thing whatever;

**9,4,5a** as, for instance, if we say king, we have indicated a name, but we have not clearly shown the one defined; for we speak of a king both of Persians and of Medes and of Elamites.

**9,4,5b** But if, with the addition of the article, we say the king, what is signified is unambiguous; for the king who is sought, or who is spoken of, or who is known, or who reigns over some people, is pointed out through the article.

**9,4,6a** Likewise, should we say god with no article, we have named any god at all, a god of the nations, or God who is.

**9,4,6b** But should we say the God, plainly we signify by the article “the” him who is, true and known, just as with man and the man.

**9,4,7** And if the law had said, “If you touch a dead man”, the sentence would have been pronounced against all, and the wording of the matter in question would have applied simply [to] every dead man; but when it says, “If anyone touches the dead man”, it refers the application to one particular person — I mean to the Lord, as has been pointed out by me above.

**9,4,8** So the law said this in a riddle because of those who were to lay hands on Christ and deliver him to the cross, that these had need of cleansing until the day should set for them and another light should rise for them through the baptism of water, “the washing of regeneration”;

**9,4,9** as Peter too bears witness for me here, saying to the Israelites in Jerusalem who said to him, “What are we to do, brothers?”, because he had said to them, “This Jesus whom you crucified”; and when they were cut to the heart he told them, “Repent, brothers, and let each one be baptized in the name of Jesus Christ our Lord, and your sins shall be forgiven you, and you shall receive the gift of the Holy Spirit”.

**9,4,10a** The law, then, does not speak about a dead man; and even if it does speak about a dead man, the law speaks about a defined one.

**9,4,10b** For [about one who is not defined] it is expressed otherwise; for it says, “If a dead man passes by, shut your doors and your windows, so that the house may not be defiled”, as if it were saying, about the hearing of sin, that if you hear a voice of sin or a form of transgression, shut your eye against desire and your mouth against evil-speaking and your ear against wicked sounds, so that the whole house — that is, soul and body — may not be put to death.

**9,4,11a** Therefore the prophet too says, “Death came up through the windows”, and he is by no means speaking about these windows; for otherwise, by shutting our windows, we should never die.

**9,4,11b** But our windows are the senses of the body — sight, hearing and the rest — through which death enters into us, if we sin through them.

**9,4,12a** Joseph, at any rate, prepared Israel for burial and did not loathe him, and after his death fell upon his face and kissed him.

**9,4,12b** And it is not written that he washed, in order to be cleansed.

**9,4,13** The angels prepared for burial the body of the holy Moses, as the tradition that has come down to us has it, and did not wash; nay, the angels were not defiled by the holy body.

**9,5,1a** And again I fear that I may drive the solution of the question to great length.

**9,5,1b** But from one or a second argument it will be granted to the wise man to be made wise in the Lord against those on the opposing side.

**9,5,2a** And if I am to speak in summary about the Spirit too, I should not shrink from it.

**9,5,2b** Expressly, then, the Lord says straight away to Moses, “Bring up to me to the mountain seventy elders, and I will take of the Spirit that is upon you and pour it out upon them, and they will support you”.

**9,5,3** And that we may know regarding the Son, [the] Father declares, “Let us make man in our image and according to likeness”; now “Let us make” does not denote one; and “The Lord rained fire and sulphur upon Sodom and Gomorrah, from the Lord, out of heaven”.

**9,5,4a** And about the prophets [...]: since the Pentateuch [alone] had already been given to them and no other Scriptures besides, they kept in line with the Pentateuch alone and not with what follows in sequence.

**9,5,4b** And if anyone now speaks to them about the others — I mean about David and Isaiah and those that follow — they do not accept them, held fast by the tradition handed on among them from their own fathers.

**9,5,5** And let my account of the Samaritans go thus far, told in brief discourse because of the fear we foresee of stretching the promise of the treatise too wide.

### Sect 10: Essenes

Against the Essenes, the first sect from the Samaritans, but the tenth in the series.

**10,1,1** These were cut into four sects, holding the same views as one another [about] circumcision and the sabbath and [the other things] in the law, but each of the three differs from its neighbours briefly and in some small measure, except only the Dositheans.

**10,1,2** For the Essenes remained in the original way of life, going beyond it in nothing; after these the Gorothenes disagreed with one another over some small matter, since a certain dispute had arisen between them — I mean between Sebuaeans and Essenes and Gorothenes.

**10,1,3** And the dispute takes this form: the law commanded the Jews to gather from every quarter to Jerusalem, often indeed, [and] at three seasons of the year, both at that of Unleavened Bread and at Pentecost and at Tabernacles.

**10,1,4a** And Jews lived dispersed also in the borders of Judaea and Samaria.

**10,1,4b** And it happened that they made their journey through the Samaritans when going up to Jerusalem.

**10,1,5** Since, then, they happened to hold their assembly for keeping the feast at one season, clashes arose from this; and moreover, at the time when Ezra was rebuilding Jerusalem after the return out of Babylon, when the Samaritans requested to join in giving help to the Jews and to share in the building, and were turned away by Ezra himself and Nehemiah —

### Sect 11: Sebuaeans

Against the Sebuaeans, the second sect from the Samaritans, but the eleventh in the series.

**11,1,1** then the Sebuaeans, out of wrath and anger, changed the times of the feasts mentioned before, first out of anger at Ezra, and secondly because of the occasion already mentioned, which provoked them to fighting through those passing by.

**11,1,2** These people fix the new month of Unleavened Bread following the new year — and that comes in autumn — that is, following Tishri, the month that bears the name August among the Romans, Mesori among the Egyptians, Gorpiaeus among the Macedonians, and Apellaeus among the Greeks.

**11,1,3a** From then on these make the beginning of the year and at once celebrate Unleavened Bread, and in the autumn they keep Pentecost, and they celebrate their Tabernacles when the Jews have Unleavened Bread and the Passover.

**11,1,3b** Yet not persuaded by the Sebuaeans were

### Sect 12: Gorothenes

Against the Gorothenes, the third sect from the Samaritans, but the twelfth in the series.

**12,1,1a** the Gorothenes and the others.

**12,1,1b** The Essenes, being close to the others, do the same as they do; but the Gorothenes and Dositheans alone have the quarrel with the Sebuaeans.

**12,1,2a** And they keep the feasts — I mean the Gorothenes and Dositheans — when the Jews celebrate them: both of Unleavened Bread and of the Passover, of Pentecost and of Tabernacles, and the fast held by custom among them, one day.

**12,1,2b** But the others do not do so, but separately, in the months we mentioned before.

### Sect 13: Dositheans

Against the Dositheans, the fourth sect from the Samaritans, but the thirteenth in the series.

**13,1,1a** The Dositheans differ from these in many ways.

**13,1,1b** For they confess a resurrection, and there are disciplined ways of life among them; they abstain from living creatures, and some of them even refrain from marriage after a period of living in it, while others remain virgins;

**13,1,2a** and likewise they hold to circumcision and the sabbath and to not touching anyone, because they loathe every human being alike.

**13,1,2b** And the account presents these same people as keeping and practising fasts.

**13,1,3** And the occasion of Dositheus’ holding these views is this: setting out from the Jews, he mingled with the nations of the Samaritans; and being advanced in [the] instruction according to the law and in the deuteroses current among them, but hunting for the first place and failing, and not being thought worthy to be held of any account among the Jews, he turned aside to the Samaritan race and founded this sect.

**13,1,4a** And withdrawing somewhere into a cave out of an excess of self-chosen wisdom, persevering vainly and hypocritically in fasting, as the account has it, he thus died for want of bread and water, of his own free will forsooth.

**13,1,4b** And after some days those who came to visit him found the body stinking and swarming out with worms, and a cloud of flies had settled upon it in a swarm.

**13,1,4c** This man, then, having ended his own life so vainly, became for them the cause of the sect, and from him those who imitate him are called Dositheans, or Dosithenes.

**13,2,1** And these are the differences of the four sects already mentioned that have come to our knowledge; and they will be overthrown by what has been said against them.

**13,2,2a** And I return again, stringing together the inroads in the succession of the times and the herds of error, and making my rebuttal to them, by uncovering their wretched practices and making, in brief discourse, the refutation of the venom of the evil and deadly reptiles.

**13,2,2b** The four sects of the Samaritans are completed.

**13,2,2c** Judaism comes next.

**13,2,2d** And Judaism was divided into seven sects:

### Sect 14: Sadducees

Against the Sadducees, the first sect from Judaism, but the fourteenth in the series.

**14,1,1** Again, after these sects of the Samaritans already mentioned and [those] of the Greeks pointed out somewhere above, there arose among the Jews sects seven in number, before the coming of Christ in the flesh, in Judaea and Jerusalem.

**14,2,1a** First are the Sadducees, being an offshoot from Dositheus, mentioned before.

**14,2,1b** And these give themselves the name Sadducees, the title forsooth deriving from righteousness; for Sedek is interpreted righteousness.

**14,2,1c** And there was also in ancient times one of the priests named Zadok.

**14,2,2a** But these did not remain in the teaching of their leader.

**14,2,2b** For they rejected the resurrection of the dead, holding views like the Samaritans; and they do not accept angels, which the Samaritans do not reject; the Holy Spirit is unknown to them, since they have not been deemed worthy of it; but in everything they keep the same as the Samaritans.

**14,2,3a** But these were not Samaritans, but Jews.

**14,2,3b** For they sacrificed in Jerusalem and joined with the Jews in all other things.

**14,3,1a** And they too will be overthrown by the trustworthy voice of the Lord, which they drew upon themselves by the solution of the question, when they came to him asking whether there can be a resurrection of the dead, and saying that there were seven brothers, and the first wed a wife and ended his life without a child, and the second took her, since Moses commands that a man take to wife the wife of his brother who has died childless, and that she be married to his brother, to raise up seed in the name of the departed.

**14,3,1b** So, he says, the first took her and the second, and they died, and likewise the seven.

**14,3,1c** But at the rising of the dead, whose wife will this woman be, seeing that the seven all have known her?

**14,3,2a** But the Lord said, “You are going astray, since you know neither the Scriptures nor God’s power.

**14,3,2b** For at the resurrection of the dead they do not marry, nor are they given in marriage, but they are the equals of angels.

**14,3,2c** And that the dead will rise, Moses shall be your teacher, as God spoke to him in revelation, saying, ‘I am Abraham’s God, and Isaac’s God, and Jacob’s God’; and he is God of the living and not of the dead”.

**14,3,2d** And “he muzzled their mouth”.

**14,3,2e** For these are easily caught and unable to withstand the truth for even one hour.

### Sect 15: Scribes

Against the Scribes, the second [sect] from Judaism, but the fifteenth in the series.

**15,1,1** After these Sadducees came the Scribes, in the course of that time, or even living out their age alongside them; they were deuterotes of the law, expounding it as a kind of grammatical science, doing in other respects what the Jews do, but introducing a superfluous and, forsooth, sophistical exposition,

**15,1,2** not only living according to the law, but keeping more abundantly “the washings of pots” “and of cups and of dishes” and of the other vessels of service, as though forsooth set upon purity and holiness, “washing their hands with the fist”, and with the fist also scouring away certain defilements by means of waters and baths,

**15,1,3** and having certain “fringes” as marks of their way of life, for a display of boastfulness and for the praise of those who see them; and they put “phylacteries” on their garments, that is, broad stripes of purple.

**15,1,4** One might suppose, since this is also recorded in the gospel, that perhaps it speaks about amulets, since some are accustomed to name amulets phylacteries.

**15,1,5a** But the passage is not about this at all.

**15,1,5b** But since such people put on robes, that is, mantles, and dalmatics, that is, colobia, made with broad stripes of purple woven with sea-purple, and the strict observers were accustomed to give the stripes of the purple the new name of phylacteries, for this reason the Lord too, speaking in their terms, called them phylacteries.

**15,1,6a** And the sequence too makes plain the expression of the naming, [the words] “and the fringes of the garments”; for [it says] the fringes, meaning tassels, and the phylacteries, meaning the stripes of the purple.

**15,1,6b** For it says, “You broaden your phylacteries and enlarge the fringes of your garments”.

**15,1,7** And each had certain little pomegranates tied on the four corners of his cloak, from the very warp, for the time during which he practised continence or kept virginity; for since each one, having made his vow, fixed a season of purity or continence, these were their emblems, to show men what they had promised, so that no one, forsooth, should touch those who had been sanctified.

**15,2,1** And they had four deuteroses: one [circulating] under the name of Moses the prophet, a second under that of their teacher, called Akiba, or Bar Akiba; another under that of Addas or Annas, also called Judah; and another under that of the sons of Hasmonaeus.

**15,2,2** Whatever from these four deuteroses is held among them in the conceit of wisdom (though for the most part of unwisdom) is boasted of and sung, and is proclaimed and noised abroad in the rank of teaching to be preferred.

### Sect 16: Pharisees

Against the Pharisees, the third [sect] from Judaism, but the sixteenth in the series.

**16,1,1** To these two sects there is joined in sequence another sect, that of the Pharisees; these held the same views as these — I mean as the Scribes, who are interpreted teachers of the law; for the Lawyers too were associated with them.

**16,1,2a** But again the Pharisees held other views besides these, having more rigorous ways of life.

**16,1,2b** For some of them, when they practised and set a period of ten years or eight or four, likewise, of virginity or continence, praying continually, took up this struggle more intensely, so that forsooth they might not suffer anything bodily or be beguiled by dreams that bring about an unpleasant flux of the body.

**16,1,3** And some fashioned for themselves a plank only a span in breadth and laid themselves on it at evening, so that if one should fall asleep and drop to the ground, he might be roused again to prayer, so as to keep his life wakeful as far as possible.

**16,1,4** And others gathered pebbles and strewed them under themselves, so that, being pricked, they might not be carried off into deep sleep, but might force themselves to procure wakefulness; and others again had thorns as their bedding, for the same purpose.

**16,1,5** And they fasted twice in the week, on the second and the fifth day; they paid the tithe, gave the firstfruits, the thirtieths and the fiftieths, and rendered the sacrifices and the vows most scrupulously.

**16,1,6** And they went about in the garb of the Scribes mentioned before, that is, with the mantle and the other attire and women’s garments, going out in broad sandals and with tongues on their shoes.

**16,1,7** And the name Pharisees was given them because they were set apart from the rest through the self-chosen excess of religion customary among them; for Phares in Hebrew is interpreted separation.

**16,2,1** And these confessed the resurrection of the dead, and believed that there are angels and spirit, but did not know the Son of God, like the others.

**16,2,2a** But fate and astrology too were very much in use among them.

**16,2,2b** For instance, the Greek names that come from the astrology of those in error have been renamed among them in the Hebrew tongue with other names:

**16,2,3** for example, the Sun hema and semes; the Moon ieree and albana, whence it is also called mene (for the month is called ieree, and the moon mene, as also among the Greeks, because of the month); Ares chochab okbol; Hermes chochab ochomod; Zeus chochab baal; Aphrodite zerua or loueth; Cronus chochab sabeth (and it is called among them by other names too, but I have not been able to set out their designation exactly).

**16,2,4** But the names that are vainly held by those in error to correspond to the number of the elements, which they call the signs of the zodiac [...], and by which they have lawlessly led the world astray into impiety, they likewise call in Hebrew thus: tela, sor, thomim, saratan, ari, bethula, mozanem, akrab, keset, gadi, dalli, deggim; they who vainly, after the Greeks — I mean the Pharisees —

**16,2,5** following them, renamed the same names in Hebrew thus: what is called the Ram among the Greeks, tela; the Bull, sor; the Twins, thomim; the Crab, saratan; the Lion, ari; the Virgin, bethula; the Balance, mozanem; the Scorpion, akrab; the Archer, keset; the Goat-horned, gadi; the Water-pourer, dalli; the Fishes, deggim.

**16,3,1** These things have not been set down by me to confuse my readers, nor in order to commend the vulgar chatter of those who brought into life the baseless and crazed folly of astrology; for it has been refuted by the truth as baseless and mistaken.

**16,3,2** Much has been said by us in other works to overthrow those who believe in nativity and fate, and in the proem of this treatise too the same people have been dealt with by us briefly; but so that no one may think that we are carried away against anyone by way of slander, and that we have not set out the truth after examining it exactly from traditions, these things have been stated by us even by name.

**16,3,3a** And the utmost perplexity and no ordinary folly is theirs, since they both confess a resurrection and lay down a just judgement.

**16,3,3b** For how can there be both judgement and fate?

**16,3,4** For one of two things must be: either, if nativity exists, there is no judgement, because the doer does not act of himself, but by the necessity of the dominion of destiny,

**16,3,5** or, if judgement exists and is truly threatened, and laws pass judgement, and those who do evil pay the penalty — the law being acknowledged as just and the judgement of God as most true — destiny is idle and has no standing whatsoever;

**16,4,1** since, because one can sin and not sin, the one is required to pay the penalty for his sins, and the other awaits praise for having done well, the difference between the two has been determined.

**16,4,2** So that one may rightly state it with a single voice, by [the] concise truth spoken through Isaiah the prophet in the person of the Lord: “If you are willing and give ear to me, you shall eat the good things of the land; but if you are unwilling and will not give ear to me, the sword shall devour you.

**16,4,3a** For the mouth of the Lord has spoken these things”.

**16,4,3b** So it is evident and clear and unambiguous to everyone that God, [who] said through himself, “If you are willing and if you are unwilling”, granted free will, so that it rests with man to do good or to aim at worthless deeds.

**16,4,4** Mistaken, then, is the mind of those who have supposed that there is nativity, and especially of the Pharisees; to whom one must cry out more frequently and in many ways, not with a single utterance but many times, the words spoken by the Saviour to them: “Woe to you, Scribes and Pharisees, hypocrites, for you have left the weighty matters of the law, judgement and mercy, and you tithe the dill and the mint and the rue, and you make clean the outer side of the cup and the dish, while the inside is full of uncleanness and intemperance.

**16,4,5a** And you judge it right to swear by what is upon the altar, but with you swearing by the altar itself is released from the oath; and you say that swearing by heaven is nothing, but if anyone swears by what is above heaven, this is held binding.

**16,4,5b** Does not the altar bear what is laid on it, and is heaven not the throne of the one seated upon it?

**16,4,6** Or because you say, if anyone says to father and mother, Corban, that is, a gift, whatever you would have profited from me, he shall no longer honour his father; and you have set aside the commandment of God through the tradition of your elders.

**16,4,7** And you circle sea and dry land to win one proselyte, and when he has become such, you make him a son of Gehenna doubly more than yourselves”.

**16,4,8a** What, then, could anyone put forward beyond the holy sayings to answer what has been said above?

**16,4,8b** Nay, I think it better to be content with the Saviour’s wise and true words, which they were unable to face even in the slightest.

### Sect 17: Hemerobaptists

Against the Hemerobaptists, the fourth sect from Judaism, but the seventeenth in the series.

**17,1,1** And there follows these a sect of those called Hemerobaptists, departing in nothing from the others, but holding the same views as the Scribes and Pharisees, yet not matching the Sadducees in their denial of the resurrection of the dead, but in the unbelief

**17,1,2a** in the other matters.

**17,1,2b** And this sect possessed this further peculiarity, that in spring and autumn, winter and summer, it was always baptized every day, whence it has also taken the name of Hemerobaptists.

**17,1,3** For this sect maintained that there is no other way for a man to live, unless one is baptized in water every day, being washed and purified from every fault.

**17,2,1a** And against this sect too the overthrow will come to us from one argument: that their words are of unbelief rather than of faith.

**17,2,1b** For their own conscience convicts them, as they are baptized every day, as though the hope of yesterday had died with them, both the faith and the purification.

**17,2,2a** For if they kept to one baptism, they would then trust in it as living and immortal for ever; but when they wash today, not for the cleansing of the body or of dirt but on account of sins, then by their reckoning they were cleansed; yet washing again on the next day, they declared that yesterday’s baptism, now past, had died.

**17,2,2b** For if the one of the day before had not died, they would not need another on the next day for the cleansing of sins.

**17,2,3** And if they do not abstain from sinning at all, supposing that the water will cleanse those who sin continually every day, their notion is vain and their practice gone and stale.

**17,2,4** For neither Ocean nor all the streams and seas, the ever-flowing rivers and springs and the whole rain-bearing nature, coming together, can wipe out sins, since this does not happen according to reason nor by God’s command; for it is repentance that cleanses, together with the one baptism through the naming of the mysteries.

**17,2,5** But I shall pass by this sect too, thinking it sufficient to have pointed out, in brief, the remedy for their madness, in the way it has been composed here for those who read.

### Sect 18: Nasaraeans

Against the Nasaraeans, the fifth sect from Judaism, but the eighteenth in the series.

**18,1,1** Next I shall undertake to describe the sect that comes after the Hemerobaptists, called that of the Nasaraeans; they are Jews by race, originating from Gileaditis and Bashanitis and the lands beyond the Jordan, as the account that has come down to us relates; the sect springs from Israel itself, it practised Judaism in every respect and held scarcely anything beyond the sects already described.

**18,1,2** For it had circumcision in the same way, kept the sabbath just as they do and held to the same feasts; it did not, however, bring in fate or astronomy.

**18,1,3** It accepted as fathers those in the Pentateuch from Adam to Moses, men who had been distinguished in excellence of piety, that is, Adam, Seth, Enoch, Methuselah, Noah, Abraham, Isaac and Jacob, Levi, Aaron and Moses, and Joshua the son of Nun; the Pentateuch itself, however, it rejected; it confessed Moses and believed he had been given a code of law, yet not this code, it says, but a different one;

**18,1,4a** hence, being Jews, they keep all the observances of the Jews, yet they would not offer sacrifice or partake of anything that has life; among them it was unlawful to eat meat or to offer sacrifice.

**18,1,4b** For they used to say that these books are fabricated and that none of these things was done by the fathers.

**18,1,5** In this the Nasaraeans differed from the other sects; and so the refutation of them, too, is plain not from a single passage but from many.

**18,2,1** First, [in] their confessing the fathers and patriarchs and Moses; for since no other Scripture speaks of these, whence do they know of the names and the excellence of the fathers, unless from the very Scriptures of the Pentateuch?

**18,2,2** And how is it possible for true and false to be in the same thing, and for the Scripture to speak truth in part and to lie in part,

**18,2,3** when the Saviour says, “Either make the tree good and its fruits good, or the tree rotten and its fruits rotten”; “for a good tree cannot bear rotten fruits, nor a rotten tree bear good fruits”?

**18,2,4** Vain, then, is their thinking and their teaching, which meets its overthrow from many quarters, since to this day not only are the things in the Scriptures sung, but the places of the wonders are also preserved: first, where Abraham offered the sheep to God,

**18,2,5a** Mount Zion, so called to this day; and also where the oak of Mamre is, where the calf was set before the angels.

**18,2,5b** And if Abraham set a dish of meats before angels, he himself was not without a share in them.

**18,3,1** Moreover, the tradition of the sheep [that was] sacrificed in the land of the Egyptians is still celebrated among the Egyptians, and among the idolaters.

**18,3,2** For at the season when the Passover took place there (this falls at spring’s beginning, when the first equinox comes), the Egyptians all, in ignorance, take red ochre and smear the sheep, and smear the trees too, the fig trees and the others, spreading the tale and saying that, as it goes, fire once on this day burned up the inhabited world; and the blood’s fiery-red look serves as a safeguard against so great and so terrible a plague.

**18,3,3** And from what source can we not give proof concerning the sequence, since even to this day the remains of Noah’s ark are shown in the country of the Cardyaeans?

**18,3,4** And certainly, if one were to seek and search it out, he would find the remains of the altar too (since it follows) at the foot of the mountain where Noah stayed after coming out of the ark, and, having offered some of the clean animals and of the fat to the Lord God, heard, “Behold, I have given you all things as the herbs of the field; sacrifice and eat”.

**18,3,5** But I shall pass by, once more, this sect’s alien and foolish notion too, content with the few words already spoken, sown here by our brevity to answer the error of the sect just described.

### Sect 19: Ossaeans

Against the Ossaeans, the sixth sect from Judaism, but the nineteenth in the series.

**19,1,1a** After this, again, comes another sect, interwoven with these, the one called that of the Ossaeans.

**19,1,1b** These are Jews, like the sects already mentioned, hypocrites in their manner, clever in their contrivance.

**19,1,2** These originated, as the tradition that has come down to us holds, from the Nabataean country and Ituraea, Moabitis and Arielitis, the lands lying above, beyond the valley called in the divine Scripture the Salt valley; and this is the sea called Dead.

**19,1,3** And this race of the Ossaeans is interpreted, by the rendering of the name, “sturdy race”.

**19,1,4** And afterwards there joined these the man called Elchasai, in the times of the emperor Trajan, after the Saviour’s coming, who became a false prophet; and this man composed a book, forsooth by way of prophecy or as by inspired wisdom; and they say that a certain other man, Iexaeus, was his brother.

**19,1,5** And this man was erring in his manner and deceitful in his mind, setting out from the Jews and holding the views of the Jews, yet not living according to the law, bringing in some things in place of others and fabricating his own sect for himself,

**19,1,6** having appointed salt and water and earth and bread and heaven and ether and wind for them as an oath in worship; and again at another time having appointed seven other witnesses — I mean heaven and water and holy spirits, [as] he says, and the angels of prayer and the oil and the salt and the earth.

**19,1,7** He loathes virginity, hates continence, compels men to marry; he brings in certain fantastic notions as though, forsooth, [from] revelation, and teaches hypocrisy,

**19,1,8** saying that it is no sin if [anyone] should happen to worship idols when a time of persecution has set in, provided only that he offers no worship in his conscience.

**19,1,9a** And whatever one may confess with the mouth, but not in the heart.

**19,1,9b** And the deceiver is not ashamed to bring in a certain witness, saying that a certain Phinehas, a priest of those of the race of Levi and Aaron and of the ancient Phinehas, in Babylon during the captivity, by worshipping Artemis in Susa escaped the ruin of death under Darius the king; so that everything of his is false and vain.

**19,1,10a** This man, then, [as has been said] above, joined the sect already mentioned, the one called that of the Ossaeans, of which remnants still exist even now in the same Nabataean land, which is also Peraea, towards Moabitis; and this race is now called that of the Sampsaeans.

**19,1,10b** And they fancy, forsooth, that they call him revealed power, because el means power, and xai hidden.

**19,1,11a** But the whole insolence of the practice has been refuted and has fallen into great disgrace among those able to understand and examine the truth exactly [...].

**19,1,11b** For even now, in the days of Constantius and of the present emperors [...].

**19,1,12a** For until Constantius, from the very race of Elchasai, a certain Marthus and Marthana, two sisters, were worshipped as gods in their country, because, forsooth, they were of the seed of the Elchasai already mentioned.

**19,1,12b** And Marthus died a short time ago, but Marthana was still living until now.

**19,1,13a** Their spittle too and the other filth of their bodies the deluded heretics in that country used to carry away, forsooth for the warding off of diseases.

**19,1,13b** They had no effect, however; but error is always presumptuous and ready for deceit; for wickedness is blind and error without understanding.

**19,3,1** And how long am I to wear myself out uttering the things about this sorcerer, and all the lies he told against the truth,

**19,3,2** teaching, first, denial of God and hypocrisy, and saying that one may partake of the abominable sacrifices of idolatry, deceive one’s hearers and deny one’s own faith with the mouth, and not have sin; so that their malady is incurable and cannot be corrected.

**19,3,3a** For if the mouth which acknowledges the truth is being made ready for falsehood, would anyone believe them that their heart is not deceived?

**19,3,3b** Since the divine word expressly declares, teaching in the Holy Spirit, that “with the heart one believes unto righteousness, and with the mouth one confesses unto salvation”.

**19,3,4** But again he professes Christ, forsooth, by name, saying that Christ is the great king; yet I have not fully grasped from his deceitful and counterfeit composition, the book of his drivel, whether he gave his account of our Lord Jesus Christ, since he does not define this, but speaks of Christ without qualification, as if, from what we have understood, he means or expects some other.

**19,3,5** For he forbids praying towards the east, saying that one must not face that way, but must have one’s face towards Jerusalem from all parts: those from the east facing west towards Jerusalem, those from the west facing east towards the same, those from the north towards the south and from the south towards the north, so that from every side the face is directly towards Jerusalem.

**19,3,6a** And see the madness of the deceiver.

**19,3,6b** For he anathematizes sacrifices and sacred rites as alien to God and as never offered to God at all from the time of the fathers and the law, and says that one must pray there, towards Jerusalem, where the altar and the sacrifices were — [he] who rejects the eating of flesh practised among the Jews and the rest, and the altar and the fire, as alien to God,

**19,3,7a** while saying that the water is right but the fire alien, in these words: “Children, do not go towards the appearance of the fire, for you go astray; for such a thing is error.

**19,3,7b** For you see it, he says, very near, and it is from far off; do not go towards its appearance, but go rather to the voice of the water”.

**19,3,7c** And his fables are many.

**19,4,1** Next he sketches Christ as being some power, of which he also indicates the measurements: twenty-four schoeni in length, that is, ninety-six miles, and in breadth six schoeni, twenty-four miles, and likewise romancing about the thickness and the feet and the other fables.

**19,4,2a** And that the Holy Spirit too is itself female, like Christ, standing like a statue above a cloud and between two mountains.

**19,4,2b** And I shall pass over the rest in silence, so as not to beguile the hearing of my readers into fable.

**19,4,3a** And with what words and empty babblings he deceives later in the book, saying, “Let no one seek the interpretation, but only say these words in prayer”, having translated them, forsooth, from the Hebrew tongue, as we have understood in part, though the things fancied by him are nothing!

**19,4,3b** For he says one should say, “abar anid moib nochile daasim ane daasim nochile moib anid abar selam”, which, when interpreted, has this meaning:

**19,4,4** “Let pass away humiliation, [that] from my forefathers, of their condemnation and of their trampling and of their toil, by trampling in condemnation through my forefathers, from humiliation passing away in the mission

**19,4,5a** of perfection”.

**19,4,5b** But in his case all these things are fulfilled.

**19,4,5c** For his power and his error have been emptied out.

**19,4,5d** And if anyone wishes to hear it interpreted word for word, in detail, we should not shrink from doing this too; but, as is fitting in every respect for those who wish to hear in detail, we shall set out side by side his very words and their interpretations, and it is thus: abar, let pass away; anid, humiliation;

**19,4,6** moib, that from my forefathers; nochile, of their condemnation; daasim, of their trampling; ane, and of their toil; daasim, by trampling; nochile, in condemnation; moib, through my forefathers; anid, from humiliation; abar, passing away; selam, in the mission of perfection.

**19,5,1** This, then, is the sect of the Ossenes described above; it lives by the Jewish way of life in keeping the sabbath, in circumcision and in doing everything the law requires; only by rejecting the books [of Moses], as the Nasaraeans do, does it make a schism, differing from the six others of these seven sects.

**19,5,2** To overturn it, it will suffice that it is estranged from God [...], since the Lord says plainly, “In the temple the priests violate the sabbath.”

**19,5,3** And what is this profaning of the sabbath, except that no one did any work on the sabbath, while in the temple the priests, in offering sacrifice, broke the sabbath, profaning it through the continual sacrifice of animals?

**19,5,4a** And I shall pass over this sect as well.

**19,5,4b** For this Elchasai, again, is joined to the Ebionites who came after Christ, and also to the Nazoraeans who arose afterwards.

**19,5,5** And four sects make use of him, being charmed by his error: [that] of the Ebionites, who [arose] afterwards, [and] of the Nazoraeans; that of the Ossaeans, who were before him and alongside him; and that of the Nasaraeans, whom I have described above.

**19,5,6** This is the [sixth] of the seven sects at Jerusalem, which lasted up to Christ’s advent and, after his advent in the flesh, up to the capture of Jerusalem, which was accomplished by the emperor Titus, brother of Domitian and son of Vespasian, in the second year of the reign of his father Vespasian.

**19,5,7** After the capture of Jerusalem this sect and the others were still heard of for a short time, I mean those of the Sadducees and the Scribes, the Pharisees and the Hemerobaptists, the Ossaeans, the Nasaraeans and the Herodians, until, each at its own time and season, every one of them was scattered and dissolved.

**19,6,1** But it is sufficient for every sensible person to prepare for himself, from their very madness and from the words of their preaching, an antidote to the deadly venom, condemning their rubbishy teaching and chatter,

**19,6,2** especially as the Lord says straight away, both in the law and in the gospel, “You shall have no other gods”, “You shall not take the name of any other in an oath”, and again says in the gospel, “not to swear, neither by heaven nor by the earth nor by any other oath, but let your yes be yes and your no no; for what is more than these is of the evil one”.

**19,6,3a** And I think that the Lord made this decree because some were going to speak of putting forward an oath in other names; first, then, that one must not swear either by the Lord himself or by any other oath; for swearing is [from] the evil one.

**19,6,3b** Evil, then, is he too who spoke in Elchasai,

**19,6,4a** who compelled men to swear not only by God, but also by salt and by water and [by bread and] by ether and by wind and by the earth and by heaven.

**19,6,4b** And it is sufficient, as in passing, for whoever wishes to be healed to use these two sayings as an antidote to answer his error.

**19,6,5a** Passing by his frivolity and the festering deceit of this sect, I shall next make the refutation of the seventh sect, the one current at that time among the same people.

**19,6,5b** And it is this:

### Sect 20: Herodians

Against the Herodians, the seventh sect from Judaism, but the twentieth in the series.

**20,1,1a** After this one, and after these, there was again a seventh, called that of the Herodians.

**20,1,1b** And these had nothing at all different, but [were] Jews through and through, idle and hypocrites.

**20,1,1c** And these regarded Herod as Christ.

**20,1,1d** Supposing that the Christ expected in all the Scriptures of the law and the prophets was Herod himself,

**20,1,2** and being deceived in him, they gloried in Herod, carried away by the text (along with seeking vainglory to win the favour of the king of the time) of the saying, “A ruler shall not fail from Judah, nor a leader from his thighs, until he comes for whom it is laid up”, or, as the other copies have it, “until he comes to whom the things laid up belong”;

**20,1,3a** since Herod was the son of Antipater, an Ascalonite, a temple-slave of the idol Apollo; and this same Antipater’s father bore the name Herod, being himself in turn a son of Antipater.

**20,1,3b** And this same Antipater was carried off captive by the Idumaeans, and living there in Idumaea he begot Herod.

**20,1,4a** And since his father was poor and unable to rescue his son — I mean Antipater — with a ransom, he remained in slavery for a long time; but later he was ransomed by a collection of the citizens, together with the boy Herod, and returned to his own home.

**20,1,4b** Therefore some call him an Idumaean, while others know him as an Ascalonite.

**20,1,5** And later he became a friend of Demetrius and was appointed procurator over Judaea, and came to the notice of the emperor Augustus, and after his procuratorship he becomes a proselyte, is circumcised himself and circumcises Herod his son, to whom the inheritance of the kingdom of the Jews came; and he was king in Judaea, being bound by treaty under the emperor Augustus.

**20,1,6** Since, then, this man reigns, sprung from foreigners, while the kingdom had continued by succession from Judah and from David, and the rulers and patriarchs from Judah [had failed], and the kingdom had passed to a foreigner, it seemed plausible, in the supposition of those in error, to be deceived and to suppose that he was Christ,

**20,1,7** from the sequence of the text mentioned before, “A ruler shall not fail from Judah, until he comes for whom it is laid up”, so that among them this is to be understood [thus]: that for this man it was laid up, he says; for those had failed, and this man is not of the race of Judah, nor indeed of Israel at all; and for [such a] one it was laid up to be Christ.

**20,2,1a** But the sequence refutes them, saying, “He himself is the nations’ expectation, and on him nations will set their hope”.

**20,2,1b** Which of the nations hoped in Herod?

**20,2,1c** What expectation of the nations waits for Herod?

**20,2,1d** And how was the saying “He lay down as a lion and as a lion’s whelp; who will rouse him?” fulfilled among them?

**20,2,2** And where did Herod “wash his robe in blood”, or “his garment in the blood of the grape”, as our Lord Jesus Christ did when he sprinkled his body with blood that was his own, and his garment in the blood of the grape?

**20,2,3a** But “understand what I say.

**20,2,3b** For the Lord will give you understanding in all things”.

**20,2,3c** For to show the purification of the state of the Lord’s people, he came, through his own blood of teaching, to cleanse the teeth of men, which had been defiled with the blood of sacrificial reek and of unlawful sacrifice.

**20,2,4a** And what need have I to recount the multitude of them?

**20,2,4b** For they are many, and time does not allow me to lengthen the discourse in answer to the sects already mentioned.

**20,3,1a** These seven, then, were in Israel, in Jerusalem and in Judaea, and the four already mentioned were among the Samaritans in Samaria; most of them have been done away with.

**20,3,1b** For there are no longer Scribes, no longer Pharisees, no longer Sadducees, no longer Hemerobaptists, no longer Herodians;

**20,3,2** only a few are found here and there, and rarely: perhaps one or two Nasarenes somewhere beyond the upper Thebaid and on the far side of Arabia, and the remnant of the Ossaeans, no longer practising Judaism but joined to the Sampsites, those in succession in the regions lying beyond the Dead Sea [...]; and now they have been joined to the sect of the Ebionites.

**20,3,3** So it has come about that they have left off from Judaism, and that, as when a root or the body of a serpent is cut, a two-headed and rootless snake has grown from it, sprouting from a single half-severed body and hanging from it.

**20,3,4a** Thus far the account of the four sects of the Samaritans and the seven of the Jews; of these no more survive than three of the Samaritans, namely the Gorothenes, the Dositheans and the Sebuaeans; of the Essenes hardly any, for they are as though buried in darkness; and among the Jews none survive but a few Ossaeans and Nasaraeans living apart.

**20,3,4b** The Ossaeans have gone over from Judaism to the Sampsaean sect, people who are now neither Jews nor Christians.

**20,3,4c** Let this be enough from me, for the present, concerning the sects described above.

## On the Incarnation

The sojourn of Christ, and the face of his coming in the flesh and of the truth: which is the sole and single faith of God.

**De Inc. 1,1a** And straightway, close upon their heels, our Lord Jesus Christ’s coming in the flesh made its sojourn, a coming that found the seven sects already mentioned in Jerusalem.

**De Inc. 1,1b** And his power quenched these and scattered them.

**De Inc. 1,1c** And thereafter, after his sojourn, all the later sects arose — I mean after the good news was brought to Mary in Nazareth through Gabriel, and, in short, after the whole coming of the Lord in the flesh, that is, his taking up.

**De Inc. 1,2** For God was pleased, for the salvation of men, that his own Son should come down and be conceived in a virgin’s womb — he who is the Word from heaven, begotten from the Father’s bosom timelessly and without beginning, but who came in the last days, being God the Word truly begotten of God the Father, being of one substance with the Father and in nothing altered from the Father, but being unchangeable and unalterable, impassible and not suffering at all, yet suffering with our race,

**De Inc. 1,3** having come down from heaven, carried in the womb not from a man’s seed but by the Holy Spirit, having truly had a body from Mary, having formed the flesh for himself from the womb of the holy Virgin, and having taken a human soul and mind and everything that man is, apart from sin, uniting it in himself with his own Godhead; born in Bethlehem, circumcised in the cave,

**De Inc. 1,4** presented in Jerusalem, taken in the arms of Simeon, acknowledged by Anna the prophetess, the daughter of Phanuel, carried away to Nazareth, coming in the following year to appear before the Lord at Jerusalem,

**De Inc. 1,5** and being in Bethlehem because of kinship, carried by his mother; brought away again to Nazareth, and in the second round of the year having come to Jerusalem and Bethlehem, carried likewise by his own mother, and lodging in a house in Bethlehem with his own mother and with Joseph, who was an old man and was with

**De Inc. 1,6** Mary; and there, in the second year after his birth, being sighted by the magi, worshipped, receiving gifts, and on that very night, when an angel had warned Joseph, being taken away into Egypt; returning from there again after two years, upon the death of Herod and the accession of Archelaus.

**De Inc. 2,1a** For in the thirty-third year of Herod, and the forty-second of the emperor Augustus, the Saviour is born in Bethlehem of Judaea.

**De Inc. 2,1b** And in Herod’s thirty-fifth year he goes down into Egypt, and he comes up from Egypt after Herod’s death.

**De Inc. 2,2** Hence in the thirty-seventh year of the same Herod’s rule the child was four years old, when Herod, having completed thirty-seven years, ended his life.

**De Inc. 2,3** And Archelaus reigns nine years; at the beginning of his reign Joseph, coming out of Egypt together with Mary and the child, and hearing that Archelaus was reigning, withdrew into the parts of Galilee and then settled in Nazareth.

**De Inc. 2,4a** And Archelaus begets Herod the younger, and this Herod reigns, by succession, in the ninth year of his father Archelaus’ reign.

**De Inc. 2,4b** And by reckoning thirteen years had passed of the coming of Christ in the flesh.

**De Inc. 2,5** And in the eighteenth year of Herod, who was also surnamed Agrippa, Jesus began his preaching, and then receives the baptism from John and preaches an acceptable year, contradicted by no one, neither by Jews nor by Greeks nor by Samaritans nor by anyone else.

**De Inc. 2,6** Then, being contradicted, he preached a second year, and it was the nineteenth year of the same Herod, and the thirty-second of the Saviour.

**De Inc. 2,7** And in the twentieth year of the Herod called the tetrarch, the saving passion takes place, and the impassibility, the tasting of death and a tasting of death even to the cross, as he suffers in truth but remains impassible in his Godhead (“For Christ having suffered for us in the flesh”, says the divine Scripture, and again, “put to death in the flesh, but made alive in the spirit”, and what follows),

**De Inc. 2,8** he is crucified, is buried, goes down to the regions under the earth in Godhead and in soul, takes captivity captive and rises on the third day with the holy body itself, having united the body with the Godhead, no longer being dissolved, no longer suffering, no longer lorded over by death (as the apostle says, “death no longer has dominion over him”),

**De Inc. 3,1** the very body truly, the very flesh, the very soul, all things themselves; not something other besides the body that was, but having empowered that very body which was and united it into one unity, into one Godhead, the fleshly as incorruptible, the bodily as spiritual, the gross as subtle, the mortal as immortal, the body having seen no corruption at all, the soul not having been left in Hades,

**De Inc. 3,2a** the instrument not having been divided towards sin, the mind not having been stained by change, but having taken all that is man’s, and having kept all things perfect.

**De Inc. 3,2b** The Godhead having yielded to the true incarnation in respect of the reasonable needs — I mean those that arise from body and soul and human mind [...], confirming the full assurance, that is, through hunger and thirst, weeping and dejection, tears and sleep, toil and rest.

**De Inc. 3,3** For these things are no form of sin, but the mark of a most true incarnation, the Godhead being in truth present with the incarnation, not suffering the things that are human, but the Godhead consenting to what is reasonable and to what lies outside sin and forbidden change.

**De Inc. 3,4a** But he also rose, and entered when the doors were shut, that he might show the coarse made fine, the very same thing that it was, with flesh and bones.

**De Inc. 3,4b** For after entering he showed hands and feet and a pierced side, bones and sinews and the rest, so that what was seen should not be an apparition; and he gave the promise of our faith and hope, having perfected all things through himself.

**De Inc. 3,5** And he takes food with them, not in seeming but in truth, and instructing them he taught them to preach the kingdom of heaven in truth, signifying to the disciples the great and most supreme [...] and telling them, “Disciple the nations”, which means, change the nations from wickedness to truth, from heresies to one unity,

**De Inc. 3,6** “baptizing them in the name of Father and Son and Holy Spirit”, into the Lord’s naming of the Trinity, the holy and royal seal, that he might show from the name that there is no alteration of the one unity.

**De Inc. 3,7** For where those being baptized are commanded by him [to be sealed] in the name of the Father, the doxology is secure; “in the name of the Son”, the invocation falls short in nothing; “in the name of the Holy Spirit”, the bond is neither divided nor estranged, since it has the seal of the one Godhead.

**De Inc. 4,1** And he was taken up into heaven in the very body and soul and mind, having united them into one unity and one spiritual subsistence and made it divine; he took his seat at the Father’s right hand, having sent heralds into the whole inhabited world, Simon Peter,

**De Inc. 4,2** Andrew his brother, James and John, Zebedee’s sons, whom he had chosen long before, Philip and Bartholomew, Matthew, and Thomas as well as Judas and Thaddaeus, Simon the Zealot; for Judas Iscariot, even if he was formerly counted among the twelve, became a traitor and was cut off from the holy roll of the apostles.

**De Inc. 4,3** And he sent out seventy-two others also to preach, among whom were the seven — Stephen, Philip, Prochorus, Nicanor, Timon, Parmenas and also Nicolaus — who had been appointed over the widows,

**De Inc. 4,4** and before these Matthias, who was voted in with the apostles in place of Judas; and after these seven, and Matthias who came before them, Mark and Luke, Justus, Barnabas and also Apelles, Rufus, Niger and [the] others among the seventy-two.

**De Inc. 4,5** And after them all, and together with them, he chose Paul the holy apostle, by his own voice from heaven, as apostle and at the same time herald of the nations and fulfiller of the apostolic teaching;

**De Inc. 4,6a** who finds the holy Luke, who belonged to the scattered seventy-two, [whom], having turned him back and made him his own follower, [he showed to be] a fellow-worker of the gospel and at the same time an apostle.

**De Inc. 4,6b** And thus the whole work of the gospel was accomplished up to this time.

**De Inc. 4,7** My account shall reach thus far concerning the twenty heresies and concerning the course, as far as I was able, of the gospel illumination related by me in brief, which came to pass in the world through Christ and his disciples,

**De Inc. 4,8a** by which it is possible, by gathering what is similar and setting beside it the forecasts and the prophecies from the law and the Psalms and the rest, to see their sequences and their courses and to grasp the exactness: that the coming of Christ in the flesh and the gospel teaching is not spurious but true, foretold from the Old Testament, and beyond doubt.

**De Inc. 4,8b** But so as not to stretch the labour of this composition to great bulk, I shall be content with these things.

**De Inc. 4,9** Proceeding next in order, I shall in like manner set out the opinions that sprang up afterwards in the world on an evil pretext, having already reckoned up in due measure the eleven from the Jews and Samaritans and the nine before them from the Greeks and barbarians and the rest, which arose before the coming of the Lord and up to his very time.

## Anacephalaeosis II

These are the contents also of this second tome of the first book, in which there are thirteen heresies, as follows:

**Anaceph. II 21,1a** 21. Simonians, those from Simon the magician of the time of Peter the apostle, of the village of Gitta in Samaria.

**Anaceph. II 21,1b** He set out from the Samaritans, but put on the name of Christ only.

**Anaceph. II 21,2** He taught obscene practice, the defiling intercourse with women as a thing indifferent; and he rejects the resurrection of bodies and asserts that the world is not God’s.

**Anaceph. II 21,3a** And an image of himself as of Zeus, and of the harlot who was with him, Helen by name, in the form of Athena [...], he handed over to his disciples for worship.

**Anaceph. II 21,3b** And he called himself the Father among the Samaritans, and the Christ among the Jews.

**Anaceph. II 22** 22. Menandrians, who took their beginning from this Simon through a certain Menander, but differ from the Simonians in some respect; he said that the world had come into being from angels.

**Anaceph. II 23,1** 23. Satornilians, who in Syria strengthened the obscene talk of the Simonians, but proclaim other things beyond the Simonians, to greater astonishment, having had their beginning from Satornilus,

**Anaceph. II 23,2** who himself, like Menander, asserted that the world had come into being by angels, by seven only, against the purpose of the Father on high.

**Anaceph. II 24,1** 24. Basilideans, practitioners of the same obscenity, from Basilides, who together with Satornilus had been a disciple of the Simonians and Menandrians, holding views like theirs but differing in some respect.

**Anaceph. II 24,2a** He says that there are three hundred and sixty-five heavens, and he sets out angelic names for them.

**Anaceph. II 24,2b** Hence also the year is of so many days, and the name Abrasax is of the same numerical value, and amounts to 365.

**Anaceph. II 24,2c** And this, he says, is the holy name.

**Anaceph. II 25,1** 25. Nicolaitans, from Nicolaus, who was appointed over the widows by the apostles, who out of jealousy over his own wife taught his disciples to practise obscenity together with the others,

**Anaceph. II 25,2** and brought in notions about Caulacau and Prunicus and other barbarous names.

**Anaceph. II 26,1** 26. Gnostics, who took over the same heresies but practise shamefulness with more frenzy than any of them; in Egypt they go by the names Stratiotics and Phibionites, in the upper regions Secundians, in other regions Socratites, and among others Zacchaeans;

**Anaceph. II 26,2a** others call them Coddians, and others call them Borborites.

**Anaceph. II 26,2b** These boast of [the] Barbelo, who is also Barbero.

**Anaceph. II 27,1a** 27. Carpocratians, from a certain Carpocrates, one of those in Asia, who taught the performance of every obscenity and every practice of sin.

**Anaceph. II 27,1b** And unless someone, he says, passes through all things and fulfils the will of all demons and angels, he cannot go up beyond into the highest heaven nor pass the principalities and the authorities.

**Anaceph. II 27,2** He said that Jesus received an intelligent soul and, knowing the things above, proclaimed them here; and that anyone who does deeds like those of Jesus stands on a level with him.

**Anaceph. II 27,3** He rejects the law, and the raising of the dead with it, as the sects from Simon onward do.

**Anaceph. II 27,4a** Marcellina at Rome came from his school.

**Anaceph. II 27,4b** Making images of Jesus, Paul, Homer and Pythagoras in secret, he burned incense to them and worshipped them.

**Anaceph. II 28a** 28. Cerinthians, also called Merinthians.

**Anaceph. II 28b** These are certain Jews deriving from Cerinthus and Merinthus, who boast of circumcision and say that the world came into being through angels, and that Jesus was called Christ by advancement.

**Anaceph. II 29** 29. Nazoraeans, who confess Jesus as Christ, Son of God, but conduct their lives in all things according to the law.

**Anaceph. II 30,1** 30. Ebionites, closely resembling the Cerinthians and Nazoraeans mentioned above; the sect of the Sampsaeans and Elkesaites was in some measure joined to them,

**Anaceph. II 30,2** who claim that Christ was created in heaven, and the Holy Spirit as well, and that Christ dwelt first in Adam, and from time to time puts off Adam himself and puts him on again; for this, they say, is what he accomplished in his coming in the flesh.

**Anaceph. II 30,3** Though they are Jews, they use gospels; they abhor the eating of meat; they hold water in place of God; and they say that Christ put on a man in his coming in the flesh, as I have said.

**Anaceph. II 30,4** And they are baptized continually in the waters, summer and winter, for purification, supposedly, like the Samaritans.

**Anaceph. II 31,1** 31. Valentinians, who reject the resurrection of the flesh and set aside the Old Testament and the prophets, reading them indeed and accepting whatever can be allegorized into a likeness of their heresy,

**Anaceph. II 31,2** but bringing in certain other mythologies, and speaking of the names of thirty male-female aeons, begotten together from the Father of all, whom they also regard as gods and aeons.

**Anaceph. II 31,3** And they hold that Christ carried a body down from heaven and went through Mary as though through a pipe.

**Anaceph. II 32,1** 32. Secundians, to whom Epiphanes and Isidore are joined; they too use the same syzygies, holding views like those of Valentinus, but setting out some things in a way somewhat different from him.

**Anaceph. II 32,2** And they add to this by teaching obscene practice; and they too reject the flesh.

**Anaceph. II 33a** 33. Ptolemaeans, likewise disciples of Valentinus themselves, to whom Flora is attached.

**Anaceph. II 33b** They too say the same things about the syzygies as Valentinus and the Secundians; but these too differ in some respect.

**Anaceph. II 33c** This, then, is the recapitulation of the second tome of the first book, of the thirteen heresies.

### Sect 21: Simonians

Against the Simonians, the first after faith in Christ, the only faith there is, but the twenty-first in the series.

**21,1,1** The first heresy, beginning from Christ onwards, is that of Simon the magician; it is a heresy of those who [believe] in the name of Christ neither rightly nor piously, but who work dreadful things in accordance with the corruption counterfeited among them.

**21,1,2a** This Simon was a sorcerer, and he came from Gitta, the city in Samaria, which is now a village.

**21,1,2b** And he bewitched the race of the Samaritans with his magic arts, deceiving and enticing them,

**21,1,3a** and he declared himself to be the great power of God and to have descended from above.

**21,1,3b** He called himself the Father to the Samaritans, but to the Jews he said that he was the Son, and that he had suffered without having suffered, but only in seeming.

**21,1,4a** This man fawned upon the apostles, and he too, on equal terms with the others, was baptized by Philip along with many.

**21,1,4b** But all except him awaited the coming of the great apostles and through the laying on of their hands received the Holy Spirit, since Philip, as a deacon, lacked the authority of the laying on of hands to bestow the Holy Spirit through it.

**21,1,5** But Simon, having neither his heart nor his reasoning right, but given over to a certain greed for base gain and love of money, and in no way departing from his wicked way of life, offered money to Peter the apostle, that he might give him authority to bestow the Holy Spirit by the laying on of hands, reckoning that he would give a little, but in return for the little would, by bestowing the Spirit on others, heap up a great mass of money and make a profit.

**21,2,1** This man, then, having his mind corrupted and deluded by the demonic error that lies in magic, and being ever ready to display through his own trickery the barbarous and demonic doings of his wickedness, came forward into public view and, under the pretext of the name of Christ, like one mixing hellebore with honey, slipped poison, under the dignity of the name of Christ, into those whom he had caught for his mischievous error, and so worked death in those who were persuaded.

**21,2,2a** Being lustful by nature, and pricked by shame at his own promises, the charlatan devised a corrupt notion for those whom he deceived.

**21,2,2b** For having found for himself a vagabond woman named Helen, who came from among the Tyrians, he takes her about with him, not letting it appear that he had intercourse with her;

**21,2,3** but, entwined with the woman in secret shamefulness, the sorcerer laid out a fabulous kind of enticement for his disciples, saying, forsooth, that he himself was the great power of God; and he has dared to say that his consort, the harlot, is the Holy Spirit, and he says that he came down on her account.

**21,2,4a** “In each heaven,” he says, “I changed my form according to the form of those in each heaven, that I might escape the notice of my angelic powers and come down to Ennoia, who is the one also named Prunicus and Holy Spirit, by means of whom I made the angels, while the angels made the world and mankind.”

**21,2,4b** And this Helen, he said, was that Helen of old, on whose account the Trojans and the Greeks came to battle.

**21,2,5a** And he told a myth about these things, that the power, coming down from above, changed her own form, and that the poets spoke of this in allegory.

**21,2,5b** For on account of the power from above (which they call Prunicus, and which among other heresies is called Barbero or Barbelo), which displayed her beauty [and] drove them to frenzy, and for this reason was sent to despoil the rulers who created this world, the same angels came to war on her account, while she herself suffered nothing, but brought them to work mutual slaughter upon one another through the desire for herself which she cast into them.

**21,2,6** And holding her fast so that she could not go back up, they had intercourse with her, each of them, in each body of womanly and female form, as she was decanted out of female bodies into bodies of different kinds, of human nature and of beasts and the rest, so that through what they themselves do, killing and being killed, they might bring about a diminishing of themselves by the shedding of blood, and that she then, gathering up the power, might be able to ascend into heaven once again.

**21,3,1** “And it was she who at that time, among the Greeks and Trojans, and on high before the world came to be, and after the world, had brought about the things of like pattern through the invisible powers.

**21,3,2a** And she it is who is now with me, and on her account I have come down.

**21,3,2b** And she too was awaiting my coming; for she is Ennoia, the one called Helen in Homer.

**21,3,2c** And for this reason Homer is compelled to portray her as standing on a tower and with a torch signalling to the Greeks the plot against the Phrygians.

**21,3,2d** And by the brightness, as I said, he signified the showing forth of the light from above.”

**21,3,3** Therefore also the wooden horse contrived in Homer, which the Greeks think came about by design, the sorcerer again said is the ignorance of the nations; and “As the Phrygians, dragging it along in ignorance, drew their own destruction upon themselves, so also the nations, that is, the men who are outside my knowledge, through their ignorance drag perdition upon themselves.”

**21,3,4a** But he also said again that Athena was the same as the one called Ennoia by him, the deceiver making use, forsooth, of the words of the holy apostle Paul and changing the truth into his own lie, the saying, “Clothe yourselves with the breastplate of faith and with the helmet of salvation, and greaves and sword and shield”, the cheat turning all these things, said by the apostle for the sake of firm reasoning and faith of pure conduct and the power of the divine and heavenly word, at last to mockery, in the manner of Philistion’s mime-talk, and to nothing else.

**21,3,4b** “What then?”

**21,3,4c** he says, “all these things he was mystically shaping into figures of Athena.”

**21,3,5a** Therefore he said again, as I said before,

**21,3,5b** hinting at that woman who was with him, the one he had taken from Tyre, who bore the same name as the Helen of old, calling her everything, both Ennoia and Athena and Helen and the rest, “and on her account,” he says, “I have come down; for this is what stands written in the gospel, the sheep that had wandered.”

**21,3,6a** But he also handed down to his followers a certain image as being, forsooth, his own, and they worship it in the form of Zeus; and likewise he handed down to them another image, of Helen, in the guise of Athena.

**21,3,6b** And those deceived by him worship these.

**21,4,1** And he laid down mysteries of shamefulness and, to put it more decently, of the flowings of bodies: of men through the emission, of women through the customary menses gathered for mysteries by a most shameful kind of collection.

**21,4,2** And these, he says, are the mysteries of life [and] of the most perfect knowledge; which anyone who possesses understanding from God would above all regard as abomination, and death rather than life.

**21,4,3** The same man posits certain names of principalities and authorities, and speaks of various heavens, and for each firmament and heaven he describes certain powers and sets out barbarous names for them; and no one, he says, can be saved otherwise, unless he learns this initiation and learns to offer such sacrifices to the Father of the universe by way of these principalities and authorities.

**21,4,4a** And this age, he says, was fashioned in deficiency by principalities and authorities of wickedness.

**21,4,4b** And he teaches the corruption and destruction of the flesh, and the cleansing of souls only, and of these only if they should be established in initiation through his erroneous knowledge.

**21,4,4c** And thus begins the origin of those called Gnostics.

**21,4,5a** And he declared that the law is not of God but of a left-hand power, and that the prophets are not from the good God but from one power and another.

**21,4,5b** And he assigns to each as he pleases: the law to a certain power, David to another, Isaiah to a different one, Ezekiel again to yet another, and he ascribes each one of the prophets to one principality.

**21,4,5c** And all these, he says, are from the left-hand power and outside the pleroma, and everyone who believes in the Old Testament is subject to death.

**21,5,1a** But this doctrine is overturned by the truth itself.

**21,5,1b** For if he himself were the great power of God, and the little harlot with him the Holy Spirit, as he says, let him say what the name of the power is, or on what ground he invented the epithet for the woman but nothing whatever for himself;

**21,5,2** and how is it that at a certain time, in the course of succession, he is found to have paid the debt of fate at Rome, when the wretch fell down in the middle of the city of the Romans and died;

**21,5,3** [and] for what reason did Peter declare that he had neither lot nor portion in the share of godliness;

**21,5,4** and how is it possible for the world not to be the good God’s, when all the good were chosen out of it;

**21,5,5** and how could the power that spoke in the law and in the prophets be a left-hand power, when it proclaimed beforehand the coming of Christ [from the] good God and forbids all base things;

**21,5,6a** and how could there fail to be one Godhead and one and the same Spirit of both the New Testament and the Old, when the Lord said, “I did not come to abolish the law, but to fulfil it”?

**21,5,6b** And to show that the law was proclaimed by him and bestowed by means of Moses, whereas the gospel grace has been proclaimed through himself and his coming in the flesh, he said to the Jews, “If you believed Moses, you would believe me also; for he wrote about me.”

**21,5,7** And there are many other [...] for the refutation of the sorcerer’s drivel; and how will shameful things be life-giving, unless the mind be that of demons, when the Lord himself speaks in the gospel to those who said to him, “If such is the case of the man and the woman, it is not expedient to marry”; and he said to them, “Not all receive this; for there are eunuchs who have made themselves eunuchs for the sake of the kingdom of heaven”, and he showed that to keep away from wedlock by nature is the kingdom of heaven’s gift.

**21,5,8** And again in another place, concerning honourable marriage, which Simon himself corrupts, shamefully contriving it for his own lust, he says that “those whom God has joined together, let no man separate.”

**21,6,1a** And how again does the deceiver, forgetting his own drivel, refute himself, as though he did not know what he says beforehand?

**21,6,1b** For having said that the angels came into being by him through his Ennoia, he said again that he changed his form in each heaven, so that he might escape their notice in his descent.

**21,6,1c** Did he then go unnoticed out of fear, and how does the babbler fear the angels whom he himself made?

**21,6,2a** And how will the seed of his error not be found by the wise to be readily refuted in every respect, when Scripture says, “In the beginning God made the heaven and the earth”?

**21,6,2b** And in harmony with this word the Lord in the gospel says, as to his own God and Father, “Father, Lord of heaven and earth.”

**21,6,3** If, then, God, the Father of Jesus Christ our Lord, is the one who made heaven and earth, everything has been said in vain by Simon the slanderer: that the world came into being in deficiency by angels, and all the other things which the deceiver, in his demonic frenzy, introduced as he babbled to the world, and by which he worked deception in some, those deceived by him.

**21,7,1** And these things, said by me in summary against his heresy, will suffice for those who read them, giving them a starting point for truth and for cure, and for exposing those who attempt to wrong the ignorant through such bestial corruption.

**21,7,2a** And I shall pass on and go again to the refutation of another heresy, having sufficiently crushed this one’s venom.

**21,7,2b** For there is in him change and ambivalence, since he is a deceiver but has put on the form of the name of Christ, like the serpentine corruption of abortions conceived from the wind-eggs of asps and of further vipers, as the prophet puts it, “Asps’ eggs have they broken,

**21,7,3a** and he that was about to eat of their eggs found a wind-egg, and in it a basilisk.”

**21,7,3b** But having struck him, as I said, with the power of Christ through the words of the truth, and having done away with his defilement, let us go on to those that follow, beloved.

### Sect 22: Menandrians

Against Menander, the second from the coming of Christ, but the twenty-second in the series.

**22,1,1a** Following this one in order comes a certain Menander, who came from the Samaritans and became a disciple of this Simon at a certain time.

**22,1,1b** And he too likewise said that the world had come to be through angels, while of himself he said that he had been sent down from above as a power of God.

**22,1,2** This man, practising a greater trickery than his predecessor for the deception of men, claimed to have been sent for salvation, forsooth, to gather some into his own mystery, so that they should not be overpowered by the angels and principalities and authorities who made the world.

**22,1,3** And weaving everything together in the same way as his own teacher, he did not leave off from sorceries and the other trickeries [...], and he differed in nothing in his teaching except only that he called himself greater than the one who had been his teacher before him.
