**22,2,1a** And caught in the same deficiency into which his teacher also fell, he too will be overturned through the same words of the truth’s refutation.

**22,2,1b** For he has been overturned, and his heresy has for the most part come to an end.

**22,2,2a** And I shall pass on, and, advancing in my exposition, come again to another.

**22,2,2b** For indeed the ancients tell a myth, that out of many asps gathered in one earthenware vessel and laid in the foundations at the four corners of each precinct of the idols built in Egypt, if one of them was found stronger than the others, it attacked the others and devoured them;

**22,2,3** and being left by itself and having no plenty of food, it turned and, beginning to eat from its own tail, devoured as far as a certain part [...] of its body; and in this way it was left no longer complete, being only half of a serpent.

**22,2,4a** Therefore they also called it the asp-gorgon, so that we should understand that this existed of old, but is now no longer found, but has vanished; just as this heresy too, having utterly perished, has its rebuttal from us, but has itself been made to vanish through the power of Christ.

**22,2,4b** And passing over this one too, let us go on to those that follow, beloved.

### Sect 23: Satornilians

Against Satornilus, the third from the coming of the Lord, but the twenty-third in the series.

**23,1,1a** After him arose a certain Satornilus, who himself also took his starting points from there, from Menander, I mean, and those before.

**23,1,1b** This man settled in Syria, that is, in Antioch by Daphne, and brought into the world a great deal of the art and practice of error.

**23,1,2** For these two became fellow-students, Basilides and Satornilus; and Basilides, going off to Egypt, has preached there the dark things of the very depth of his error, while Satornilus, staying in the place mentioned before, proclaimed, like Menander, that the world came into being by angels.

**23,1,3** And there is, he says, one unknown Father, and he made powers and principalities and authorities; and the angels have been separated from the power above, and certain seven made the world and the things in it; and the world has been allotted to each angel by portions.

**23,1,4** And these same angels, coming together, took thought and in common made man according to the form of the luminous image that peered down from above, which, when it peered down, they were unable to hold fast because it at once ran back up, and so they wished to imitate it.

**23,1,5a** And man was formed by them for no other reason than on such an occasion.

**23,1,5b** For since, he says, the same light, peering down from above, stirred up a certain excitement in these angels, they, in longing for the likeness above, set about making the formation of man.

**23,1,6** For since they fell in love with the light above, held fast by longing for it and by pleasure, when it appeared and vanished from them, and since, being enamoured of it, they were unable to be filled with its loveliness because the same light flew back up at the touch, for this reason, as the same sorcerer stages the drama, the angels said, “Let us make man after an image and after a likeness”;

**23,1,7** cutting away the word spoken in Genesis by the holy God, “our”, [and] leaving “after an image”, so that his error might have plausibility; as though, forsooth, it said in the words, “Let us make man after an image and after a likeness”, that others were doing the making, but the image was another’s.

**23,1,8** And when man had come into being, he says, because of their feebleness they could not bring him to completion, but he lay and writhed, lying on the ground like a crawling worm, and could neither stand upright nor do anything else, until the power above, peering down and moved with compassion for its own image and form, in pity sent a spark of the same power, and through it raised the man upright and so brought him to life; calling the spark, forsooth, the human soul.

**23,1,9** And for this reason, he says, the spark will by all means be saved, but the whole of the man will perish; what came down from above will be carried back up into what is above at certain times, but all that is from below, formed by the angels, is left here to them.

**23,1,10** And the sorcerer says that Christ too came in the fashion of a man and in appearance only, and did everything in seeming, that is, being born, walking about, being seen, having suffered.

**23,2,1** From this man the knowledge falsely so named starts once more to add to the abyss of the same wickedness, having taken its beginning and pretext from Simon, but adding other, more excessive nonsense, as we shall say later in its refutation.

**23,2,2** For this man, speaking of the angels, says that the God of the Jews also is one of them, and that he and they have been separated from the power above, and that the Saviour was sent from the Father against the will of the powers for the overthrow of the God of the Jews and for the salvation of those who obey [him]; and that the adherents of this heresy are themselves those who possess the spark of the Father on high.

**23,2,3** For he says that two men were formed from the beginning, one good and one bad; and from them there are two kinds of human beings in the world, both good and wicked.

**23,2,4** And since the demons were helping the wicked, for this reason in the last days, as I said before, the Saviour came to bring help to good men and to bring about the overthrow of the wicked and of the demons.

**23,2,5** And the same charlatan says that marrying and begetting are from Satan; hence most of them also abstain from things that have life, so that by this feigned way of life, forsooth, they may draw some into their deception.

**23,2,6a** And the same sorcerer says again that of the prophecies some were prophesied by the world-making angels, and others by Satan.

**23,2,6b** For he says that Satan himself is an angel, working against the world-making angels, and above all against the God of the Jews.

**23,3,1a** But when the brutish man says these things, surely he himself too will be shown to confess one God and to trace everything back to the one unity of the sole rule.

**23,3,1b** For if the angels have made [man], and the angels in turn have had the cause of their being from the power above, then they are not themselves the cause of the formation of man, but the power above which made the angels, from whom the formation of man also came about.

**23,3,2** For it is not the tool that is the cause of the things made by it, but the one who does the work through the tool by which the work is done, as it is also written, “The saw shall not be glorified without the one who draws it”, and so on.

**23,3,3** Thus we observe that the sword is not what causes the killing, but rather the one who set about the killing with the sword; and the wax-mould cannot of itself make what is moulded, but the one who made the wax-mould and the figure.

**23,3,4** So neither are the angels the cause, but he who made the angels, even if he had [not] instructed them to make man.

**23,3,5** For either he condemns the power above of ignorance and unawareness of what was going to be, as things accomplished against its will, or that by its own good pleasure [...] it made the angels for the useful purpose of the construction of man, which it wished to construct when it made the angels, even without having commanded them to carry out the undertaking, that is, the moulding of man, as his fabulous craftsmanship has it.

**23,4,1a** Or, then, we must question the mythmaker again: did the power above know what they were going to do?

**23,4,1b** Yes, he says.

**23,4,1c** Then if it knew, it made it itself, and not they; but if it knew, but did not wish it, and they undertook the undertaking against its purpose, for what reason did it not prevent them?

**23,4,2** But if it was unable to prevent them, the first baseness is that it fashioned against itself the angels made by it, for opposition to itself and provocation; and the second, that though able it did not prevent them, but cooperated in the wicked work done by the angels.

**23,4,3a** But if it did not cooperate, and though it wished was unable to prevent them, there is great weakness in one that wished and was not able, and the band of angels made by it would be mightier than the very power that is the cause of the angels that came into being through it.

**23,4,3b** From every side, then, the premise of the heresy is caught, falling rather into incoherence and not into truth.

**23,4,4** But if it knew, but of necessity made these, who were [going] to do what is not lawful against its will, it will fall again into another deficiency, and not even one pleroma will be found in the power above, according to his account.

**23,4,5a** And let us add to this by asking him: tell us, you there, who peered in through the windows (that I may so mock your drivel), and in peering observed how the angels were created, and then spied out their [...] craftsmanship concerning the human figure, and tracked down the contrivance of the power above, did these know what they were forming, or were they ignorant?

**23,4,5b** And if they were ignorant, by whom were they compelled to accomplish in ignorance [...]?

**23,4,5c** No, he says, they were not ignorant; for they were aware of what they were about to do.

**23,4,6a** Then did the power from above know that these were going to undertake this, or was it ignorant?

**23,4,6b** It was not ignorant.

**23,4,6c** Did it then make them in order that they should do this, or not?

**23,4,6d** No, he says; for it only made them, but these, against the power above, undertook to make a figure.

**23,4,7a** So these knew, but that power above was ignorant, according to your account, you most stupid of all men.

**23,4,7b** And the fashioning of men will be a coherent whole, and the angels who caused it will be in knowledge, but the power that made the angels in ignorance.

**23,4,8a** But this would be drivelling and silly, that the work should be more perfect than the craftsman, and the craftsman feebler than the angels made by him, who are the causes of the human constitution.

**23,4,8b** So then by every necessity you would confess that [one must] trace everything back to the same one Maker and to the one sole rule.

**23,5,1a** For God the Father made man and everything according to his own good pleasure, and not the angels, nor did anything come about according to the counsel of the angels.

**23,5,1b** For when God said, “Let us make man”, he said “after our image”, and not “after an image” only.

**23,5,2** For he was calling his Word and Only-begotten into his own work of creation as fellow-creator, as both the understanding of the faithful that springs from the truth and the very exactness of the truth hold, and as we have also made our confession about this clearly and at length in many other places, that the Father calls the Son to create man with him, through whom he also made all other things.

**23,5,3a** And I would say that he calls not only the Son, but also the Holy Spirit.

**23,5,3b** “For by the word of the Lord the heavens were made firm, and all their power by the spirit of his mouth.”

**23,5,4** And whether he wishes it or does not wish it (I mean Satornilus, the founder of this heresy), he will be compelled by every means to confess one God, God and Lord, who is the creator and fashioner of the universe, and of man as well.

**23,5,5a** And he will be put to shame in every way as a slanderer, both in his account concerning the prophets and in his base accusation against honourable marriage.

**23,5,5b** For our Lord Jesus Christ himself expressly cries out in the gospel, and he says in harmony with the prophet, “He who speaks in the prophets, behold, I am here”, and again, “My Father works until now, and I work.”

**23,5,6a** And to show what his Father works, and he himself, he declared, saying to those who asked him whether one ought to dismiss one’s wife on any ground at all, “How does it stand written?

**23,5,6b** When God made man, male and female he made them”, and again, after other things, that “For this reason a man will forsake his father and his mother and will be joined fast to his wife, and the two will be one flesh”, and at once he added, saying, “What God has joined together, let man not separate”; so that by all means the Saviour teaches that the maker of men is the God of all, and his own Father.

**23,5,7a** And that marriage is not from Satan but from God, first the Lord says, “What God”, he says, “has joined together, let man not separate”; then the holy apostle, “Marriage is honourable, and the bed undefiled.”

**23,5,7b** And to those widowed in the course of nature he likewise gives charge, saying through Timothy, “Refuse younger widows; for when they have grown wanton against Christ, they wish to marry”, and after other things, “Let them marry, bear children, rule their households”, laying down a law not to be transgressed, which is from God and graciously given to men in honour.

**23,6,1** Concerning the notions with which he has slandered the prophecies of God, that they are not from God, there are countless things to say, as the Only-begotten himself says, of which, proclaiming the good news of himself to the world, he says first, “Abraham our father longed to behold my day, and he beheld it and rejoiced”, and again he says, “If you believed Moses, you would believe me; for he wrote about me.”

**23,6,2** And who of those who think rightly and have understanding from God would not refute the deceiver Satornilus, knowing that the Saviour, revealed in glory for the demonstration of truth, did not display his own glory otherwise than between Elijah and Moses, who were themselves also seen with him in their own glory?

**23,6,3** And there are other such things which, spoken by the Lord in person and in the whole of the New Testament, demonstrate the agreement of the law, the prophets and the entire Old Testament with the New Testament, showing that both belong to the one God, as he says, “They will come and will recline in the bosoms of Abraham and Isaac and Jacob within the kingdom of the heavens, and will take their rest, from east and west”, and what follows,

**23,6,4** and again what is prophesied about him in the person of David, “The Lord said to my Lord, Sit at my right hand”, and again [what is] said by him to the Pharisees, “Did you never read, ‘The stone which those who were building rejected’?”

**23,6,5a** And as Luke affirms that the Saviour himself, after rising from the dead, was seen on the road by Nathanael and Cleopas and those with them, and that he instructed them from the Psalms and from the prophets “that it was necessary thus for the Christ to suffer and to rise up out of the dead on the third day.”

**23,6,5b** And there is nothing at all at variance between the coming of Christ in the flesh and the prophecies of the prophets.

**23,7,1** But let this much be said about the heresy of Satornilus, so that I may not spend time entangled in his foolish questions and in the overthrowing of them.

**23,7,2a** Passing on from this one, I shall next set forth that of Basilides, this man’s fellow-student, who was also deceived along with him.

**23,7,2b** For these share [in the same things], as if borrowing the venom from one another, according to the vivid proverb, an asp borrowing venom from a viper.

**23,7,2c** For they belong to the same school and assembly with one another, but each has stood on his own as the head of his heresy; and they pooled their wickedness from one another, but set up disagreement among themselves.

**23,7,3a** Whether, then, this one, like a viper, having received his share from those higher up, passed it on to Basilides, or that one to this one, nevertheless let the venom of these men, lethal though it is and issuing from serpents of this kind, be left behind, dimmed and done away with by us through the teaching of the Lord as by an antidote.

**23,7,3b** And let us, calling upon God, go on to those that follow, beloved.

### Sect 24: Basilideans

Against Basilides, the fourth, but the twenty-fourth in the series.

**24,1,1** Now Basilides, as has been shown above, having set out on his journey into the land of the Egyptians, spent his time there; then he comes into the parts of the Prosopite and the Athribite, and also around the Saite and the Alexandriopolite region, or nome.

**24,1,2a** For the Egyptians call the surrounding district or neighbourhood of each city a nome.

**24,1,2b** And you would find this too, lover of learning, to your own benefit, for the love of learning and for clarity, for a pious establishing and explaining of the things set down in the divine Scripture which cast some into perplexity through inexperience.

**24,1,3a** For where you find written in the holy prophet Isaiah about the nomes of Egyptian cities, such as of Tanis or of Memphis, or the nome of Bubastis, it signifies the circuit of the city in question.

**24,1,3b** And let this be explained for the sake of the love of learning.

**24,1,4** At any rate the aforementioned charlatan had his way of life in these places, and it is from these that his heresy, which flourishes even to this day, appears, having taken its occasion from his teaching.

**24,1,5** And he begins to preach in excess of his fellow-student, the sorcerer left behind in Syria, so that, forsooth, by relating things beyond that man he might seem to dazzle his hearers the more, and to please them and gather a multitude beyond his comrade Satornilus.

**24,1,6** And then, putting forward certain fantastic myth-makings, he begins thus (though, to [tell] the truth, he did not begin these dreadful and destructive things for us from his own notion, but took his pretexts from Satornilus and from Simon, who has been refuted before; but he wishes to handle them differently and to relate the mythologies on a greater scale);

**24,1,7** that, he says, there was one Unbegotten, who alone is Father of all; from him, he says, Mind was emitted, and from Mind Word, and from Word Prudence, and out of Prudence came Power and Wisdom, and out of Power and Wisdom came principalities, authorities, angels.

**24,1,8** And from these powers and angels, he says, there came into being a first, higher heaven, and other angels came into being from them; and the angels that came into being from them in turn made a second heaven, and they again made angels.

**24,1,9** And those who came into being from them in turn made a third heaven, and so those of each heaven, constructing yet another heaven and other angels, have driven the number up to three hundred and sixty-five heavens, from the highest down to this heaven of ours.

**24,2,1** It would be for the senseless to yield to this allure and believe his raving nonsense; but to the wise his account and his opinion are readily refuted and drivelling, showing how he has driven his own mind out into an excessive and boundless perversity.

**24,2,2a** For the pitiful little man, thunderstruck as though by some poetic frenzy, lays down and sets out names for each ruler of the heavens, so that through the names which he invents he may be believed by those who do not have a sound mind, to the destruction of the soul of those who are deceived.

**24,2,2b** And not only that, but the deceiver did not cease from devoting himself to magical contrivances and curious arts.

**24,2,3** Later, he says, this creation came into being through the angels in this heaven of ours and the power in it; and of these angels he says God is one, whom, setting him apart, he said to be the God of the Jews only, ranking him as one and of equal number with the angels named by him in mime-like fashion; and he says that man was formed by him.

**24,2,4** And these together with him divided the world by distribution of lots among the multitude of the angels, and to this one, the aforementioned God of the Jews, fell the race of the Jews; and blaspheming the same almighty Lord, who alone truly is God and no other (for we confess that this same one is Father of our Lord Jesus Christ), that man, denying him, himself wishes to represent him as one of the angels spoken of by him, as I have shown before.

**24,2,5** And the Jews came into his lot, and he protects them; and he is more self-willed than all the angels, and led Israel’s sons forth from Egypt through the self-will of his own arm, because he is bolder than the others and more self-willed.

**24,2,6a** Hence, because of his self-will, as the sorcerer blasphemously says, this same God of theirs resolved to bring all the other nations into subjection under the race of Israel, and for this reason prepared wars.

**24,2,6b** And loosing his tongue and lifting up his mouth, the most pitiful man feels no shame in uttering many other things as well against the holy God.

**24,2,7a** For this reason, he says, the other nations also made war on this nation and did it much harm, because of the jealousy of the other angels, since they too, provoked (as being despised by him), set their own nations upon the nation of Israel that was subject to him.

**24,2,7b** And for this reason wars and disturbances have always risen up against them.

**24,3,1a** Such is the deceiver’s plausible talk.

**24,3,1b** And he too likewise holds that Christ appeared in seeming.

**24,3,1c** And he says that he was an apparition in his appearing, but was not a man and did not take flesh.

**24,3,2** And this second mime-actor stages for us another drama in what he says about Christ’s cross, saying that it was not Jesus who suffered, but Simon of Cyrene; for since, when the Lord was being led out of Jerusalem, as the sequence of the gospel has it, they compelled a certain Simon of Cyrene to carry the cross,

**24,3,3a** from this he finds [...] to make the tragedy of his trickery, and he says: he transformed that man, while he was carrying the cross, into his own form, and himself into Simon.

**24,3,3b** And in place of himself he handed over Simon to be crucified.

**24,3,4** And while that man was being crucified, Jesus stood invisibly opposite, laughing at the men who were crucifying Simon, while he himself flew up to the heavenly places, having handed Simon over to be impaled, and departed without suffering into heaven;

**24,3,5** and Simon himself was crucified, and not Jesus; for Jesus, he says, flying up into heaven, passed through all the powers, until he was restored to his own Father.

**24,3,6a** For this, he says, is the Son of the Father spoken of before, who was sent for the help of the sons of men, because of the disorder which the Father saw both among men and among the angels.

**24,3,6b** And this, he says, is our salvation, the one who came and disclosed this truth to us only.

**24,3,7a** Such are the rhapsodies of the charlatan’s myth-making.

**24,3,7b** But from this point on too, as the uncleanness that began from Simon advanced, he permits those who are his disciples to practise every pursuit of mischief and licentiousness, teaching those persuaded by him a certain promiscuous intercourse of men with women, of evil design;

**24,3,8a** as the apostle says concerning these and those like them, that “the wrath of God and righteous judgment are revealed upon those who hold down the truth in unrighteousness.”

**24,3,8b** For it is for this reason, the indulgence of pleasure, that many fall into the heresy, finding that they may fulfil their own pleasure without fear in obscene practices.

**24,4,1a** And again he teaches and permits, saying that one ought not to bear witness as a martyr.

**24,4,1b** For he who bears witness will be found without reward, since he does not bear witness for the one who made man; for he bears witness for Simon who was crucified.

**24,4,2a** And whence would there be a reward for him, when he dies for Simon who was crucified, but confesses that he does this for Christ, whom he does not know, dying for one whom he does not know?

**24,4,2b** One must therefore deny, and not die rashly.

**24,5,1** But this man will be caught introducing a devilish power against souls, teaching them the denial of God, when the Lord himself says, “Whoever denies me before men, I will deny before my Father who is in heaven.”

**24,5,2a** But the charlatan says, “We,” he says, “are the men, and all the others are swine and dogs.

**24,5,2b** And for this reason he said, ‘Do not cast the pearls before the swine, nor give what is holy to the dogs.’”

**24,5,3a** For he hides his wickedness from those who have sense, but reveals it to his own clan and to those deceived by him.

**24,5,3b** For since the things said and done among them are truly “shameful even to speak of”, he says that it is “before men” that the truth must be confessed: “for we are the men, and the others swine and dogs”, as I said before.

**24,5,4** And he says that one [must] reveal nothing at all to anyone concerning the Father and his own mystery, but keep it silent among themselves, disclosing it to one in a thousand and to two in ten thousand; and he instructs his disciples, saying, “Know all, but let no one know you.”

**24,5,5** And he and those from him, when questioned, declare that they themselves are no longer Jews, but have not yet become Christians either, and that one must deny at all times, and keep the faith in silence within oneself and show it to no one, since he is wary of his own shame because his obscene practice and evil teaching cannot be spoken of openly.

**24,6,1a** The beginning of this evil pretext had its cause from inquiring and saying whence evil comes.

**24,6,1b** But everyone will be shown from his own business what sort of man he is.

**24,6,1c** These, then, are merchants of evils, lovers of evil and not of good things, as Scripture also said, “Those who seek evils, evils shall overtake.”

**24,6,2a** For neither was [the] evil ever, nor did a root of wickedness come into being, nor is evil a thing that subsists in itself.

**24,6,2b** For evil never was, but it is present, brought in by occasion, in each of those who do evil; and in him who does not do it, it is not, as has been shown in the discussions above.

**24,6,3** For the Lord, after making all things, says, “Behold, all things are very good”, showing that evil is not of ancient origin and was not there from the start before it began from man; for it comes to be through us, and through us it does not come to be.

**24,6,4a** Since, then, every man is able not to do evil and able to do evil, when he does it, evil exists, and when he does not do it, evil does not exist.

**24,6,4b** Where, then, is the root of evil, or the subsistence of wickedness?

**24,7,1** This man has driven on into great folly, saying that the Power [above] emitted Mind, and Mind Word, and Word Prudence, while Prudence put forth Power and Wisdom, and out of Power and Wisdom came authorities and powers and angels.

**24,7,2** And he says that the power and principle above these is Abrasax, because the numerical value of Abrasax has the number three hundred and sixty-five, as though trying in this way to confirm the testimony to his myth-making about the three hundred and sixty-five heavens.

**24,7,3a** And taking pains, like the astrologers, to mark out and establish locations for these heavens as well, he contrives it in earnest.

**24,7,3b** For taking the vain speculations of those men, he himself and those under him transferred them into his own stamp, for the purpose of his own idol-making and fictitious teaching.

**24,7,4** And they wish to establish these things from like numerical values, as I said before, since Abrasax has the number of three hundred and sixty-five, showing, forsooth, that for this reason the year has the number of three hundred and sixty-five days in its cycle.

**24,7,5a** But his drivelling account has fallen.

**24,7,5b** For the year is found to be of three hundred and sixty-five days and three hours.

**24,7,6a** Then, from this, he says, man too has three hundred and sixty-five members, so that one member is assigned to each of the powers.

**24,7,6b** Hence in this too his invented and spurious teaching has fallen; for in man there are three hundred and sixty-four members.

**24,8,1** And the blessed Irenaeus, who followed in succession to the apostles, treating this man with close precision, refuted his stupidity in admirable fashion.

**24,8,2** For now too the nonsense of Basilides will be overturned, who came down from above and clearly spied out the things above, or rather fell from above, away from the mark of the truth,

**24,8,3** because, if this heaven came into being by the angels belonging to it, and they by those higher up, and those higher up by those yet higher, then the Power above, which is also called Abrasax, will be found to be the one that made all things and the cause of all that exists, and nothing will be found to have come into being without it,

**24,8,4** since it is proclaimed by them as the cause and the first archetype, and the thing called by them the deficiency of this world did not come into being through some other, but from the primal beginning and cause of all that came to be later.

**24,8,5a** And we must ask the same man: for what reason do you carry us off to so great a multitude, you there, rather than to the origin, namely to the one almighty God?

**24,8,5b** Since, by every necessity, whether one speaks in this way or in that, one must confess the one cause of all things as Master.

**24,8,6a** But answer also concerning the matter of Christ, you fabricator of this fabulous business.

**24,8,6b** Supposing it was Simon the Cyrenian who was crucified, it follows that our salvation came about not through Jesus but through Simon, and the world could no longer expect salvation through Jesus Christ, who had not suffered on our behalf.

**24,8,6c** For neither can Simon save, being a mere man and nothing else.

**24,8,7** And at the same time you condemn the only-begotten Son of God of false dealing, if the good God handed over another in his own place, forcing him to slaughter.

**24,8,8** And such a thing will then be a dream, or rather a work of malice and trickery, that the Lord, hiding himself by some trickery, handed over another in his own place; and your foolish talk will be slandering the truth, yet unable to do so, but refuted by the truth itself, introducing this fabrication without coherence.

**24,9,1a** For in every way the truth in the Old and New Testament refutes this heresiarch.

**24,9,1b** For it is plain to everyone that Christ came willingly to his passion, and by his own will and that of his own Father, and by the good pleasure of the Holy Spirit, took flesh, becoming man among us, being God in perfection from the beginning,

**24,9,2** begotten of the Father without beginning and without time, but in the last days deigning to come to be in the womb of a virgin, and forming for himself a body, and being born in truth, and becoming man in certainty, so that in it he might suffer for us in the flesh and give up his life on behalf of his own sheep;

**24,9,3** as he refutes these men, saying, “Behold, we are ascending to Jerusalem, and the Son of Man will be handed over and will be killed, and on the third day he will rise”, and saying to the sons of Zebedee, “Are you able to drink the cup which I am about to drink?”,

**24,9,4** as the apostle Peter too says, “put to death in flesh, but brought to life in spirit”, and again, “who suffered on our behalf in the flesh”;

**24,9,5** and again John says, “If anyone does not say that Christ has come in the flesh, this man is antichrist”, and the holy Paul says, “having tasted death, yes, death on a cross”, just as Moses also, proclaiming it beforehand, said, “You shall see your life hanging upon a tree.”

**24,9,6** And our life is not Simon, but the Lord who suffered for us, that he might undo our sufferings; and having died in the flesh he became death to death, that he might break the sting of death, going down into the regions beneath the earth, that he might shatter the adamantine bars; and having done this he brought up the captivity of the souls and emptied Hades.

**24,10,1a** So then Christ did not become the cause of death to Simon, since he handed himself over.

**24,10,1b** For what do you say, you most god-forsaken of men?

**24,10,1c** Could he not have spoken out freely and withdrawn from them, if he had not wished to be crucified?

**24,10,2** Or was God the Word, the [Son] of God, playing a game of trickery and handing over another in his own place to die and be crucified, he who says, I am the truth: “For I am,” he says, “the truth and the life”?

**24,10,3a** But the life would not prepare death for another, and the truth would not hide what is done by it in truth and show some things in place of others.

**24,10,3b** For the truth will no longer be found to be truth if it works error and hides its own work, and deals through a contrary sophistry.

**24,10,4a** And, to put it simply, out of everything, so that I may not lengthen my account, “Woe to the world because of offences” and “those who work lawlessness.”

**24,10,4b** How many have found darkness for themselves and for others who afterwards were persuaded by their darkness! But to those of understanding the truth will become manifest, and the business of Basilides and of those like him will be refuted as a work of error.

**24,10,5** And let this be said by me concerning this heresy and this myth; and from here I shall go on to another heresy.

**24,10,6** For to whom will such a heresy not be detectable, being a kind of myth and, like a horned viper buried in the sand, but peeping out into the air with its horn and working destruction for those who come upon it?

**24,10,7** But indeed “the Lord has broken the horn of sinners, but the horn of the righteous alone shall be exalted”, which is the faith of truth.

**24,10,8** Therefore, having crushed this one too by means of the truth’s teaching, let us go through to those that come after, calling upon God as our helper, to whom be the glory and the honour and the worship for ages of ages, amen.

### Sect 25: Nicolaitans

Against the Nicolaitans, the fifth, but the twenty-fifth in the series.

**25,1,1** Nicolaus came to be one of the seven deacons whom the apostles selected along with Stephen, the holy and first martyr, and Prochorus and Parmenas and the others.

**25,1,2** This man, coming from the city of the Antiochenes, becomes a proselyte; and afterwards, having received the message of the preaching about Christ, he too was joined to the disciples, and [was] counted among the first of the most eminent; hence he was also numbered among those chosen at that time for the care of the widows.

**25,1,3** But afterwards the devil crept into him and led his heart astray, so that he was wounded by the same error as the ancients mentioned before, more gravely than those before him.

**25,1,4a** For this man, having a comely wife and abstaining from the wife, as if in imitation of those he saw devoted to God, held out for a time, but did not bear to the end to master his own incontinence; but wishing, like a dog, to return to his own vomit, hunting out for himself certain pretexts that were not good, he devised them in defence of his own passion of licentiousness; for whom it would have been better [...].

**25,1,4b** Then, falling from his aim, he was joined with his own wife without more ado.

**25,1,5** And this man, ashamed of his own defeat and wary of being found out, dared to say that unless one indulges in lust every day, one cannot share in eternal life.

**25,1,6a** For he was carried from one pretext to another.

**25,1,6b** For seeing his own spouse, distinguished in beauty but conducting herself with humility, he grew jealous of her, and supposing others to be after his own licentiousness, he at first kept on behaving drunkenly towards his own wife and bringing certain slanders against her in words, but in the end he dragged himself down not only into the use of the flesh that is according to the nature of men, but also into blasphemous notion, and the harm of perversity, and the error of wickedness creeping in.

**25,2,1a** And from here those of the knowledge falsely so called [...] begin to spring up evilly in the world, I mean those called Gnostics and Phibionites and those called the followers of Epiphanes, and Stratiotics and Levitics and Borborites and the rest.

**25,2,1b** For each of these, drawing his own heresy after his own passions, devised countless ways of wickedness.

**25,2,2** For some of them glorify a certain Barbelo, who they say is above in the eighth heaven, and they say that she was emitted from the Father; and some say that she is the mother of Ialdabaoth, and others of Sabaoth.

**25,2,3** And her son, they say, took control of the seventh heaven with a certain boldness and tyranny, and said to those beneath, “I am,” he says, “the first and the one after these things, and besides me there is no other God.”

**25,2,4a** And Barbelo heard this word and wept.

**25,2,4b** And she always appears to the rulers in a certain comeliness and plunders the seed from them through pleasure and emission, so that, forsooth, she may recover again her own power that was sown into various beings.

**25,2,5a** And so, from such a premise, he brought into the world the mystery of his obscene talk.

**25,2,5b** And some, as I said, of those mentioned before, through much mischief, taught men to live somehow, in ways not lawful to speak of, in promiscuous intercourse with women and in incurable obscenities, as the most holy apostle says somewhere, “For the things done by them in secret are shameful even to speak of.”

**25,3,1** And he who wishes to see the overturning of this man’s heresy, set against it by the Holy Spirit, must know it from the Revelation of holy John, how, writing to one of the churches in the person of the Lord, that is, to the bishop established there together with the power of the holy angel who is over the altar, he says, “But you have this good thing, that you hate the works of the Nicolaitans, which I also hate.”

**25,3,2** And others, honouring a certain Prunicus, as these too, again in fabulous fashion, carry out their own passions in this notion of obscene practice, say: we gather the power of Prunicus from bodies, through their fluids, I mean of semen and menses [...];

**25,3,3** of which I shall give an exact account a little later, when we undertake to speak about them separately, not in order to defile the ears of those who hear or read, but rather to produce in the wise a loathing towards them and a turning away from the working of evils, not slandering the wretches, but in truth exposing to public view the things done by them.

**25,3,4** And others glorify the aforementioned Ialdabaoth, saying that he is the first son, as I said, of Barbelo; and for this reason, he says, one must render him honour, because he revealed many things.

**25,3,5a** Hence they also compose certain books in the name of Ialdabaoth, fashioning countless barbarous names of rulers, [as] they say, and of authorities in each heaven, opposed to the soul of men.

**25,3,5b** And, in a word, great is the plot against the race of men that comes about through their error.

**25,3,6a** And others likewise glorify Caulacau, calling a certain ruler by this name, striving to dazzle the inexperienced by the astonishment of the names and the fictitious barbarous form of the name.

**25,3,6b** But to the experienced, and to those who have received from God the grace of the true knowledge of God concerning each name and subject, how are the teachings of their myth and error not readily refuted and [un]trustworthy?

**25,4,1a** For if they say Prunicus, the whole is a belching forth of voluptuousness and incontinence.

**25,4,1b** For everything that is called prunicus suggests the name of lust, and the undertaking of corruption.

**25,4,2** For in reference to those who deflower bodies there is a certain Greek expression, “he prunicized this woman”; hence also the deceivers among the Greeks who wrote erotic works record in their tales, speaking of beauty thus, that beauty is prunicus.

**25,4,3** But concerning Caulacau too, who of those who know would not laugh, that the words well spoken in Hebrew and well interpreted in Greek, and plain [even] now among those who read in Hebrew, and having nothing crooked about them, they themselves refashion into idol-making and into shapes and into self-subsistent principles and, so to speak, into the moulding of statues, sowing error in the simple through fantasy, for the sowing of their shameful and fabulous art?

**25,4,4** For caulacau is written in Isaiah, being a word in the twelfth vision, where he says, “Affliction upon affliction, hope upon hope, yet a little, yet a little, wait”;

**25,4,5** as I shall set down here the Hebrew words themselves most completely, in the very terms in which they are written: for saulasau saulasau is interpreted “affliction upon affliction”, caulacau caulacau “hope upon hope”, zeersam zeersam “yet a little, yet a little, wait.”

**25,4,6a** Where, then, is their mythology?

**25,4,6b** Where is the craving of their fantastic talk?

**25,4,6c** Whence come the tares to the world?

**25,4,6d** Who is it that compelled men to draw destruction upon themselves?

**25,4,7a** For if they knowingly changed the names into fantasy, they have plainly become the causes of destruction to themselves; but if in ignorance they spoke of what they did not know, nothing is more wretched than they.

**25,4,7b** For these things are foolish in truth, [as] indeed it is possible for everyone who has understanding in God to see.

**25,4,8** For on account of voluptuousness they have ruined, and go on ruining, both themselves and those who are persuaded by them.

**25,4,9** For it is a spirit of error, like the breath in a pipe, moving each of the senseless with various motions against the truth; for the pipe itself is an imitation of the dragon by means of which the evil one spoke and led Eve astray.

**25,4,10a** For it was in imitation of that figure that the pipe was fashioned for men, for deception.

**25,4,10b** And see the figure which the piper himself makes with the pipe.

**25,4,10c** For in piping he throws his head back upwards and bows it down downwards, and bends to the right and the left, like that serpent.

**25,4,11** For the devil too makes use of these very postures, so as to display his blasphemy against the heavenly things, to destroy utterly the things on earth, and at the same time to take the whole inhabited world into his grasp, ravaging on the right and on the left those who are persuaded by the error and charmed, as though by a musical instrument, by its counterfeit sounds.

**25,5,1** Some others of them fabricate certain novel names, saying that there was darkness and an abyss and water, and that the Spirit, in the midst of these, set a boundary between them; but the darkness was angry and resentful towards the Spirit, and this darkness rushed up, entwined itself about the Spirit and gave birth, he says, to one called Womb, who, once she had been born, conceived by the Spirit itself.

**25,5,2** From the Womb four aeons were emitted, and from the four aeons fourteen others, and there came to be right and left, light and darkness.

**25,5,3** Later, after all these, a certain shameful aeon was emitted, they say, and this one mingled with the Womb mentioned above; and from this shameful aeon and the Womb there came to be gods and angels and demons and seven spirits.

**25,5,4a** But the farce of their error is easily detected.

**25,5,4b** For after speaking of one Father at the beginning and defining him so, they later intimated many gods, which shows that error itself arms against itself the falsehoods it utters and undoes itself, while the truth is always found [...] on every side.

**25,6,1a** What, then, shall I say to you, Nicolaus?

**25,6,1b** What argument shall I hold with you?

**25,6,1c** From where have you come, you there, bringing us a shameful aeon and a root of wickedness and a generating Womb and many gods and demons?

**25,6,2a** For the apostle, in saying, “if indeed there are so-called gods”, intimates that they do not exist.

**25,6,2b** For in saying “so-called” he showed that they exist only in being so called, having no existence in substance, but only through the supposition of certain people.

**25,6,3a** “But for us,” he says, “there is one God”, that is, for those who have knowledge of the truth.

**25,6,3b** And he did not say a so-called God, but a God who is; and if for us there is one God, there are no longer many gods.

**25,6,4** And the Lord says in the gospel, “that they may know you, the only true God”, in order to overturn the thinking of those who tell myths and suppose that there is a multitude of gods; for our God is one, Father and Son and Holy Spirit, three hypostases, one lordship, one Godhead, one glorification, and not many gods.

**25,6,5** And where, by your account, Nicolaus, is the saying of the Saviour fulfilled, “There are eunuchs who were made eunuchs by men, and there are eunuchs who were born so from birth, and there are eunuchs who made themselves eunuchs for the sake of the kingdom of heaven”?

**25,6,6** If, then, there are eunuchs for the sake of the kingdom of heaven, how is it that you have led astray both yourself and those who obey you, holding down the truth of God in unrighteousness through lust and obscenity, and teaching [...]?

**25,6,7** And where with you is the saying fulfilled, “Concerning virgins I have no command of the Lord, but I give my opinion as one who has received mercy, that it is good to be so”, and again, “The virgin takes thought for the Lord’s concerns, how to please the Lord, in order to be holy both in body and in spirit”?

**25,6,8a** And how many things are there to say concerning purity and continence and virginity?

**25,6,8b** For with you the whole mire of impurity is shamelessly laid down as the rule.

**25,6,8c** But from these two or three texts, [...] to those who read them for the overthrow of the ridiculous heresy, let my purpose here be laid out.

**25,7,1** Proceeding in order, I shall point out the heresy yoked to this one, like a wood run riot with grass, or a thicket of thorns knotted together on every side, or a mass of timber and brushwood dried out in a field and made ready for burning, because of its [...] to the very heresy of the pitiable Nicolaus.

**25,7,2** [For] just as, through grafting or a wild itch or leprosy, some bodies [...] to be infected from other bodies, so in part the so-called [Gnostics] are joined [to these], having taken their pretexts from Nicolaus himself and from those before him (I mean Simon and the others), called Gnostics [indeed], but wholly condemnable because of the depravity and obscenity of their unclean practice in their deeds.

**25,7,3** For in truth, having struck and destroyed this man too, who practised continence for a little while and then fell away from continence, like the reptile called the hydrops, which comes up out of the waters onto land and goes back again into water, with the reed that was put into the hand of Christ, let us go on to the next heresies.

### Sect 26: Gnostics (Borborites)

Against the Gnostics, who are also Borborites, the sixth, but the twenty-sixth in the series.

**26,1,1** Again, these Gnostics, deceived men who sprouted in various ways from this one like fruits from a dung-heap, have shot up in the world, as is plain to everyone and detectable by the test of truth, not only, I would say, to believers but perhaps even to unbelievers; for to speak of a Womb and of Shamefulness and the rest, how could it not appear ridiculous to all men, “to Greeks as well as barbarians, to wise as well as foolish”?

**26,1,2** And it is a heavy calamity and, so to say, a very great misery that the founders of the heresies, men under condemnation and in error, should be brandished at us and rise up against us like a swarm of vermin, causing us annoyances and foul smells, itchings and irritations through their myth-making error.

**26,1,3** And these people, who are joined to this Nicolaus, born from him in turn like scorpions from a snake’s wind-egg, or [...] born from asps, introduce to us certain names of empty babbling and fabricate books, calling one book Noria; and by recasting, out of a notion of Greek superstition, the mythical rhapsody and fancy current among the Greeks, they thus interweave falsehood with the truth.

**26,1,4** For they say that this Noria is the wife of Noah; and they call her Noria so that, by recasting in barbarian names what the Greeks sang in Greek fashion, they may produce an illusion in those deceived by them, so as, indeed, to make a translation of the name of Pyrrha, naming her Noria.

**26,1,5** For since nura means fire in the Hebrew, not in the deep tongue but in the Syriac dialect (for among the Hebrews fire is called hesath in the deep tongue), it has befallen them for this reason, through ignorance and inexperience, to use this name.

**26,1,6** For neither the Pyrrha of the Greeks nor the Noria of these people’s fables, but Barthenos, was the wife of Noah (for the Greeks too say that the wife of Deucalion was called Pyrrha).

**26,1,7** Then these people, who once more bring Philistion’s pieces before us, supply the reason: that though she often wished to be with Noah in the ark, she was not permitted, because the archon, he says, who created the world wished to destroy her along with all the others in the flood.

**26,1,8** And she, he says, would sit down upon the ark and set it on fire, a first and a second and a third time; hence, indeed, the construction of the ark of Noah himself stretched over many years, since she had set it alight again and again.

**26,1,9** For Noah, he says, was obedient to the archon, while Noria disclosed the powers above and Barbelo, who comes from the powers and is opposed to the archon, as the other powers are too; and she intimated that what had been plundered from the Mother above through the archon who made the world and the other gods and angels and demons with him must be gathered up from the power in bodies, through the emission of males and females.

**26,2,1a** And in short, I am worn out with pointing out the whole blindness of their darkness.

**26,2,1b** For I should spend a long time if I chose to go minutely here through the system that has been composed concerning them, setting out one by one the absurd teachings of their knowledge falsely so called.

**26,2,2** Others of them, chafing us again in a different way, both striking their own eyes and being battered all round, bring in a certain prophet, Barkabbas, worthy of his own name.

**26,2,3** For kabba means fornication in the Syriac dialect and murder in the Hebrew, and again it means the fourth part of the measure; such a thing deserves mockery and laughter, or rather indignation, among those acquainted with this name in their own tongues.

**26,2,4** And from this most wonderful prophet they bring us a shameful tale, so that we may be persuaded to approach perishable bodies and fall from the hope on high, not ashamed to recount in these very words the doings of fornication, the amorous verses of Cypris once more.

**26,2,5a** Others of them again bring in a fabricated, seductive poem, to which composition they have given a name, calling it a Gospel of Perfection.

**26,2,5b** And truly it is not a gospel, but the mourning of perfection.

**26,2,5c** For the whole perfection of death is carried within such a sowing of the devil.

**26,2,6a** Others do not blush to name a Gospel of Eve.

**26,2,6b** For in her name, as though she had found the food of knowledge by a revelation of the serpent that spoke to her, they put forward a [...] sowing.

**26,2,6c** And as, in the unsteady mind of a man who is drunk and raving, the words would not be consistent, but some are made for laughter and others full of weeping, so the deceivers’ sowing of wickedness has come to be in every manner.

**26,3,1a** They take their start from foolish visions and testimonies in the gospel they profess.

**26,3,1b** For they speak thus: “I stood upon a high mountain and saw a tall man and another, a short one, and I heard as it were a voice of thunder, and I drew near to hear, and he spoke to me and said, ‘I am you and you are I, and wherever you are, there I am, and I am sown in all things.’”

**26,3,1c** “‘And from wherever you wish, you gather me.’”

**26,3,1d** “‘But in gathering me, you gather yourself.’”

**26,3,1e** And oh, this sowing of the devil!

**26,3,2a** From where has it turned aside the mind of mankind and dragged it away from the word of truth into foolish and incoherent things?

**26,3,2b** For anyone with sense has scarcely any need to make their refutation from Scripture or from examples or from anything else.

**26,3,2c** For to right reason the play-acting of their foolish talk and their adulterers’ practice are plain and easily detected.

**26,3,3** Introducing these and suchlike things, the devotees of Knowledge, who are joined to the heresy of Nicolaus, have fallen away from the truth, not only turning aside the minds of those persuaded by them, but also bringing both bodies and souls into slavery to fornication and promiscuity, defiling their very assembly, forsooth, with the shamefulness of promiscuity, and eating and handling both human flesh and impurities.

**26,3,4** Of these things I shall not dare to tell the whole, unless I should be compelled by the excess of the pain in my mind over the things vainly done by them, astounded at the bulk and the abyss of evils to which the devil, the enemy of mankind, brings those who obey him, so as to defile the mind and heart and hands and mouths and bodies and souls of those whom he has ensnared in such great darkness.

**26,3,5a** But I fear that I may perhaps uncover this whole great venom, like the face of some basilisk serpent, for the injury rather than the amendment of those who read.

**26,3,5b** For in truth the blasphemous collection of their great audacity, the gathering and recounting of its shamefulness, and the miry malice of their charlatan obscenity defile the ears,

**26,3,6** so that among some they are naturally called Borborians, while others give them the name Koddians (for in the Syriac dialect a side-dish or bowl is called kodda), because no one can eat with them, but the food is given separately to those who are defiled, and because, owing to the defilement, nobody can share even bread with them;

**26,3,7a** for this reason their neighbours, regarding them as set apart, named them Koddians.

**26,3,7b** The same people are called Stratiotics and Phibionites in Egypt, as I have said in part above; some call them Zacchaeans, and others Barbelites.

**26,3,8a** Yet I shall not be able to pass it over either, but I am compelled to speak it out.

**26,3,8b** [For] since the holy Moses too, filled by the Holy Spirit, writes, “If anyone sees a murder and does not report it, may such a man be accursed”, I am unable to pass over this great murder and this great murderous practice without making it plain by declaring it.

**26,3,9a** For by making this carcass known to the wise, like “the pit of destruction”, I shall perhaps produce fear and shuddering, so that the wise not only flee from it but also stone this crooked serpent and basilisk in the pit, so that no one dares even to approach it.

**26,3,9b** And let these things already said about them, few as they are, be set down in their part.

**26,4,1** But I shall pass on into the house of the depth of their deadly story (for among them the evil teaching of their pleasure takes various forms): first, that they hold their wives in common.

**26,4,2** And if any stranger belonging to their doctrine should arrive, there is a sign among them, of men to women and of women to men: in stretching out the hand, supposedly in greeting, they make a tickling touch under the palm, intimating by this that the newcomer is of their religion.

**26,4,3a** From then on, having recognised one another, they turn at once to feasting; and they set out lavish dishes of meat and wine, even if they are poor.

**26,4,3b** And after drinking together on this and, so to speak, filling their veins with surfeit, they turn to frenzy.

**26,4,4a** And the husband withdraws from his wife and speaks, saying to his own wife, “Get up, make the love-feast with the brother.”

**26,4,4b** And the wretches, having mingled with one another, and — how truly I am ashamed to tell the shameful things done among them (since, according to the holy apostle, the things done among them “it is shameful even to speak of”), yet I will not blush to state what they do not blush to do, so that in every way I may make those who hear shudder at the obscenities they dare —

**26,4,5** for after they have had intercourse in the passion of fornication, then, lifting up their blasphemy to heaven besides, the woman and the man receive the emission of the male into their own hands, and they stand looking up to heaven, holding the impurity on their hands, and pray, supposedly,

**26,4,6** those called Stratiotics and Gnostics, offering to him who is by nature the Father of the universe the very thing that is on their hands, and they say, “We offer you this gift, the body of Christ.”

**26,4,7** And so they eat it, partaking of their own shamefulness, and they declare, “This is Christ’s body, and this is the Passover, for the sake of which our bodies suffer and are forced to acknowledge the suffering of Christ.”

**26,4,8a** Likewise also with what comes from the woman: when it happens that she is in her flow of blood, they gather from her the menstrual blood of impurity, take it likewise and eat it together.

**26,4,8b** And, “This,” they say, “is Christ’s blood.”

**26,5,1** Hence also, when they read in apocryphal writings, “I saw a tree bearing twelve fruits in the year, and he said to me, ‘This is the tree of life’”, they allegorise it of the woman’s monthly flow.

**26,5,2a** But while they have intercourse with one another, they forbid the begetting of children.

**26,5,2b** For with them the corruption is pursued not for the begetting of children but for the sake of pleasure, the devil making sport of such people and mocking the creature fashioned by God.

**26,5,3** They bring the pleasure to its completion, but take back into themselves the seed of their impurity, not depositing it for the bearing of children, but themselves eating the product of their shamefulness.

**26,5,4** And if one of them is overtaken and implants the deposit of the natural emission, and the woman becomes pregnant, hear what more terrible thing such people dare.

**26,5,5** For they drag out the embryo at whatever stage they manage it, take this aborted infant and pound it in a mortar with a pestle, and mix in honey and pepper and certain other spices and perfumes so that they will not be sickened; and then all the members of the [...] of these swine and dogs gather, and each partakes with his finger of the pounded child.

**26,5,6a** And having thus carried out this eating of human flesh, they then pray to God: “We have not been made sport of,” he says, “by the archon of desire, but we have gathered up the brother’s lapse.”

**26,5,6b** And this, supposedly, they regard as the perfect Passover.

**26,5,7a** And how many other terrible things have been dared by them!

**26,5,7b** For when they again become frenzied among themselves, they smear their hands with their own shameful emission, rise up, and with their defiled hands pray naked in the whole body, as though by such a practice [...] to find boldness of access to God.

**26,5,8a** Night and day they pamper their bodies, silly women and paltry men alike, perfuming themselves, bathing, banqueting, giving their leisure to beds and drinking bouts.

**26,5,8b** And they curse anyone who fasts, saying, “One must not fast; for fasting is the concern of this archon who fashioned the age.”

**26,5,8c** “Rather, one must be nourished, so that the bodies may be strong, so as to be able to yield their fruit in its season.”

**26,6,1a** They make use of the Old Testament and the New, but they renounce the one who spoke in the Old Testament.

**26,6,1b** And whenever they find a saying whose sense can tell against them, they say that this was spoken by the spirit of the world;

**26,6,2** but if any word can be shaped into a likeness of their desire, not as the saying stands but as their deceived mind takes it, they adapt it to their own desire and claim that it was spoken by the Spirit of truth.

**26,6,3a** And this, he says, is what the Lord said about John, “What did you go out into the wilderness to behold?

**26,6,3b** A reed shaken by the wind?”, namely that John, he says, was not perfect; for he was inspired by many spirits, like the reed that is moved by every wind.

**26,6,4a** And whenever the archon’s spirit arrived he proclaimed Judaism, but whenever the Holy Spirit did, he spoke about Christ.

**26,6,4b** And this, he says, is the meaning of “the least in the kingdom [and so on]”.

**26,6,4c** He spoke about us, he says, meaning that the least among us is greater than he.

**26,7,1a** And at once such people are muzzled by the truth itself.

**26,7,1b** For from the words joined to each expression the truth will be shown openly, and the true warrant of the phrase will be restored.

**26,7,2a** For if John had been dressed in soft clothing and lived in the houses of kings, the saying would have been right with regard to him and straightforward for his refutation; but if [he says], “What did you go out to see?

**26,7,2b** A man clothed in soft garments?”, and it was not so, the charge of the saying is no longer to be brought against John, who did not wear soft clothing, but its application is to those who supposed that they would find John so, and who were often flattered hypocritically by those within the houses of kings.

**26,7,3** For these people, when they went out, supposed that they would find praise and blessings from John too for the transgressions committed by them day by day.

**26,7,4a** But when they did not find them, the Saviour said to them by way of refutation, “What did you think to find?

**26,7,4b** A man who would go along with your passions, as with those who wear soft clothing?

**26,7,4c** Not so; for John is not a reed shaken by the opinions of men, like a reed tossed about by the power of each of the winds.”

**26,7,5** But since he said, “Among those born of women there is none greater than John”, lest some should suppose that John is greater even than the Saviour himself, since he too was born of a woman, Mary the ever-virgin, through the Holy Spirit, he safeguarded us by saying that the one less than he, that is, in the time of his coming in the flesh, is greater than he in the kingdom of heaven.

**26,7,6a** For since the Saviour was born half a year after John’s birth, it is evident that [...], being greater than he; for he always was and is.

**26,7,6b** And to whom is this not plain?

**26,7,6c** So then, everything among them has been fabricated in vain, changed from good things into worthless ones.

**26,8,1a** And their books are many.

**26,8,1b** For they put out certain Questions of Mary, while others put forward many books about the Ialdabaoth mentioned before and in the name of Seth; others they call Revelations of Adam, and they have dared to compose other gospels in the name of the disciples, and they are not ashamed to say that our Saviour and Lord Jesus Christ himself revealed this obscenity.

**26,8,2** For in the so-called Great Questions of Mary (for there are also Lesser ones fabricated by them) they suppose that he made a revelation to her: that he took her to the mountain, prayed, brought out a woman from his side, began to have intercourse with her, and then, supposedly, partook of his emission and showed that “we must do so, that we may live”,

**26,8,3** and that when Mary was alarmed and sank to the earth, he lifted her up once more and told her, “Why did you doubt, you of little faith?”

**26,8,4a** And they say that this is what is said in the gospel, “If I told you earthly things and you do not believe, how will you believe heavenly things?”

**26,8,4b** And the saying, “When you see the Son of Man ascending where he was before”, that is, the emission being taken in again at the place from which it came out.

**26,8,5a** And as for the saying, “Unless you have eaten my flesh and drunk my blood”, and the disciples’ being troubled and saying, “Who can listen to this?”

**26,8,5b** They say that the word was about the shamefulness.

**26,8,6** That is why they were disturbed and went back; for they were not yet, he says, established in the fullness.

**26,8,7a** And David’s saying, “He shall be like the tree planted by the outlets of the waters, which will yield its fruit in its season”, speaks, he says, of the man’s shamefulness.

**26,8,7b** “By the outlet of the waters” and “which will yield its fruit” mean, he says, the emission of pleasure; and “its leaf shall not drop away”, he says, signifies that we do not allow it to fall on the ground, but consume it ourselves

**26,9,1** And so that, by bringing their testimonies into the open, I may not do harm rather than good, I shall for this reason pass over most of them, since otherwise I should be setting out and recounting here all the things they wrongly say.

**26,9,2** For the saying that Rahab put scarlet in the window, he says, does not mean scarlet, he says, but the parts of the female nature and the scarlet blood of the menses; and the saying, “Drink waters from your own vessels”, speaks of the same thing.

**26,9,3** They say that the flesh perishes and is not raised, and that it belongs to the archon.

**26,9,4a** And the power in the menses and in the seed, they say, is soul, “which we collect and eat; and whatever it is we eat, whether meat or vegetables or bread or anything else, we do the created things a kindness, collecting the soul from all things and carrying it with us to the heavenly places.”

**26,9,4b** That is why they partake of every kind of meat, saying, “so that we may show mercy to our kind.”

**26,9,5** And they claim that it is the same soul, sown in living creatures and in wild beasts and fishes and snakes and men, and in vegetables and in trees and in crops.

**26,9,6** Those among them called Phibionites present their shameful sacrifices of fornication, which we have described here already, in three hundred and sixty-five names which they themselves invented, supposedly of archons, making sport of the poor women and saying, “Have intercourse with me, that I may bring you up to the archon.”

**26,9,7a** And with every act of intercourse they utter the barbarous name of one of the beings invented among them, and supposedly pray, saying, “I offer to you, so-and-so, that you may offer to so-and-so.”

**26,9,7b** And at the next act of intercourse he again proposes in the same way to offer to another, so that this one too may offer to the next.

**26,9,8a** And until he ascends, or rather descends, through three hundred and sixty-five falls of lust, the man doing this calls upon some name at each one.

**26,9,8b** And he begins again to go down through the same ones, performing the same obscenities and making sport of the deluded women.

**26,9,9** So when he has driven on to so great a sum, a number of seven hundred and thirty falls, I mean shameful acts of intercourse and of the names invented among them, then at last such a man dares to say, “I am Christ, since I have come down from above through the names of the three hundred and sixty-five archons.”

**26,10,1** And the names of the greater archons, in their account, are these, they say, and they mention many: Iao is the archon in the first heaven, and in the second, he says, is Saklas, the archon of fornication, and in the third the archon Seth, and in the fourth, he says, is Davides.

**26,10,2a** For they posit a fourth heaven and a third, and another, a fifth heaven, in which they say is Eloaeus, who is also Adonaeus.

**26,10,2b** And in the sixth, some say, is Ialdabaoth, others Elilaeus.

**26,10,3** And they posit another, a seventh heaven, in which they say is Sabaoth; but others say no, rather Ialdabaoth is in the seventh.

**26,10,4** And in the eighth heaven is the one called Barbelo, and the Father of the universe and Lord, the same one, self-fathered, and another Christ, self-born, and this Christ who came down and showed men this knowledge, whom they also call Jesus;

**26,10,5a** and that he was not born of Mary, but displayed through Mary.

**26,10,5b** And that he did not take flesh, but was only an appearance.

**26,10,6a** And some say that Sabaoth has the form of an ass, others of a swine; that is why, he says, he commanded the Jews not to eat swine.

**26,10,6b** And he, they say, made heaven and earth and the heavens that come after him and his own angels.

**26,10,7** And the soul, when it departs from here, passes through these archons, but cannot get through unless [one] comes to be in the fullness of this knowledge, or rather of this condemnation, and, being filled with it, escapes the hands of the archons and the authorities.

**26,10,8** And the archon who holds this world is shaped like a dragon, and he swallows the souls that are not in knowledge and returns them through his tail into the world, here into swine and into other animals [...], and brought up again through the same ones.

**26,10,9** But if anyone, he says, comes to be in this knowledge and gathers himself out of the world through the menses and through the flow of desire, he is no longer held fast here, but passes beyond the archons mentioned before.

**26,10,10** And they say that he comes to Sabaoth and treads upon his head, blaspheming with unbridled tongue, and so passes beyond to the region above, where the mother of the living is, Barbero, also called Barbelo, and so the soul is saved.

**26,10,11** And the wretches say that Sabaoth also has hair like a woman’s, supposing the name Sabaoth to be some archon, not knowing that where it says, “Thus says the Lord Sabaoth”, it did not speak the name of some person, but a name of glorification of the Godhead.

**26,10,12** For from the Hebrew dialect Sabaoth is translated Lord of the powers; for wherever the name Sabaoth is written in the Old Testament, it signifies power, which is why Aquila everywhere translates “Adonai Sabaoth” by saying “Lord of armies”.

**26,10,13** But these people, raving in every way against their own Master, are in search of one who is not, and have lost him who is, or rather they have brought themselves to ruin.

**26,11,1a** And how many other things [...], in which one is worn out telling the doings of their madness.

**26,11,1b** Some of them, supposedly not approaching women, corrupt themselves with their own hands, and they take their own corruption into their hands and so eat it, using a testimony slanderously twisted,

**26,11,2** the text, “These hands have ministered not only to me but also to those with me”, and again the text, “working with your own hands, so that you may be able to share also with those who have not”;

**26,11,3** as indeed, I think, the Holy Spirit was moved concerning these people too in Jude the apostle, that is, in the catholic epistle written by him (and this Jude is the one called the brother of James and of the Lord); for through the voice of Jude the Holy Spirit pointed them out as corrupted and corrupting like brute beasts, as he says, “Whatever they do not know, they are caught in their ignorance; and whatever they know, they are corrupted in like the irrational animals.”

**26,11,4** For they accomplish their corruption after the manner of dogs and swine; for dogs and swine, and indeed other animals too, are corrupted in this way and eat the emission from their bodies.

**26,11,5a** For indeed, in truth, “dreaming, they defile the flesh, set aside lordship and blaspheme glories.”

**26,11,5b** “But Michael the archangel, when disputing with the devil about the body of Moses, did not bring a word of blasphemy, but said, ‘The Lord rebuke you.’”

**26,11,6** “But these by nature blaspheme whatever they do not know”, because they blaspheme the holiest of holy things, which were handed down to us with sanctification, turning them into shamefulness.

**26,11,7** And these are the things they ventured to say against the apostles, in the way that blessed Paul too says, “so that certain persons, [slandering] us, dare to say, ‘Let us do the evil things, so that the good things may come upon us’; their judgement is just.”

**26,11,8a** And how many other testimonies might I bring against the blasphemers?

**26,11,8b** For these who corrupt themselves with their own hands, and not only they but also those who approach women, having at length taken their fill of promiscuity with women, burn for one another, men with men, as it is written, “receiving back within themselves the requital owed for their error”.

**26,11,8c** For these, having become utterly abandoned, congratulate one another, as having taken up the preferred rank.

**26,11,9a** But also, deceiving the female sex that is persuaded by them, “laden with sins and led away by various desires”, they tell the women they have deceived that such-and-such a woman is a virgin, she who has been corrupted for so many years and is being corrupted every day.

**26,11,9b** For among them lust has no satiety; but the more obscene a man is among them, the more praiseworthy he is among them.

**26,11,10** And they say that those women are virgins who have never, in the worldly intercourse of marriage according to the nature of custom, gone as far as the deposit of seed, but who are always having intercourse and fornicating, yet before the pleasure is completed withdraw the evil-working corrupter of their union and take the shamefulness mentioned before for food,

**26,11,11** as in the likeness [of the] wickedness of Shelah towards Tamar, [...] but having taken up from girlhood this art of being corrupted, yet of not receiving the mingling and the emission of the corruption.

**26,11,12** And they blaspheme not only Abraham and Moses and Elijah and the whole choir of the prophets, but also the God who chose them.

**26,12,1a** And countless other writings have been fabricated and ventured among them.

**26,12,1b** For they say that there is a book, the Birth of Mary, in which they insert certain terrible and pernicious things and speak them there.

**26,12,2a** For on this account, he says, Zacharias was put to death in the temple, since, he says, he had beheld a vision, and, wanting to describe the vision, he had his mouth shut through fear.

**26,12,2b** For at the hour of the incense, he says, while he was offering incense, he saw a man standing, he says, who had the form of an ass.

**26,12,3a** And when he came out, he says, and wanted to cry out, “Woe to you, whom do you worship?”

**26,12,3b** The one who had appeared to him within the temple closed his mouth, so that he should be unable to speak.

**26,12,3c** But once his mouth was opened for him to speak, he then disclosed it to them, and they put him to death.

**26,12,3d** And so, he says, Zacharias died.

**26,12,4** For this is why the priest was ordered by the lawgiver himself, he says, to have bells, so that whenever he went in to perform his priestly service, the one who was worshipped might hear the sound and hide, so that the phantom face of his form might not be detected.

**26,12,5a** But the whole contrivance of their silliness is easily refuted and full of absurdity.

**26,12,5b** For if the one served in worship was visible at all, he could not hide either; and if he could hide at all, he was no longer visible.

**26,12,6a** And again it must be said to them in another way that if he was visible, he was in that case a body and no longer a spirit; but if he was a spirit, he is no longer reckoned among visible things.

**26,12,6b** How then did what is not reckoned among visible things take thought, at the sound of the bells, to withdraw itself?

**26,12,6c** For, being invisible by nature, it would not have been seen unless it wished.

**26,12,7a** And even if it was seen, it did not appear under the compulsion of a nature leading it to appear, but by grace, not through fear and through being caught unawares against expectation without the sound, as it brought its own vision into plain view.

**26,12,7b** And so in every way their false and fabricated story has collapsed.

**26,12,8a** And there are many other things they say that are mere drivel.

**26,12,8b** [...] And yet Zacharias was not killed at once, but lived on after the birth of John and prophesied both about John and about the Lord’s coming and his fleshly birth of Mary, the holy virgin, by the Holy Spirit — for he says, “And you, little child, will be called a prophet of the Most High;”

**26,12,9a** “for you will go ahead of the Lord’s face to make ready his ways”, “to turn the fathers’ hearts towards the children, and the disobedient to prudence”, and what follows.

**26,12,9b** And how many other things could one say about their false speaking and their pollution?

**26,13,1a** And those who are called Levites among them do not have intercourse with women, but have intercourse with one another.

**26,13,1b** And these, supposedly, are the preferred and praiseworthy ones among them.

**26,13,1c** And they go on to ridicule those who practise the way of life, and purity and virginity, as people who have taken up their labour in vain.

**26,13,2a** They also bring forward a gospel fabricated in the name of Philip the holy disciple, to this effect, he says: “The Lord revealed to me what the soul must say as it ascends to heaven, and how it is to answer every one of the powers above: ‘I have come to know myself,’ it says, ‘and I have gathered myself from every side, and I have not sown children for the archon, but I have uprooted his roots and gathered the scattered members, and I know who you are.’”

**26,13,2b** “‘For I,’ it says, ‘am of those from above.’”

**26,13,2c** And so, he says, it is set free.

**26,13,3** But if, he says, it is found to have borne a son, it is held fast below until it is able to take up its own children and bring them back to itself.

**26,13,4** And so drivelling and fabulous are their notions that they venture to blaspheme even concerning the holy Elijah, and to say, he says, that on being taken up he was thrown back down into the world.

**26,13,5a** For there came, he says, a female demon, who took hold of him and said to him, “Where are you going?”

**26,13,5b** “For I have children by you, and you cannot go up and leave your children here.”

**26,13,5c** And, he says, he replies, “How do you have children by me, when I was living in purity?”

**26,13,5d** She answers, he says, “Yes; when you were dreaming dreams and were often emptied in the emission of bodies, it was I who received the seed from you and bore you sons.”

**26,13,6a** Great is the folly of those who say such things.
