**26,13,6b** For how can a demon, being an invisible and incorporeal spirit, receive [from] bodies?

**26,13,6c** And if it does receive from bodies and conceives, it is no longer a spirit, but a body.

**26,13,6d** And being a body, how is it invisible and a spirit?

**26,13,7a** And great is the absurdity of their foolish talk.

**26,13,7b** For they wish, supposedly, to draw over rather to their own side the testimony against them from the epistle of Jude, in the words, “And these, dreaming, defile the flesh, set aside lordship and blaspheme glories.”

**26,13,7c** But the blessed Jude, the brother of the Lord, did not speak of people dreaming in their bodies; for he at once adds and shows that he is speaking of those who dream [...], who speak their words as though from dreams and not in the sober wakefulness of their reasonings.

**26,13,8a** For Isaiah too says about the teachers in Jerusalem, “All are dumb dogs, unable to bark, dreaming of a bed”, and so on; and here too, in the epistle of Jude, he shows it by saying, “speaking of things they do not know”.

**26,13,8b** And he showed that he is not speaking of the dreaming of sleep, but of their mythical drama and nonsense, as being uttered as though in sleep and not from a sound mind.

**26,14,1a** But I am truly worn out with telling it all.

**26,14,1b** For it belongs to God alone to shut up the depth of this stench.

**26,14,1c** And I shall pass on from the place, having prayed to God, the King of all, that no one may be stuck fast in the mire, nor take in even the notion of its stinking impurity.

**26,14,2** For first the apostle Paul cuts out their whole root of wickedness, giving instruction about younger widows: “Refuse younger widows,” he says, “for when they grow wanton against Christ they wish to marry, incurring condemnation because they have set their first faith aside”; “but let them marry, beget children, manage their households.”

**26,14,3a** If, however, the apostle bids them bear children, while these people refuse the bearing of children, the undertaking is of the serpent and of evil teaching.

**26,14,3b** For, overcome by the pleasure of fornication, they devise for themselves pretexts for impurity, so that they may seem to carry out their own licentiousness with full assurance.

**26,14,4** And truly these things ought neither to be spoken nor deemed worthy of being written in treatises, but buried like some corpse that stinks and breathes out a pestilential reek, so that it may not harm people through their hearing as well.

**26,14,5a** And if such a heresy had passed away and no longer existed in the world, it would have been good to bury it and say nothing whatever about it.

**26,14,5b** But since it both exists and is practised, and we were urged by your honours to speak about them all, I have of necessity recounted parts of it, so that I might not silence the love of truth, but set it out, to turn away those who hear and to bring out those who practise it.

**26,14,6** For from what quarter are we unable to show their murders and monstrous deeds and rites of the devil, given to the thunderstruck through the inspiration of the devil himself?

**26,15,1a** And so they are refuted straightaway on the point they fancy and plead concerning the tree spoken of in the first psalm, that “it will yield its fruit in its season, and its leaf shall not fall off”.

**26,15,1b** For higher up it is set down, “In the Lord’s law is his will, and on his law he shall meditate by day and by night”.

**26,15,2a** These men, however, deny both the law and the prophets.

**26,15,2b** And if they deny the law of the Lord, they slander along with the law the one who spoke in the law as well; they go astray in their supposition and have fallen away from the truth, and they neither acknowledge a judgement nor confess a resurrection.

**26,15,3** And whatever they do in the body, as they become filled with pleasure, this they reap as their fruit, raving in Bacchic frenzy with the pleasures and desires of the devil, so that they are refuted wholly and everywhere by the word of truth;

**26,15,4a** for John says, “If anyone comes,” he says, “to you and does not bring this teaching”.

**26,15,4b** And which teaching, he says?

**26,15,4c** “If anyone does not confess Christ come in the flesh, he is antichrist”, “and now many antichrists have come”, since those who do not confess Christ come in the flesh are antichrists.

**26,15,5** But the Saviour himself also says, “Those who have been counted worthy of the kingdom of the heavens do not marry and are not given in marriage, but are the equals of angels”.

**26,15,6** And not only so, but also, to show plain purity and the holiness that comes through living apart, he says to Mary, “Do not touch me; for I have not yet ascended to the Father”, to show purity as having no share in bodies and unmixed.

**26,15,7** But in another place too the Holy Spirit, prophesying in advance with a view to those of former times and [those] afterwards, says, “Blessed is the barren woman who is undefiled, who has not known a bed in transgression, and the eunuch who has not wrought lawlessness with his hands”, to cut off the obscenities done with the hands through their myth.

**26,16,1** And how many other things there are to say, as when the apostle says in one place, “The unmarried man and the virgin care for the things of the Lord, how they may please the Lord” — and this he says to show purity in truth, being directed by the Holy Spirit not in passing — and then says about those who have honourable marriage, “Marriage is honourable and the bed undefiled, but God will judge fornicators and adulterers”.

**26,16,2** But he also cries out against them when writing to the Romans, uncovering the shameful things of those who do evil deeds, saying, “For their females exchanged the natural use for that which is against nature”, and about the males, that “males with males committing indecency”;

**26,16,3** but also in the epistle to Timothy he says about these people, “In the last days hard times will set in; for human beings will be pleasure-lovers”, and again, “men who forbid marriage, cauterized in their conscience”,

**26,16,4** because they forbid marrying with self-control and bearing children, but are burned in their conscience, indulging lust and corrupting themselves, while forbidding the bearing of children.

**26,16,5** For indeed the so-called sacrifice itself among them, being a filthy thing, is already shown from long ago through the prophet to be the flesh of a serpent and not of the Lord (God forbid!), when he speaks thus, “You crushed the head of the dragon and gave him as food to the peoples, the Ethiopians”.

**26,16,6** For in truth the abominable religion of these people is food of the serpent, and Ethiopians blackened by sin are those who perform this rite of Zeus, now a demon but formerly a sorcerer,

**26,16,7a** who is vainly regarded as a god by some.

**26,16,7b** For all the heresies, gathering their error to themselves out of Greek myths, have transformed it, recasting it into another and worse sense.

**26,16,8a** For the poets bring in Zeus as swallowing Metis, his own daughter.

**26,16,8b** But no one could swallow an infant, as the holy Clement too said, mocking the obscenity of the gods of the Greeks, that in swallowing Metis he could not have swallowed the infant, but his own seed, [...] signifying.

**26,17,1a** But what else should I say?

**26,17,1b** Or how shall I shake off this muddy labour, wishing to speak and yet not wishing, but compelled, so that I may not seem to conceal anything that is the case, and afraid lest by revealing their horrors I either defile or wound, or spur on those who are led away by pleasures and desires to busy themselves with this?

**26,17,2** Yet may I be innocent, and the whole hope of the holy catholic church, and all who read this writing, of such a sowing and evil contrivance of the devil;

**26,17,3** for if I undertake to tell the other things said and done among them, all that are of this kind and so many, and things still greater than these and lesser, and wish also to set against each statement, like antidotes, a protective remedy of healing, I shall drive the work of this treatise to a great bulk.

**26,17,4** For I myself, beloved, also came upon this heresy, and from the very mouths of those who practise it I was in fact instructed in these things in person; and women deceived in this way not only offered us this talk and revealed such things to us, but, like that Egyptian woman, the chief cook’s wife, deadly and wicked, they too, with unbridled boldness of tongue, tried to drag us down in our youth, having desired us.

**26,17,5a** But he who then stood by the holy Joseph stood by us too.

**26,17,5b** And we, the unworthy and incapable, having called upon him who rescued that man there, obtained mercy and escaped their deadly hands, and singing a hymn to the all-holy God, we also could say, “To the Lord let us sing, for gloriously has he been glorified; horse and rider he has thrown into the sea”.

**26,17,6a** For we were saved not by a power like that of his righteousness, but by obtaining mercy through our groaning to God.

**26,17,6b** For when I was reproached by these very deadly women, I laughed, as such women intimated to one another (jesting, as it were), “We could not save the young man, but have left him to perish in the hands of the archon”;

**26,17,7a** for whichever of them is more beautiful offers herself as a bait, as it were, so that they claim that through her they save, instead of destroying, those who are deceived.

**26,17,7b** And then the ugly one is reproached by the more beautiful ones: “I,” she says, “am a vessel of election, able to save those who are deceived, but you could not.”

**26,17,8a** Now the women who expounded this seductive myth were most comely in the form of their appearance, but in the mind of their wickedness they possessed the whole ugliness of the devil.

**26,17,8b** But the merciful God rescued us from their wickedness, so that, after we had read their books as well and had fixed our mind on the truth and were not carried away with them, we escaped and were not caught by the bait,

**26,17,9** and at that time we made it our business to point them out to the bishops in that place and to expose [the] names hidden in the church, [so that] they were driven out of the city, about eighty names, and the city was cleansed of their tare-like and thorny growth.

**26,18,1** And someone who remembers our promise above may perhaps also give praise, since we announced beforehand that some of them we had encountered, some we had learned of through writings, and some through the reports and testimonies of trustworthy men able to show us the truth; so that here too, out of love of truth, we have not passed over the matter, but have shown that this is one of the heresies we encountered.

**26,18,2** And we have been able to speak about it clearly, not from having done these things (God forbid!), but having learned them accurately [we have come to know them] from those who tried to persuade us to it and did not prevail, but lost their hope of our destruction and did not attain the aim of the scheme attempted against our wretched soul by them and by the devil in them,

**26,18,3** so that we too can say in harmony with the most holy David, “Their blows have become the arrows of children”, and so on, and, “Their toil shall return upon their head, and their wrongdoing shall come down upon their crown”.

**26,18,4** So as we, having encountered them and escaped, having read, known and condemned, and having been saved, have passed by, so we exhort you too, the reader, having read and condemned the [...], to pass by, so as not to fall into the venom of the wickedness of these reptiles.

**26,18,5** But if you should ever encounter any from this snakelike school, at once take up the wood prepared for us through the Lord, on which Christ our Lord was fixed, [and], striking at once at the serpent’s head, say, “Christ was crucified for us, leaving us a pattern” of salvation;

**26,18,6a** for he would not have been crucified had he not had flesh.

**26,18,6b** But having flesh and being crucified, he crucified our sins.

**26,18,6c** By faith I hold fast to the truth, and I am not led away by error, by the spuriousness of the serpent and the whispering of his teaching.

**26,19,1** Having passed by this one, then, beloved, I shall go on in order to the other rough paths, not in order to walk them, but to point out from afar to those who wish it, so that they may recognise the roughest, but flee by the narrow and hard-pressed way that carries one to eternal life, and forsake the broad and spacious way, thorny and full of stumbling blocks, miry and filled with licentiousness and fornication,

**26,19,2** the likeness of whose fornication and licentiousness can be seen in the most terrible reptile, to which the ancients gave the name ‘the viper that knows no labour’.

**26,19,3a** For such a viper’s nature resembles these people’s wickedness.

**26,19,3b** For they perform the shameful act through the male or female nature, yet forbid this deposit of seed, doing away with the bearing of children given by God to his creatures, as the apostle too declares, “for receiving back in themselves the requital which their error deserved”, and the rest.

**26,19,4** For thus, he says, when the viper that knows no labour is charmed by the passion of desire, the female towards the male and the male towards the female, and they have entwined together, the male thrusts his head through the mouth of the gaping female, and she, seized by passion, cuts off his head, and so, swallowing the venom that has dripped from his mouth, she conceives within herself such a pair, male and female;

**26,19,5a** and this pair, grown large in the belly and having no way to be born, pierces the side of its mother and so is born, with the result that both their father and their mother perish.

**26,19,5b** That is why they gave it the name ‘knowing no labour’, because it does not experience the pangs of labour.

**26,19,6a** And it is terrible and fearful beyond all reptiles, working destruction within itself and receiving the shamefulness through the mouth; and this crazed heresy is like it, and having here crushed its head and body and the brood born of it by means of the wood of life, let us advance to the examination of the rest, invoking God as our helper.

**26,19,6b** To him be honour and might to the ages of ages, amen.

### Sect 27: Carpocratians

Against the Carpocratians, the seventh, but the twenty-seventh in the series.

**27,1,1** Another, one Carpocrates, arises, who set up for himself a lawless school of his falsely named opinion, a man whose ways were worse than those of all.

**27,1,2** For from all these, Simon and Menander, Satornilus and Basilides and Nicolaus and Carpocrates himself, and further from the pretext of Valentinus, the heresy of knowledge falsely so called sprang up, which named its adherents Gnostics; from it come the Gnostics already described by me, who are condemnable in their way of life.

**27,2,1** This man, again, speaks of one first principle above, and, like the others, wishes to bring in a Father of the universe, unknown and unnameable; and he says that the world and what is in the world came into being through angels who stand far below the unknown Father; for he says that these revolted from the power above and so made the world.

**27,2,2a** He says that our Lord Jesus was begotten from Joseph, in the same manner as all mankind too were born of the seed of a man and a woman.

**27,2,2b** And he was like everyone else, but excelled in his life, in self-control and virtue and a life of righteousness.

**27,2,3** But since, he says, he had a soul more vigorous than other men, and remembered the things seen by it above when it was in the revolution of the unknown Father, powers were sent, he says, by the same Father into his soul,

**27,2,4** so that, recalling the things it had seen and being strengthened, it might escape the world-making angels by passing through all the affairs in the world and the deeds done by men, and the absurd and lawless works done in secret,

**27,2,5** and so that, set free through all the deeds, the same soul of Jesus, he says, might ascend to the same unknown Father, who had sent powers to it from above, in order that, having passed through all the deeds and been set free, it might go through to him above.

**27,2,6** And not only so, but souls like it too, [those] that embrace the same things as it, are set free in the same way and fly up to the unknown Father, in that, having done all the deeds [and] likewise having been rid of all things, they are at last set free.

**27,2,7** And the soul of Jesus, brought up in the customs of the Jews, despised them and for this reason received powers, through which, having been able to perform the passions that attach to men for their punishment, it had strength to pass beyond the world-makers.

**27,2,8** And not only was the soul of Jesus itself able to do this, but also the soul that is able to pass through [all] the deeds can pass beyond the world-making angels themselves; and it too, if it receives powers and does the like, [...], just as that of Jesus, as I said before.

**27,2,9** Hence these people, deceived by this deceiver, have driven on to great conceit and consider themselves superior even to Jesus himself.

**27,2,10** Others of them say that they are superior not to Jesus but to Peter and Andrew and Paul and the rest of the apostles, because of the excellence of their knowledge and the greater abundance of their performance of various courses; and others of them claim that they do not differ in any way from our Lord Jesus Christ.

**27,2,11a** For the souls are from the same revolution, and, having likewise made their contempt of all things like that of Jesus, [...].

**27,2,11b** [For] all souls, he says, have been deemed worthy of the same power of which that of Jesus too has been deemed worthy; that is why they say that these too pass through every deed, just as that of Jesus, of course, has passed through.

**27,2,11c** And if anyone, again, should be able to despise more than Jesus, he will be superior to him.

**27,3,1a** Those of this lawless school set their hands to all kinds of undertakings of terrible and pernicious deeds.

**27,3,1b** For magic arts have been devised among them, and they have invented various incantations for every contrivance, [both] love potions and spells of attraction.

**27,3,1c** And moreover they draw familiar demons to themselves, so as [to come] through much sorcery into great authority over all things, [so that], he says, each may be lord over whatever he wishes and over whatever deed he may dare to undertake;

**27,3,2** deceiving themselves, of course, to give full assurance to their blinded mind, that the [souls] which undertake such things, having prevailed through such deeds and having despised the world-making angels and the things in the world, pass beyond the authority of their myth-makers (for I would not say world-makers), so that they may embrace the freedom above and gain the flight upwards.

**27,3,3a** And they have been prepared and put forward by Satan for the reproach and scandal of the church of God.

**27,3,3b** For they gave themselves the name of Christians, Satan having contrived this so that the Gentiles might be scandalised through them and turn away from the benefit of the holy church of God and from the true preaching because of their lawless deeds and incurable evil practices,

**27,3,4** so that the Gentiles, observing the successive works of their lawless deeds and supposing that those of the holy church of God are such too, might turn their ears, as I said before, away from the true teaching of God, or else, seeing some [...], might blaspheme all [of us] alike.

**27,3,5** And for this reason most of the Gentiles, wherever they see such people, neither approach us for fellowship in giving and receiving, or in counsel, or in hearing the divine word, nor lend us their ear, being frightened off by the unholy practice of the lawless.

**27,4,1a** These people pass their lives in profligacy and do anything whatever for the comfort of their bodies, but do not approach us at all, except to entice unstable souls with their evil teaching.

**27,4,1b** For in nothing do they resemble us, except that they pride themselves on being called by the name, so that by means of the name they may go hunting with the counterfeit of their own wickedness.

**27,4,2** But “their judgement”, according to what stands written, “is just”, as the holy apostle Paul declared; for on account of their evil deeds the recompense will be paid back to them.

**27,4,3a** For having fearlessly given up their mind to frenzy, they have handed themselves over to the passions of countless pleasures.

**27,4,3b** For they say that whatever is thought among men to be evil is not evil but good by nature (for nothing is evil by nature), but is thought by men to be bad.

**27,4,4** And if anyone does all these things in this one sojourn, his soul is no longer re-embodied so as to be cast down again, but, having done every deed at once, it will be released, set free and no longer owing anything of what pertains to action in the world.

**27,4,5a** But what deeds these are, I am again afraid to say, lest I uncover a channel as of covered filth and seem to some to produce a surfeit of pestilential stench.

**27,4,5b** But nevertheless, since we are constrained by the truth to reveal what is found among the deceived, I shall compel myself both to speak with some decency and not to step outside the truth.

**27,4,6** What, indeed, but that these people practise every unspeakable and lawless deed, which it is not lawful to bring to the lips, and every kind of intercourse of men with men and of more lustful dealings with women in every member of the body;

**27,4,7** and performing acts of magic and sorcery and idolatry, they say that this is the work of discharging the debts in the body, so that they are no longer accused nor will any deed of action be demanded of them, and that for this reason the soul is not turned back after its departure from here to pass again into re-embodiment and transfusion.

**27,5,1** For their treatises are such that a sensible man, on reading them, is amazed and astounded and cannot believe that these things would be done by human beings, not only by those who live in cities as we do, but even by those among wild beasts and like wild beasts and cattle [...], and who dare, one might almost say, to do the deeds of dogs and swine.

**27,5,2** For they say that one must by all means make every use of these things, so that the souls, on departing, may not for this reason, if they have fallen short of some deed, be cast back again into bodies to do once more what they did not do.

**27,5,3** And this, they say, is what Jesus said in the gospel through the parable, “Be well disposed towards your adversary while you are on the way with him, and make every effort to be rid of him, lest the adversary hand you over to the judge and the judge to the officer, and the officer throw you into prison; truly I say to you, you will not come out from there until you have paid the last farthing”.

**27,5,4** But these same people fabricate a myth to explain this parable, and say that that adversary is one among the angels who made the world, and that he was made for this very purpose, to lead away to the judge the souls that depart from here out of their bodies and are examined there; and that if they have not done every work, they are handed over by the archon to the officer.

**27,5,5a** And the officer is an angel who serves the world-making judge, to bring the souls back again and to pour them into different bodies.

**27,5,5b** And this adversary, whom we said the Lord spoke of in the gospel, these people say is an angel, one of the world-makers, who has the name Devil.

**27,5,6a** For the prison, they say, is the body, while by the last farthing they mean the re-embodiment; [...] in each sojourn in bodies to complete the last deed and no longer be left short of doing anything lawless.

**27,5,6b** For it must, they say, as we said before, having passed through all things and done each one and been set free, go up to the unknown one above, having passed beyond the world-makers and the world-maker.

**27,5,7** And again they say that, if they have done it in a single sojourn of re-embodiment, they must then depart above, set free; but if they do not do it in a single sojourn, they are at last set free when, in each sojourn of re-embodiment, they have done little by little the work of every lawless deed.

**27,5,8** And again they say, “We deem it right to recount these things to the worthy, so that they may do what seem to be evils but are not evil by nature, so that, having learned, they may be set free.”

**27,5,9** These followers of Carpocrates put a seal, with a branding iron or by the use of a razor or a needle, on the right lobe of the ear of those who are deceived by them.

**27,6,1** A certain Marcellina, deceived by them, has already somehow come to our notice, who corrupted many in the times of Anicetus, bishop of Rome, who came after the succession of Pius and those before.

**27,6,2** For in Rome the first were the apostles Peter and Paul themselves, who were bishops as well; next came Linus, after him Cletus, and then Clement, a contemporary of Peter and of Paul, of whom Paul makes mention in his epistle to the Romans.

**27,6,3** And let no one wonder that others received the episcopate from the apostles before him, although he was a contemporary of Peter and Paul; for he too is a contemporary of the apostles.

**27,6,4** Whether, then, he received the laying on of hands for the episcopate from Peter while they were still living, and declined it and remained inactive — for he says in one of his epistles, “I step aside, I go away; let God’s people remain in good order”, giving this advice to certain persons (for we have found this set down in certain memoirs) — or whether he was appointed by Cletus the bishop after the death of the apostles, we do not know very clearly.

**27,6,5** Nevertheless, even so it was possible, while the apostles were still living, I mean Peter and Paul, for other bishops to be appointed, because the apostles often set out on journeys to other countries for the preaching of Christ, while the city of the Romans could not be without a bishop.

**27,6,6a** For Paul even reaches Spain, while Peter often visited Pontus and Bithynia.

**27,6,6b** And it was possible that, after Clement had been appointed and had declined (if indeed it happened so; for I am conjecturing, not asserting), he was later, after the death of Linus and Cletus, who had each held the episcopate for twelve years following the death of the holy Peter and Paul, which took place in the twelfth year of Nero, compelled to take up the episcopate again.

**27,6,7a** However that may be, the succession of the bishops in Rome follows this sequence: Peter and Paul, Linus as well as Cletus, Clement, Evaristus, Alexander, Sixtus, Telesphorus, Hyginus, Pius, Anicetus, the one already named above in the list.

**27,6,7b** And let no one wonder that we have gone through each detail so exactly; for through these things what is clear is always shown.

**27,6,8a** In the times of Anicetus, then, as we said, the Marcellina mentioned before came to Rome, vomited forth the corruption of the teaching of Carpocrates, and corrupted and destroyed many of those there.

**27,6,8b** And from there came the beginning of the so-called Gnostics.

**27,6,9** They have images painted in colours, and some have them made of gold, silver and other materials, which they claim to be likenesses of Jesus, and that these were made by Pontius Pilate, that is, the likenesses of the same Jesus when he sojourned among the race of men.

**27,6,10a** They keep such images secretly, and also those of certain philosophers, Pythagoras and Plato and Aristotle and the rest, and with these philosophers they also place other likenesses of Jesus; and having set them up, they worship them and perform the mysteries of the Gentiles.

**27,6,10b** For having set up these images, they then practise the customs of the Gentiles.

**27,6,10c** And what are [the] customs of the Gentiles but sacrifices and the rest?

**27,6,11** They say that there is salvation of the soul alone and not of bodies.

**27,7,1a** These people, then, must be overthrown with all our strength; for let no one think little of argument, especially against deceivers.

**27,7,1b** But someone might say, “Are these things not easily detected and full of folly?”

**27,7,1c** But there are times when even foolish things persuade the senseless and lead the intelligent astray, if the mind trained in the truth is not present.

**27,7,1d** Since, then, this man as well has been caught in the sorcery of Simon and the others, we shall overthrow him too with the same arguments.

**27,7,2** For if the unknown and unnameable power was responsible for other angels, then either there is ignorance in this power — in the Father of the universe, that is — supposing he was unaware of what the angels he was fashioning were about to do, not knowing that they would revolt and do what he himself did not wish; or he made them knowing that they would do what he himself does not wish, and it came about through them, and he himself will be the one who, through his knowledge and good pleasure, did what they dared.

**27,7,3** If, then, as I said before, he knew what they would do but did not wish them to do it, on what ground was he creating those who would do the making, for them to carry out what he himself did not wish?

**27,7,4a** But if he himself made the angels so that they might make the things that were made by them, then he wanted these to come into being; and that is why he prepared beforehand the angels who made them.

**27,7,4b** And if he prepared them beforehand for making, yet disowns the things made by them, there would be chicanery in the middle of it.

**27,7,5** But if he allowed them to make, yet wishes to take back to himself the things made by them (that is, men and souls) against their will, then there will be rapacity in the middle of it and nothing else, since the men [...] by the angels are snatched away by the one above against their will; and there would be impotence too, since, not being able himself to make for himself, he snatches the things made by those who were made by him.

**27,7,6a** And then it will be a myth and foolish talk, with those below able to surpass those in the middle, and those in the middle punished as being to blame for those below, while those below are saved and set free, beyond those in the middle, to the one above — I mean the souls of those in this creation.

**27,7,6b** And the one above, unable to make, would be judged weak, while those made by him would be [powerful], able to make what he himself did not wish — or wished, and was not able.

**27,7,7a** For what that one longs for cannot be evil to him, nor have been made by evil beings.

**27,7,7b** For if it were evil, it ought to perish.

**27,7,7c** But if any part of the work at all is saved, the work is no longer bad; and if any part of it is found to be saved, neither are those who made it bad, [those] who fashioned what is saved.

**27,7,8a** But if the soul is from angels at all, and receives power from above once it has been brought forth, then surely the angels will all the more obtain salvation, because the soul that comes from them is saved, though it is from bad beings.

**27,7,8b** And once it is saved, neither is the soul itself that was made by the angels bad any longer, nor are the angels from whom the soul itself derives.

**27,8,1** Anyone who has a sound mind must know that this whole charlatan’s workshop is a thing of folly.

**27,8,2a** And these people will be put to shame again by their other arguments too.

**27,8,2b** For if Jesus is not from the virgin Mary, but from the seed of Joseph and the same Mary, and yet he is saved, then those who begot him will also be saved.

**27,8,2c** And if Mary and Joseph are from the demiurge, then they have declared the demiurge creator [of Jesus too], and he who made Joseph and Mary, of whom Jesus has his being from the unknown Father above, will no longer be in deficiency.

**27,8,3** But if Jesus himself also comes from the angels, and the demiurge is himself one of the angels, then surely all of them will fall into the one absurdity — the same into which the angels too have fallen — and the drama they have staged would be incoherent, full of poison and filled with every venomous teaching.

**27,8,4** And having routed this one in its turn, smashing it upon the ground like a serpent’s head with the wood of faith and of truth, let us draw near to the other beast-shaped ones for the destruction [...] of these, and compel ourselves to come on because of the promise.

### Sect 28: Cerinthians (Merinthians)

Against the Cerinthians, or Merinthians, the eighth, but the twenty-eighth in the series.

**28,1,1** Cerinthus in turn, from whom come those called Cerinthians, has arrived from this bestial seed bringing his venom to the world; he brings forth almost nothing different from the aforementioned Carpocrates, but sprouts the same drugs, harmful to the world.

**28,1,2** For this man too, slandering Christ in the same way as the one mentioned before, expounds that Christ was born of Mary and of the seed of Joseph, and that the world likewise was made by angels.

**28,1,3a** For this man differed in nothing from the first in the introduction of his teaching, except in this alone: in adhering to Judaism in part.

**28,1,3b** This man asserts that the law and the prophets were given by angels, and that the one who gave the law was one of the angels by whom the world was made.

**28,1,4** This Cerinthus was in Asia, spending his time there, and it was there that he had made the beginning of his preaching.

**28,1,5** Of him we have said already that he too preached that the world did not come into being from the first and supernal power, and that, after Jesus — who was born of the seed of Joseph and Mary — had grown to maturity, the Christ came down into him from above, from the God on high, that is, the Holy Spirit, in a dove’s form, in the Jordan, and revealed the unknown Father to him and, through him, to those with him.

**28,1,6** And that for this reason, since a power had come into him from above, he performed mighty works; and that when he suffered, what had come from above flew up on high away from Jesus.

**28,1,7** And that Jesus suffered and was raised again, but that the Christ who had come into him from above flew up without suffering — that is, what came down in the form of a dove — and that Jesus is not the Christ.

**28,2,1a** This man too has fallen away, as all you lovers of the truth see.

**28,2,1b** For he asserts that the one who gave the law is not good, and yet he professes to obey his law — plainly as a good one.

**28,2,2a** How, then, has the wicked one given the good law?

**28,2,2b** For if refraining from adultery is good, and refraining from murder is good, how much better would he be who gave the command, if indeed the one who refrains from these acts is acknowledged to be good; and how shall the one who advises the good and bestows a good law be charged with evildoing?

**28,2,2c** But the man is a maniac to attempt such things.

**28,2,3** This man, beloved, is one of those who caused the disturbance in the time of the apostles, when James and those with him wrote the letter to Antioch, saying, “We have learned that certain of our number came to you and unsettled you with words, though we had given them no orders”;

**28,2,4** and this man is one of those who opposed the holy Peter because he had gone in to the holy Cornelius, when Cornelius, having been granted a vision of an angel, sent for him, and Peter hesitated, and he saw the sheet in the vision and the things it contained, and heard the Lord tell him to call nothing common or unclean.

**28,2,5** This man, then, stirred up the crowds of those of the circumcision about Peter, when Peter went up to Jerusalem, saying, “He went in to men who were uncircumcised.”

**28,2,6a** Cerinthus did this before he preached his own preaching in Asia and fell into the deeper pit of his perdition.

**28,2,6b** For since he was circumcised, he hunted for his pretext through circumcision, supposedly for the sake of opposing the believers who were in uncircumcision.

**28,3,1** But since the Lord always takes care of mankind, and secures the clearness of the truth among the sons of the truth, and granted the holy apostle Peter to make the refutation of him and of his followers, the folly of Cerinthus becomes manifest.

**28,3,2a** For the holy Peter says, “I was in the city of Joppa, and in the middle of the day, near the sixth hour, I saw a sheet being lowered, bound at its four corners, and in it were all the four-footed animals and creeping things.

**28,3,2b** And it said to me, ‘Kill and eat.’

**28,3,2c** But I said, ‘By no means, Lord, for nothing common or unclean has ever entered my mouth.’

**28,3,2d** And a voice from heaven answered me a second time, ‘What God has cleansed, you must not call common.’

**28,3,2e** And at once, behold, two men stood at the house, and the Spirit said to me, ‘Go with them, doubting nothing.’”

**28,3,3** And then he relates how these things were said to him in a parable, and how he hesitated at that time, until the Lord plainly showed him what he had been teaching him through the words and figures.

**28,3,4a** For no sooner had he opened his mouth, on his arrival at Caesarea, than the Holy Spirit fell on Cornelius.

**28,3,4b** And seeing this, Peter said, “Can anyone keep back the water from these, who were deemed worthy to receive the Holy Spirit just as we were at the beginning?”

**28,3,5a** This whole thing was a mystery and a work of God’s love for mankind, so that both the holy Peter and everyone else might know that it is not from men that the Gentiles’ salvation comes, but from God.

**28,3,5b** For God was beforehand in giving the gift of the Holy Spirit, the angel’s vision, and the acceptance of prayer, fasts and good deeds, so that the apostles — above all the holy Peter, and the other apostles — might not withhold what had been entrusted to them from one truly called by God.

**28,4,1** But these things were done at that time at the instigation of the aforementioned false apostle Cerinthus, just as on another occasion he himself and those with him caused a riot in Jerusalem itself, when Paul went up with Titus, and as this man said, “He has brought uncircumcised men in with him” — speaking here of Titus — “and has defiled,” he says, “the holy place.”

**28,4,2** Therefore Paul too says, “Yet not even Titus, my companion, a Greek though he was, was forced to undergo circumcision; but on account of the false brothers smuggled in, who crept in to spy on the freedom that is ours in Christ — to them we did not give way in submission even for an hour”; and, instructing those in uncircumcision, he said, “Do not have yourselves circumcised; for if you are circumcised, Christ will profit you nothing.”

**28,4,3** Circumcision lasted, serving for a season, until the greater circumcision came — that is, the washing of rebirth — as is evident to everyone and is shown still more clearly by what the apostles said, above all the holy apostle Paul; for he speaks thus: “to them we did not give way in submission even for an hour.”

**28,4,4** Anyone who wishes to consider what was done among the apostles at that time may well wonder how the things wrought in this sect by the inspiration of a spirit of error show the stamp of those who, through these sects, stirred up the agitation among the apostles.

**28,4,5** For after these had fallen away and turned into false apostles, [and] had sent other false apostles, as has already been said, to Antioch at the beginning and to other places, saying, “Unless you are circumcised and keep the law of Moses, you cannot be saved,” no ordinary disturbance arose at that time, as has been said before.

**28,4,6** And these are the ones spoken of by the apostle Paul as “false apostles, crafty workmen, transforming themselves into apostles of Christ.”

**28,5,1a** For they make use of the Gospel according to Matthew — partly, not in full, but because of the genealogy according to the flesh — and they adduce this testimony, again quoting the gospel: “It suffices the disciple that he become as his teacher.”

**28,5,1b** What then, he says?

**28,5,2a** Jesus was circumcised: be circumcised yourself too.

**28,5,2b** Christ, he says, lived according to the law: you too do the same.

**28,5,2c** Hence some of these too, carried off as if by poisons, are persuaded by the plausible arguments because Christ was circumcised.

**28,5,3a** But they reject Paul because he does not comply with circumcision.

**28,5,3b** They even cast him out for having said, “As many of you as are justified by the law have fallen from grace,” and, “If you are circumcised, Christ will profit you nothing.”

**28,6,1** This Cerinthus, a fool and a teacher of fools, makes bold again to assert that Christ suffered and was crucified, but has not yet been raised, and that he is to rise when the universal resurrection of the dead comes about.

**28,6,2a** These things of theirs, then, the words and the thoughts alike, are incoherent.

**28,6,2b** Hence the apostle too, in amazement, said to those who disbelieve the resurrection of the dead that is to come, “If the dead are not raised, neither has Christ been raised,” and, “Let us eat and drink; for we die tomorrow,” and, “Do not be deceived: bad company corrupts good habits.”

**28,6,3a** And again, to those who say that Christ has not yet been raised, he likewise brings the refutation, saying, “If Christ has not been raised, then empty is our preaching, and empty too is our faith.

**28,6,3b** And we are found, moreover, to be false witnesses of God, [since we bore witness against God] that he raised Christ, if indeed he did not raise him”; [...] as though the apostles were preaching that Christ has not yet been raised, and that the dead are not raised.

**28,6,4** For their school flourished greatly in this region — Asia, I mean — and in Galatia as well; and among them a certain matter of tradition has also reached us: that when some among them died too soon, without baptism, others were baptized in their stead in their name, so that, when they rose at the resurrection, they might not pay the penalty of punishment for not having received baptism, and become subject to the authority of the world-maker.

**28,6,5a** And on this account the tradition that has reached us says that the same holy apostle had said, “If the dead are not raised at all, why indeed are they baptized for them?”

**28,6,5b** But others rightly interpret the saying, declaring that those who are about to die, if they are catechumens, are counted worthy of the washing before their death in this hope, showing that the one who has died will also rise, and for this reason needs the remission of sins through the washing.

**28,6,6** Of these, some have proclaimed that Christ has not yet been raised, but will rise with all, and others that the dead will in no way rise again.

**28,6,7** Therefore the apostle, stepping into the middle against both of these and against the other sects, accomplished their overthrow in one labour, in the matter of the resurrection of the dead, and in the testimonies by which, going through them in detail, he made a firm demonstration of the resurrection of the dead, and of salvation and hope,

**28,6,8** declaring, “This perishable thing must clothe itself with imperishability, and this mortal thing must clothe itself with immortality,” and again, “Christ has been raised, firstfruits of those who have fallen asleep,” so that, having refuted both positions of the sects, he might teach from the truth the unstained proclamation of the doctrine to those who wish to know the truth of God and the saving doctrine.

**28,7,1** Cerinthus, then, and those about him would be detected on every side — the wretch who was deceived and has become a cause of destruction to others too — since the divine Scriptures instruct us clearly and in detail about everything.

**28,7,2** For Christ is not from the seed of Joseph; otherwise how could the birth still be a sign, and how will what was spoken by Isaiah still hold good, as he said, “The Lord himself shall give you a sign: see, the virgin will be with child and will bear a son,” and what follows;

**28,7,3** and how will what was said by the holy virgin to Gabriel still be fulfilled, “How shall this be, since I do not know a man?” — and his saying, “The Holy Spirit shall come upon you, and the power of the Most High shall overshadow you,” and so on —

**28,7,4** and how, again, will their folly not be refuted, when the gospel says plainly, “She was found to be with child before they came together”?

**28,7,5a** And it can be clearly known that they never came together at all.

**28,7,5b** For God forbid that this should be said, since otherwise he would not, after the cross, have made provision to commit her to the holy virgin John, as he says, “Behold your mother,” and to her, “Behold your son.”

**28,7,6a** For he ought to have handed her over to her own kinsfolk, or to the children of Joseph, if indeed they had been hers — I mean James and Joses and Judas and Simeon, who were sons of Joseph by another wife.

**28,7,6b** For he had no relations with the virgin at all, God forbid; for the virgin is found undefiled after having given birth.

**28,7,7** But these matters have already been treated clearly by me in another discourse, and will be treated again; here something has been said in part, as in a digression, about their subject, so that, while wishing to provide a cure for other bites and a remedy and antidote for other poisons, I may not [...] turn my readers aside to other matters.

**28,7,8** However, to anyone who possesses understanding their ridiculous teaching will be shown in every way to be vain, refuted by the apostles, condemned by the wise, and cast out by God and by his own proclamation of the truth.

**28,8,1a** These people are also called Merinthians, as the report that has come to us contains.

**28,8,1b** For whether Cerinthus himself was also known as Merinthus — about this we know nothing very clearly — or whether some other man by the name of Merinthus was a fellow worker of his, God knows.

**28,8,2a** For we have already said that not only did he himself often oppose the apostles in Jerusalem, but those with him did so too, in Asia as well; but whether it was he himself, or another fellow worker with him, thinking the same as he and acting with him to the same ends, makes no difference.

**28,8,2b** For the whole perversity of their teaching bears this stamp, and these people are called Cerinthians and Merinthians alike.

**28,8,3** And having gone through these things concerning this dreadful and reptilian wickedness, we proceed once more to those that follow, giving thanks that we have swum unharmed through the sea of these evil doctrines, and praying that, as we come upon those that follow, we may not be harmed as though falling upon stormy and beast-infested shoals, but may obtain the salvation that comes from the truth which is to be revealed to us in the course of our account, as we set forth both the truth itself and the empty babble raised against it.

**28,8,4** For to one who wishes to look into their forms and to record them, this sect too will be likened to a two-headed snake indeed, because of its double name, and to the viper called the sepedon, which has its whole body covered with red hair, yet has the nature or hide neither of a goat nor of a sheep but of a reptile, and works ruin through its bite on those who come upon it,

**28,8,5** overturning those who trust it, now obliterating the things of the new covenant on the strength of the old worship, now spreading, through false words as though from the new covenant, its slanders against the apostles who came from the circumcision to believe in Christ; and having struck and crushed its putrefaction, its venom and its teeth with the wood of the truth, let us hasten, as I said before, in the power of God to go through those that come after.

### Sect 29: Nazoraeans

Against the Nazoraeans, the ninth, but the twenty-ninth in the series.

**29,1,1a** The Nazoraeans follow next after these; they existed at the same time as these, whether before them, alongside them or after them, but in any case as contemporaries; for I cannot say more precisely which succeeded which.

**29,1,1b** For, as I said, they were contemporaries of one another and hold opinions similar to one another.

**29,1,2** For these did not call themselves by Christ’s name, nor by the very name of Jesus, but by that of Nazoraeans.

**29,1,3** At that time all Christians were likewise called Nazoraeans; and for a short time they also came to be called Jessaeans, before the disciples first took the name of Christians at Antioch.

**29,1,4** They were called Jessaeans, I suppose, on account of Jesse, since David was from Jesse, and Mary was descended by succession of seed from David, in fulfilment of the divine Scripture, for in the Old Testament the Lord says to David, “Of the fruit of your body I will set upon your throne.”

**29,2,1** But I am afraid, with every subject arising from a word, as with this subject that I am treating, when the truth moves me to disclose the insights contained in the word itself, of giving great length to the composition of my account.

**29,2,2** For when the Lord has told David, “Of the fruit of your body I will set upon your throne,” and, “The Lord has sworn to David, and he will not repent,” it is clear that God’s promise cannot be changed.

**29,2,3a** And first, as to the oath: what is an oath with God, but the words, “By myself I have sworn, says the Lord”?

**29,2,3b** “For God has no one greater by whom to swear”; indeed the Godhead does not even swear, but the word has its force as a demonstration of certainty.

**29,2,3c** For with an oath the Lord swore to David that of the fruit of his body he would set upon his throne.

**29,2,4** And the apostles testify that the Christ had to be born of the seed of David, as indeed our Lord and Saviour Jesus Christ was born; but I shall pass over the multitude of testimonies, so as not to swell my discourse to a great bulk, as I said before.

**29,2,5a** But perhaps someone may say: since Christ was born, as to the flesh, from the seed of David — from Mary the holy virgin, I mean — for what reason does he not sit upon the throne of David?

**29,2,5b** For “they came,” says the gospel, “to anoint him king, and knowing it he withdrew,” “and hid in Ephraim, a city of the wilderness.”

**29,2,6a** Now that we have reached the place of this text, and are asked about this testimony and this question — for what reason the sitting upon the throne of David has not been fulfilled in the Saviour according to the flesh (for some have supposed that this was not fulfilled) — we shall nevertheless say how it is.

**29,2,6b** For no word of the holy Scripture of God fails.

**29,3,1** For the throne and royal seat of David is the priesthood in the holy church; this dignity, at once royal and high-priestly, the Lord has joined together in one and bestowed on his holy church, transferring into it the throne of David, which does not fail for ever.

**29,3,2** For there the throne of David lasted in succession until Christ himself, the rulers from Judah not failing until he came “for whom the things laid up were kept, and he himself the expectation of the nations,” [as] it says.

**29,3,3a** For the rulers from Judah in succession came to an end at the coming of Christ.

**29,3,3b** For until him [...] leaders, but the order lapsed and [was transferred] from the time of his birth in Bethlehem of Judaea, under Alexander, who was of priestly and royal descent.

**29,3,4** From the time of this Alexander this inheritance lapsed — from the times of Salina, who was also called Alexandra — in the times of Herod the king and of Augustus, emperor of the Romans; and this Alexander also put a diadem upon himself, being one of the anointed ones and the leaders.

**29,3,5** For when the two tribes, the royal and the priestly, had been joined — I mean Judah and Aaron and the whole tribe of Levi — they appointed kings and priests; for nothing went astray from the dark saying of the holy Scripture.

**29,3,6** But from then on Herod, a foreigner, was king, and those descended from David no longer put on the diadem.

**29,3,7** And when the royal seat had passed over, in Christ the royal dignity was transferred from the house of the fleshly Judah and Israel to the church, and the throne is established in the holy church of God for ever, holding its dignity on two grounds, the royal and the high-priestly —

**29,3,8** the royal deriving from our Lord Jesus Christ on two counts: both because he is of the seed of David the king according to the flesh, and because he is — as indeed he is — a greater king from eternity according to the Godhead; and the priestly because he himself is high priest and chief of high priests — with James at once appointed,

**29,3,9** who was called the brother of the Lord and apostle, as first bishop; he was by nature a son of Joseph, but was called the Lord’s brother in rank because they were brought up together.

**29,4,1** For this James was born to Joseph of his [first] wife and not of Mary, as I have stated in many places and have set out more clearly.

**29,4,2** Moreover we find that he was of David’s line because he was Joseph’s son, and that he became a Nazirite, for he was Joseph’s firstborn and consecrated; and further we have found that he exercised the priesthood after the manner of the old priesthood.

**29,4,3** For this reason he was allowed to go into the Holy of Holies once each year, as the law commanded the high priests according to what is written; for so many before us have recorded concerning him, Eusebius, Clement and others.

**29,4,4** He was even permitted to wear the plate upon his head, as the trustworthy men already mentioned have testified in those same memoirs.

**29,4,5** Our Lord Jesus Christ, then, as I said, is “a priest for ever according to the order of Melchizedek,” and likewise king according to the order that is from above, so that he might transfer the priesthood together with the legislation.

**29,4,6a** And since the seed of David through Mary sits upon a throne, [...] for ever, and there will be no end to his kingdom.

**29,4,6b** For it was needful for him to transfer now the order of the kingship of that time.

**29,4,6c** For indeed his kingdom is not from the earth, as he said in the gospel to Pontius Pilate, “My kingdom is not of this world.”

**29,4,7a** For since Christ was fulfilling all things through enigmas, the foreshadowings reached up to a certain limit.

**29,4,7b** For he who reigns for ever did not come in order to gain advancement in kingship; but he bestowed the kingship on those appointed by him, so that he might not be thought to advance from lesser things to greater.

**29,4,8** For his throne endures, and there will be no end to his kingdom, and he sits upon the throne of David, having transferred the kingship of David and bestowed it, together with the high priesthood, on his own servants, that is, on the high priests of the catholic church.

**29,4,9** Much indeed could be said on this, but nevertheless, since I have arrived at the question of why those who had come to believe in Christ bore the name of Jessaeans before being called Christians, for this reason we said that Jesse is the father of David, and that they were named Jessaeans either on account of this Jesse, or from the name of Jesus our Lord, because they took their origin from Jesus, being his disciples, or from the derivation of the name of the Lord; for Jesus, in the Hebrew tongue, means healer, that is, physician and saviour.

**29,4,10a** Be that as it may, they bore this name as their designation before they were called Christians.

**29,4,10b** But at Antioch, as we mentioned above and as the account of the truth has it, the disciples, and all God’s church with them, began to bear the name of Christians.

**29,5,1** And you will find the account of these too, lover of learning, if you read the writings of Philo in his book entitled On the Jessaeans: how, in describing their way of life and their praises, and in recording their monasteries in the region around the Marean lake, the man wrote about no others but about Christians.

**29,5,2** For he was in that country (they call the place Mareotis), and having lodged with them in the monasteries of that region, he was edified.

**29,5,3a** For being there in the days of the Passover, he observed their ways of life, and how some kept a continuous fast through the holy week of the Passover, while others ate every two days, and others again each evening.

**29,5,3b** And all these things were written up by the man on the subject of the faith and way of life of the Christians.

**29,5,4** While, then, they were called Jessaeans for a short time after the Saviour’s ascension, and after Mark had preached in the land of Egypt, at about this time certain people went out again, followers of the apostles in appearance — I mean the Nazoraeans whom I am describing here — being Jews by race, devoted to the law and keeping circumcision,

**29,5,5** but like people who see a fire from a distance without understanding why those who kindled the pyre did so, or for what use they do it, whether to cook over the fire the provisions of their living, or to destroy some kind of combustible wood or brushwood that is commonly consumed by fire, so these too, in imitation, lit a fire and set themselves alight.

**29,5,6a** For hearing only the name of Jesus, and seeing the divine signs wrought through the apostles’ hands, they too believe in Jesus.

**29,5,6b** And knowing that he was conceived in the womb at Nazareth and brought up in Joseph’s house, and that for this reason the gospel calls him Jesus the Nazoraean, just as the apostles declare: “Jesus the Nazoraean, a man commended by signs and wonders,” and what follows, they take this name for themselves, to be called Nazoraeans; not Nazirites, which is interpreted “consecrated ones.”

**29,5,7** For this was the dignity belonging of old to the firstborn and to those dedicated to God, of whom Samson was one, and many others after him and before him; and John the Baptist too was himself one of these same men consecrated to God; for “he drank no wine or strong drink”; for this way of life, befitting the dignity, was prescribed for such people.

**29,6,1** Nor did they call themselves Nasaraeans; for the sect of the Nasaraeans existed before Christ and did not know Christ —

**29,6,2** moreover, all men called the Christians Nazoraeans, as I said before, as they say in accusing the apostle Paul: “We have found this man a plague, one who turns the people aside, a ringleader of the sect of the Nazoraeans.”

**29,6,3** The holy apostle does not deny the name; he does not confess their sect, but gladly accepts the name that the malice of his opponents had fastened on him on account of Christ.

**29,6,4a** For he says before the judgement seat, “They did not find me in the temple disputing with anyone or stirring up a crowd, nor have I done any of the things of which they accuse me.

**29,6,4b** But this I confess to you, that according to the Way which they call a sect, so I worship, believing all things that are in the law and in the prophets.”

**29,6,5** And it is no wonder that the apostle confessed himself a Nazoraean, since at that time everyone called the Christians by this name on account of the town of Nazareth, the name having no other use at the time; so that people called those who had believed in Christ [Nazoraeans], of whom it is also written, “He shall be called a Nazoraean.”

**29,6,6** For even now people call all the sects by the same name, I mean Manichaeans, Marcionites, Gnostics and others, calling Christians those who are not Christians; and yet each sect, though it goes by another name, gladly accepts this, because it is adorned by the name; for they think they can pride themselves on the name of Christ, though not on faith and works.

**29,6,7** So too the holy disciples of Christ at that time called themselves disciples of Jesus, as indeed they were; but when they heard themselves called Nazoraeans by others they did not refuse it, seeing the intention of those who called them so: that they called them this on account of Christ, since our Lord Jesus himself was called [the] Nazoraean, as is stated in the gospels and in the Acts of the Apostles,

**29,6,8** because he was brought up in Joseph’s house in the town of Nazareth, now a village, having been born according to the flesh at Bethlehem of Mary the ever-virgin, who was betrothed to Joseph; and Joseph had moved to that same Nazareth after he left Bethlehem and settled in Galilee.

**29,7,1** But these aforementioned sectarians, whom I am now describing, setting aside the name of Jesus, have neither called themselves Jessaeans, nor kept the name of Jews, nor taken the name of Christians, but that of Nazoraeans, as if from the name of the place Nazareth; yet in everything they are Jews and nothing else.

**29,7,2a** They make use not only of the New Testament but of the Old as well, as the Jews do.

**29,7,2b** For the Law, the Prophets and the Writings, as the books are called among the Jews, are not rejected among them, as they are by the sects mentioned before; nor do they hold anything different, but they confess everything rightly according to the proclamation of the law, as the Jews do, except that they have, so they say, believed in Christ.

**29,7,3** For among them the resurrection of the dead is confessed, and that all things came into being from God; and they proclaim one God and his child Jesus Christ.

**29,7,4a** They are thoroughly trained in the Hebrew language.

**29,7,4b** For among them the entire Law, the Prophets and what are called the Writings (that is, the books in verse, Kings, Chronicles, Esther and all the others) are read in the Hebrew tongue, as is of course the case among the Jews also.

**29,7,5** In this alone they differ from Jews and Christians: they do not agree with the Jews, because they have believed in Christ; and they are not of one mind with Christians, because they are still bound by the law, by circumcision, the sabbath and the rest.

**29,7,6** Concerning Christ, I cannot say whether they too, led astray by the wickedness of the followers of Cerinthus and Merinthus mentioned above, regard him as merely a man, or whether they hold, as the truth has it, that he was begotten of Mary through the Holy Spirit.

**29,7,7** This sect of the Nazoraeans is found at Beroea in the region of Coele Syria, in the Decapolis in the district of Pella, and in Bashanitis, at a place named Kokabe, which in Hebrew is called Chochabe.

**29,7,8a** For from there it took its beginning, after all the disciples had moved from Jerusalem and were living at Pella, since Christ had told them to leave Jerusalem and withdraw on account of the siege that was coming upon it.

**29,7,8b** For this reason they settled in Peraea and, as I said, lived there.

**29,7,8c** From that point the sect of the Nazoraeans had its beginning.

**29,8,1a** These too are in error, boasting of circumcision; and such people remain “under a curse,” being unable to fulfil the law.

**29,8,1b** How, indeed, can they fulfil the law’s saying, “Three times a year you shall appear before the Lord your God, at Unleavened Bread, at Tabernacles and at Pentecost,” at the place of Jerusalem?

**29,8,2** For since the place has been shut off and the things in the law cannot be fulfilled, it should be plain to anyone of sense that Christ came as the fulfiller of the law, not to abolish the law but to fulfil it, and to remove the curse laid down for the breaking of the law.

**29,8,3** For when Moses had laid down every commandment, he reached the close of the book and “shut up everything under a curse,” saying, “Cursed is everyone who does not abide by all the words written in this book, to do them.”

**29,8,4** He came, then, loosing what had been fettered by the bonds of the curse, and granting us, in place of the small things which could not be fulfilled, the greater things, which do not conflict with one another for the fulfilment of the work, as the former did;

**29,8,5** for so, in every sect, whenever we reached the question of sabbath-keeping and circumcision and the rest, we have often worked out how the Lord has granted us the more perfect things.

**29,8,6** And how will such people be able to make a defence, when they have not heeded the Holy Spirit, who spoke through the apostles to those of the Gentiles who had come to believe, “Lay no burden upon them beyond what is necessary: to abstain from blood, and from what is strangled, and from fornication, and from what is sacrificed to idols”?

**29,8,7** And how will they not fall away from God’s grace, since the holy apostle Paul says, “If you are circumcised, Christ will profit you nothing,” and, “You who boast in the law have fallen from grace”?

**29,9,1a** For this sect, too, my brief treatment will suffice.

**29,9,1b** Such people are easily refuted and readily caught, and are rather Jews than anything else.

**29,9,2a** Yet they are bitterly hated by the Jews.

**29,9,2b** For not only do the children of the Jews bear hatred toward them; the Jews also, rising in the morning, at noon and at evening, three times daily when they say their prayers in the synagogues, call down curses on them and pronounce them anathema, declaring three times a day, “May God curse the Nazoraeans.”

**29,9,3** For they bear a heavier grudge against these, because, being themselves of Jewish origin, they proclaim that Jesus is [the] Christ, which is contrary to those who remain Jews and have refused to receive Jesus.

**29,9,4a** They have the Gospel according to Matthew complete, in Hebrew.

**29,9,4b** For among them it is clearly still preserved, as it was originally written, in Hebrew letters.

**29,9,4c** Whether they have also removed the genealogies from Abraham down to Christ, I do not know.

**29,9,5** But now that we have detected this one too, like a small wasp, feeble yet painful through its venom, and have crushed it with the words of truth, let us proceed to the next ones, dearly beloved, asking God for his help.

### Sect 30: Ebionites

Against the Ebionites, the tenth, but the thirtieth in the series.

**30,1,1** Ebion, from whom the Ebionites take their name, following next in order and holding views similar to these, rose up in his turn upon the world, a many-shaped monster who, so to speak, reproduced in himself the serpent form of the fabled many-headed hydra; he belonged to their school, but preached and taught other things beyond theirs.

**30,1,2** For just as someone might gather for himself an ornament of various precious stones and a garment of many colours and adorn himself conspicuously, so this man, on the contrary, took from each sect every dreadful, destructive and abominable teaching, shapeless and unconvincing and full of rivalry, and modelled himself on them all.

**30,1,3** For he has the loathsomeness of the Samaritans, the name of the Jews, the thinking of the Ossaeans, the Nazoraeans and the Nasaraeans, the shape of the Cerinthians, the perversity of the Carpocratians; and he wishes to have only the title of Christians (certainly not their conduct, their opinion, their knowledge, or the agreement of the gospels and the apostles concerning faith);

**30,1,4** and being, so to speak, in the middle of them all, he is nothing, but in him is fulfilled what is written: “I came close to every evil, in the midst of church and synagogue.”

**30,1,5** For while he is a Samaritan in his loathsomeness, he disowns the name; and while confessing himself a Jew, he opposes the Jews, though agreeing with them in part, as we shall show later in the demonstrations of our refutation of them on this point, with God’s help.

**30,2,1** For this Ebion was a contemporary of these, and he sets out from them, [being] one of them.

**30,2,2** At first he said that Christ was born of sexual union and the seed of a man, that is, of Joseph; as has already been said, in all respects he held the same views as the others and differed in this alone, that he adhered to the law of Judaism in sabbath-keeping, in circumcision and in all the other observances practised among Jews and Samaritans.

**30,2,3a** Moreover, like the Samaritans, he does still more than the Jews.

**30,2,3b** For he added the rule of taking care not to touch anyone of another nation;

**30,2,4** and the rule of immersing himself in water every day, whenever he had been with a woman and had left her, wherever he could find plenty of water, whether the sea or any other.

**30,2,5** Further, if he should meet anyone as he came up from his plunge and immersion in the water, he likewise runs back to immerse himself again, often with his clothes on.

**30,2,6a** But now virginity and continence are altogether forbidden among them, as also among the other sects like this one.

**30,2,6b** For once they took pride in virginity, supposedly because of James the brother of the Lord; [therefore] they also address their writings to elders and virgins.

**30,2,7a** Their origin came after the capture of Jerusalem.

**30,2,7b** For since all who had believed in Christ were at that time living for the most part in Peraea, in a city called Pella of the Decapolis mentioned in the gospel, near Batanaea and the region of Bashanitis, having moved there at that time and staying there, this gave Ebion his opportunity.

**30,2,8a** And he first took up residence in a village called Kokabe in the district of Karnaim, also called Ashtaroth, in the region of Bashanitis, as the information that has reached us relates.

**30,2,8b** From there he begins his evil teaching, from the same place, it seems, as the Nazoraeans whom I described above.

**30,2,9a** For he was joined to them and they to him, and each shared his own wickedness with the other.

**30,2,9b** And they differ from one another in some respect, but in their malice they have taken the impress of one another.

**30,2,9c** I have already spoken at length, in other discourses and in treating the other sects, about the location of Kokabe and of Arabia.

**30,3,1** And at first this Ebion, as I said, defined Christ as from the seed of a man, that is, of Joseph; but from a certain time up to the present his followers, as though they had turned their own mind to what is incoherent and impossible, give accounts of Christ among them, one in one way and another in another;

**30,3,2** and this, I suppose, perhaps from Elchasai the false prophet having joined them — the one among those called Sampsenes, Ossenes and Elkesaeans, [already described by me] — for, as he does, they relate a certain fantasy about Christ and about the Holy Spirit.

**30,3,3** For some of them even say that Christ is Adam, the one who was first formed and was breathed into by the inbreathing of God.

**30,3,4** Others among them, however, say that he is from above, yet was created before all things, being spirit, and higher than angels and ruling over all, and is called Christ, and has been allotted the age that is there;

**30,3,5** but that he comes here whenever he chooses, just as he came in Adam too, and showed himself to the patriarchs, putting on the body; and having come to Abraham and Isaac and Jacob, the same one came at the end of the days and put on the very body of Adam, and was seen by men, and was crucified, and rose, and ascended.

**30,3,6a** And again, when they wish, they say: No, but the Spirit, which is the Christ, came into him and put on the one called Jesus.

**30,3,6b** And there is great darkness among them, as they suppose him to be now one thing and now another.

**30,3,7a** And they too receive the Gospel according to Matthew.

**30,3,7b** For they too, like the followers of Cerinthus and Merinthus, use this alone.

**30,3,7c** And they call it the Gospel according to the Hebrews, as it is true to say, since Matthew alone in the New Testament set forth and proclaimed the gospel in Hebrew and in Hebrew letters.

**30,3,8** And some, too, have said somewhere that the Gospel according to John, translated from the Greek language into Hebrew, is to be found in the treasuries of the Jews — I mean those at Tiberias — and is stored away among the hidden books, as some of those who have believed from among the Jews have related to us in detail;

**30,3,9** and not only this, but report has it that the volume of the Acts of the Apostles too, likewise translated from the Greek tongue into Hebrew, is laid up in the treasuries there, so that through it the Jews who read it — those who related this to us — came to believe in Christ.

**30,4,1** There was among them a certain Josephus — not that writer and historian of old, but the one from Tiberias, [who lived] in the times of the blessed Constantine the elder, the emperor — who also obtained from the emperor himself the rank of count, and received authority to establish a church to Christ in Tiberias itself, as well as in Diocaesarea, in Capernaum and in the others; and he suffered much at the hands of the Jews themselves before he was presented to the emperor.

**30,4,2a** For this Josephus was numbered among the men of rank among them.

**30,4,2b** These are [those] next after the patriarch, called apostles.

**30,4,2c** They attend upon the patriarch, and often spend their time with him continually, by night and by day, in order to advise him and to refer to him the matters of the law.

**30,4,3** The patriarch at that time was named Ellel (for I think that Josephus gave his name thus, unless I am mistaken because of the lapse of time), and he was of the family of Gamaliel, who had been patriarch among them.

**30,4,4** And one may suppose, as others too have assumed, that these were of the family of that first Gamaliel in the Saviour’s time, who in godly fashion counselled abstaining from the abuse of the apostles.

**30,4,5** When Ellel was dying, he made a request of the bishop of that time, who lived near the city of the Tiberians, and received from him the holy washing at his departure, under the pretext of a medical matter.

**30,4,6** For having sent for him through the aforementioned Josephus, as though he were a physician, and having made everyone go outside, he entreated the bishop, saying, “Grant me the seal in Christ.”

**30,4,7a** He called in the attendants and ordered water to be prepared, as though he were going to apply some remedy for the illness by means of water to the patriarch, who was gravely ill.

**30,4,7b** They did what they were ordered; for they did not know.

**30,4,7c** And the patriarch, having made them all go out on the pretext of modesty, is counted worthy of the washing and of the holy mysteries.

**30,5,1a** Josephus himself related [this] to me.

**30,5,1b** For I heard all these things from his own mouth, not from someone else, in the years of his old age, he being about seventy or even older.

**30,5,2a** For I lodged with him at Scythopolis; for he had moved away from Tiberias and had acquired notable properties there in Scythopolis.

**30,5,2b** For in his house the blessed Eusebius, bishop of the city of Brincella in Italy, was being entertained as a guest, when he had been exiled by Constantius for the orthodox faith; and when I and the other brethren came there to visit him, we too lodged with him.

**30,5,3** And meeting Josephus at his house, and asking about his circumstances, and learning that he had been one of the prominent men among the Jews, and inquiring into his story and how he came over to Christianity, we heard all these things clearly, and not from the report of anyone else.

**30,5,4** Hence, judging the man’s experiences deserving of mention for the building up of the faithful, on account of the matter of what had been translated into Hebrew in the treasuries [...], it is not as a side issue that we have set out the whole story of the aforementioned Josephus.

**30,5,5a** For the man had not only been counted worthy to become a faithful Christian, but he also vehemently denounced the Arians.

**30,5,5b** For in that city, I mean Scythopolis, he alone was orthodox, and all the others were Arians.

**30,5,6** And had he not been a count, and had the rank of count not kept the persecution of the Arians away from him, the man could not have endured even to live in the city, especially under Patrophilus, the Arian bishop, who was very powerful through his wealth and severity and through his acquaintance and freedom of speech with the emperor Constantius.

**30,5,7** There was also another, a younger man in the city, of Hebrew origin, who believed rightly; he did not dare [...] openly, but visited us secretly.

**30,5,8a** And Josephus told a story both plausible and amusing, and I think perhaps that he was telling the truth [in this] too.

**30,5,8b** For he said that when his wife had died, fearing that the Arians might somehow seize him and make him a cleric (for they had often flattered him to win him over to their sect, promising him greater advancement and, if need be, to make him a bishop) — he said that for this reason he had married a second wife, so as to escape their ordinations.

**30,6,1** But I return to the account of the story of the patriarch and of Josephus himself, to bring it into the open in detail for those who wish to read it, through the words spoken to me by him.

**30,6,2** Now, as he related, when the patriarch was being counted worthy of the washing, peeping in through the joints of the doors, he says, I observed what was being done by the bishop to the patriarch, and I took it to heart and stored it up within myself.

**30,6,3a** For the patriarch himself, having at hand a very large weight of gold, stretched out his hand, he says, and gave it to the bishop, saying, “Offer it for me.

**30,6,3b** For it is written that through God’s priests there is binding and loosing on earth, and [that] these things shall be loosed and bound in heaven.”

**30,6,4** So when these things had been done, he says, and the doors were opened, the patriarch was asked by those visiting him how he was after the remedy, and he declared that he was very well; for indeed he knew what he was saying.

**30,6,5** Then, [after] two or three days, during which the bishop often visited him on the pretext of being a physician, the patriarch fell asleep, making a good end, having entrusted his own son, who was very young, to Josephus and to another most worthy man [...].
