**31,2,2b** But a certain report has come to us, as by hearsay; therefore we shall not pass it by, and though we do not point out his place — it is in dispute, if the truth must be told — we shall nevertheless not keep silent about the account that has come to us.

**31,2,3** For some have said that he was born a Phrebonite, of the coastal region of Egypt, and that he was educated at Alexandria in the learning of the Greeks.

**31,2,4** Hence, in imitation of Hesiod’s Theogony [and] of the thirty gods spoken of by Hesiod himself, he too took the heathen mythical poem into his own mind, and, having acquired his notion from those who with him and before him had fallen away from the truth, he too, recasting things equal to Hesiod’s from some names into others, wished to beguile the world with his fantasy.

**31,2,5** For he too wishes to introduce thirty gods and aeons and heavens, of which the first is Depth, as he himself said in the folly of his mind — just as, no doubt, Hesiod too, the begetter of his notion, said, “Chaos first of the gods”; and to whom would it not be clear that Chaos and Depth carry the same meaning?

**31,2,6a** But see the charlatan’s theatrical myth-making and wicked teaching.

**31,2,6b** For he wishes, as I said, to present thirty aeons, whom he also calls gods, saying that fifteen are male and as many female.

**31,2,7a** And he and his followers say that each aeon is male-female and a pair; and they say there are fifteen dyads, which they call syzygies.

**31,2,7b** And that their number is thirty aeons, and that each female begets from the male the aeons that follow in order; and that these are as set out below, with each male name placed opposite the female name, and it is as follows:

**31,2,8** Ampsiou, Auraan, Boukoua, Thardouou, Ouboukoua, Thardeddein, Merexa, Atar, Barba, Oudouak, Esten, Ouananin, Lamertarde, Athames, Soumin, Allora, Koubiatha, Danadaria, Dammo, Oren, Lanaphek, Oudinphek, Emphiboche, Barra, Assiou, Ache, Belim, Dexariche, Masemon.

**31,2,9** And thus they are arranged as in male-female pairing, but in the sequence of succession thus: Ampsiou, Auraan, Boukoua, Thardouou, Ouboukoua, Thardeddein, Merexa, Atar, Barba, Oudouak, Esten, Ouananin, Lamertarde, Athames, Soumin, Allora, Koubiatha, Danadaria, Dammo, Oren, Lanaphek, Oudinphek, Emphiboche, Barra, Assiou, Ache, Belim, Dexariche, Masemon;

**31,2,10** the interpretations of which names are these: Depth [and] Silence, Mind and Truth, the Word and Life, Man and Church, Paraclete and Faith, Fatherly and Hope, Motherly and Love, Ever-Mind and Understanding, Desired (who is also Light) and Blessedness, Ecclesiastic and Wisdom, Deep and Mingling, Ageless and Union, Self-Grown and Blending, Only-Begotten and Oneness, Immovable and Pleasure.

**31,2,11** And according to the number of succession and sequence, from the highest, unnameable Father, who among them is named also Depth, down to this heaven of ours, the number of the thirty stands thus: Depth, Silence, Mind, Truth, the Word, Life, Man, Church, Paraclete, Faith, Fatherly, Hope, Motherly, Love, Ever-Mind, Understanding, Desired (who is also Light), Blessedness, Ecclesiastic, Wisdom, Deep, Mingling, Ageless, Union, Self-Grown, Blending, Only-Begotten, Oneness, Immovable, Pleasure.

**31,3,1** And this, then, is the tragedy of the thirty Aeons as it is turned into myth among them, and the empty babble of a Pleroma, spiritual forsooth, arranged in pairs; which, if anyone, comparing it with what is

**31,3,2** in Hesiod and Stesichorus and the other poets of the Greeks, should set it alongside, he would find, side by side, the same things and nothing different; and from this he will know that what the heresiarchs of these doctrines profess to tell in the manner of a mystery is nothing marvellous.

**31,3,3** For they have done nothing else than take an impression of the fictitious poetic manner of the Greeks’ heathen-mythical error and teaching, altering nothing except the barbarous coining of names which they have refashioned.

**31,3,4** For thus Hesiod and his followers also say: first of all comes Chaos, so that they may say Depth; then Night, Erebus, Earth, Aether, Day, Eros, Metis, Doom, Woe, Fate, Nemesis, Blame, Affection, Death, Lawlessness, Old Age, Ruin, Longing, Forgetfulness, Sleep, Battle, Limb-Loosener, Insolence, Cheerful, Radiance, Care-Ender, Deceit, Sweet-Song, Strife.

**31,3,5a** And this number, in male-female sequence, makes up the thirty in this way.

**31,3,5b** And if anyone should wish to see how these people, in their fabrications, join one to one, he would find joined and yoked to one another in this way what pleased the poets,

**31,3,6** how, joining Depth to Night and Silence, they made Earth come to birth; and others say that Heaven, whom indeed they have also called Hyperion — this same one, joined with Earth — begot both males and females, and those after them in the same way, down to the whole of their creation, as the endless, babbling nonsense of the myth has it.

**31,3,7** And he would find them joined and yoked and able to be put together in this way: Chaos and Night, Erebus and Earth, Aether and Day, Eros and Metis, Doom and Woe, Fate and Nemesis, Blame and Affection, Death and Lawlessness, Old Age and Ruin, Longing and Forgetfulness, Sleep and Battle, Limb-Loosener and Insolence, Cheerful and Radiance, Care-Ender and Deceit, Sweet-Song and Strife.

**31,3,8** But if anyone should attend to their fabrication and wish to know how, from the worldly and Greek poets by whom these people were thunderstruck, raving in vain [over] what they ought not, into vain labour and profitless toil, [...], he would find them all the more astray.

**31,4,1** Hence, prying still more loftily, forsooth, they imagined that by their own demon-possessed reasoning they had found a Deficiency as well, which Deficiency they also call Almighty and Demiurge and creator of substances;

**31,4,2** from which, again, they say a later Ogdoad also was created, with seven heavens, made in the likeness of the first Ogdoad, he himself being in the Ogdoad and having made seven heavens after it.

**31,4,3** To this Deficiency they wish to join a certain Aeon, unmingled and unfeminised, but one who came to this place out of the Pleroma to seek out the soul that came from above, from the mother Wisdom, whom indeed they wish to portray and fashion under the name Achamoth; and him they wish to call Saviour as well [...], and Limit, and Cross, and Limit-Setter, and Conveyor, and Jesus who passed through Mary as through a pipe.

**31,4,4** And that he is light from the Christ above, and for this reason is called by names after his fathers: Light because of the Light above, and Christ because of the Christ above, and Word because of the Word above, and likewise is said to be Mind [and] Saviour.

**31,4,5** And that he always passes beyond his own father, the Demiurge, and carries up with himself those who obey him to the conjugal pairs above in the Pleroma.

**31,4,6a** And oh, the drivel of these people, and such vain talk!

**31,4,6b** But I will set out (as I said before) also how they yoked their own babble to the poetic and heathen-mythical fabrications.

**31,4,7a** For after the Triacontad [...] the one name, middle and unfeminised, and following it, once more, the paired Ogdoad found by the Demiurge, which can also be put together in this way.

**31,4,7b** And the names are these: first, Exepaphus, Porphyrion, Clotho, Rhyacus, Lachesis, Epiphaon, Atropos, Hyperion, Asterope.

**31,4,8** And this is the stage-setting of these poets, which also contains many other names of gods named among them, male and female, called variously by various people, able to make up even the number three hundred and sixty-five, and further to serve as a pretext for the other heresies which, in their dreams, have again set up this tragic plot.

**31,4,9a** For after the names mentioned before, Hesiod and Orpheus and Stesichorus and their followers say that there came into being Heaven and Tartarus, Cronus and Rhea, Zeus and Hera and Apollo, Poseidon and Pluto, and, for the rest, the countless [gods] named among them.

**31,4,9b** For great is their error, deceptive and born of conjecture, which, having conceived a craving for empty babble and invented many myths, has turned them into poetry.

**31,4,10a** And this is what seems to beguile the mind of these deceived people with fancies.

**31,4,10b** But these things are at once found laughable by all whose minds have been enlightened according to God.

**31,4,11** And passing on from these things, I shall again, with the sequences from their books [...], make here, word for word and verbatim, the citation of the reading current among them, I mean of their book; and it runs as follows:

**31,5,1** Among the wise, and among the psychics, and among the fleshly, and among the worldly, and before the Greatness [...], an indestructible mind to the indestructible: greeting.

**31,5,2** I make mention to you of mysteries unnameable and ineffable and above the heavens, which can be comprehended neither by principalities nor by authorities nor by subordinate powers nor by any confusion, but have been made manifest to the Thought of the Unchangeable alone.

**31,5,3** For when [at] the beginning the Self-Father himself contained all things in himself, which were in him in unknowing — he whom some call an ageless Aeon, ever young, male-female, who always contains all things and is not contained — then the Thought within him wished —

**31,5,4** she whom some have called Thought, others Grace, fittingly, because she has supplied treasures of the Greatness to those who are from the Greatness, but those who spoke truly have called Silence, because the Greatness perfected all things through reflection without speech —

**31,5,5a** as I said, then, the incorruptible [Thought], wishing to break the eternal bonds, feminised the Greatness, in a longing for its rest.

**31,5,5b** And she, mingling with him, showed forth the Father of truth, whom those who are perfect fittingly named Man, because he was the antitype of the pre-existent Unbegotten.

**31,5,6a** After this Silence, putting forth a natural unity of Light together with Man (their coming together was the willing), shows forth Truth.

**31,5,6b** And Truth was fittingly so named by the perfect, because in truth she resembled Silence, her own mother, Silence having willed this: that the apportionment of lights, both of the male and of the female, should be equal, so that through themselves the [...] in them too might be made manifest to those from them who have been divided into perceptible lights.

**31,5,7a** After this Truth, putting forth a maternal wantonness, feminised her Father towards herself, and they came together with each other in incorruptible mingling and ageless blending, and show forth a spiritual male-female tetrad, antitype of the pre-existent tetrad (which was Depth, Silence, Father, Truth).

**31,5,7b** And this is the tetrad from the Father and from Truth: Man, Church, Word, Life.

**31,5,8a** Then, by the will of Depth who contains all things, Man and Church, remembering paternal words, came together with each other and show forth a dodecad of wanton ones, male-female.

**31,5,8b** The males, then, are: Paraclete, Fatherly, Motherly, Ever-Mind, Desired, which is Light, Ecclesiastic; and the females: Faith, Hope, Love, Understanding, Blessed, Wisdom.

**31,5,9a** And afterwards Word and Life, they too refashioning the gift of union, had communion with each other (their communion was the will), and coming together they showed forth a decad of wanton ones, they too male-female.

**31,5,9b** The males are: Deep, Ageless, Self-Grown, Only-Begotten, Immovable (these adopted their surname [for] the glory of the one who contains all things), and the females: Mingling, Union, Blending, Oneness, Pleasure; and these acquired their surname for the glory of Silence.

**31,6,1** So when the Triacontad according to the Father of Truth had been completed — which those on earth, not understanding it, count, and whenever they come to it, finding no further number, they go round again, counting it afresh; and it comprises Depth, Silence, Father, Truth, Man, Church, the Word, Life, Paraclete, Fatherly, Motherly, Ever-Mind, Desired, Ecclesiastic, Faith, Hope, Love, Understanding, Blessed, Wisdom, Deep, Ageless, Self-Grown, Only-Begotten, Immovable, Mingling, Union, Blending, Oneness, Pleasure —

**31,6,2** then he who contains all things, having by his unsurpassable understanding decreed that another Ogdoad be called in place of the pre-existent, original Ogdoad, which should remain within the number of the Triacontad (for it was not the Greatness’s purpose to fall into number), set up males in place of the males: Sole, Third, Fifth, Seventh; and the females: Dyad, Tetrad, Hexad, Ogdoad.

**31,6,3** This Ogdoad, then, which was called in place of the pre-existent Ogdoad (of Depth, Father, Man, the Word, and Silence, Truth, Church, Life), was united to the lights and became a completed Triacontad.

**31,6,4a** And the pre-existent Ogdoad [was] at rest.

**31,6,4b** And Depth went forth by the support of the Greatness to be united to the Triacontad; for he came into union with Truth, while the Father of Truth joined himself to Church, and Motherly held Life, and Paraclete the Henad, and the Henad was united to the Father of Truth, and Truth’s Father was with Silence, while the spiritual Word had communion [...] in spiritual mingling and incorruptible blending, the Self-Father making the consummation his own undivided rest.

**31,6,5a** So the Triacontad, having completed deep mysteries, having consummated a marriage among the incorruptibles, showed forth incorruptible lights, which were named children of the Midst and were without distinctive stamp, since the intellectual was not present, resting outside understanding, apart from Thought.

**31,6,5b** For whatever anyone does, unless he understands it wholly, he does not do.

**31,6,6** Then, when the lights had come into being — whose great number it is not necessary to state by count, but rather to grasp in thought (for each one has received by lot its own name, for the sake of the knowledge of ineffable mysteries) —

**31,6,7a** Silence, then, wishing to save all things for the election of knowledge, came together with the second, substituted Ogdoad in incorruptible mingling, but by intellectual will; and her intellectual will was the Holy Spirit, the one in the midst of the holy churches.

**31,6,7b** This, then, she sent into the second Ogdoad and persuaded it too to be united to her.

**31,6,7c** So marriage was consummated in the parts of the Ogdoad,

**31,6,8** the Holy Spirit being united to Sole, and the Dyad to Third, and Third to the Hexad, and the Ogdoad to Seventh, and Seventh to the Dyad, and the Hexad to Fifth.

**31,6,9a** And the entire Ogdoad came together with pleasure that does not age and with incorruptible mingling (for there was no parting of them from each other, but there was a blending with pleasure beyond reproach) and showed forth a pentad of unfeminised wanton ones, whose names are these: Emancipator, Limit-Setter, Thank-Offering, Released, Conveyor.

**31,6,9b** These were named sons of the Midst.

**31,6,10a** And I wish you to know: Amphiu Auraan Bucua Tharduu Ubucua Thardeddin Merexa Atar Barba Uduac Esten Uananin Lamertarde Athames Sumin Allora Cubiatha Danadaria Dammo Oren Lanaphec Udinphec Emphiboche Barra Assiu Ache Belim Dexariche Masemon.

**31,6,10b** And let these things, set out in part [from] their books, be enough said by me up to this point.

**31,7,1** This man also carried on his preaching in Egypt; and hence his seed still remains in Egypt, like the remnants of a viper’s bones, in the Athribite and Prosopite and Arsinoite nomes, and in the Thebaid, and in the lower parts of the Coast, and in the Alexandreiopolite nome; but he also went up to Rome and preached.

**31,7,2a** But having come to Cyprus, as though he had undergone a shipwreck of a bodily kind, he departed from the faith and was turned aside in mind.

**31,7,2b** For before this he was thought to have a share of piety and right faith in the places mentioned before.

**31,7,2c** But in Cyprus he finally drove on to the extreme of impiety and sank himself deep in this wickedness proclaimed by him.

**31,7,3** And he himself and his followers call our Lord Jesus Christ, as I said, Saviour, and Christ, and Word, and Cross, and Conveyor, and Limit-Setter, and Limit.

**31,7,4** And they say that he carried his body down from on high and went through Mary the virgin like water through a pipe, receiving nothing from the virgin’s womb, but having his body from above, as I said before.

**31,7,5** And that he is not the first Word, nor yet the Christ who comes after the Word, the one who is above in the Aeons above; but they assert that this one was emitted for no other reason than solely to come and save the spiritual race from above.

**31,7,6** And they deny the resurrection of the dead, asserting something mythical and silly: that it is not this body that rises, but another one out of it, which they call spiritual; and only of those who among them are spiritual and of the others, [the] so-called psychics, [...], if indeed the psychics act righteously; but that those called material and fleshly and earthy perish altogether and are not saved at all.

**31,7,7** And that each substance goes to its own emitters, the material being given up to matter, and the fleshly and earthy to the earth.

**31,7,8a** For they hold that there are three orders of men, spiritual, psychic, fleshly; and they say that they themselves are the spiritual order, as also Gnostics, needing no toil, but only the knowledge and the incantations of their mysteries.

**31,7,8b** And that each of them does anything whatever without fear and has no care for anything; for they say that their order, being spiritual, will be saved in any case.

**31,7,9a** And that the other order of men in the world, which they call psychic, cannot be saved of itself, unless perhaps it should save itself by toil and righteous action.

**31,7,9b** And the material order of men in the world, they say, is able neither to hold knowledge nor to accept it, even should one who springs from this order wish to, but perishes together with soul and body.

**31,7,10** But that their own order, being spiritual, is saved with another body, one that is more inward, which they themselves, in their imaginings, call a spiritual body.

**31,7,11** And that the psychics who have toiled much and have risen above the Demiurge will be given above to the angels who are with Christ, recovering nothing at all of their bodies; but only the souls found in the fullness of their knowledge and having passed beyond the Demiurge are handed over as brides to the angels in Christ’s company.

**31,8,1** Such is the tragedy as they tell it, though it contains still more than this; but I have dictated only those things which I judged by their nature necessary to bring into the open, as the knowledge came to us:

**31,8,2** whence he set out, in what times he lived, from what sources he took his pretexts, and what his teaching is, and at the same time among whom he sprang up as an evil for the world; and I have recorded his teaching in part, as I said.

**31,8,3a** The rest of his subtleties I did not wish to compose on my own, having found the treatise against him produced by the most holy Irenaeus of old.

**31,8,3b** Having gone through these few things up to this point, I shall make in full the citation of what follows from the works of the man mentioned before, the servant of God, I mean Irenaeus.

**31,8,3c** It runs as follows:

**31,8,3d** [From] the writings of the holy Irenaeus

**31,9,1** Since certain people, setting the truth aside, bring in lying words and “endless genealogies, which provide questions rather”, as the apostle says, “than God’s building up which is in faith”, and by a plausibility cunningly contrived lead astray the mind of the less experienced and make captives of them, falsifying the sayings of the Lord,

**31,9,2** becoming bad interpreters of what was well spoken, they also overthrow many, leading them away, under pretext of knowledge, from him who constituted and ordered this universe, as though they had something loftier and greater to point to than the God who made heaven and earth and all things in them,

**31,9,3** persuasively drawing on the simple, by the art of words, into the way of inquiry, but unpersuasively destroying them, in making their opinion blasphemous and impious towards the Demiurge, while they are not able even to distinguish the false from the true —

**31,9,4** for error does not display itself by itself, lest, stripped bare, it should be detected, but, cunningly adorned with a plausible cloak, it makes itself appear to the less experienced, through its outward show, [...] truer than the truth itself.

**31,9,5** As one better than we has said of such things, that glass, artfully made to resemble it, insults the precious stone, the emerald, highly prized by some, whenever there is no one present able to test it and expose the cunningly made artifice; and when copper is mixed into the silver, who, [being] simple, will easily be able to test it?

**31,9,6** — so then, lest [also] through our fault some be snatched away like sheep by wolves, not recognising them because of the sheepskin they have put on outwardly, those whom the Lord charged us to guard against, who speak like [us] but think unlike us, I have thought it necessary, having come upon the commentaries of those who are, as they themselves say, disciples of Valentinus, and having also conversed with some of them and grasped their opinion, to disclose to you, beloved,

**31,9,7** the monstrous and deep mysteries, which “not all receive”, since not all have spat out their brains, so that you too, having learned them, may make them plain to all who are with you and exhort them to guard against the depth of folly and of blasphemy against God.

**31,9,8** And, as far as we are able, we shall report briefly and clearly the opinion of those who are now teaching falsely, I mean the followers of Ptolemy, which is an offshoot of the school of Valentinus, and we shall give [others], according to our modest ability, starting points for overturning it, showing what they say to be strange and incoherent and incongruous with the truth,

**31,9,9a** being neither accustomed to writing nor practised in the art of words, but because love spurs us on to make known to you and to all those with you the teachings that until now have been hidden, but have now, by the grace of God, come into the open.

**31,9,9b** “For there is nothing covered that will not be revealed, nor hidden that will not be made known.”

**31,10,1** And you will not look for from us, who live among the Celts and are occupied for the most part with a barbarian tongue, the art of words, which we have not learned, nor the skill of a writer, which we have not practised, nor elegance of diction, nor plausibility, which we do not know;

**31,10,2** but simply and truly and plainly you will receive with love what has been written to you with love, and you yourself will make it grow by your own effort (being more capable than we), taking it from us as seeds and beginnings,

**31,10,3** and in the breadth of your mind you will bring to abundant fruit what we have said in few words, and will present with power to those who are with you what we have reported with weakness.

**31,10,4** And as we have been eager, since you have long been seeking to learn their opinion, not only to make it plain to you, but also to give you provisions for showing it to be false, so you too will eagerly minister to the rest, according to the grace given you by the Lord, so that people are no longer dragged along by their persuasive talk, which is of this kind.

**31,10,5a** For they say that there is in invisible and unnameable heights a perfect Aeon, pre-existent; and him they call also First-Beginning and Forefather and Depth.

**31,10,5b** And that, being uncontainable and invisible, eternal and unbegotten, he existed in great stillness and quiet for infinite ages of time; and that with him there co-existed also Thought, whom they name also Grace and Silence.

**31,10,6** And that at some time this Depth conceived the thought of emitting from himself the beginning of all things, and deposited this emission, which he had thought of emitting, like a seed, as in a womb, [in] the Silence co-existing with him.

**31,10,7** And that she, having received this seed and become pregnant, brought forth Mind, like and equal to him who emitted it, and alone containing the greatness of the Father; and this Mind they call also Only-Begotten and Father and Beginning of all things.

**31,10,8a** And that Truth was emitted together with him, and that this is the first and original Pythagorean tetractys, which they likewise name the root of all things.

**31,10,8b** For there are Depth and Silence, then Mind and Truth.

**31,10,9** And that this Only-Begotten, perceiving the purposes for which he had been emitted, himself in turn emitted Word and Life, father of all those that were to be after him, and beginning and shaping of the whole Pleroma; and that from Word and Life were emitted, as a pair, Man and Church.

**31,10,10a** And that this is the original Ogdoad, root and substance of all things, called among them by four names: Depth and Mind and Word and Man.

**31,10,10b** For each of them is male-female, thus: first the Forefather was united as a pair with his own Thought, and the Only-Begotten, that is Mind, with Truth, and Word with Life, and Man with Church.

**31,10,11a** And that these Aeons, emitted for the glory of the Father, wishing themselves also to glorify the Father through something of their own, emitted emissions in pairs: Word and Life, after emitting Man and Church, emitted ten other Aeons, whose names they give as these: Deep and Mingling, Ageless and Union, Self-Grown and Pleasure, Immovable and Blending, Only-Begotten and Blessed.

**31,10,11b** These are [the] ten Aeons which they assert were emitted from Word and Life.

**31,10,12** And that Man too, with Church, emitted twelve Aeons, on whom they bestow these names: Paraclete and Faith, Fatherly and Hope, Motherly and Love, Ever-Mind and Understanding, Ecclesiastic and Blessedness, Desired and Wisdom.

**31,10,13a** These are the thirty Aeons of their error, those kept in silence and not known.

**31,10,13b** This is the invisible and spiritual Pleroma according to them, divided threefold into ogdoad and decad and dodecad.

**31,10,14** And it is on this account, they say, that the Saviour (for they will not call him Lord) did nothing in public for thirty years, displaying the mystery of these Aeons.

**31,10,15a** But also in the parable of the labourers sent into the vineyard, they say, these thirty Aeons are most plainly indicated.

**31,10,15b** For some are sent at about the first hour, others about the third, others about the sixth, others around the ninth, and still others around the eleventh; so the hours mentioned, added together, make up the number thirty.

**31,10,15c** For one and three and six and nine and eleven make thirty.

**31,10,15d** And through the hours they will have it that the Aeons are indicated.

**31,10,16** And that these are the great and marvellous and secret mysteries which they themselves bear as fruit, and whatever anywhere, among the multitude of things said in the Scriptures, might be fitted and likened to their fabrication.

**31,11,1a** Their Forefather, then, they say, is known only to the Only-Begotten who came into being from him, that is, to Mind, but is invisible and incomprehensible to all the rest.

**31,11,1b** And Mind alone, according to them, delighted in beholding the Father, and rejoiced in contemplating his immeasurable greatness.

**31,11,2** And he had it in mind to share with the rest of the Aeons as well the greatness of the Father, how great and how vast he was, and that he was without beginning and uncontainable and not to be comprehended by sight; but Silence restrained him by the will of the Father, because he wished to lead them all up to the thought and longing of seeking their aforesaid Forefather.

**31,11,3** And the rest of the Aeons likewise, in a quiet way, longed to see the emitter of their seed and to learn about the root without beginning.

**31,11,4a** But the very last and youngest Aeon of the dodecad emitted by Man and Church, that is, Wisdom, sprang forward and suffered a passion without the embrace of her [consort] Desired; it began among those about Mind and Truth, but broke out in this Aeon, which had been turned aside, under pretext of love, but of daring, because she had not had communion with the perfect Father, as Mind had.

**31,11,4b** And that the passion was a seeking after the Father; for she wished, as they say, to comprehend his greatness.

**31,11,5** Then, not being able, because she had set her hand to an impossible thing, and being in very great struggle, because of the greatness of the depth and the unsearchableness of the Father and her affection for him, stretching ever forward, she would at last have been swallowed up by his sweetness and dissolved into the universal substance, had she not met with the power that establishes all things and guards them outside the ineffable greatness.

**31,11,6a** And this power they call also Limit.

**31,11,6b** And that by it she was checked and established, and, having with difficulty turned back to herself and been persuaded that the Father is incomprehensible, laid aside her former Intention together with the passion that had come upon her from that astounding wonder.

**31,12,1** But some of them tell the myth of Wisdom’s passion and return in this way: that she, having attempted an impossible and incomprehensible thing, brought forth a formless substance, such a nature as a female could bring forth;

**31,12,2** and that, perceiving it, she was first grieved because of the imperfection of the birth, then afraid lest even its very being should come to an end; then she was beside herself and at a loss, seeking the cause and in what way she might hide what had come to be.

**31,12,3** And that, being immersed in the passions, she received a turning back, and tried to run back up to the Father, and, having dared for a time, grew weak and became a suppliant of the Father.

**31,12,4a** And that the rest of the Aeons also, and especially Mind, joined her in supplication.

**31,12,4b** From this, they say, the substance of matter had its first beginning, from the ignorance and the grief and the fear and the consternation.

**31,12,5** And the Father, in view of these things, emits through the Only-Begotten the Limit mentioned before, in his own image, without consort, unfeminised; for they will have it that the Father is sometimes with his consort Silence, and sometimes also above male and above female.

**31,12,6a** And this Limit they call also Cross and Redeemer and Emancipator and Limit-Setter and Conveyor.

**31,12,6b** And through this Limit, they say, Wisdom was purified and established and restored to her pair.

**31,12,7a** For when her Intention had been separated from her together with the passion that had come upon her, she herself remained within the Pleroma, but her Intention together with the passion was marked off by the Limit and staked off, and, having come to be outside it, was a spiritual substance, [as] being a natural impulse of an Aeon, but formless and shapeless, because it had comprehended nothing.

**31,12,7b** This is why they speak of it as a weak and female fruit.

**31,13,1** And after this one had been marked off outside the Pleroma of the Aeons, and her Mother had been restored to her own pair, the Only-Begotten, by the forethought of the Father, emitted again another pair, so that none of the Aeons might suffer as she did: Christ and Holy Spirit, for the fixing and establishing of the Pleroma, by whom, [they say], the Aeons were set in order.

**31,13,2** For Christ taught them the nature of the pair, that those who know the comprehension of the Unbegotten are sufficient, and proclaimed among them the knowledge of the Father: that he is uncontainable and incomprehensible, and that it is not possible to see or hear him, but he is known through the Only-Begotten alone;

**31,13,3a** and that the cause of eternal continuance for the rest is the incomprehensibility of the Father, but the cause of their coming into being and formation is what is comprehensible of him, which is the Son.

**31,13,3b** And these things the Christ newly emitted wrought among them.

**31,13,4a** And the Holy Spirit taught them all, having been made equal, to give thanks, and [introduced] the true rest.

**31,13,4b** And in this way, they say, the Aeons were rendered equal in form and in purpose, all becoming Minds and all Words and all Men and all Christs, and the females likewise all Truths and all Lives and Spirits and Churches.

**31,13,5** And that all things, established in this and perfectly at rest, sang hymns, it is said, with great joy to the Forefather, sharing in much gladness.

**31,13,6** And that, in return for this benefaction, by a single will and mind, the whole Pleroma of the Aeons, with Christ and the Spirit consenting and their Father also setting his seal upon it, each one of the Aeons bringing together and contributing whatever it had in itself most beautiful and most flourishing, and weaving these fittingly and uniting them harmoniously, emitted an emission for the honour and glory of Depth,

**31,13,7** a most perfect beauty and star of the Pleroma, the perfect Fruit, Jesus, who was also called Saviour and Christ and Word, after his fathers, and the All, because he is from all; and that angels of like kind were emitted together with him as bodyguards for their honour.

**31,14,1** This, then, is the system they tell of within the Pleroma, and the calamity of the Aeon who suffered and all but perished, as in much matter, through the seeking of the Father [...], and the compacting, out of struggle, of Limit and Cross and Redeemer and Emancipator and Limit-Setter and Conveyor, and the generation, later than the Aeons, of the first Christ with the Holy Spirit by their Father out of repentance, and the composite construction, out of a common contribution, of the second Christ, whom they also call Saviour.

**31,14,2** And that these things were not spoken openly, because not all contain the knowledge of them, but were indicated mysteriously by the Saviour through parables to those able to understand, in this way:

**31,14,3** for the thirty Aeons are indicated through the thirty years, as we said before, in which they assert the Saviour did nothing openly, and through the parable of the labourers in the vineyard.

**31,14,4** And they say that Paul most plainly names these Aeons often, and further has kept their order as well, saying thus: “unto all generations of the aeons of the aeon”.

**31,14,5** But also that we, when we say at the thanksgiving “to the aeons of the aeons”, signify those Aeons; and wherever aeon or aeons are named, they will have it that the reference is to those.

**31,14,6** And that the emission of the dodecad of the Aeons is indicated through the Lord’s discussing with the teachers of the Law when he was twelve years old, and through the selection of the apostles; for the apostles are twelve.

**31,14,7** And that the remaining eighteen Aeons are made manifest through their saying that after the resurrection [from] the dead he spent eighteen months with the disciples; but also that through the two letters that begin his name, iota and eta, the eighteen Aeons are clearly indicated.

**31,14,8a** And the ten Aeons likewise, they say, are signified through the letter iota, which begins his name.

**31,14,8b** And that for this reason the Saviour said: “One iota or one stroke shall not pass away until all things come to pass”.

**31,14,9** And the passion that happened concerning the twelfth Aeon, they say, is hinted at through the apostasy of Judas, the twelfth of the apostles, and through the fact that he suffered in the twelfth month; for they will have it that he preached for a single year after his baptism.

**31,14,10** And further that this is shown most clearly in the case of the woman who had a haemorrhage; for she, having suffered twelve years, was cured by the Saviour’s coming, when she touched the hem of his garment; and for this reason the Saviour said “Who touched me?”, teaching the disciples the mystery that had taken place in the Aeons and the cure of the Aeon who had suffered.

**31,14,11a** For that power which suffered for twelve years, as she stretched out and her substance flowed into the boundless, as they say, had she not touched the garment of the Son, that is, Truth of the first tetrad, which is indicated by the hem, would have been dissolved into her [whole] substance.

**31,14,11b** But she stood still and ceased from her passion; for the power that went out from the Son (and they will have this to be Limit) healed her and parted the passion from her.

**31,14,12** And that [the] Saviour, who is from all, is the All, they say is shown through the saying “Every male that opens the womb”; for he, being the All, opened the womb of the Intention of the Aeon who suffered and was banished outside the Pleroma, which they also call the second Ogdoad, about which we shall speak a little later.

**31,14,13** And they say that for this reason it was also said openly by Paul, “And he is all things”, and again, “All things are to him, and from him are all things”, and again, “In him dwells the whole fullness of the Godhead”; and the saying “to sum up all things in Christ through God” they interpret as having been said [thus], and whatever other such sayings there are.

**31,15,1** Next, concerning their Limit, whom they call by more names as well, they declare that he has two operations, the establishing and the dividing; and in so far as he establishes and supports, he is Cross, but in so far as he divides and delimits, Limit.

**31,15,2** And they say that the Saviour indicated his operations in this way: first the establishing one, in saying “Anyone who does not carry his cross and follow me can be no disciple of mine”, and [again] “Taking up the cross, follow me”;

**31,15,3a** and his delimiting one, in saying “I did not come to bring peace, but a sword”.

**31,15,3b** And they say that John too indicated this very thing, saying “The winnowing fan is in his hand; he will thoroughly cleanse the threshing floor and gather the wheat into his granary, but the chaff he will burn up with unquenchable fire”.

**31,15,4** And that through this he indicated the operation of Limit; for they interpret that winnowing fan to be the Cross, which indeed consumes all material things as fire does chaff, but cleanses those who are being saved as the winnowing fan does the wheat.

**31,15,5** And they say that Paul the apostle also makes mention of this cross thus: “For the word of the cross is foolishness to those who are perishing, but to those who are being saved it is the power of God”, and again: “But far be it from me to boast in anything except in the cross of Christ, through which the world has been crucified to me, and I to the world”.

**31,15,6a** Such, then, are the things they say about their Pleroma and the fabrication of all things, forcing what has been well said to fit what has been badly devised by them.

**31,15,6b** And not only from the gospels and the apostolic writings do they try to make their proofs, perverting the interpretations and tampering with the explanations, but also from the Law and the Prophets,

**31,15,7** inasmuch as many parables and allegories have been spoken and can be drawn in many directions, the ambiguous through interpretation, [...] fitting them more cleverly and deceitfully to their fabrication, they take captive from the truth those who do not keep their faith firm in one God, the Father Almighty, and in one Lord Jesus Christ, God’s Son.

**31,16,1** And the things said by them about what is outside the Pleroma are as follows: the Intention of the Wisdom above, whom they also call Achamoth, having been marked off from the [upper] Pleroma together with the passion, was, they say, of necessity cast out into places of shadow and emptiness; for she came to be outside light and Pleroma, formless and shapeless like an untimely birth, because she had comprehended nothing.

**31,16,2** And that the [upper] Christ, taking pity on her and stretching himself out through the Cross, formed her by his own power with a formation according to substance only, but not that according to knowledge; and that, having done this, he ran back up, drawing in his power, and left [her], so that, perceiving the passion that surrounded her because of her separation from the Pleroma, she might long for the things that are superior, having a certain fragrance of incorruption left in her [by] Christ and the Holy Spirit.

**31,16,3** Wherefore she too is called by both names, Wisdom after her father (for her father is called Wisdom), and Holy Spirit, from the spirit that is about Christ.

**31,16,4a** And that, having been formed and become intelligent, but at once emptied of the Word who was invisibly with her, that is, of Christ, she rushed off to look for the light that had forsaken her, and could not apprehend it because she was hindered by Limit.

**31,16,4b** And that here Limit, hindering her from her forward rush, said “Iao”; whence, they assert, the name Iao came about.

**31,16,5** And that, not being able to pass through Limit because she was entangled with the passion, and being left alone outside, she fell under every part of the passion, which is manifold and very varied, and suffered grief, because she had not apprehended, and fear, lest, as the light had, so life too should fail her, and perplexity besides these.

**31,16,6a** And all of it in ignorance; and it was not, as with her mother, the first Wisdom, an Aeon, alteration that she had in her passions, but opposition.

**31,16,6b** And that there befell her also another disposition, that of turning back towards him who had given her life.

**31,16,7a** This, they say, became the composition and substance of matter, from which this world has been constituted.

**31,16,7b** For from the turning back the whole soul of the world and of the Demiurge took its origin, and from the fear and the grief the rest had their beginning.

**31,16,7c** For from her tears came every moist substance, from her laughter the luminous, and from her grief and consternation the bodily elements of the world.

**31,16,8** For at times she wept and grieved, as they say, because she had been left alone in the darkness and the emptiness; at times, coming to a thought of the light that had left her, she was cheered and laughed; at times again she was afraid; and at other times she was at a loss and beside herself.

**31,17,1a** And what indeed?

**31,17,1b** Henceforth there was much tragedy here, and fantasy, each one of them pompously expounding in one way or another from what sort of passion, from what element, substance took its origin;

**31,17,2** which, with good reason, they seem to me not to wish to teach openly to all, but only to those able to pay large fees for such great mysteries.

**31,17,3** For these things are no longer like those about which our Lord said, “Freely you received, freely give”, but are recondite and monstrous and deep mysteries, acquired with much toil by lovers of falsehood.

**31,17,4** For who would not spend all his possessions to learn that from the tears of the Intention of the Aeon who suffered, seas and springs and rivers and every watery substance took their origin, and from her laughter the light, and from her consternation and helplessness the bodily elements of the world?

**31,17,5a** And I myself wish to contribute something to their fruit-bearing.

**31,17,5b** For since I see that some waters are sweet, such as springs and rivers and rains and the like, while those in the seas are salty, I reflect that not all were emitted from her tears, since a tear is salty in quality.

**31,17,6a** It is plain, then, that these salty waters are the ones from the tears.

**31,17,6b** And it is likely that she, being in great agony and helplessness, also sweated.

**31,17,6c** From this, then, according to their system, one must suppose that springs and rivers and whatever other sweet waters there are have had their origin from her [sweat].

**31,17,7a** For it is implausible that, the tears being of one quality, both salty and sweet waters came forth from them.

**31,17,7b** But this is more plausible: that some are from the tears and others from the sweat.

**31,17,8a** [And] since there are also some hot and acrid waters in the world, you ought to consider what she did and from what part she discharged these.

**31,17,8b** For such fruits suit their system.

**31,17,9** And their Mother, having passed through every passion and having barely emerged, turned, they say, to supplication of the light that had left her, that is, of Christ; who, having gone up into the Pleroma, was himself, it is likely, reluctant to come down a second time, but sent out the Paraclete [to] her, that is, the Saviour,

**31,17,10** the Father having given him all power and handed over everything under his authority, and the Aeons likewise, so that “in him all things might be created, the visible and the invisible, thrones, deities, lordships”.

**31,17,11a** And he is sent out to her with his peers, the angels.

**31,17,11b** And they say that Achamoth, abashed before him, first put on a veil out of modesty, but afterwards, seeing him with all his fruitage, ran to him, receiving power from his appearing.

**31,17,12** And that he formed her with the formation according to knowledge and effected the healing of her passions, separating them from her, yet not neglecting them (for it was not possible for [them] to be made to vanish like those of the former one, because they were already habitual and powerful), but, having set them apart, he mingled them separately and congealed them, and changed them from incorporeal passion into incorporeal matter;

**31,17,13** and that then he thus implanted in them an aptitude and a nature such as to enter into compounds and bodies, so that two substances came to be, the evil one [from] the passions, and that of the turning back, subject to passion; and for this reason they assert that the Saviour performed the work of creation in potency.

**31,17,14** And they teach that Achamoth, having come to be outside the passion, having conceived with joy the contemplation of the lights who accompanied him (that is, of the angels with him), and having craved them, conceived fruits according to [their] image, a spiritual offspring that came into being after the likeness of the Saviour’s bodyguards.

**31,18,1** So, these three now being in place according to them — the first arising from the passion, which was matter; the second from the turning back, which was the psychic; and the third, what she brought forth, that is, the spiritual — she thereupon turned to the forming of them.

**31,18,2a** But the spiritual she was not able to form, since it was of the same substance as she.

**31,18,2b** And that she turned to the forming of the psychic substance that had come into being from her turning back, and put forth the teachings received from the Saviour.

**31,18,3** And first, they say, she formed out of the psychic substance the Father and King of all things, both of those of the same substance as he, that is, the psychics, which they call right, and of those from the passion and matter, which they call left.

**31,18,4** For they assert that [he] formed all the things after him, being moved unawares by the Mother; whence they call him also Mother-Father and Fatherless and Demiurge and Father, calling him father of the right, that is, of the psychics, and demiurge of the left, that is, of the material, and king of all together.

**31,18,5a** For this Intention, wishing to make all things for the honour of the Aeons, made images of them, they say, or rather the Saviour did so through her.

**31,18,5b** And that she herself has kept in the image of the invisible Father, not being known by the Demiurge, and he in that of the Son, the only-begotten, and the archangels and angels brought into being by him in that of the remaining Aeons.

**31,18,6a** So they say that he became Father and God of what lies outside the Pleroma, being the maker of all things psychic and material.

**31,18,6b** For, having separated the two substances that were confused and having made bodies out of incorporeal things, he created the things in heaven and those on earth, and became demiurge of the material and the psychic, of the right and the left, of the light and the heavy, of what tends upward and what tends downward.

**31,18,7a** For he constructed seven heavens, above which, they say, the Demiurge is.

**31,18,7b** And for this reason they call him Hebdomad, and the Mother, Achamoth, Ogdoad, as preserving the number of the original and first Ogdoad of the Pleroma.

**31,18,8** And they say that the seven heavens are intelligent, and they suppose them to be angels, and the Demiurge himself also an angel, but resembling God; as also they say that paradise, which is above the third heaven, is in power a fourth archangel, and that Adam, having spent time in it, received something from it.

**31,18,9a** And they assert that the Demiurge supposed that he was constructing these things [in every respect] of himself, but that he made them as Achamoth put them forth.

**31,18,9b** [For] he made a heaven without knowing heaven; and he moulded a man, not knowing man; and he displayed earth, not understanding earth;

**31,18,10** and in all things alike, they say, he was ignorant of the ideas of the things he was making, and of the Mother herself, and supposed that he alone was all things.

**31,18,11** And they assert that the Mother was the cause of this making for him, she who wished thus to bring him forth as head and beginning of his own substance, and lord of the whole business.

**31,18,12** And this Mother they call also Ogdoad, and Wisdom, and Earth, and Jerusalem, and Holy Spirit, and Lord, in the masculine; and that she has the place of the Midst, and is above the Demiurge, but beneath the Pleroma, or outside it, until the consummation.

**31,19,1** Since, then, they say that the material substance was constituted from three passions, fear and grief and perplexity, and that from the fear and the turning back the psychic things received their constitution;

**31,19,2** from the turning back they will have it that the Demiurge had his origin, and from the fear all the rest of the psychic existence, such as the souls of irrational animals and of beasts and of men.

**31,19,3** [And] for this reason, being too weak to know spiritual things, he supposed that he alone was God, and said through the prophets, “I am God, and besides me there is none”.

**31,19,4** And from the grief, they teach, came the spiritual things of wickedness; whence [also] the devil had his origin, whom they also call World-Ruler, and the demons and the angels and the whole spiritual existence of wickedness.

**31,19,5a** But they say that the Demiurge is a psychic son of their Mother, and the World-Ruler a creature of the Demiurge.

**31,19,5b** And that the World-Ruler knows the things above him, because he is a spirit of wickedness, but the Demiurge is ignorant of them, inasmuch as he is psychic.

**31,19,6** And that their Mother has her dwelling in the supercelestial place, that is, in the Midst, the Demiurge in the heavenly place, that is, in the Hebdomad, [and] the World-Ruler in our world.

**31,19,7** And that from the consternation and helplessness, as from what is more [stupefied], the bodily elements of the world came into being, as we said before: earth according to the standing still of consternation, water according to the motion of fear, air according to the congealing of grief; and that fire is inherent in all of them as death and corruption, just as they teach that ignorance too lies hidden in the three passions.

**31,19,8** And that, having created the world, he made also the earthy man, taking him not from this dry land but from the invisible substance, from the poured-out and fluid part of matter; and into him, they define, he breathed the psychic man.

**31,19,9** And that this is the one who came into being according to image and likeness, and that the material man is according to the image, similar to God but not of the same substance; and the psychic according to the likeness, whence also his substance was called a spirit of life, being from a spiritual outflow.

**31,19,10** And they say that later the garment of skin was put around him; and this they will have to be the perceptible flesh.

**31,19,11** And the offspring of their Mother Achamoth, which she brought forth according to her contemplation of the angels about the Saviour, being spiritual, of the same substance as the Mother: of this too, they say, the Demiurge was ignorant, and it was secretly deposited in him without his knowing, so that, sown through him into the soul that is from him and into this material body, and [both] carried in the womb in these and grown, it might become ready for the reception of the perfect [Word].

**31,19,12a** So, as they say, the spiritual man, sown by Wisdom together with his breathing, escaped the notice of the Demiurge by ineffable [power and] providence.

**31,19,12b** For as he was ignorant of the Mother, so also of her seed; which itself, they say, is the Church, antitype of the Church above.

**31,19,13** And they deem this to be the man that is in them; so that they possess the soul from the Demiurge, the body from the dust and the fleshly from matter, but the spiritual man from the Mother, Achamoth.

**31,20,1** So, there being three, the material, which they also call left, they say must of necessity perish, as being unable to receive any breath of incorruption; the psychic, which they also name right, inasmuch as it is midway between the spiritual and the material, goes to whichever side it inclines;

**31,20,2a** and the spiritual has been sent forth so that here, yoked with the psychic, it may be formed, being educated together with it in its conduct.

**31,20,2b** And this, they say, is the world’s salt and light.

**31,20,2c** For the psychic needed perceptible teachings as well; and for this reason, they say, a world was also constructed.

**31,20,3** And that the Saviour came to this psychic element, since it has free will, in order to save it.

**31,20,4a** For of what he was to save, they assert, he took the first-fruits: from Achamoth the spiritual, and from the Demiurge he put on the psychic Christ, and from the dispensation there was put around him a body that had psychic substance, but was constructed with ineffable art so as to become visible and tangible and subject to suffering.

**31,20,4b** And they say that he took nothing material whatever; for matter is not capable of salvation.

**31,20,5** And that the consummation will come when all that is spiritual has been formed and perfected in knowledge, that is, the spiritual men who have the perfect knowledge about God and have been initiated into [the] mysteries of Achamoth; and they suppose that these are [themselves].

**31,20,6** And the psychic men were educated in psychic things — those who are confirmed by works and mere faith and lack the perfect knowledge; and they say that these are we of the church.

**31,20,7** Wherefore they declare that good conduct is necessary for us (for otherwise it is impossible to be saved), but they lay it down as dogma that they themselves will be saved entirely and in every way, not through conduct, but because they are spiritual by nature.

**31,20,8** For as the earthy cannot partake of salvation (for, they say, it is not capable of receiving it), so again the spiritual, which they will have themselves to be, cannot admit corruption, whatever deeds they may be involved in.

**31,20,9** For just as gold placed in mire does not cast off its beauty but preserves its own nature, the mire being unable to harm the gold in any way, so also they say of themselves that, whatever material deeds they may be involved in, they are not harmed at all, nor do they cast off their spiritual substance.

**31,21,1** Wherefore the most perfect among them do without fear all the forbidden things, about which the Scriptures solemnly affirm that those who practise them shall not inherit God’s kingdom.

**31,21,2** For they eat things sacrificed to idols indifferently, thinking themselves in no way defiled by them, and they are the first to gather at every festive entertainment of the gentiles held in honour of the idols, so that some of them do not even keep away from the murderous spectacle of beast-fighting and single combat, hateful to God and men;

**31,21,3** and others, serving the pleasures of the flesh to the point of surfeit, say that fleshly things are rendered to the fleshly and spiritual things to the spiritual.

**31,21,4** And some of them secretly corrupt the women who are taught this teaching by them, as women often deceived by some of them, having afterwards returned to the church of God, have confessed this too along with the rest of the error;

**31,21,5** and others, having openly cast off all shame, take whatever women they fall in love with away from their husbands and treat them as their own wives.

**31,21,6** And others again, behaving with gravity at the beginning, pretending to live with them as with sisters, were exposed as time went on, when the sister became pregnant by the brother.

**31,21,7** And while doing many other abominable and godless things, they run us down, who through the fear of God guard against sinning even in thought and word, as unlearned people who know nothing, but they exalt themselves, calling themselves perfect and seeds of election.

**31,21,8** For we, they say, receive grace as a loan, and for that reason shall also be deprived of it; but they themselves have grace as their own possession, come down from above from the ineffable and unnameable pair, and for this reason it will be added to them; wherefore they must in every way always practise the mystery of the pair.

**31,21,9** And they persuade the foolish of this, speaking in these very words: “Whoever, being in the world, has not loved a woman so that she was mastered, is not from the truth and shall not pass into the truth; but he who is from the world, having been mastered by a woman, shall not pass into the truth, because he was mastered in desire for a woman.”

**31,21,10a** And that for this reason, then, continence and good conduct are necessary for us, whom they name psychics and say are from the world, so that through it we may come to the place of the Midst, but for themselves, who are called spiritual and perfect, by no means.

**31,21,10b** For it is not conduct that leads into the Pleroma, but the seed, sent out from there as an infant [but] brought to perfection here.

**31,21,11a** And when all the seed has been perfected, they say that Achamoth their Mother passes from the place of the Midst and enters within the Pleroma and receives her bridegroom, the Saviour, who came into being from all, so that a pair may come to be of the Saviour and of Wisdom, Achamoth.

**31,21,11b** And that this is the bridegroom and the bride, and the bridal chamber is the whole Pleroma.

**31,21,12** And that the spiritual, having put off their souls and become intellectual spirits, entering within the Pleroma unhindered and invisibly, will be given as brides to the angels about the Saviour.

**31,21,13a** And that the Demiurge too passes into the place of his Mother, Wisdom, that is, into the Midst.

**31,21,13b** And that the souls of the just will likewise find rest in the place of the Midst; for nothing psychic goes within the Pleroma.

**31,21,14a** And when these things have so come about, they teach, the fire that lurks in the world will blaze out and be kindled and, having overcome all matter, will be consumed together with it and pass into non-existence.

**31,21,14b** And they maintain that the Demiurge had known nothing of these things before the coming of the Saviour.

**31,22,1a** But there are some who say that he too emitted a Christ, his own son, but [...] psychic as well, [and] that he spoke about him through the prophets.

**31,22,1b** And that this is he who made his way through Mary, just as water makes its way through a pipe, and that upon him at the baptism came down that Saviour from the Pleroma, made from all, in the form of a dove; and that there was in him also the spiritual seed from Achamoth.

**31,22,2** So they assert that our Lord was composed of these four, keeping the type of the original and first tetractys: of the spiritual, which was from Achamoth; and of the psychic, which was from the Demiurge; and of the dispensation, which was constructed with ineffable art; and of the Saviour, which was [the] dove that came down upon him.

**31,22,3a** And that this one remained impassible (for it was not possible for him to suffer, being unconquerable and invisible), and for this reason the spirit of Christ that had been deposited in him was taken away when he was brought before Pilate.

**31,22,3b** But neither, they say, did the seed from the Mother suffer; for it too is impassible, being spiritual and invisible even to the Demiurge himself.

**31,22,4** And so there suffered, according to them, the psychic Christ and the one mysteriously constructed out of the dispensation, so that [through] him the Mother might show forth the type of the Christ above, that one who stretched himself out upon the Cross and formed Achamoth with the formation according to substance; for all these things, they say, are types of those.

**31,22,5** And they say that the souls which had the seed of Achamoth were better than the rest; wherefore they were also loved more than the others by the Demiurge, who did not know the cause, but reckoned that they were such because of himself.

**31,22,6a** Wherefore, they say, he also appointed them as prophets and priests and kings.

**31,22,6b** And they explain that many things were spoken by this seed through the prophets, inasmuch as it is of a loftier nature.

**31,22,6c** And they say that the Mother too spoke many things about the higher realms, but also through him and through the souls that came into being by him [...].

**31,22,7** And so they cut up the prophecies, holding that one part was spoken by the Mother, another by the seed, and another by the Demiurge.

**31,22,8** But also that Jesus likewise spoke one part from the Saviour, another from the Mother, and another from the Demiurge, as we shall show as our account proceeds.

**31,22,9** And that the Demiurge, inasmuch as he was ignorant of the things above him, was moved by what was said, but despised it, attributing it at one time to one cause and at another to another, either to the prophesying spirit, which itself also had a certain motion of its own, or to the man, or to the admixture of the inferior elements.

**31,22,10** And that he continued thus in ignorance until the coming of the Saviour; but when the Saviour came, they say, he learned all things from him and gladly went over to him with all his power,

**31,22,11** and that he is the centurion in the gospel, who says to the Saviour: “For I also have under my own authority soldiers and slaves, and whatever I command, they do”.

**31,22,12** And that he will complete the dispensation pertaining to the world until the appointed time, chiefly for the sake of his care for the church, and because of his knowledge of the prize made ready for him: that he will pass into the place of the Mother.

**31,23,1** And they posit three races of men, spiritual, earthy, psychic, as Cain, Abel and Seth came into being, [so that they may show] from these also the three natures, no longer in individuals but by race.

**31,23,2** And that the earthy goes to corruption, and the psychic, if it chooses the better things, will rest in the place of the Midst, but if the worse, it too will go to things like them.

**31,23,3** And they lay it down as dogma that the spiritual elements which Achamoth sows, from then until now, having been educated and nourished here in righteous souls, because they are sent forth as infants, and having later been deemed worthy of perfection, will be given as brides to the angels of the Saviour, while their souls necessarily rest for ever in the Midst with the Demiurge.

**31,23,4a** And again subdividing the souls themselves, they say that some are good by nature and some evil by nature.

**31,23,4b** And that these good ones are those that become receptive of the seed, but those evil by nature would never receive that seed.

**31,24,1** Such being their system, which neither prophets proclaimed, nor the Lord taught, nor apostles handed down, which they boast that they have known more abundantly than the rest, reading from unwritten sources and, as the saying goes, endeavouring to plait ropes out of sand,

**31,24,2** they try to fit to what they say, in a manner meant to win belief, either parables of the Lord or prophetic sayings or apostolic words, so that their fabrication may not seem to be without witness, overstepping the order and the connection of the Scriptures and, as far as in them lies, dismembering the truth.

**31,24,3** And they transfer and refashion and, making one thing out of another, they deceive many by the ill-composed fantasy of the oracles of the Lord which they adapt.

**31,24,4** Just as if someone, when a beautiful image of a king had been carefully constructed out of precious stones by a skilful craftsman, were to break up the existing form of the man and transfer those stones and rearrange them and make the shape of a dog or a fox, and that badly constructed,

**31,24,5** and were then to declare and say that this is that beautiful image of the king which the skilful craftsman constructed, pointing to the stones that had been well put together by the first craftsman into the image of the king, but badly transferred by the later one into the shape of a dog, and by the show of the stones were to deceive the less experienced, who have no comprehension of a king’s form, and persuade them that this rotten form of the fox is that beautiful image of the king —

**31,24,6** in the same way these people too, having stitched together old wives’ tales, and then plucking sayings and words and parables from here and there, wish to fit the oracles of God to their myths.

**31,25,1a** And what they fit to the things within the Pleroma, we have stated.

**31,25,1b** And the things from the Scriptures that they try to appropriate to what is outside their Pleroma are such as these:

**31,25,2** that the Lord came to his passion in the last times of the world for this reason, they say: so that he might show forth the passion that occurred concerning the last of the Aeons, and through this end make manifest the end of the business concerning the Aeons.

**31,25,3** And that maiden of twelve years, the synagogue ruler’s daughter, whom the Lord stood over and raised from the dead, they explain to be a type of Achamoth, whom their Christ, stretching himself out, formed and brought to a perception of the light that had left her.

**31,25,4** And that the Saviour manifested [himself] to her when she was outside the Pleroma in the condition of an untimely birth, they say Paul has stated in the First Epistle to the Corinthians: “And last of all, as to one untimely born, he appeared also to me”.

**31,25,5** And that he likewise made plain the coming of the Saviour with his peers to Achamoth in the same epistle, when he said “A woman must have a veil on her head on account of the angels”; and that, when the Saviour came to her, Achamoth out of modesty put a veil [upon her face], Moses made plain by putting a veil upon his own face.

**31,25,6** And they assert that the Lord also marked out her passions, which she suffered [...]: in saying on the cross “My God, why have you forsaken me”, he indicated that Wisdom had been left by the light and hindered by Limit from her forward rush; her grief, in saying “My soul is very sorrowful”; her fear, in saying “Father, if it is possible, let the cup pass from me”; and her perplexity likewise, in having said “And what I shall say I do not know”.

**31,25,7** And they teach that he showed the three races of men in this way: the material, in answering him who said “I will follow you”: “The Son of Man has nowhere to lay his head”; the psychic, in having said to him who said “I will follow you, but permit me first to take leave of those at home”: “No one who puts his hand to the plough and looks back is fit for the kingdom of heaven”

**31,25,8** (for this one, they say, is of the middle ones; and likewise that one too who professed to have done the greater part of righteousness, and then was not willing to follow, but was overcome by wealth so as not to become perfect, him too they will have to be of the psychic race);

**31,25,9** and the spiritual, in his saying “Leave the dead to bury their own dead; but you, go and proclaim the kingdom of God”, and in saying to Zacchaeus the tax collector “Hurry and come down, since today I must stay in your house”; for these, they announce, were of the spiritual race.

**31,25,10a** And the parable of the leaven, which the woman is said to have concealed in three measures of flour, they say shows the three races.

**31,25,10b** For they teach that the woman is said to be Wisdom, and the three measures of flour the three races of men, spiritual, psychic, earthy; and they teach that the leaven spoken of is the Saviour himself.

**31,25,11a** And that Paul [too] expressly spoke of earthy, psychic and spiritual men: in one place, “As is the earthy, such also are the earthy”; in another, “But the psychic man does not receive the things of the Spirit”; and in another, “The spiritual man judges all things”.

**31,25,11b** And the saying “But the psychic man does not receive the things of the Spirit”, they say, was spoken of the Demiurge, who, being psychic, knew neither the Mother, who is spiritual, nor her seed, nor the Aeons in the Pleroma.

**31,25,12** And they teach that Paul said “And if the firstfruit is holy, so is the lump” to show that the Saviour took up the firstfruits of those whom he was to save, saying that the firstfruit means the spiritual element and the lump means us, that is, the psychic church, whose lump, they say, he took up and raised together with himself, since he himself was leaven.

**31,26,1** And they say that he signifies that Achamoth strayed beyond the Pleroma, was given form by Christ and was searched out by the Saviour, when he says that he has come for the [sheep] that had gone astray.

**31,26,2** For they explain that the sheep that went astray is said of their Mother, from whom they hold that the church here was sown; and by the straying they understand her sojourn outside the Pleroma in [all] the passions, out of which they suppose that matter came into being.

**31,26,3a** And the woman who sweeps the house and finds the drachma, they relate, is said of the Wisdom above, who, having lost her Intention, finds it later, when all things have been cleansed through the coming of the Saviour.

**31,26,3b** Hence this too, according to them, is restored within the Pleroma.

**31,26,4** [And] Symeon, who took Christ into his arms and gave thanks to God and said, “Now you release your servant, Master, according to your word, in peace”, they say is a type of the Demiurge, who, when the Saviour came, learned of his own removal and gave thanks to Depth.

**31,26,5** And by Anna, who is proclaimed a prophetess in the gospel, who lived seven years with a husband and remained a widow all the rest of her time, until, seeing the Saviour, she recognised him and spoke about him to everyone, they declare that Achamoth is most plainly indicated, who, having [then] seen the Saviour for a short while together with his peers, remained in the Midst for all the rest of the time and awaited him, looking for when he would come again and restore her to her own pair.

**31,26,6** And her name too, they say, was indicated by the Saviour in saying “And Wisdom was justified by her children,” and by Paul as follows: “But we speak wisdom among the perfect.”

**31,26,7** And they claim that Paul spoke of the pairings within the Pleroma too, showing them in the case of one; for writing about the pairing that belongs to this life he said, “This mystery is great, but I speak with reference to Christ and the church.”

**31,27,1** Further, they teach that John the disciple of the Lord indicated the first Ogdoad, saying in these very words as follows:

**31,27,2** John, the disciple of the Lord, wishing to speak of the origin of all things, according to which the Father emitted all things, posits a certain beginning, that which was first begotten by God, which he has also called Son and only-begotten God, in whom the Father emitted all things in seed.

**31,27,3a** And by this, he says, the Word was emitted, and in him the whole substance of the Aeons, which the Word himself later gave form to.

**31,27,3b** Since, then, he is speaking of the first origin, he does well to make his teaching begin from the Beginning, that is, from the Son and the Word.

**31,27,4** And he speaks as follows: “In the beginning the Word was, and the Word was in the presence of God, and the Word was God.”

**31,27,5** “He was in the beginning in the presence of God”: having first distinguished the three, God and Beginning and Word, he unites them again, so as to show both the emission of each of them, of the Son and of the Word, and their union with one another and at the same time with the Father.

**31,27,6a** For the Beginning is in the Father and from the Father, and the Word is in the Beginning and from the Beginning.

**31,27,6b** Rightly, then, he said, “In the beginning the Word was”; for he was in the Son.

**31,27,6c** “And the Word was in the presence of God”; for the Beginning too [...].

**31,27,6d** And “the Word was God”, consistently; for that which is born of God is God.

**31,27,6e** “He was in the beginning in the presence of God” showed the order of the emission.

**31,27,7a** “All things came into being through him, and without him not one thing came into being”; for to all the Aeons after him the Word became the cause of form and of origin.

**31,27,7b** But “what came into being in him,” he says, “is life.”

**31,27,7c** Here he indicated a pairing as well; for he said that all things as a whole came into being through him, but life in him.

**31,27,8** This, then, which came into being in him, is more closely his own than the things that came into being through him; for it is with him and bears fruit through him.

**31,27,9** For since he adds, “And the life was the light of men,” in saying “Man” just now he indicated the Church as well, under the same name as Man, so that by the one name he might make plain the partnership of the pairing; for from the Word and Life come Man and Church.

**31,27,10a** And he called Life the light of men because they have been enlightened by her, which is to say, given form and made manifest.

**31,27,10b** And Paul too says this: “For everything that is made manifest is light.”

**31,27,10c** Since, then, Life made manifest and begot both Man and the Church, she is said to be their light.

**31,27,11a** Clearly, then, John has by these words made plain, among other things, the second Tetrad: the Word and Life, Man and Church.

**31,27,11b** But indeed he also indicated the first Tetrad.

**31,27,12** For in giving an account of the Saviour and saying that all things outside the Pleroma were given form through him, he says that he is the fruit of the whole Pleroma.

**31,27,13** For he has called him the light that shines in the darkness and was not overcome by it, since, though he had fitted together everything that came into being from the passion, he remained unknown to them.

**31,27,14** And he calls him Son and Truth and Life and “Word become flesh, whose glory we beheld,” he says, “and his glory was such as was that of the Only-begotten, given him by the Father, full of grace and truth.”

**31,27,15** And he speaks as follows: “And the Word became flesh and made his dwelling among us, and we beheld his glory, glory as of the Only-begotten from the Father, full of grace and truth”; precisely, then, he also indicated the first Tetrad, in naming Father and Grace and Only-begotten and Truth.

**31,27,16a** Thus John has spoken of the first Ogdoad, the mother of all the Aeons.

**31,27,16b** For he has named Father and Grace and Only-begotten and Truth and Word and Life and Man and Church.

**31,28,1a** You see, beloved, the method by which they deceive themselves, abusing the Scriptures and trying to build up their fiction out of them.

**31,28,1b** For this is why I have set out their very words, so that from them you may perceive the craftiness of their method and the wickedness of their error.

**31,28,2** For in the first place, if John had intended to indicate the Ogdoad above, he would have kept the order of the emission, and he would have placed the first Tetrad, which is, as they say, the most venerable, among the first names, and so joined the second to it, so that through the order of the names the order of the Ogdoad might be shown; and he would not have made mention of the first Tetrad last, after so great an interval, as though he had forgotten it and then remembered.

**31,28,3** Then too, if he had wished to signify the pairings, he would not have left out the name of the Church either; but either in the case of the other pairings too he would have been content with the designation of the males, since those others could equally be understood along with them, so that he might have preserved the unity throughout, [or,] if he was listing the consorts of the others, he would also have indicated the consort of Man and would not have left us to take her name by divination.

**31,28,4a** Plain, then, is the distortion of [their] interpretation.

**31,28,4b** For whereas John heralds one God, the Almighty, and one Only-begotten, Christ Jesus, by whom, as he says, everything came into being, this one the Son of God [...], this one the Only-begotten, this one the Maker of all things, this one the true light that enlightens every man, this one the Maker of the world, this one who came to his own, this one who himself became flesh and made his dwelling among us —

**31,28,5** these men, twisting the interpretation with a show of plausibility, want the Only-begotten, whom they also call Beginning, to be one according to the emission, and want the Saviour who came into being to be another, and the Word, son of the Only-begotten, another, and Christ, emitted for the restoration of the Pleroma, another.
