**34,2,4a** And by saying some such things and driving the wretched woman into a frenzy he appeared a wonder-worker, the large cup being filled from the small one, so that it even overflowed from it.

**34,2,4b** And doing certain other things similar to these, he has deceived many and drawn them away after him.

**34,2,5** It is likely that he also has a certain demon as his familiar, through whom he himself seems to prophesy, and he makes as many women as he deems worthy [to become] partakers of his grace prophesy.

**34,2,6a** For he busies himself above all with women, and of these with the finely dressed, those clad in purple-bordered robes, and the wealthiest, whom he often tries to lead astray, and flattering them he says to them, “I wish to give you a share of my Grace, since the Father of all things continually sees your angel before his face.

**34,2,6b** And the place of the Greatness is in us;

**34,2,7a** we must be established into the One.

**34,2,7b** Receive first from me and through me the Grace.

**34,2,7c** Make yourself ready as a bride awaiting her bridegroom, in order that you may be that which I am, and I that which you are.

**34,2,7d** Set firmly in your bridal chamber the seed of the light.

**34,2,7e** Receive from me the bridegroom, and make room for him and be contained in him.

**34,2,7f** Behold, the Grace has come down upon you; open your mouth and prophesy.”

**34,2,8** And when the woman replies, “Never yet have I prophesied, and I do not know how to prophesy”, he makes certain invocations a second time, to the astonishment of the deluded woman, and says to her, “Open your mouth [and] speak whatever it may be, and you will prophesy.”

**34,2,9** And she, puffed up and made a simpleton by what has been said, her soul heated through by the expectation that she is about to prophesy, her heart throbbing more than it should, ventures to utter nonsense and whatever comes to her,

**34,2,10** all of it emptily and audaciously, as one heated by an empty spirit (as one better than we said of such [prophets], that a soul heated by empty air is an audacious and shameless thing)

**34,2,11** and from then on she supposes herself a prophetess, and gives thanks to Mark, who imparted to her of his own grace, and tries to repay him, not only by the gift of her possessions (which is how he has amassed a great quantity of money), but also by the fellowship of the body, eager to be united with him in every way, so that she may go down with him into the One.

**34,2,12** But already some of the more faithful women, who have the fear of God and were not deceived, whom he set himself to win over like the rest by bidding them prophesy, have blown upon him in contempt and anathematized him, and have separated themselves from such a company,

**34,2,13** knowing well that prophesying does not come to people from Mark the magician, but those upon whom God sends his grace from above have prophecy given by God, and they speak then, where and when God wills, and not when Mark commands.

**34,2,14a** For that which commands is greater and more authoritative than that which is commanded, since the one leads and the other is subordinate.

**34,2,14b** If then Mark commands, or someone else, as these men are always accustomed at dinners to play [by] the lot and to bid one another prophesy and to divine for themselves according to their own desires, the one who commands, though he is a man, will be greater and more authoritative than the prophetic spirit, which is impossible.

**34,2,15** But such spirits as are commanded by them and speak whenever they themselves wish are earthly and feeble, but audacious and shameless, sent out by Satan for the deception and destruction of those who do not keep vigorously the faith which they received from the beginning through the church.

**34,3,1** That this Mark also contrives love potions and charms for some of the women, if not for all, so as to outrage even their bodies, these women, having often returned to the church of God, have confessed, both that they had been defiled in body by him and that they had loved him with a great passion;

**34,3,2** so that even a certain deacon of ours in Asia, who received him into his house, fell into this misfortune, his wife, who was comely, having been corrupted in both mind and body by this magician and having followed him for a long time;

**34,3,3** then, when the brethren had converted her with much labour, she spent all her time in confession, mourning and lamenting over the corruption she had suffered from the magician.

**34,3,4** And some disciples of his, roaming about in the same regions, have deceived and corrupted many silly women, proclaiming themselves perfect, as though no one could be made equal to the greatness of their knowledge, not even if you were to name Paul or Peter or any other of the apostles,

**34,3,5** but that [they themselves] know more than all, and that they alone have drunk down the full greatness of the ineffable power’s knowledge, and that they themselves are on high, above every power.

**34,3,6a** Hence they also do all things freely, having fear of no one in anything.

**34,3,6b** For because of the redemption they become [both] beyond seizing and invisible to the judge.

**34,3,6c** And if he should lay hold of them, they would stand before him with the redemption and say these words:

**34,3,7** “O assessor of God and of the mystic Silence before the ages, through whom the Greatnesses, which continually behold the face of the Father, using you as guide and introducer, draw their forms up on high, forms after which that over-bold one, having formed a fancy through the goodness of the Forefather, put forth us, the images, holding at that time the thought of the things above as a dream: behold, the judge is near and the herald bids me make my defence;

**34,3,8** but you, as one who knows the affairs of both, present to the judge the account on behalf of both of us as being one.”

**34,3,9** And the Mother, hearing these words, quickly put the Homeric cap of Hades upon them, so that they might escape the judge unseen, and at once drawing them up she brought them into the bridal chamber and gave them back to their own bridegrooms.

**34,3,10** Saying and doing such things, they have deceived many women in our regions too, those of the Rhône country, who, being seared in their conscience, some even confess openly, while others, ashamed of this, quietly somehow [...] themselves, and, having despaired of the life of God, some have apostatized altogether, while others waver between the two and have suffered what the proverb says, being neither outside nor inside, and having this as the fruit of the seed of the children of knowledge.

**34,4,1** This Mark [then], saying that he alone has become the womb and receptacle of the Silence of Colorbasus, inasmuch as he is only-begotten, has brought forth the very [seed] of the Deficiency deposited in him somewhat as follows.

**34,4,2** The all-highest Tetrad itself, he says, came down to him from the invisible and unnameable places in womanly form (since, he says, the world could not bear her male form), and disclosed to him who she was, and the generation of all things, never yet revealed by her to anyone, whether of gods or of men, recounting it to this man alone, speaking thus:

**34,4,3** when first the Father, [who has no father] at all, the inconceivable and insubstantial, the one neither male nor female, wished that what was ineffable in him should become uttered and what was invisible should take on form, he opened his mouth and put forth a word like himself, who, standing beside him, showed him what he was, appearing himself as the form of the invisible.

**34,4,4a** And the utterance of the name came about thus: he uttered the opening word of his name, one that was a beginning, and its syllable consisted of four elements.

**34,4,4b** [And] he joined to it the second, and it too was of four elements.

**34,4,4c** Next he spoke the third, and it was of ten elements.

**34,4,4d** And he spoke the one after these, and it was of twelve elements.

**34,4,4e** So the utterance of the whole name came to thirty elements and four syllables.

**34,4,5** And each of the elements has its own letters and its own character and its own utterance and shapes and images, and none of them is such as to behold the form of that one whose element it is; nor does it even know him, nor indeed does each know the utterance of its neighbour, but it thinks that in uttering what it itself utters, as the all, it names the whole.

**34,4,6** For each of them, being a part of the whole, names its own sound as the all, and they do not cease sounding until, uttering singly, they arrive at the last letter of the last element.

**34,4,7a** And it was then, he said, that the restoration of all things would come about, when all things, having come down into the one letter, sound one and the same utterance; and of this utterance he supposed the Amen spoken by us together to be an image.

**34,4,7b** And the sounds are those which give form to the Aeon who is insubstantial and unbegotten; and these are the forms which the Lord called angels, those which continually behold the face of the Father.

**34,4,8** The common and utterable names of the elements he called Aeons and words and roots and seeds and pleromas and fruits, but he said that the individual and particular names of each of them are to be understood as contained in the name of the Church.

**34,4,9** Of these elements, the final letter of the [last] element sent forth its own voice; and [the] sound of it, going out, begot elements of its own after the image of the elements; and from these, he says, the things here have been set in order and the things before them have been begotten.

**34,4,10** The letter itself, however, whose sound followed along with the sound downward, he says was taken back up by its own syllable, for the filling up of the whole; but the sound remained below, as though cast outside.

**34,4,11** But the element itself, from which the letter came down below together with its own utterance, he says is of thirty letters, and each one of the thirty letters has in itself other letters, through which the name of the letter is named;

**34,4,12a** and again the others are named through other letters, and the others through others, so that the multitude of the letters runs out into infinity.

**34,4,12b** You might learn more clearly what is meant in this way:

**34,5,1** The element delta has five letters in itself, the delta itself and the epsilon and the lambda and the tau and the alpha, and these letters in turn are written through other letters, and the others through others.

**34,5,2** If then the whole substance of the delta runs out into infinity, other letters ever begetting others and succeeding one another, how much greater than that element must the sea of the letters be?

**34,5,3** And if the one letter is thus infinite, consider the whole name’s depth of letters, out of which Mark’s Silence taught that the Forefather is composed.

**34,5,4** Hence too the Father, knowing his own incomprehensibility, granted to the elements, which he also calls Aeons, to each one of them, to cry out its own utterance, because one could not utter the whole.

**34,5,5a** And when the Tetractys had made these things plain to him, she said: Now I wish to show you Truth herself as well.

**34,5,5b** For I have brought her down from the dwellings above, so that you may behold her naked and learn her beauty, and also hear her speaking and marvel at her wisdom.

**34,5,6a** See, then: the head above, the alpha and the omega; the neck, beta and psi; the shoulders together with the hands, gamma and chi; the breast, delta and phi; the diaphragm, epsilon and upsilon; the belly, zeta and tau; the private parts, eta and sigma; the thighs, theta and rho; the knees, iota and pi; the shins, kappa and omicron; the ankles, lambda and xi; the feet, mu and nu.

**34,5,6b** This is the body of Truth according to the magician, this the shape of the element, this the character of the letter.

**34,5,7** And he calls this element Man; and he says that it is the fountain of every word, and the beginning of every voice, and the utterance of all that is ineffable, and the mouth of the silent Silence.

**34,5,8** And this is her body; but you, raising aloft [the] thought of [the] mind, hear from the mouths of Truth the self-begetting and father-giving word.

**34,6,1** When she had said these things, Truth looked at him and opened her mouth and spoke a word; and the word became a name, and this name is the one we know and speak, Christ Jesus; and having named it she at once fell silent.

**34,6,2** And when Mark was expecting her to say something more, the Tetractys again came forward into the midst and said:

**34,6,3a** how lightly you have esteemed the word which you heard from the mouths of Truth.

**34,6,3b** This, which you know and suppose you [have possessed] of old, is not the name; for you have only its sound, but you do not know its power.

**34,6,4** For Jesus is an episemon name having six letters, known by all those of the calling; but the one among the Aeons of the Pleroma, being manifold, is of another form and a different type, known by those kindred beings whose Greatnesses are with him continually.

**34,6,5** Know, then, that these twenty-four letters among you are emanations, bearing the image of the three powers which embrace the whole number of the elements above.

**34,6,6** For consider the nine mute letters to be of the Father and Truth, because they are without voice — unspeakable, that is, and inexpressible;

**34,6,7** and the semivowels, which are eight, of the Word and Life, because they are, as it were, midway between the mutes and the vowels, and receive the outflow from those above and the ascent from those beneath them;

**34,6,8a** and the vowels, which are themselves seven, of Man and the Church, since [the] voice, going forth through Man, gave form to all things.

**34,6,8b** For the sound of the voice clothed them with form.

**34,6,9** The Word, [then], and Life have the eight, and Man and the Church the seven, and the Father and Truth the nine.

**34,6,10** But in the matter of the number that fell short, the one who had been withdrawn into the Father came down, sent forth to the one from whom he had been separated, for the correction of what had been done, so that the unity of the pleromas, possessing equality, might bear as fruit in all the one power that comes from all.

**34,6,11** And thus the one of the seven received the power of the eight, and the [three] places became alike in their numbers, being ogdoads, which, coming three times upon themselves, displayed the number of twenty-four.

**34,6,12** The three elements, however, which he himself says belong to the three powers in conjunction, and which are six, from which the twenty-four elements flowed forth, when quadrupled by the ratio of the ineffable Tetrad, make the same number as they; and these, he says, belong to the Unnameable.

**34,6,13** And they are borne by the three powers into the likeness of the invisible, and of these elements the double letters among us are images of images, which, counted together with the twenty-four elements, by the power that goes by proportion make [the] number of thirty.

**34,7,1** He says that the fruit of this account and of this dispensation appeared in the likeness of an image, namely that one who after the six days went up the mountain as the fourth and became the sixth, who came down and was held fast in the hebdomad,

**34,7,2** being the episemon ogdoad and having in himself the whole number of the elements, [which] the descent of the dove made manifest when he came to baptism, the dove which is omega and alpha; for its number is eight hundred and one;

**34,7,3** and for this reason Moses said that man came into being on the sixth of the days, and that the dispensation too [...] on the sixth of the days [...], which is [the] Preparation, the last man was made manifest for the regeneration of the first man, and that the beginning and end of this dispensation is the sixth hour, in which he was nailed to the wood.

**34,7,4a** For the perfect Mind, knowing that the number six possesses the power of making and of regeneration, made manifest to the sons of the light the regeneration which, through the Episemon that appeared, came about into him and through him.

**34,7,4b** Hence also, he says, the double letters have the episemon number.

**34,7,4c** For the episemon number, mingled with the twenty-four elements, completed the name of thirty letters.

**34,7,5a** And he has used as minister the greatness of the seven numbers, as Mark’s Silence says, so that the fruit of the self-willed counsel might be made manifest.

**34,7,5b** This episemon number, however, he says, understand for the present as the one formed upon the Episemon, the one who, so to speak, was parted or halved and stayed outside, who by his own power and wisdom, through the emanation from himself, gave soul to this world of the seven powers in imitation of the power of the hebdomad, and appointed it to be the soul of the whole visible universe.

**34,7,6** This one himself, then, makes use of this work as one that came about by his own free choice, while the rest, being imitations of the inimitable, serve the Thought of the Mother.

**34,7,7** And the first heaven voices the alpha, the heaven following it the epsilon, the third [the] eta, while the fourth, the middle one of the seven, gives utterance to the iota’s power; the fifth sounds the omicron, the sixth the upsilon, and the seventh, fourth from the middle, cries out the element omega, as Mark’s Silence, which babbles much but says nothing true, affirms.

**34,7,8a** And these powers, he says, all intertwined together with one another, sound forth and glorify him who emitted them, while the sound’s glory is sent up to the Forefather.

**34,7,8b** And the sound of this doxology, borne to the earth, he says, became the moulder and begetter of the things upon the earth.

**34,7,9a** And he brings the proof from newly born infants, whose soul, as soon as it comes out of the womb, cries out the sound of each one of these elements.

**34,7,9b** As then the seven powers, he says, glorify the Word, so also the soul in infants, weeping and wailing [...] Mark, glorifies him.

**34,7,10** And for this reason David too said, “From the mouth of infants and of those at the breast you have prepared praise”, and again, “The heavens tell out God’s glory”; and on this account the soul, when it comes into toils and miseries, for its own refining, cries out the omega in token of praise, in order that the soul above, recognizing its kindred, may send down help to it.

**34,8,1** Concerning, then, the whole name, which is of thirty letters, and the Depth, which grows from the letters of this name, and further [the] body of Truth, which [is] twelve-membered, [each member] consisting of two letters, and her voice, which she [spoke without] speaking, and concerning the explanation of the unspoken name, and concerning the soul of the world and of man, inasmuch as they have the dispensation that is according to the image, thus he talked his nonsense.

**34,8,2** Next we shall report how the Tetractys showed him a power equal in number from the names, so that nothing of what is said by him that has come to us may escape you, beloved, as you have often asked of us.

**34,8,3** Thus, then, their all-wise Silence announces the origin of the twenty-four elements: that with Oneness there coexists Unity, from which come two emanations, as has been said before, Monad and the One, which, being twice two, became four; for twice two are four.

**34,8,4** And again the two and the four, put together, made manifest the number six, and these six, quadrupled, brought forth the twenty-four forms.

**34,8,5** And the names of the first Tetrad, understood as holy of holies and not able to be spoken, are known by the Son alone, and the Father knows what they are; but those named by him with reverence and with faith are these: Ineffable and Silence, Father and Truth.

**34,8,6a** And the total number of this Tetrad is of twenty-four elements.

**34,8,6b** For the name Arrhetos has seven letters in itself, and Seige five, and Pater five, and Aletheia seven, which, when combined — twice five and twice seven — made up the number of twenty-four.

**34,8,7** In the same way Word and Life, Man and Church, the second Tetrad, displayed the same number of elements as well.

**34,8,8a** And the utterable name of the Saviour, Jesus, is of six letters, but his ineffable name of twenty-four letters; Huios Chreistos has twelve letters, whereas the ineffable in Christ has thirty letters.

**34,8,8b** And for this reason, he says, he is alpha and omega, so that he may point to the dove, since this bird has this number.

**34,9,1a** And Jesus, he says, has this ineffable origin.

**34,9,1b** For from the Mother of all things, the first Tetrad, there issued, in the place of a daughter, the second Tetrad, and it became an Ogdoad, from which a Decad issued.

**34,9,1c** Thus there came to be a Decad and an Ogdoad.

**34,9,2** The Decad, then, coming together with the Ogdoad and making it tenfold, brought forth the number eighty, and again making the eighty tenfold it begot the number eight hundred, so that the whole number of the letters, proceeding from Ogdoad to Decad, is eight and eighty and eight hundred, which is Jesus.

**34,9,3a** For the name Jesus, according to the number in its letters, is eight hundred and eighty-eight.

**34,9,3b** You have, plainly, the supercelestial origin of Jesus too, according to them.

**34,9,4a** Hence also the alphabet of the Greeks has eight units and eight tens and eight hundreds, displaying the sum of eight hundred and eighty-eight, that is, Jesus, who consists of all the numbers.

**34,9,4b** And for this reason he is named alpha and omega, signifying his origin from all.

**34,9,5a** And again thus: when the first Tetrad is added into itself by progression of number, the number ten appeared.

**34,9,5b** For one and two and three and four, put together, make ten, which is iota, and they will have this to be Jesus.

**34,9,6** But Chreistos too, he says, being of eight letters, signifies the first Ogdoad, which, intertwined with the iota, brought forth Jesus.

**34,9,7** And he is also named, he says, Huios Chreistos — the Dodecad, that is; for Huios as a name has four letters, and Chreistos eight; and these, combined, displayed the greatness of the Dodecad.

**34,9,8** Before, then, he says, the episemon of this name appeared, that is, Jesus the Son, men were in great ignorance and error;

**34,9,9** but when the name of six letters was made manifest, which clothed itself with flesh so that it might come down to the perception of man, having in itself the six and the twenty-four, then, knowing him, they ceased from their ignorance and went up from death to life, the name having become for them a way to the Father of truth.

**34,9,10a** For the Father of all things willed to dissolve ignorance and to destroy death.

**34,9,10b** And the dissolution of ignorance was the knowledge of him.

**34,9,10c** And for this reason the Man was chosen who had been ordered according to his will after the image of the power above.

**34,10,1a** For from a Tetrad [...] the Aeons came forth.

**34,10,1b** And the Tetrad contained Man and Church, Word and Life.

**34,10,1c** From these, then, he says, powers flowed forth and generated the Jesus who appeared on earth.

**34,10,2** And the angel Gabriel filled the place of the Word, the Holy Spirit that of Life, the power of the Most High that of Man, while the virgin displayed the place of the Church.

**34,10,3** And thus, in his teaching, the man according to the dispensation is generated through Mary, whom, when he had passed through the womb, the Father of all things chose through the Word for the knowledge of himself.

**34,10,4** And when he came to the water, there came down upon him as a dove the one who had run back up on high and filled up the twelfth number, in whom is the seed of those who were sown together with him and came down with him and went up with him.

**34,10,5** And the power itself that came down, he says, is the seed of the Father, having in itself both the Father and the Son, and the unnameable power of Silence, known through these, and every one of the Aeons.

**34,10,6a** And this is the spirit that spoke through the mouth of Jesus, the one that acknowledged itself as Son of man and revealed the Father; and having [indeed] come down into Jesus, it was united with him.

**34,10,6b** And the Saviour of the dispensation, [Jesus], he says, destroyed death, and made known the Father, Christ.

**34,10,7** He says, then, that Jesus is the name of the man of the dispensation, but that it was given for the likening and forming of the Man who was to come down into him, and that, having made room for him, he possessed both Man himself and the Word himself, and the Father, the Ineffable, Silence, Truth, Church and Life.

**34,11,1a** These things are now beyond “Alas, alas” and “Woe”, and beyond every tragic [out]cry and lamentation.

**34,11,1b** For who would not hate the ill-contriving maker of such monstrous lies, seeing the truth made an idol by Mark, and that tattooed with the letters of the alphabet?

**34,11,2** Recently, compared with the beginning, yesterday and the day before, as the saying goes, the Greeks acknowledge having received at first sixteen letters from Cadmus, and then afterwards, as time went on, they themselves discovered now the aspirates, now the double letters, and last of all, they say, Palamedes added the long ones to these.

**34,11,3a** So until these things existed [among] the Greeks, Truth was not; for by your reckoning, Mark, her body is later than Cadmus and those before him, later than those who added the remaining elements, and later even than yourself.

**34,11,3b** For you alone brought down [as] an idol the one called Truth by you.

**34,11,4** And who will endure your Silence, which babbles so much, which names the Unnameable and expounds the Ineffable and recounts the Untraceable, and says that he opened his mouth, he whom you call bodiless and formless, and brought forth a Word, as though he were one of the composite living creatures;

**34,11,5** and that his Word, being like the one who emitted him and having become the form of the invisible, is of thirty elements and four syllables; so then the Father of all things, as you say, will be, according to the likeness of the Word, of thirty elements and four syllables.

**34,11,6** Or again, who will endure you, as you shut up into shapes and numbers, now thirty, now twenty-four, now only six, the Word of God, the founder and fashioner and maker of all things, and chop him up into four syllables and thirty elements, and the Lord of all things,

**34,11,7** who established the heavens, bring down to the number eight hundred and eighty-eight, like the alphabet, and the Father himself, who contains all things but is himself uncontained, subdivide into a Tetrad and an Ogdoad and a Decad and a Dodecad, and through such multiplications expound what is, as you say, ineffable and inconceivable in the Father?

**34,11,8** And of him whom you name bodiless and insubstantial you construct the substance and the subsistence out of many letters, some begotten from others, having yourself become a false Daedalus and a bad craftsman of the all-highest power;

**34,11,9** and the [substance] which you say is indivisible you subdivide into mute and vowel and semivowel sounds, falsely ascribing the mute among them to the Father of all things and to his Thought, and you have cast all who believe you into the uttermost blasphemy and the greatest impiety.

**34,11,10** Therefore [also], justly and fittingly for such audacity of yours, the divine elder, herald of the truth, has raised his voice against you in verse, speaking thus:

**34,11,11** “Idol-maker Mark and watcher of portents, skilled in astrology and the magic art, by which you strengthen the teachings of error, showing signs to those led astray by you, the enterprises of apostate power, which your father Satan ever supplies you to perform through the angelic power Azazel, having you as forerunner of villainy against God.”

**34,11,12a** And so much for the God-loving elder; but we shall try to go through briefly the rest of their initiation, which is long, and to bring into the open what has been hidden for a long time.

**34,11,12b** For in this way they would become easy for all to refute.

**34,12,1** These men, then, who bring all things down to numbers, uniting into one the origin of their Aeons and the straying and finding of the sheep, attempt to set them forth in a more mystical way, asserting that all things consist of a monad and a dyad;

**34,12,2a** and counting from the monad up to four, they thus beget the decad.

**34,12,2b** For one and two and three and four, put together, brought forth the number of the ten Aeons.

**34,12,2c** And again the dyad, advancing from itself as far as the episemon, as two and four and six, displayed the dodecad.

**34,12,3** And again, when we count likewise from the dyad up to ten, the triacontad was displayed, in which are an ogdoad and a decad and a dodecad.

**34,12,4** They say, then, that the dodecad, because the episemon followed along with it, had the episemon passion, and that for this reason, since the slip occurred about the twelfth number, the sheep frisked away and went astray, since they assert that the defection came about from the dodecad.

**34,12,5** In the same way they divine that one power, having departed from the dodecad, was lost, and that she is the woman who lost her drachma, lit a lamp and found it.

**34,12,6a** Thus, then, the numbers that are left too, in the case of the drachma the nine, and in that of the sheep the eleven, interwoven with one another, bring forth the number ninety-nine, since nine times eleven make ninety-nine.

**34,12,6b** Hence they say that the Amen too has this number.

**34,12,7a** And I will not shrink from reporting to you how they explain it in yet another way, so that you may perceive their fruit from every side.

**34,12,7b** For the element eta together with the episemon they will have to be an ogdoad, since it lies in the eighth place from the alpha; then again, reckoning the number of the elements themselves without the episemon and adding them up as far as the eta, they display the triacontad.

**34,12,8** For if [one] begins from the alpha and ends at the eta in the number of the elements, leaving out the episemon and adding up the increase of the letters, he will find the number thirty.

**34,12,9a** For up to the element epsilon they come to fifteen; then the number seven, added to them, made twenty-two; and the eta, which is eight, joining these, filled up the most wonderful triacontad.

**34,12,9b** And from this they demonstrate that the Ogdoad is mother of the thirty Aeons.

**34,12,10a** Since, then, the number thirty is united from three powers, by becoming itself three times over it made ninety; for three times thirty is ninety.

**34,12,10b** And the triad itself too, put together upon itself, begot nine.

**34,12,10c** And thus the ogdoad brought forth among them the number ninety-nine.

**34,12,11** And since the twelfth Aeon, departing, left the eleven above, they say that the type of the letters is fittingly laid down in the shape of the reckoning (for the lambda is placed eleventh of the letters, and it is the number thirty), and that it is laid down after the image of the dispensation above, since from the alpha, without the episemon, the number of the letters themselves up to the lambda, added together according to the increase of the letters, with the lambda itself makes the number ninety-nine.

**34,12,12** And that the lambda, being eleventh in order, came down in search of what is like it, in order to fill up the twelfth number, and having found it was filled up, is evident from the very shape of the element.

**34,12,13** For the lambda, having come as it were in search of what is like it, and having found it and snatched it to itself, filled up the place of the twelfth, the element mu being composed of two lambdas.

**34,12,14** Hence, too, they flee through knowledge the place of the ninety-nine, that is, the Deficiency, a type of the left hand, and pursue the one, which, added to the ninety-nine, has transferred them to the right hand.

**34,13,1a** You, beloved, as you go through these things, will laugh a great deal, I know well, [...] at such conceited folly of theirs.

**34,13,1b** But worthy of mourning are those who so frigidly and forcedly disparage so great a religion, and the greatness of the truly ineffable power, and such great dispensations of God, by means of alpha and beta and by means of numbers.

**34,13,2** And as many as depart from the church and are persuaded by these old wives’ fables truly [are] self-condemned; and these Paul bids us avoid after a first and a second admonition.

**34,13,3** And John, the disciple of the Lord, made their condemnation more severe, wishing that not even a greeting should be spoken to them by us; “For he who greets them”, he says, “shares in their wicked works.”

**34,13,4a** And rightly; “for there is no rejoicing for the ungodly”, says the Lord.

**34,13,4b** And ungodly beyond all ungodliness are these, who say that the maker of heaven and earth, the sole almighty God, beyond whom there exists no other God, was emitted from a Deficiency, which itself came into being from another Deficiency; so that according to them he is the emanation of a third Deficiency.

**34,13,5** Having truly blown upon this opinion in contempt and anathematized it, we must [also] flee far away from them, and know that the more they insist and rejoice in their inventions, the more they are worked upon by the ogdoad of the wicked spirits,

**34,13,6a** just as those who have fallen into a state of frenzy, the more they laugh and think themselves strong and do everything as though they were healthy, and some things even beyond being healthy, the worse off they are.

**34,13,6b** And likewise these too, the more they think themselves wise beyond measure and unstring themselves by shooting with overstrained bows, the less are they of sound mind.

**34,13,7** For the unclean spirit of folly, having gone out, and then having found them occupied not with God but with worldly inquiries, has taken along with it seven other spirits more wicked than itself, and having puffed up their mind as though they were able to conceive the things that are above God, and having made it ready for derangement, has lodged in them the ogdoad of the folly of the wicked spirits.

**34,14,1** And I wish to recount to you also how they say that the creation itself was fashioned after the image of the invisible things by the Demiurge through the Mother, without his knowing it.

**34,14,2** First, they say, the four elements, fire, water, earth, air, were emitted as an image of the first Tetrad above, and their operations, counted together, that is, hot and cold, dry and moist, portray the Ogdoad exactly.

**34,14,3a** Next [...] they enumerate ten powers thus: seven bodily circle-shaped things, which they also call heavens; then the circle that encompasses them, which they also name the eighth heaven; and besides these, sun and moon.

**34,14,3b** These, being ten in number, they say are images of the invisible decad which came forth from Word and Life.

**34,14,4a** And the dodecad is indicated through the so-called zodiacal circle.

**34,14,4b** For the twelve signs of the zodiac, they say, most clearly sketch in shadow the Dodecad that is daughter of Man and Church.

**34,14,5** And since, they say, the heaven above, which presses heavily on the very vault and counterbalances by its own slowness the swiftness of those others, has been yoked against the motion of the universe, which is most swift, so that in thirty years it completes its circuit from sign to sign, it is, they say, an image of Limit, which encompasses their Mother of thirty names.

**34,14,6** And the moon again, going round her own heaven in thirty days, represents through the days the number of the thirty Aeons.

**34,14,7** And the sun, going round in twelve months and completing its circular return, makes the Dodecad manifest through the twelve months; and the days themselves, having the measure of twelve hours, are a type of the Dodecad that is [not] shining.

**34,14,8** Moreover, they say that the hour too, the twelfth part of the day, has been made up of thirty degrees because of the image of the Triacontad.

**34,14,9a** And the circumference of the zodiacal circle itself is of three hundred and sixty degrees; for each sign has thirty degrees.

**34,14,9b** Thus, then, they say, the image of the conjunction of the twelve with the thirty has been preserved through the circle too.

**34,14,10** Moreover, asserting that the earth too is divided into twelve zones, and that in each zone it receives [one] power from the heavens perpendicularly and bears children like the power that sends down the outflow, they confidently affirm that it is a most clear type of the Dodecad and of its children.

**34,14,11** Besides these things, they say that the Demiurge, wishing to imitate the boundless and eternal and unlimited and timeless nature of the Ogdoad above, and not being able to express its permanence and everlastingness, because [he] is the fruit of a Deficiency, laid down its eternity in times and seasons and numbers of many years, thinking to imitate its boundlessness by the multitude of the times.

**34,14,12** And here, they say, since Truth escaped him, falsehood followed, and for this reason his work receives dissolution when the times are fulfilled.

**34,15,1a** And saying such things about the creation, each of them every day begets, as best he can, [something] newer.

**34,15,1b** For no one among them is perfect who has not borne great lies as fruit.

**34,15,2a** And it is necessary that we point out all that they transform from the prophetic writings and bring the refutation against them.

**34,15,2b** For Moses, they say, beginning his account of the creation, at once in the beginning pointed to the Mother of all things, saying, “In the beginning God made the heaven and the earth.”

**34,15,3a** Naming, then, these four, God and beginning, heaven and earth, he depicted their Tetractys, as they themselves say.

**34,15,3b** And indicating its invisible and hidden nature he said, “Now the earth was invisible and unfashioned.”

**34,15,4** [And] they will have it that he spoke thus of the second Tetrad, offspring of the first Tetrad, naming abyss and darkness, and in them water, and the spirit borne over the water.

**34,15,5** After this, making mention of the Decad, he speaks of light and day and night, firmament and evening and what is called morning, dry land and sea, and further herbage, and in the tenth place the tree; and thus through the ten names he indicated the ten Aeons.

**34,15,6a** And the power of the Dodecad was portrayed by him thus: for he speaks of the sun and the moon, of stars and seasons, of years and sea-monsters, [and further] of fishes and creeping things, birds and four-footed creatures, wild beasts, and, after all these, of man as the twelfth.

**34,15,6b** Thus they teach that the Triacontad was spoken by the Spirit through Moses.

**34,15,7** Moreover, the moulded man too, after the image of the power above, has in himself the power from the one fountain (and this is seated in the region of the brain), from which four powers flow forth after the image of the Tetrad above, called, the first sight, the second hearing, the third smell, and the fourth taste.

**34,15,8** And the Ogdoad, they say, is indicated through man thus: he has two senses of hearing and as many of sight, and further two of smell and a twofold taste, of bitter and of sweet.

**34,15,9a** And they teach that the whole man has the whole image of the Triacontad in this way: in the hands, through the fingers, he carries the Decad, and in the whole body, divided into twelve members, the Dodecad.

**34,15,9b** And they divide it in the same way as, in their teaching, the body of Truth has been divided, of which we have spoken already.

**34,15,9c** And the Ogdoad, being ineffable and invisible, is understood as hidden in the entrails.

**34,16,1** Again, they assert that the sun, the great luminary, came into being on the fourth of the days because of the number of the Tetrad.

**34,16,2** And the curtains of the tabernacle constructed by Moses, made of fine linen and blue and purple and scarlet, displayed among them the same image.

**34,16,3** And they lay it down that the priest’s long robe, adorned with four rows of costly stones, signifies the Tetrad; and if any such things [at all] lie in the Scriptures that can be brought to the number four, they say that they came about because of their Tetractys.

**34,16,4** And again the Ogdoad is shown thus: on the eighth of the days, they say, man was moulded; for at one time they will have him made on the sixth, at another [on] the eighth, unless perhaps they will say that the earthy man was formed on the sixth of the days, and the fleshly on the eighth; for these are distinguished among them.

**34,16,5** And some will have it that the man who came into being after the image and likeness of God, male and female, is [also] another, and that this one is the spiritual man, and that the one formed from the earth is another.

**34,16,6a** And the dispensation of the ark at the deluge, in which eight human beings were preserved, they say most clearly indicates the saving Ogdoad.

**34,16,6b** And David too, being eighth by birth among his brothers, signifies the same.

**34,16,6c** Moreover, circumcision too, being performed on the eighth day, shows the cutting-off of the Ogdoad above.

**34,16,7** And in short, whatever things are found in the Scriptures that can be brought under the number eight, they say fulfil the mystery of the Ogdoad.

**34,16,8a** And the Decad too, they say, is signified through the ten nations which God promised to give to Abraham for a possession.

**34,16,8b** And the dispensation [concerning] Sarah, how after ten years she gives him her own slave-girl Hagar so that he might beget children by her, shows the same.

**34,16,9** And the servant [of] Abraham, sent to Rebecca and giving her at the well ten bracelets of gold, and her brothers detaining her for ten days, and further Rehoboam receiving the ten sceptres, and the ten curtains of the tabernacle, and the pillars of ten cubits, and the ten sons of Jacob sent into Egypt the first time for the purchase of grain, and the ten apostles, before whom after the resurrection the Lord was made manifest, Thomas being absent, depicted, according to them, the invisible Decad.

**34,17,1** And the Dodecad — about which, they will have it, the mystery of the passion of the Deficiency took place, and from which passion the visible things were fashioned — lies, they say, conspicuously and plainly everywhere,

**34,17,2** such as the twelve sons of Jacob, from whom also [the] twelve tribes come, and the embroidered oracle having twelve stones, and the twelve bells, and the twelve stones set up by Moses beneath the mountain, and likewise those which Joshua placed in the river, together with others on the farther bank, and those who carried the ark of the covenant, and those set by Elijah at the burnt offering of the calf, and the number of the apostles, and in short all things that preserve the twelfth number, they say, mark out their Dodecad.

**34,17,3** And the union of all these, called the Triacontad, they contentiously strive to display by means of such things as these: through the ark in the time of Noah, thirty cubits in height, and through Samuel making Saul recline first among the thirty invited guests, and through David, when he hid in the field for thirty days, and through the thirty who went in with him into the cave, and through the length of the holy tabernacle being thirty cubits, and whatever other things they find equal in number to these.

**34,18,1** [And] I thought it necessary to add to these also all that they attempt to persuade people of concerning their Forefather, who was unknown to all before the coming of Christ, picking things out of the Scriptures, so that they may show that our Lord proclaimed another Father besides the maker of this universe, whom, as we said before, they impiously say is the fruit of a Deficiency.

**34,18,2** The prophet Isaiah, at any rate, when he said, “But Israel has not known me, and the people has not understood me”, they twist so as to make him speak of the invisible Depth’s being unknown.

**34,18,3** And what was said through Hosea, “There is no truth in them, nor knowledge of God”, they force to bear on the same thing; and “There is none who understands or seeks after God; all have turned aside, together they have become worthless” they set down as referring to the ignorance of the Depth.

**34,18,4a** And what was said through Moses, “No one shall see God and live”, they try to persuade people refers to him.

**34,18,4b** For, telling lies against the maker, they say that he was seen by the prophets, and they will have it that the [scripture] “No one shall see God and live” was spoken of the invisible Greatness that is unknown to all.

**34,18,5** And that “No one shall see God” was said with reference to the Father who is invisible, the maker of all things, is evident to us all; but that it was spoken not of the Depth that these men have invented besides, but of the Demiurge, and that it is he who is the invisible God, will be demonstrated as the discussion proceeds.

**34,18,6** And Daniel too, they say, signifies this very thing in questioning the angel about the explanations of the parables, as one who did not know; and the angel too, hiding from him the great mystery of the Depth, said to him, “Go away, Daniel; for these words are shut up, until those who understand understand and the white are made white”; and they boast that they themselves are the white and those who understand.

**34,18,7** Besides these, they bring forward an untold multitude of apocryphal and spurious writings, which they themselves fabricated, to the astonishment of the foolish and of those who do not know the writings of the truth.

**34,18,8** And for this they take up also that piece of knavery: that while the Lord was a boy and was learning his letters, and the teacher said to him, as is the custom, Say alpha, he answered, Alpha.

**34,18,9a** And again, when the teacher bade him say beta, the Lord answered, “First tell me what alpha is, and then I shall tell you what beta is.”

**34,18,9b** And this they explain as meaning that he alone knew the Unknown, which he made manifest in the figure of the alpha.

**34,18,10a** And some of the things set down in the gospel they also adapt to this pattern, such as the answer he gave his mother when he was twelve years old, “Do you not know that I must be in the things of my Father?”

**34,18,10b** The Father whom they did not know, they say, he was proclaiming to them; and for this reason he sent out the disciples to the twelve tribes, preaching the God unknown to them.

**34,18,11a** And to the one who said to him, “Good teacher,” he confessed the truly good God, saying, “Why do you call me good?

**34,18,11b** One there is who is good, the Father who is in the heavens”; and by “heavens” here, they say, the Aeons are meant.

**34,18,12** And by his not answering those who said to him, “By what power do you do this?”, but leaving them at a loss through his counter-question, they explain that he showed the ineffable nature of the Father in [not] speaking.

**34,18,13** But also in his having said, “Many times have I longed to hear one of these words, and I had no one to speak it,” they say that through the word “one” he was indicating the God who is truly one, whom they had not known.

**34,18,14a** Further, they say that when he drew near to Jerusalem and wept over it and said, “If only you too had known this day the things that make for peace!

**34,18,14b** But they were hidden [from] you,” — by the word “were hidden” he made known the hiddenness of the Depth.

**34,18,15a** And again, that when he said, “Come to me, all you who labour and are heavy laden, and learn from me,” he proclaimed the Father of truth.

**34,18,15b** For the thing of which they were ignorant, so they say, this he promised to teach them.

**34,18,16a** And as [proof] of the very highest things and, as it were, the crowning point of their system they bring forward these words: “I confess to you, Father, Lord of the heavens and of the earth, that you hid these things from the wise and understanding and revealed them to infants.

**34,18,16b** Ah, my Father, for so it was well-pleasing before you.

**34,18,16c** All things were handed over to me by my Father, and no one knew the Father except the Son, nor the Son except the Father and anyone to whom the Son makes revelation.”

**34,18,17** [For] in these words, they say, he showed plainly that the Father of truth invented by them had never been known by anyone at all before his coming; and they wish to establish that, since the Maker and Creator has always been known by all, the Lord said these things about the Father unknown to all, whom they themselves proclaim.

**34,19,1a** The tradition of the redemption [among] them happens to be invisible and incomprehensible, since it is the Mother of things ungraspable and invisible and for this reason is unstable; it is not possible to report it simply or in a single account, because each one of them hands it on as they themselves please.

**34,19,1b** For as many as are the mystagogues of this opinion, so many also are the redemptions.

**34,19,2** And that this form has been put forward by Satan for the denial of the baptism of rebirth into God and for the renunciation of the whole faith, we shall report when we refute them in the proper place.

**34,19,3a** They say that it is indispensable to those who have obtained the perfect knowledge, in order that they may be born again into the Power that is above all things.

**34,19,3b** For otherwise [it is] impossible to enter within the Pleroma, since it is this that leads down into the deep of the Depth, according to them.

**34,19,4** For the baptism of the visible Jesus, they maintain, [is] for remission of sins, whereas the redemption of the Christ within him serves for perfection; and the one is psychic, the other spiritual; and the baptism was proclaimed by John for repentance, but the redemption was brought by Christ for perfection.

**34,19,5a** And this, they hold, is what he speaks of when he says, “And another baptism I have to be baptized with, and I am in great haste towards it.”

**34,19,5b** Moreover, to Zebedee’s sons, when their mother was asking that they might sit with him on his right and on his left in the kingdom, the Lord, they say, put forward this redemption, saying, “Are you able to be baptized with the baptism with which I am to be baptized?”

**34,19,6** And they assert that Paul expressly and often pointed to the redemption in Christ Jesus, and that it is this same redemption which they hand down in varied and conflicting forms.

**34,20,1** For some of them prepare a bridal chamber and perform a mystic rite with certain incantations for those who are being initiated, and they say that what is done by them is a spiritual marriage after the likeness of the conjugal pairs above;

**34,20,2** others bring them to water and, as they baptize, pronounce over them these words: “Into the name of the unknown Father of all things, into Truth the Mother of all things, into him who descended into Jesus, into union and redemption and communion of the powers.”

**34,20,3a** Others pronounce certain Hebrew names over them, so as to strike the initiates with greater awe, thus: “basema chamossē baaianoora mistadia rhouada kousta babophor kalachthei.”

**34,20,3b** And the interpretation of these is as follows: “Above every power of the Father I call upon [...] named light and good spirit and life, because you reigned in a body.”

**34,20,4** Others again pronounce the redemption [...] thus: “The name hidden from every deity and dominion and truth, which Jesus the Nazarene put on in the zones of the light of Christ, of Christ who lives through the Holy Spirit, unto angelic redemption, the name belonging to the restoration:

**34,20,5a** Messia oupharegna mempsai men chal daian mosomē daea akphar nepseu oua Iēsou Nazaria.”

**34,20,5b** And the interpretation of these too is as follows: “I do not divide the spirit, the heart and the supercelestial power, the merciful one; may I have the benefit of your name, Saviour of truth.”

**34,20,6** These words the initiators themselves pronounce, and the one who has been initiated answers: “I have been established and I have been redeemed, and I redeem my soul from this age and from all that comes from it, in the name of Iaō, who redeemed his own soul unto redemption in the living Christ.”

**34,20,7a** Then those present add: “Peace to all upon whom this name rests.”

**34,20,7b** Next they anoint the one who has been initiated with the juice that comes from the balsam.

**34,20,7c** For this ointment, they say, is a type of the fragrance that is above all things.

**34,20,8a** Some of them say that to bring them to the water is superfluous, but, mixing oil and water together, with [certain] incantations similar to those we have mentioned before, they pour it on the head of those who are being initiated, and they will have this to be the redemption.

**34,20,8b** These too anoint with the balsam.

**34,20,9** But others, rejecting all these things, say that the mystery of the ineffable and invisible Power ought not to be performed through visible and perishable creatures, nor that of things inconceivable and incorporeal through things perceptible and bodily.

**34,20,10** The perfect redemption, they say, is the very knowledge of the ineffable Greatness; for since the Deficiency and the passion came into being through ignorance, the whole structure that arose from ignorance is undone by means of knowledge, so that knowledge is the inner man’s redemption.

**34,20,11** And it is neither bodily (for the body is perishable) nor psychic, since the soul too [is] from the Deficiency and is, as it were, a dwelling-place of the spirit; the redemption, therefore, must also be spiritual.

**34,20,12a** For by knowledge the inner man, the spiritual one, is redeemed, and they find the knowledge of all things sufficient.

**34,20,12b** And this is the true redemption.

**34,20,12c** Thus far the extracts from Irenaeus.

**34,21,1a** This whole exact inquiry the blessed elder Irenaeus made, setting out in order everything of their whole corrupted teaching.

**34,21,1b** Therefore, as I have already declared before, being content with what he laboured over, we have set it all down word for word as it is presented by him.

**34,21,2a** And they will be overturned by the very things said by the holy man in refutation of their wickedness.

**34,21,2b** For we believe as the truth in every way shows and sound reasoning suggests, and as is in harmony with the rule of piety, with the Law and the Prophets and the patriarchs from of old in due succession, and with the teaching of the Saviour himself,

**34,21,3** since [the Lord] and his apostles clearly instruct us to acknowledge one God, the Father, Almighty over the universe, and Jesus Christ our Lord, and his Holy Spirit, a single holy and uncreated Trinity, all other things having come into being out of nothing after Father, Son and Holy Spirit.

**34,21,4** And since these things have been plainly confessed and believed by all the holy prophets, evangelists and apostles mentioned before, no gambler’s contrivance could hold out against the ray of the truth, as we have indeed often said at length in the refutation of each heresy;

**34,21,5** so that this charlatan and death-dealer too is detected on every side, having cobbled together and contrived these grand things for display and out of meddlesome curiosity, after the same pattern as the heresies mentioned before.

**34,22,1** Having passed by the wickedness of this man too, and of those called Marcosians who derive from him, let us hasten on to the next, beloved, once more searching out their roots and destroying the bitterness of their fruits, and showing both the refutation and everything that concerns them, not to the harm of those who read but to turn them away,

**34,22,2** so that they may not draw near to any one of the heresies mentioned before or of those that come after, but, having read the things written among them and learnt the things they celebrate, and having condemned the error and the reptilian wickedness of each of them, may flee from them and, as I said, not draw near.

**34,22,3** For those who have investigated these things speak of a certain viper, the dipsas, which seems to inflict harm of this kind in this way: in certain places, where there are troughs in the rocks or small trickles running from rocks into some basin, this dipsas, finding water and drinking, after drinking casts its venom into the pools of water mentioned, so that every animal that comes near and takes its fill of drink seems to be benefited by partaking, but at once, having taken in the venom of the dipsas, falls down beside the basin of the waters and dies.

**34,22,4** And further, if this same dipsas strikes anyone, by reason of the exceedingly burning venom in it the affliction of the pain drives him to a craving of thirst and drink, and provokes him to take it continually and to drink.

**34,22,5** And the one who has been struck thinks he gains some relief against the harm whenever, in such deadly pain, he takes [...]; but in the very drinking, when his belly is filled and can take in [nothing] more, he at last gives up his breath together with the drink.

**34,22,6** So this man too works death by a drink for those deceived by him; having been rescued from his venom through God’s power, let us proceed to those that follow.

### Sect 35: Colorbasians

Against the Colorbasians, the fifteenth, but the thirty-fifth in the series.

**35,1,1** Colorbasus follows these, having gathered his contributions from the sorcery of Mark mentioned before, and having shot up, thorn-fashion, out of Ptolemy’s root as well; [and] he devised other things again beyond these men to trouble the world like stings, contriving, forsooth, a greater expertise, as though he had come from above.

**35,1,2** And at first he was with Mark, who shared his mind, their heresy resembling a snake with two heads; but afterwards, like a head severed from a reptile’s body and still breathing, he did harm, destroying many, as though he were showing something greater and more expert than his associates and those before him.

**35,1,3a** For he says that in the first Ogdoad one Aeon was not emitted by another in descending order, but that the emission of the six Aeons was brought forth together and all at once by the Forefather and his Thought, as he himself insists, as though he had acted as midwife.

**35,1,3b** And he himself and his followers say that Man and Church were not, as the others hold, brought forth from Word and Life, but Word and Life from Man and Church.

**35,1,4a** But they also put this another way: when the Forefather thought of emitting, this was called Father; and since what he emitted was truth, this was named Truth.

**35,1,4b** And when he wished to display himself, this was called Man.

**35,1,4c** And those whom he took account of beforehand when he emitted, this was named Church; and Man the Word: this is the first-born Son.

**35,1,4d** And Life follows upon the Word.

**35,1,4e** And thus the first Ogdoad was completed.

**35,1,5a** There is much conflict among them about the Saviour too.

**35,1,5b** For some say that he came into being from all, and that for this reason he is called the Well-pleasing, because the whole Pleroma was well pleased to glorify the Father through him; others, that he came from the ten Aeons alone that were emitted from Word and Life, [and that for this reason he is called Word and Life], preserving the ancestral names;

**35,1,6** others, from the twelve Aeons that came into being from Man and Church, and that for this reason he confesses [himself] Son of Man, as being a descendant of Man; others say that he came into being from Christ and the Holy Spirit, [who were emitted] for the establishment of the Pleroma, and that for this reason he is called Christ, preserving the title of the Father from whom he was emitted.

**35,1,7a** And others of them, rhapsodists so to speak, say that the Forefather of all things, the Pre-beginning and the Pre-inconceivable, is called Man.

**35,1,7b** And this, they say, is the great and hidden mystery, that the Power which is above all things and encompasses all things is called Man; and for this reason the Saviour calls himself Son of Man.

**35,2,1** Such too is the empty babble of Colorbasus, useless for life and full of fancies through mere noises; whoever studies it closely will know from those before him that ambition is [the cause] of the notion of each of these men.

**35,2,2** For each of them, being vainglorious and wishing to gather a crowd for himself to follow him, told as lies whatever crept into his mind, neither speaking by prophecy (for it was not the Holy Spirit that spoke in them) nor having even a single pretext from the truth of the prophets and the gospels.

**35,2,3a** And the refutation of all these things of the knowledge falsely so called is the same word of truth that was spoken against the first.

**35,2,3b** For since all these belong to the school of Valentinus and of those before him, and have expounded his meaning each in a different way, they will incur the same shame as they.

**35,2,4a** For this Colorbasus too has come bringing us a great deception of empty babble.

**35,2,4b** For, playing the poet, he has given us the name Man for the incomprehensible, invisible and holy God, the Father of all things, so that he might yoke to his own error the saying of the Saviour, who calls himself Son of Man, and might lead the minds of those who have dealings with him away from Christ’s true and most evident confession about himself into perplexity and into the empty babble of inquiry, as they suppose that there exists in the heavenly places what does not exist.

**35,2,5** For let us grant that, according to the account of this pitiable Colorbasus, he names himself Son of Man because his Father, somewhere above, is called Man, and not from the flesh he received out of a virgin’s womb (born, that is, of holy Mary through the Holy Spirit) —

**35,2,6** what would he say about what our Lord Jesus Christ himself says, when he said to the Jews, “But now you seek to kill me, a human being who has spoken to you the truth which I heard from my Father”;

**35,2,7** and here he no longer said “of my Father the Man”, but in speaking of the Father he confessed and signified the God of all things, and of himself, because he truly became man, he declared himself a human being;

**35,2,8** as the apostles too say — so that the truth might be established from every side and it might be known whence come the names joined to the Lord — “Jesus, a man shown forth to us by signs and wonders,” and so on.

**35,2,9** What then would you say to this, most pitiable of all men, since you come from above bringing us new names and pride yourself on daring to fasten the name Man upon the very Master of all and Father of the universe, so that, from this, the Lord should be called Son of Man on the ground that the Father is called Man?

**35,2,10a** Find us also some other title fitting the Father [in accordance with] the words “a man shown forth”; but you would never find one.

**35,2,10b** For even if a man is a human being, and we call a human being a man in distinction from the woman, still you could not contrive anything by this either.

**35,2,11a** For without shapes and members, both hidden and visible, one cannot be called a man.

**35,2,11b** For we call the woman too a human being, but not a man.

**35,2,11c** Hence we say both “he, the human being” and “she, the human being”.

**35,2,12a** But distinguishing the sex specifically, we call the female woman and the male man.

**35,2,12b** For “man” and “woman” is the distinction of the male and the female sex, when the relation of the sexes is divided.

**35,2,12c** But by a common name both the man and the woman are called human being.

**35,3,1** Since these things are so, come to me, all you servants of God who love the truth, and laugh to scorn the deceiver and charlatan Colorbasus, or rather mourn those who have been deceived and have ruined both themselves and many others.

**35,3,2** As for ourselves, let us give God thanks that through few and simple words the truth is wont to guide her own sons into the straight way, and scatters, overturns and destroys the wretched and boastful things that have been embellished with much contrivance, walking quietly, as one can clearly see from the prophetic word.

**35,3,3** For the prophet, reproaching those who busy themselves with dreadful things and devise high-sounding things to their own deception, said, “Because you do not want the water of Siloam that flows quietly, the Lord brings the river’s water up against you — the king of the Assyrians.”

**35,3,4a** For the water of Siloam is the teaching of the one who was sent.

**35,3,4b** And who could this be but our Lord Jesus, who was sent by God his Father?

**35,3,4c** And “quietly”, because nothing empty-sounding or fabricated [...], but in truth [...] his holy bride, who in the Songs of Solomon is called by him a dove because of the innocence and gentleness and perfect purity of the creature.

**35,3,5** And it is a wonder [how] he named the others — not his, but having called themselves after his name — concubines, and queens on account of the royal name, which each takes pride in, inscribing Christ upon herself.

**35,3,6** But even if there are eighty concubines, which are the heresies, and then young women past numbering, he says, “My dove, my perfect one, is one” — that is, the holy bride herself, the catholic church: a dove, as I said, because of the creature’s gentleness, innocence and purity, and perfect because from God she has received the perfect grace and the knowledge that comes from the Saviour himself through the Holy Spirit.

**35,3,7** The Bridegroom himself, then, who is interpreted “the one sent”, that is, Siloam, has water that flows quietly, that is, a teaching without din and without clatter, without fantasy and without boasting.

**35,3,8** And his bride too is a quiet dove, having neither venom nor jaw-teeth nor stings, as do all these serpent-shaped men who gush up venom, each eager to concoct some poison for the world and to bring ruin on those who have been persuaded by him;

**35,3,9** of whom this man too happens to be one, whom I have been eager here to have detected through the divine word and the help from above, and crushed like the creeping thing called the four-jawed spider, or broken off in brief, like a head cut off from the amphisbaena, the two-headed viper.

**35,3,10** Passing him by, I shall again examine the next ones, and as I go through them I shall ask in prayer to declare the facts about them in truth, and to harm no one nor be harmed myself.

### Sect 36: Heracleonites

Against the Heracleonites, the sixteenth, but the thirty-sixth in the series.

**36,1,1** A certain Heracleon succeeds this Colorbasus, from whom come those called Heracleonites, practised in [...] no less than the empty babble among these men.

**36,1,2** And whatever those men say, this man too lays down in every way, inasmuch as he starts out from them and has taken on their venom from them; but, having fashioned in himself certain things supposedly beyond them, he wishes to surpass them, so that he too may make for himself a congregation of the deceived.

**36,1,3** For all these, having fashioned themselves into a hundred-headed or hundred-handed body, imitated the one once told of in fable among the poets of the Greeks, Cottus or Briareus, whom they also call Aegaeon, or Gyes, or the one called Argus of the many eyes.

**36,1,4** Just as those poets in their rhapsodies tell marvels of such beings, inventing and saying that the one had a hundred hands and sometimes fifty heads, sometimes a hundred, and the other a hundred eyes — and for this reason, they say, Hermes is called Argeiphontes, as having slain Argus of the many eyes —,

**36,1,5a** so too each of these, wishing to set up a sovereignty for himself, named himself a head, introducing other things beyond the vain labour and frenzied teaching of his teachers.

**36,1,5b** But so as not to drive the composition of the preface to great length, I shall proceed to the subject before us.

**36,2,1** This Heracleon, then, and the Heracleonites who derive from him, as I said before, speaks like Mark and some of those before him concerning the Ogdoads — the higher, I mean, and the lower; and further he holds like opinions about the conjugal pairs of the thirty Aeons.

**36,2,2a** He too says that the Father of all things above is Man, whom he also named Depth.

**36,2,2b** And he too will have it that he is neither male nor female, but that from him is the Mother of all things, whom he names both Silence and Truth.

**36,2,3a** And from her, he says, is the second Mother, who came to be in forgetfulness, whom he too calls Achamoth.

**36,2,3b** From her, then, all things were established in deficiency.

**36,2,4** And this man too wishes to say more than those before him, namely the following: those of them who are dying and have reached the very point of departure, having taken his pretexts from Mark mentioned before, he no longer redeems as that man did, but handles the matter differently, redeeming, forsooth, at the point of death those who have been deceived by him.

**36,2,5a** For at times some of them, mixing oil with water, pour it on the head of the one who has departed, and others the ointment called opobalsam and water, though they keep the invocation in common, as Mark before him also used to make it, with the addition of certain names.

**36,2,5b** And the invocation is this:

**36,2,6** “Messia oupharegna mempsai men chal daian mosomē daea akphar nepseu oua Iēsou Nazaria.”

**36,2,7** And they do this so that those who at their departure receive these invocations, with the water and oil, or ointment, mixed together, may become ungraspable and invisible to the principalities and authorities above, so that their inner man may pass beyond them invisibly, they say,

**36,2,8** since these bodies are left behind in the creation, while their soul is presented to the Demiurge above, who came into being in the Deficiency, so as to remain there with him, but the inner man, as I said, [the one] more inward than soul and body, rises beyond; this, indeed, they will have it, came down from the Pleroma above.

**36,3,1** And they enjoin those who have thus been made sport of among them that, he says, if you come to the principalities and authorities, keep in mind to say this after your death here:

**36,3,2a** “I am a son from the Father, the Father who pre-exists, and a son in the present; [and] I came to see all things, my own and those of others — and yet not altogether others’, but Achamoth’s, who, being female, produced these things for herself.

**36,3,2b** But I trace my lineage from the one who pre-exists, and I am going back to my own, whence I came.”

**36,3,3a** And having said this, they say, he escapes the authorities and comes to those around the Demiurge, above, around the first Ogdoad; for they too will have it that after the Demiurge below there is a Hebdomad.

**36,3,3b** And the Demiurge himself, they say, being an eighth, is in the seventh [heaven], yet in deficiency and in ignorance;

**36,3,4a** and the one who has departed from life says to those around the Demiurge, “I am a vessel held in higher honour than the female who made you.

**36,3,4b** If your Mother does not know her own root, I for my part know myself, and I know whence I am, and I call upon the incorruptible Sophia, who is in the Father and is the mother of your Mother, she who has no father, nor even a male consort;

**36,3,5a** but, a female born of a female, she made you, ignorant even of her own mother and supposing herself to be alone.

**36,3,5b** But I call upon her mother.”

**36,3,6a** And those around the Demiurge, they say, on hearing this are greatly troubled and condemn their root and the race of the Mother, while he goes to his own, having cast off his bond and his angel, that is, [the] soul.

**36,3,6b** For they think that besides body and soul there is something else too in man.

**36,3,6c** And concerning the redemption, this is what has come to us.

**36,4,1** But the intelligent, on hearing their excessive mockery, would laugh at the buffoonery, how each of them legislates things different from the other as he pleases and is not checked in his own audacity, but each devises whatever he can.

**36,4,2a** And of those who are still to this day being born among them and sprouting up, who day after day discover some novelty to utter and with which to beguile those they lead on, it is hard to search out or to declare all the [...] of these men.

**36,4,2b** Hence I shall once more rest content with the things said concerning this heresy as well, having pointed out what has reached us.

**36,4,3a** To whom would it not be plain that such teaching is wholly fable and mockery?

**36,4,3b** For whence, you there, you or those before you, have you received the body?

**36,4,3c** And the soul, whence?

**36,4,3d** And your innermost man, whence?

**36,4,4a** And if from above, from the spiritual one above, as you say in your play-acting, having befooled with a promise of hope those deceived by you, so that, lifted up by some goal, they may be swept along, bewitched by your theatrical show — tell me, what fellowship has the spiritual one above with the material?

**36,4,4b** And what has the material with the psychic?

**36,4,5a** And how did the Demiurge fashion things that were not his own?

**36,4,5b** And how did the [spiritual one] above supply the spiritual power that comes from him to the Demiurge, who did not work well?

**36,4,5c** And from what motive did the Demiurge, mixing his own psychic element with the material, choose rather to bind his own power fast in matter?

**36,4,6a** But if he wishes to mix his own power into it, then matter is not alien.

**36,4,6b** And if it is in fact alien, who gave him authority over matter?

**36,4,7** And first tell me, deceiver: did he bind the soul fast to matter out of hatred for it, or in ignorance of what was to be?

**36,4,8a** But I know that you will say neither of these.

**36,4,8b** For we do not call the body matter, God forbid, nor the creatures of God; but Scripture knows, besides this ordinary matter which lies ready for the constructions of every craft and work, a certain other matter — I mean the filthy imagining that springs from the mind and [the] miry reasonings of sin.

**36,4,9** For, like a stench and an unclean exhalation from mud, there spring up pestilential and miry [...], as blessed David said when he was pursued and slandered by wicked men, “I am stuck fast in the matter of the deep,” and so on.

**36,5,1** But since you think, Heracleon, that this is what is called matter — the bodies of men and the entire world of this place — with what aim did the Demiurge mingle his own soul into matter?

**36,5,2a** If it was in ignorance of the evil, then he who does not know what he wishes to fashion cannot fashion it.

**36,5,2b** For neither do we do anything in any craft, working at what we do not understand.

**36,5,2c** For we both consider beforehand what we wish to accomplish and know, before we make it, what we have chosen to accomplish.

**36,5,3a** And yet we are weak and fall far short of the power of God, but through the understanding graciously given by him to men we know and understand.

**36,5,3b** But the understanding that comes from God has turned out to your harm, Heracleon, because you do not employ it according to God, but according to a wicked practice.

**36,5,4** And again I shall ask further: whence comes the mixing of the spiritual with the psychic and the material — what is called by you the inner man, joined to the second and third, the outward man — soul and body, I mean?

**36,5,5** And if it was by the will of the Power above, the Father of all things — I mean your Depth — then the creation here around us is not without fellowship with the things above (as I said), being mingled, through the good pleasure of the Father above, with the spark sent down from above by him, which is, according to you, the spiritual and innermost man.

**36,5,6a** But if you claim that the Demiurge, he that is in inferiority and deficiency, or else the Mother you call Achamoth, received power from above — the spiritual element, that is — then the Demiurge is no longer in deficiency and ignorance, nor is the one called Mother according to you.

**36,5,6b** For how could anyone be in ignorance of what he desires?

**36,5,6c** For if he reaches out at all for the better, he knows the beautiful and the good; and he who knows the good and does not loathe it but reaches out for it is no stranger to the good.

**36,6,1a** And so that I may not wear myself out busying myself with the contrivances of the charlatan, I shall be content with these points.

**36,6,1b** For all his silly talk has fallen, since the Master of all is plainly confessed by all to be good, foreknowing and able to make all things, and since every existing created nature has come into being well through him.

**36,6,2** For nothing can exist apart from God, save sin alone, which has no beginning of a root nor remains to the end, but arises in us as something brought in from outside and ceases again from us; as I have shown, making my argument everywhere against all the heresies,

**36,6,3** that God, who made and fashioned all things, is one, the Father of Jesus Christ our Lord; and his only-begotten Child is one, our Lord and Saviour and God; and his Holy Spirit is one,

**36,6,4** one holy and consubstantial Trinity, from which all things have been well created, not evil things but good, called from non-existence into being by the goodness that was so well pleased.

**36,6,5a** To this God, Father in Son and Son in Father, with the Holy Spirit, be glory, honour and dominion unto the ages of the ages.

**36,6,5b** Amen.

**36,6,6** Having again made the refutation of this heresy briefly, I shall go on to the next, making the refutation of each according to my ability and thus accomplishing the overthrow of their destructive wickedness.
