**36,6,7** For this man might justly be called a seps, which is not a snake but, as they say, a coppery creature of a sort, a four-footed creeping thing like a gecko, the harm of whose bite counts for nothing, but whose spittle, shot into food or into drink, works death at once for those who partake; such too is this man’s teaching.

**36,6,8** But having detected this man’s venom too, and having wiped it away by the power of the Lord from the throats or lips of those who were about to be harmed, let us go through the next ones, as I said before, making the refutation of the harm they do.

### Sect 37: Ophites

Against the Ophites, the seventeenth, but the thirty-seventh in the series.

**37,1,1a** As we have promised, then, through God’s power, God helping us, we shall also describe the heresy that comes next after this one, that of the Ophites, which followed the former vain imaginings, in some respects running on the same lines and in others differing — I mean in the customs and outward forms of those who adhere to it — so that from the airy error of their disagreement it may be plain to everyone that they are led by error and not by truth.

**37,1,1b** And their folly will now be detected by the argument and refuted.

**37,1,2a** For the Ophites, as I said before, took their pretexts from the heresies of Nicolaus and the Gnostics and those before these.

**37,1,2b** And they are called Ophites on account of the snake that they glorify.

**37,1,2c** For these too belch out absurdities, as men filled with the stench of the food mentioned before, and, deceived as by new demons, these men glorify the snake, as I said before.

**37,1,3a** And see to what great wicked contrivance the snake that deceived them has advanced.

**37,1,3b** For just as from the beginning he deceived Eve and Adam, so too now, hiding himself both at the present time and in the time of the Jews until the coming of Christ.

**37,1,4** Then, as the times advanced, still corrupting men, greedy as they are for the food taken through him in disobedience, and provoking them to greater deception, he leads them away from the God who is, always promising much, as he did from the beginning; for then too he deceived, saying, “You shall be as gods,” and then, as time went on, he worked up for them the many-shaped and monstrous fantasy.

**37,1,5a** For having led them away from the one true God, he long ago bred in them a craving for the blasphemous empty babble of idolatry and polytheism.

**37,1,5b** For they were not gods, just as they are not now, but God [alone] is; but he had beforehand bred the craving for idol-madness and polytheism and a deceitful mind.

**37,1,6** Yet the snake that appeared then was not alone responsible, but the snake who spoke from within the snake ([the] devil, I mean) and threw the man’s hearing into confusion through the woman.

**37,1,7** Nor was the tree sin (for God plants nothing evil), but the tree produced knowledge, the knowing of good and bad.

**37,1,8a** And death came not because of the knowing, but because of the disobedience.

**37,1,8b** For the whole scheme of the enemy at that time came about not for the sake of the food, but for the sake of producing disobedience in them.

**37,1,9a** Hence, having disobeyed then, they are put outside paradise, rebuked most justly, not out of hatred but out of God’s care.

**37,1,9b** For the Lord says to them, “Earth you are, and to earth you shall depart.”

**37,1,10** For it was fitting that the true craftsman, caring like a potter for his own moulded work and vessel, fashioned by his hands — which afterwards fell into inferiority through the disobedience, the vessel, so to speak, while it was still clay, having become a reject through a crack — should not leave it so, but should change it back into the original lump,

**37,1,11** so that in the regeneration he might rebuild the vessel again at the resurrection to its original brightness and still more,

**37,1,12** that is, [...] the bodies of those who did the most unjust deeds and repented and turned away from their own failings and were made perfect in the knowledge of Jesus Christ our Lord, in order that the body’s resurrection may come about from the earth, as the lump softened by the craftsman is fashioned again into its original and still better shape.

**37,2,1a** Such was the snake’s plot against Eve.

**37,2,1b** For from the beginning human nature has been greedy and ever led on by the doctrines of empty babble and by empty promises.

**37,2,2** And long ago the same one, lying hidden, did not reveal the full extent of his venom-casting, but later, after the coming of Christ in the flesh, he vomited out and spat forth the whole venomous and wicked invention of his malice; for this one put himself forward in the mind of those deceived to be glorified as God and to be worshipped.

**37,2,3a** And this same snake is recognized as producing the deception both through this school of his and through the perceptible snake.

**37,2,3b** And the divine Scripture, as is plain, calls the devil a snake, not because he is altogether of such a form, but because he appears to men most crooked, and because of the device of deception that was worked at the very first in a snake.

**37,2,4a** In the eyes of those, then, who know the truth, this doctrine is ridiculous, and so are those who adhere to it, glorifying the snake as God.

**37,2,4b** For since the devil can no longer deceive the manly mind that has received from the Lord the power of the truth, he turns to the female, that is, to the ignorance of men, and persuades those who are in ignorance, since he cannot deceive solid reasoning.

**37,2,5** For he always draws near to womanish thoughts, to pleasure and desire, that is, to the ignorance that grows effeminate in men, and not to the solid reasoning which understands all things rightly and recognizes God from the law of nature.

**37,2,6** For the snake among them says that he is Christ — or rather not he himself (for he cannot utter a sound), but the devil, who has prepared their mind to think so.

**37,2,7a** Who, then, on seeing the snake will not recognize the enemy and flee?

**37,2,7b** For this reason the Lord too set this very snake at enmity with the human race, since, being a brute beast, it had become wholly a vessel of the devil, and through it the devil deceived man in paradise, so that, seeing the enmity of this perceptible snake, men might run away from the plot of deception and might hate, so to speak, even to look upon its form.

**37,3,1** And these so-called Ophites in their turn attribute the whole of knowledge to this snake, declaring that it has been the source of knowledge for mankind, bringing in by way of fable what they consider mysteries, though these are buffooneries full of mockery and vain thinking.

**37,3,2** For these are truly fables: for they say that from the Aeon above Aeons were emitted, and that Ialdabaoth came into being lower down; and that he was emitted through the feebleness and the ignorance of his own mother — of Prunicus above, that is.

**37,3,3** For they say that this Prunicus came down into the waters and was mingled with them, and was unable to make her way to the things above because she had been mixed with the heaviness of matter; for she had become intermingled with the waters and with matter, and could no longer withdraw.

**37,3,4a** But she raised herself by force to the higher regions and stretched herself out, and thus [the] higher heaven came into being.

**37,3,4b** And she has been found fixed fast, no longer able either to go up above or to come down below, but she remained fixed and stretched out in the very middle.

**37,3,5** For she could not be carried down below, because she was not akin to it; and she could not make her way above, because she was weighed down by the matter which she had taken on.

**37,3,6a** And Ialdabaoth, emitted in accordance with her ignorance, came to the lowest regions and made for himself seven sons, who made seven heavens for themselves.

**37,3,6b** And he, having shut off the things above him, kept them hidden, so that the seven sons emitted by him, being lower than he, might not know the things above him, but him alone.

**37,3,6c** And this Ialdabaoth is, according to them, the God of the Jews.

**37,3,7** But it is not so, God forbid; rather God the Almighty is going to judge them, being God of Jews and of Christians and of all, and not some Ialdabaoth according to their silly mythology.

**37,4,1a** Then, they say, when the things above had been shut off through the device of Ialdabaoth, these seven sons begotten by him — whether Aeons or gods or angels (for they are called by various names among them) — fashioned man after the pattern of Ialdabaoth their father, neither readily nor quickly, but in the same way that the earlier heresies also contrived in their empty babble.

**37,4,1b** For these too say: man was a crawling creature, like a worm, unable either to lift up his head or to stand upright.

**37,4,2** But to thwart Ialdabaoth, the Mother above, who is called Prunicus, wishing to empty Ialdabaoth of the power of which he had partaken from her, worked in him upon the man fashioned by his children, intending to empty out his power and to send forth from him a spark upon the man (the soul, forsooth).

**37,4,3** And then, he says, the man stood on his feet and passed in thought beyond the eight heavens, and came to know the Father above, the one who is over Ialdabaoth, and gave him praise.

**37,4,4** And then Ialdabaoth, carried away with distress because the things far above him had been recognized, looked down in bitterness at the dregs of matter and begot a power having a serpent-shaped form, which they also call his son.

**37,4,5a** And thus, he says, this one was sent and deceived Eve.

**37,4,5b** And she listened to him and believed him as the son of God, and having believed she ate from the tree of knowledge.

**37,5,1** Then, when they have related this folly and the absurdity of this practice, having made a tragic plot, so to speak, and this comic drama, they begin to hint to us certain things in support of the false gods spoken of among them: “Are not our entrails too, through which we live and are nourished, snake-shaped?”

**37,5,2a** And other things besides, as many as they bring in for those they have deceived, to their own error and silly notion.

**37,5,2b** And for this reason, they say, we glorify the snake: because the snake has become the cause of knowledge for the multitude.

**37,5,3a** But Ialdabaoth, they say, did not wish the Mother above or the Father to be remembered by men.

**37,5,3b** But the snake persuaded them and brought knowledge, and instructed the man and the woman in the entire knowledge of the mysteries above.

**37,5,4** Therefore his father, that is, Ialdabaoth, angered because of the knowledge which he had shown to men, cast him down from heaven.

**37,5,5a** And hence those who possess the portion of the snake, and nothing else, call the snake a king from heaven.

**37,5,5b** So they glorify him on account of such knowledge, as they say, and offer bread to him.

**37,5,6a** For they keep an actual snake, rearing it in a kind of chest; at the hour of their mysteries they bring it out of its den, and heaping up loaves on a table they call this snake to them; and once the den has been opened, out it comes.

**37,5,6b** And so the snake, coming up by its own shrewdness and cunning, already knowing their folly, climbs onto the table and coils itself among the loaves.

**37,5,6c** And this, they say, is the perfect sacrifice.

**37,5,7** Hence too, as I have heard from someone, they not only break the loaves among which the snake has coiled itself and hand them to those who receive them, but each one also kisses the snake on the mouth, whether the snake has been lulled into tameness by some spell of sorcery, or the creature is made to fawn by some other working of the devil for their deception.

**37,5,8** And they worship such a creature, and give the name of eucharist to this rite performed through its coiling itself about, and, again sending up a hymn through it, as they say, to the Father above, they thus accomplish their mysteries.

**37,6,1a** One might call [this] folly and full of mockery; and it will not need a refutation sought from divine Scripture, but of itself it will be shown ridiculous to anyone who has a mind strong in God.

**37,6,1b** For at once their whole empty babble will be found to be a piece of silly talk.

**37,6,2a** For if they say that she is Prunicus, how will the unsoundness of their thinking not be detected from the very name, as I have already said?

**37,6,2b** For everything that is debauched is useless; and if useless, it is not to be ranked among things to be preferred.

**37,6,2c** And how could what is useless be praiseworthy?

**37,6,3** And how is it not mythical to say that Prunicus emptied Ialdabaoth, and that the spark came down to the regions below from the one who had been emptied, and having come to be in man recognized the one higher than him who had been emptied?

**37,6,4a** And it is a very great marvel that man, in whom is the least spark, has knowledge beyond the angels who fashioned him.

**37,6,4b** For the angels — or sons of Ialdabaoth — who made man failed to recognize what lies above Ialdabaoth, whereas the one made by them recognized it through the spark.

**37,6,5** They overturn themselves, then, by their own doctrines, at one point glorifying the snake, at another saying that it was a deceiver towards Eve, when they say, “It deceived Eve”;

**37,6,6** and at one point they proclaim it as Christ, at another as the son of Ialdabaoth, the higher one, who wronged his sons by shutting off from them the knowledge of [the things] above, and who set aside the Mother and the Father above, so as not to let the sons begotten by him glorify the Father who is higher than he.

**37,6,7a** How then is the snake a heavenly king, if indeed he has risen up against the Father?

**37,6,7b** And how, if he gives knowledge, is he proclaimed to have deceived Eve by deception?

**37,6,7c** But he who implants knowledge through deception no longer gives knowledge, but ignorance instead of knowledge, as one can truly see that this is brought to completion in them.

**37,6,7d** For though they have ignorance they think it knowledge, telling the truth in this one thing, in calling their own knowledge deception and ignorance.

**37,7,1a** And they bring other testimonies too, saying that Moses also, in the wilderness, set up the bronze snake and lifted it up, so that it might become a cure for those bitten by a snake.

**37,7,1b** For they say that this form is a cure for the bite.

**37,7,1c** But again they lay down these things against themselves.

**37,7,2a** For if the bites were those of snakes, and these were harmful, then the snake is not good.

**37,7,2b** And what was lifted up by Moses then brought about the healing through being looked upon, not through the snake’s nature but through the good pleasure of God, who worked through a snake, as it were, an antidote for those who had been bitten at that time.

**37,7,3** And it is nothing wonderful if one obtained healing through the very things through which one was harmed; and let no one disparage God’s creation, as others in their error say in turn.

**37,7,4** This was prefigured for those in the wilderness for the reason which the Lord, when he came, states in the gospel: “Just as Moses lifted up the snake in the wilderness, in the same way the Son of Man must be lifted up” — which in fact came to pass.

**37,7,5a** For, having dishonoured the Saviour as though he were a snake, they were being wronged through the plot of the snake, I mean of the devil.

**37,7,5b** And just as at the lifting up of the snake there came healing for those who had been bitten, so at the crucifixion of Christ a ransom came about for our souls from the bites of sin that had been inflicted on us.

**37,7,6a** And these same people bring forward this very saying as testimony, saying, “Do you not see how the Saviour said, ‘In the manner in which Moses lifted up the snake in the wilderness, even so the Son of Man has to be lifted up’?

**37,7,6b** And for this reason,” they say, “in another place too he says, ‘Be wise as the snake and innocent as the dove.’”

**37,7,6c** And the things that have been well ordered for us by God as symbols of teaching, these men turn to their own deluded notion.

**37,8,1** For our Lord Jesus Christ and God the Word, who was begotten timelessly and without beginning from the Father before all the ages, is not a snake (God forbid), but came himself against the snake.

**37,8,2** And if he says, “Be wise as the snake and innocent as the dove,” we must ask and learn why he brought in these two figures for our teaching, that of the dove and that of the snake.

**37,8,3** Now there is nothing wise in the snake except [these] two things: that when it is pursued, knowing that its whole life lies in its head, and fearing the command spoken against it of old by God on man’s account, “You shall watch for its head, and it for your heel,” it wraps its whole body about its head and conceals its own skull, but gives up the rest of its body, in an excess of cunning.

**37,8,4** So the only-begotten God, he who proceeded from the Father, wishes us in time of persecution and in time of temptation to give up all that is ours to sword and to fire, but to guard our own head, that is, not to deny Christ, seeing that “of every man the head is Christ, and of a woman the head is the man, and of Christ the head is God,” according to the apostle.

**37,8,5a** The snake has another mark of shrewdness again, as the naturalists say about this creature.

**37,8,5b** For when, being thirsty, it comes out of its den to some water to drink, it does not take its venom along with it, but leaves it in the den, and so goes and takes its drink of the waters.

**37,8,6** Let us too, then, imitate this, so that when we come to the holy church of God, or to prayer, or to the mysteries of God, we may not bring with us malice or pleasure or desire or enmity or anything else in our minds.

**37,8,7** Otherwise how could we imitate the dove too, if we have not come to be free of malice — though the dove kind is not praised in many respects?

**37,8,8** For the dove kind is licentious and not restrained from frequent mating, lustful and given over to pleasure at every season, and besides this feeble and weak.

**37,8,9a** But because of the creature’s innocence and long-suffering and freedom from resentment, and moreover because the Holy Spirit appeared in a dove’s form, the divine word would have us copy the Holy Spirit’s will and the innocence of the innocent dove, being wise as to what is good and innocent as to what is evil.

**37,8,9b** And the whole play-acting of these men has been undone.

**37,8,10** For the holy apostle at once lays down craftiness and treachery against no one else more particularly than against the devil and the snake, saying, “But I fear lest somehow, as the snake deceived Eve by his craftiness, your minds be corrupted from the simplicity and purity of Christ and of righteousness.”

**37,8,11** You see how the apostle declared what the snake contrived against Eve to be corruption and dreadful craftiness and deception, and did not show anything praiseworthy to have been done by it.

**37,9,1** From every side, then, the folly of these men is detectable and manifest to everyone who desires to know the truth’s teaching and the knowledge of the Holy Spirit.

**37,9,2** But so as not to spend time, having sailed through this harsh and perilous squall of the sea too, I shall fit out my vessel for the next voyages over the sea, keeping a careful grip on the tillers in the

**37,9,3** power of God and by the prayers of the saints, so that as we sail past we may observe the swell of the wild waves and the forms of the venomous beasts in the seas, and may be able to pass through unstung by the venom of the moray, and of the stingray, the sea-dragon, the shark and the scorpion-fish, and through prayer and supplication to reach the calm harbour of the truth,

**37,9,4a** the saying being fulfilled in us too, “Those who go down to the sea will tell of the excellences of the Lord.”

**37,9,4b** I shall therefore make my way to the next one after this, to give an account of it.

### Sect 38: Cainites

Against the Cainites, the eighteenth, but the thirty-eighth in the series.

**38,1,1a** Certain people are called Cainites, having taken the name of their heresy from Cain.

**38,1,1b** For these praise Cain and reckon him their father; and they too, so to speak, setting out as from a different upsurge of waves, are not outside the same tossing and surge, and, peeping out as from a thorny thicket, are not apart from the whole heap of thorns, even if they differ in name.

**38,1,1c** For there are many kinds of thorns, yet in all of them is the wickedness of stinging injury.

**38,1,2** These say that Cain is from the stronger Power and from the authority above, and Esau too, and Korah’s company and the Sodomites, but that Abel is from the weaker Power;

**38,1,3a** and [...] all these as praiseworthy among them and as of their own kindred.

**38,1,3b** For they pride themselves on being kin of Cain and of the Sodomites and of Esau and Korah.

**38,1,3c** And these, they say, are of the perfect knowledge from above.

**38,1,4a** Hence they say that the maker of this world, though he busied himself with their destruction, was unable to harm them at all.

**38,1,4b** For they were hidden from him and were transferred to the Aeon above, whence the strong Power is.

**38,1,4c** For Sophia took them to herself, since they were her own.

**38,1,5a** And for this reason they say that Judas had exact knowledge of the things concerning these.

**38,1,5b** For they will have him too to be their kinsman and count him among those of surpassing knowledge, so that they even carry a little treatise in his name, which they call the Gospel of Judas.

**38,1,6** And likewise they fabricate certain other writings against the Womb — the Womb being what they call the maker of this whole vault of heaven and earth — and they say that some cannot be saved otherwise unless they pass through all things, as Carpocrates also says.

**38,2,1** For each of them, forsooth, on this pretext committing unspeakable deeds and performing obscenities and doing every sin there is, invokes the name of each angel, both of the angels who exist and of those spoken of fictitiously among them; and to each of these he attaches some lawless deed among the sins on earth, referring his own act to the name of whatever angel he wishes.

**38,2,2** And when they do these things they speak thus: “O angel so-and-so, I make use of your work; O power so-and-so, I perform your act.”

**38,2,3** And this is what is called perfect knowledge among them, since they have, forsooth, taken their pretexts for rushing fearlessly into lawless obscenities from the mother and father heresies mentioned before — I mean those of the Gnostics and of Nicolaus and of those with them, Valentinus and Carpocrates.

**38,2,4** And there has already come to us a book too, in which they fabricated certain sayings full of lawlessness, with contents such as these: “This,” it says, “is the angel who blinded Moses, and these are the angels who hid Korah and Dathan and Abiram and removed them elsewhere.”

**38,2,5a** Again, others fabricate another little treatise in the name of Paul the apostle, full of unspeakable deeds, which the so-called Gnostics also make use of, and to which they give the name Ascent of Paul, having found their pretext in the apostle’s saying that he ascended as far as the third heaven and heard unspeakable words which a man may not utter.

**38,2,5b** And these, they say, are the unspeakable words.

**38,2,6a** And they teach these things and things like them, honouring the wicked and forbidding the good.

**38,2,6b** For Cain, [they say], as I said before, belongs to the stronger Power, and Abel to the weaker.

**38,2,6c** And these powers, having had intercourse with Eve, begot Cain and Abel, and Cain is of the one and Abel of the other;

**38,2,7** and Adam [too] and Eve were begotten by such powers, or angels; and the children who were born, I mean Cain and Abel, quarrelled with each other, and the one begotten by the strong Power killed the one from the lesser and weaker Power.

**38,3,1** And these too weave the same fables into their giving of these same poisons of ignorance, plotting against those who are persuaded, saying that every man must choose for himself the stronger Power and separate himself from the lesser and feeble one, that is, from the one that made heaven and the flesh and the world, and pass beyond to the highest regions through the crucifixion of Christ.

**38,3,2a** For it was for this reason, they say, that he came from above, so that a strong Power might be at work in him, winning the trophy over the weaker Power and handing over the body.

**38,3,2b** Some of them say this, and others something else.

**38,3,3a** For some say that it was because Christ was wicked that he was handed over by Judas, since he wished to pervert the things of the Law.

**38,3,3b** For, as I have said, they praise Cain and Judas, and they say: “That is why he handed him over, since he wanted to overthrow what had been rightly taught.”

**38,3,4a** But others of the same people say: “No; rather, he handed him over, good as he was, in keeping with the heavenly knowledge.”

**38,3,4b** “For the archons were aware,” they say, “that if Christ were given over to the cross, their feeble power would be emptied.”

**38,3,5a** “And Judas,” he says, “knowing this, made haste and set everything in motion to betray him, doing a good work for us, for our salvation.

**38,3,5b** And we must praise him and render him the praise, because through him there was prepared for us the salvation of the cross and the revelation of the things above that came through such an occasion.”

**38,3,6a** But these men have been deceived in every way, honouring or praising no one who is good.

**38,3,6b** And it is plain that these things have been secretly sown among them by the devil.

**38,3,6c** I mean the things of ignorance and deception.

**38,3,7** And in them is fulfilled what is written: “Woe to those who call the good evil and the evil good, who make darkness light and light darkness, who call the sweet bitter and the bitter sweet.”

**38,3,8a** For the Old and the New Testament cries out on every side, heralding the impiety of Cain.

**38,3,8b** But these men, on the contrary, are lovers of darkness and imitators of evildoers.

**38,3,8c** They hate Abel, but love Cain and give praise to Judas.

**38,3,9a** They [fashion] a ruinous knowledge, positing two powers, a weaker and a stronger, fighting against each other and producing in the world not a change of will, but that of those who have come into being some have their substance by nature from evil, and others from goodness.

**38,3,9b** And they say that it is not by choice but by nature that anyone is good or bad.

**38,4,1** And first let us see about Cain, how the Old Testament says, “Cursed are you from the earth, which opened its mouth to receive your brother’s blood from your hand,” and again, “Cursed are you in your works, and you shall be groaning and trembling upon the earth.”

**38,4,2a** And in agreement with the Old Testament the Lord speaks about him in the gospel, when the Jews were saying, “We have God as our father.”

**38,4,2b** And the Lord said to them, “You are sons of the devil, your father, for he is a liar, for his father was a liar.

**38,4,2c** He was a murderer and did not stand in the truth; when he speaks the lie, he speaks from his own, for his father too was a liar.”

**38,4,3** Hence the other heresies too, hearing this saying, claim that the father of the Jews is the devil, and that he in turn has another father, and his father again a father.

**38,4,4a** But they speak with an unbridled tongue, blind in their understanding.

**38,4,4b** For they carry that one’s kinship back to the Master of all, the God of Jews and Christians and all, saying that the one who legislated through Moses and worked such great wonders is the father of that one’s father.

**38,4,4c** But this is not so, beloved.

**38,4,5** For the Lord himself at once — taking care for us in all things, so that we might not stray from the matter in hand, devising and imagining one thing in place of another — in saying that their father was the devil, [...] Judas,

**38,4,6a** whom he also called Satan and devil, saying to the disciples, “Was it not you, the twelve, that I chose, and one of you is a devil?”

**38,4,6b** Calling him a devil not by nature, but by his choice.

**38,4,7a** And again in another place he says, “Father, Lord of heaven and of earth, keep these whom you have given me.

**38,4,7b** When I was with them, I guarded them, and none of them was lost except the son of perdition.”

**38,4,8a** Again, at another time he says, “The Son of Man must be handed over in accordance with what is written about him, yet woe to the one through whom he will be handed over.

**38,4,8b** For it would have been to his advantage had he not been born,” and what follows.

**38,4,9** So then we know from every side that he was speaking about Judas to the Jews: “For by what a man is overcome, to that he is also enslaved,” and whomever a man obeys, him he has as his father and as the leader of his obedience.

**38,4,10** The Lord says, then, “You are sons of the devil, your father,” since they obeyed Judas instead of Christ, just as Eve, turning away from God, obeyed the snake in the beginning.

**38,4,11a** Then, that he was not only a liar but also a thief, according to what is said in the gospel.

**38,4,11b** Therefore he also entrusted the money-box to him, so that he might be without excuse, having betrayed his own Master into the hands of men out of love of money.

**38,4,12a** Who then is the father of this man, he who was a liar before him, if not Cain, of whom Judas is the imitator?

**38,4,12b** For he, lying to his brother as though in brotherly love, deceived him and won him over by the lie, and leading him out into the plain, he raised his hand and killed him.

**38,4,13a** So too Judas says, “What will you give me, and I will hand him over to you?”

**38,4,13b** And, “Whomever I kiss, he is the one; seize him.”

**38,4,13c** And the traitor came and said, “Hail, Rabbi,” honouring him with his lips, but having a heart far removed from God.

**38,5,1** So then this Judas, who became their father in the denial of God and in betrayal, being Satan and devil not by nature but by choice, has himself become, by imitation, a son of Cain the murderer, the liar — since the father of Cain too was a liar before him, not Adam but the devil,

**38,5,2a** whom he imitated in fratricide and hatred and lying and in contradicting God, when he said, “Am I a guardian of my brother?

**38,5,2b** I do not know where he is”; just so the devil too says to the Lord, “Does Job worship God for nothing?”

**38,5,3** For since the devil himself deceives Eve and Adam by a lie, saying one thing in place of another and making a show of pretended friendship — “You shall be as gods, and you shall not die” — Cain, imitating him, deceived his brother under a pretence of brotherly love, saying, “Let us go out into the plain.”

**38,5,4a** For this reason the holy John too said, “Whoever hates his brother has not been perfected in love, but is from Cain, who slew his brother.

**38,5,4b** And why did he kill him?

**38,5,4c** Because his works were evil, and he was jealous of his works, because they were good.”

**38,5,5** So too these men, who rather envy Abel, who had good works, and honour Cain — how are they not refuted, when the Saviour expressly says and lays down a severe sentence against them, “From this generation shall be required all righteous blood that has been shed from the beginning, from Abel the righteous to Zacharias the prophet, whom you slew between the shrine and the altar,” and what follows?

**38,6,1** It was not, then, through knowledge, as these men say, that Judas betrayed the Saviour, nor do the Jews have a reward for crucifying the Lord, even though we have our salvation through the cross.

**38,6,2** For Judas handed him over not that it might serve for our salvation, but out of ignorance and jealousy and the love of money that belongs to the denial of God.

**38,6,3** For even if Scripture says that Christ was to be handed over to the cross, or even if the divine Scripture speaks of the outrages to be committed by us at the end of the days, no one of us that does lawless things would by any means find a defence by bringing forward the testimony of Scripture, which foretold that these things would be.

**38,6,4** For we do not do things because Scripture said them, but Scripture foretold them because we were going to do them, on account of God’s foreknowledge, and so that God, being good, yet bringing his wrath upon sinners, might not be supposed to be in the grip of passion.

**38,6,5a** For the wrath from God against each of those who sin does not spring from passion; for the divine is impassible, and it brings its wrath upon human beings not as one seized by the passion of indignation, nor as one overcome and mastered by anger.

**38,6,5b** For God shows his impassibility by foretelling to us the judgement that is to come and the just punishment that comes from him, so as to signify the impassibility of the Godhead.

**38,6,6** The Scripture, then, foretold it by foreknowledge, securing us in advance and teaching us, so that we might not fall into the incurable wrath of God, the wrath that is not determined by passion nor arises from defeat, but has been prepared most justly for those people who carry sin through to its completion and do not repent in truth.

**38,7,1** So too in the matter of the cross: it is not because the divine Scripture said so that the Jews crucified and Judas betrayed the Saviour; but because Judas was going to betray him and the Jews to crucify him, for this reason the divine Scripture foretold it in the old covenant, as the Lord did in the gospel.

**38,7,2** So then Judas did not hand the Lord over in knowledge of the benefit that was to come to the world, as those people say, but knowing indeed that this was his own Master, yet not knowing that there would be salvation for the world.

**38,7,3** For how could he be one who cared for the salvation of human beings, who heard from the Saviour himself “son of perdition”, and “It would profit him had he never been born”, and “Friend, for what are you here?”, and “One of you will betray me”, and “He who eats bread with me has lifted up his heel against me” (as the gospel has it, from the earlier testimony of the Psalter), and “Woe to him through whom the Son of Man is betrayed”?

**38,7,4** For Judas himself disclosed the whole truth about himself, and also exposed, [from] himself, the folly of those who praise him, albeit unwillingly, but nevertheless, when after taking the price, the thirty pieces of silver, he afterwards repented and returned the silver, as one who had done something evil: evil for himself, and evil also for those who crucified him.

**38,7,5** But the Lord, accomplishing good through himself both for us and for the world, handed himself over, so that salvation might come about for us.

**38,7,6** So then it is not to Judas the traitor that we acknowledge our thanks, but to the merciful Saviour, who laid down his life for us, his own sheep, as he himself said.

**38,7,7** For how is it that the one who supposed he had accomplished good afterwards says, “I have repented of having handed over innocent blood”, and returned the silver, just as it had been written about him in the prophets: “And he gave back the thirty silver pieces, the price of the one on whom a price was set by the sons of Israel”, and once more, in another prophet: “If you think it just, give me my hire, or refuse it”,

**38,7,8** and once more, in another prophet: “And they paid out the silver, the price of the one on whom a price was set; and he said, ‘Put it into the furnace and see whether it stands the test, as I was tested by the sons of Israel’”?

**38,8,1** And how much there is to gather from the divine Scripture concerning the things brought to fulfilment in our Lord, not by virtue of any working of Judas towards the good, but by virtue of the handing over for us of Jesus Christ, our Saviour and Lord, the Son of God (not of necessity but of his own free choice), and of the dispensation of the cross for our salvation!

**38,8,2** I know that I am bringing the testimonies to a great bulk, as again another prophet says, “Let his homestead become desolate, and let another take his office of oversight” [...],

**38,8,3** who, “having fallen headlong, burst open in the middle, and all his bowels gushed out”, and in the end, having made use of a noose, showed that he had destroyed the whole of his salvation by falling away from hope, in keeping with his disorderly and reckless plot against his own Master, and for love of money, unto the denial of God [...];

**38,8,4** just as the apostles too appointed Matthias to their number in his place, saying, “from which Judas turned aside to go to his own place”.

**38,8,5a** And what place is this, if not the one the Saviour declared for him when he said that he is a son of perdition?

**38,8,5b** For this was the place marked out for him, the place of perdition, where he received a portion in exchange for a portion, and in place of the apostolic office of oversight, the place of perdition.

**38,8,6** Considering that what has been said about this is sufficient, let us go on again, in order, to another, beloved, once more uncovering the crooked, savage and venomous teachings of those among the rest who have been stupefied by a counterfeit devilish inspiration, to the ruin of the world.

**38,8,7** For having uncovered the mind of such people, who reach after what is worse, a mind resembling the breed of the buprestis beetle, and having crushed it in the power of God, harmful as it is, let us set our mind to the investigation of the others and call upon God as our helper, children of Christ.

### Sect 39: Sethians

Against the Sethians, the nineteenth, but the thirty-ninth in the series.

**39,1,1a** The Sethians, again, are another sect so called; but this one is not to be met with everywhere, any more than the one before it, called that of the Cainites; and perhaps most of these have by now been rooted out of the world.

**39,1,1b** For whatever is not from God shall not stand: for a season it is in its prime, but it does not remain permanently to the end.

**39,1,2** I think I perhaps came across this sect too in the country of the Egyptians (for I do not remember exactly the country in which I came across them); and some things about it we came to know by inquiry, through actual eyewitness, while others about it we learned from writings.

**39,1,3a** For these Sethians pride themselves on tracing their descent from Seth the son of Adam; they glorify him and ascribe to him all that is of virtue [...], the proofs of virtue and of righteousness and whatever else is of that kind.

**39,1,3b** Not only that, but they even call him Christ and firmly assert that he is Jesus.

**39,1,4** And they teach their doctrine thus: that all things, it says, have come into being from angels and not from the power above.

**39,2,1** For on this point these say things in harmony with the former sect, that of the Cainites: that two human beings came into being straightaway at the very beginning, and that from the two came Cain and Abel; that the angels, falling into faction over these, came to [war with] one another, and in this way caused Abel to be killed by Cain;

**39,2,2** for the faction arose among the angels as they contended over the races of human beings, [...] of these two, the one who had begotten Cain and the one who had begotten Abel.

**39,2,3a** And they say that the power above prevailed, which they name Mother and Female; for it is their opinion that above there are mothers too, both females and males, and they very nearly speak of kindreds and patriarchies as well.

**39,2,3b** Since, then, as they say, the one called Mother and Female prevailed,

**39,2,4** learning that Abel had been killed, she took thought and caused Seth to be born, and placed her own power in him, laying down in him seed of the power from on high, and the spark sent from above, for the first sowing of the seed and of the establishment.

**39,2,5** And this, they say, is an establishment of righteousness and an election of seed and of race, so that by means of [such] an establishment and of this seed the angelic powers that made the world, and the two human beings at the beginning, might be overthrown.

**39,2,6** For this reason, then, the race of Seth too, being set apart, is descended from that point, since it is of election and distinct from the other race.

**39,2,7** For as the times went on, they say, and the two races, that of Cain and that of Abel, were together, [and] had come together into one through great wickedness and had mingled together, the Mother of all, looking on, wished to make the seed of human beings pure, because, as I said before, Abel had been slain; and she selected this Seth, showed him to be pure, and in him alone deposited the seed of her power and purity.

**39,3,1** And seeing again much intermingling and a disorderly impulse of the angels and of the human beings, since the two races had come to mixing, and their disorder producing certain combinations of races, the same Mother and Female went again and brought a flood, and destroyed every stock of humankind [and] of every opposing race, so that, supposedly, the pure and righteous race that is from Seth might alone remain in the world, for the establishment of the race from above and of the spark of righteousness.

**39,3,2a** But the angels again escaped her notice and slipped Ham into the ark, since he was of their seed.

**39,3,2b** For of the eight souls saved in the ark of Noah at that time, seven, they say, were of the pure race, but the one, Ham, belonged to the other power, and he slipped in unnoticed by the Mother above.

**39,3,3** This, contrived by the angels, was accomplished in the following way: since, they say, the angels knew that all their seed was going to be wiped out in the flood, by a certain cunning they smuggled in the aforementioned Ham for the preservation of the race of wickedness created by them.

**39,3,4a** And from this, forgetfulness and error came about among human beings, and disorderly rushes of sins, and a manifold mingling of wickedness in the world.

**39,3,4b** And so the world turned back again to its ancient disorder and was filled with evils, as at the beginning before the flood.

**39,3,5** And from Seth, according to seed and according to the succession of the race, Christ came, Jesus himself, appearing in the world not by birth but wondrously; he is Seth himself, the one of that time, who has now also visited the race of human beings, sent from the Mother above.

**39,4,1a** All these things, those people say, came about in this way.

**39,4,1b** But such proclamations are foolish and feeble and full of empty babbling, as is plain to anyone.

**39,4,2** For it was not two human beings who were formed, but one, Adam, and from Adam came Cain and Abel and Seth; and no longer from two human beings, but from one [...] until the flood, all the races [from] Adam having their own existence in the world.

**39,4,3** And after the flood, again from Noah, the one man, and not from different men, but from the one Noah, who was in succession from the seed of Seth, derives the whole race of human beings, not divided into two, but into one race.

**39,4,4** Hence Noah and his wife, and Shem, Ham and Japheth, his sons, and the three wives of his sons all trace their descent from Seth, and not from two human beings who never existed, as these people’s mythmaking has it.

**39,5,1** And composing certain books under the names of great men, they say that there are seven books under the name of Seth, and other, different books they call Allogeneis, and one more under the name of Abraham, which they declare to be an apocalypse as well, crammed with every wickedness, and others under the name of Moses, and others under the names of other men.

**39,5,2a** And leading their mind down into great folly, they say that a certain Horaea was the wife of Seth.

**39,5,2b** But look, I pray you, beloved, at the senselessness of these people, so that you may in every way condemn their play-acting, their fabulous vain-mindedness and their fabricated drivel.

**39,5,3** For there are certain other sects which say that there is a certain power, which they call by name Horaea; so the one that is supposed by others to be a power and is called Horaea, these people say is the wife of Seth.

**39,5,4** Hence, beloved, we can demonstrate, as you are aware, both that Seth was by nature a man and received nothing extraordinary from above, and that by nature he was a brother of Cain and Abel, born of the one father and the one mother.

**39,5,5** For “Adam knew Eve his wife”, it says, “and she, having conceived, bore Cain”; and he gave him the name Cain, which, interpreted, is ‘possession’, saying, “I have gained possession of a son through the Lord God”.

**39,5,6** And again, [concerning] Abel: “Adam knew Eve, his wife, and on conceiving she bore a son and called his name Abel”.

**39,5,7** And long afterwards, after Abel had died, “And Adam knew Eve, his wife, and on conceiving she bore a son and called his name Seth”, which, interpreted, is ‘exchange’; “for God has raised up for me a seed in place of Abel, whom Cain killed”.

**39,5,8** And the words “I have gained possession through God” and “God has raised up for me” show one God, the maker of all things and the giver of what has been born.

**39,5,9** And that these, Cain and Seth, had wives is clear; for Abel was killed while still young, not yet having married.

**39,6,1** And, as is found in the Jubilees, also known as the Little Genesis, the book contains even the names of the wives of both Cain and Seth, so that in every way these people may be put to shame who have strung their myths together for the world.

**39,6,2** For since Adam had begotten sons and daughters, necessity required at that period that the children be joined to their own sisters; for such a thing was not unlawful.

**39,6,3a** Since there was no other race.

**39,6,3b** For indeed Adam himself, so to speak, was joined to what was almost his own daughter, the one formed from his body and bones, formed for him by God as his partner, and it was not unlawful.

**39,6,4** And his sons were joined in marriage: Cain to the elder sister, who was called Saue, and Seth, born the third son after Abel, to his sister called Azura.

**39,6,5** Other sons, too, were born to Adam, according to the contents of the Little Genesis: nine after these three, so that there were two daughters of his and twelve males, one of them slain and eleven left alive.

**39,6,6** And you find a hint of these as well in Moses’ Genesis of the world, his first book, which says: “And Adam lived nine hundred and thirty years, and he begot sons and daughters, and he died”.

**39,7,1** And as human beings spread abroad and the generation of Adam extended, the strictness concerning honourable marriage broadened, advancing in due order.

**39,7,2** And since children had been born to Adam, and children’s children, and daughters had been born from these in the succession of the race, thenceforth men no longer took their own sisters to wife; rather, even before the written law through Moses, the ordinance concerning honourable marriage was settled into good order, and they took their wives from their fathers’ brothers.

**39,7,3** And so, as human beings were now spreading abroad, the two races were mingled, that of Cain with the race of Seth and that of Seth with the other, as well as the other races of the sons of Adam.

**39,7,4** From then on, when the flood had utterly destroyed the whole stock of human beings, Noah alone was preserved, having found favour with God, being found righteous in the generation of that time.

**39,7,5** And he built himself an ark, as we have already said before, by the command of God, as the true Scriptures have it, in which, the same book of the truth says, he was saved together with the aforementioned seven souls with him: I mean his own wife and his three sons, and likewise their three wives.

**39,7,6a** And the truth again establishes that from him there came to be remnants of the human stock in the world.

**39,7,6b** From then on, as they advanced generation by generation [...] and in succession, son succeeding father, the age ran on through five generations.

**39,8,1** And at that time came the founding of Babylon in the land of the Assyrians, and the tower then built by them.

**39,8,2a** And at this time there were, as I have already related in the earlier sects, following the sequence of the generations treated by me above, seventy-two men in all in number, leaders and chiefs: thirty-two of the race of Ham, fifteen of Japheth, and twenty-five of Shem.

**39,8,2b** And so the tower and Babylon came to be.

**39,8,3a** From this, tribes and tongues were scattered over all the earth.

**39,8,3b** And since the seventy-two [who] were then building the tower were dispersed in their tongues, being confounded, and from the one they knew [...], being inspired by the will of God, each has come to have a different one.

**39,8,4** From these the establishment of the languages has stood until now, so that whoever wishes can find each leader of each tongue: thus Javan had Greek,

**39,8,5a** from whom too the Ionians have been named, who have the ancient tongue of the Greeks; Tiras [that] of the Thracians, Meshech the language of the Mossynoeci, Tubal that of the Thessalians, Lud that of the Lydians, Gephar that of the Gasphenians, Mizraim that of the Egyptians, Psous that of the Axumites, Armot that of the Arabs; and each of the rest, not to name them one by one, was inspired with his own tongue.

**39,8,5b** And so the succession of each tongue has spread abroad in the world.

**39,9,1a** From where, then, did these people falsely utter their words, inserting their own mythmaking, imagining and dreaming of things that are not as though they were, and scattering from their own understanding the things that are?

**39,9,1b** Rather, the whole is the will of the devil, which he has instilled into the souls of human beings.

**39,9,2** And one may see and marvel how in many absurdities he deceived man and dragged him down into lawlessness, into fornications and adulteries and licentiousness, idol-madness and sorcery and bloodshed, robberies and greed, dicing and gluttony and all such things; but nowhere before the coming of Christ did he dare to utter any blasphemous word against his own Master or to contemplate revolt.

**39,9,3** For he was awaiting the coming of Christ, as he says, “It is written concerning you that he will give his angels charge concerning you, and on their hands they will bear you up”.

**39,9,4** For he always heard the prophets announcing the coming of Christ, [and] a redemption that would come to those who had sinned and were repenting through Christ, and he supposed that he would obtain some mercy.

**39,9,5** But when the wretch saw that Christ did not accept his turning back for salvation, he opened his mouth and vomited out blasphemy against his own Master, casting into human beings the notion of denying him who is and seeking him who is not.

**39,9,6a** So these people too will be shown in every way to have been deceived.

**39,9,6b** For Seth died, and his years are recorded.

**39,9,6c** For having lived nine hundred and twelve years he paid the debt of nature, having begotten sons and daughters, as the divine Scripture says.

**39,9,7** And his son succeeded him, and Enosh was his name; and he too, having lived nine hundred and five years, departed from this life of ours, having begotten sons and daughters, according to that same book of the truth.

**39,10,1** How, then, will Seth, who died at that time, and whose sons too in succession lived and then ceased from life, be found to be the Lord, who was born of Mary the ever-virgin, having been pleased to do so for the life of human beings, who was begotten timelessly, who is ever with the Father, the subsistent God the Word,

**39,10,2** who came at the last of the days and formed flesh for himself from a virgin womb and took the human soul, and so became man most perfectly,

**39,10,3** who preached to us the mysteries of life, appointed his disciples workers of righteousness, and through himself and through them trained the human race in his teaching, without revealing the things of the Sethians or naming himself Seth, as these people, babbling and gripped by a kind of drunkenness, have fallen away from the truth?

**39,10,4a** But as things stand, since the [...] sect is brief, I have no need to extend the refutation against it, and I content myself with what is before us alone.

**39,10,4b** For the folly of these people is easily wounded, and able both to refute and to detect itself, not only concerning the conduct of Christ and the slandered teaching by which they suppose and firmly assert that he is Seth himself, but also with regard to the hypothesis concerning the two human beings.

**39,10,5a** For if the powers have had their beginning from above, the things done by the two would not have come about and been done without the one whom they indeed call the Mother of all things.

**39,10,5b** For the one proves to be the cause of both powers, and without her none of the things that came to be has come to be.

**39,10,6** And they will run back again to confessing that there is one Master of all things, Maker and Creator of the universe, once one first principle has been shown.

**39,10,7** But having said these things about this sect too, and having uncovered the venom of their reptilian brood from the race of asps, let us go on again to another, beloved, in the same sequence of the treatise.

### Sect 40: Archontics

Against the Archontics, the twentieth, but the fortieth in the series.

**40,1,1** A certain sect of the Archontics follows these; it is not current in many places, but only in the province of Palestine; but they have by now carried their venom somewhere into Greater Armenia too; and indeed in the

**40,1,2** Lesser Armenia this tare has already been sown by a certain man who had sojourned in the land of Palestine, coming from Armenia, in the times of Constantius, towards his end, Eutactus by name, but rather disorderly in his ways, who learned this evil teaching and then returned to his own country and taught it.

**40,1,3** And he received it, as I said, in Palestine, like venom from an asp, from a certain old man Peter, unworthily called Peter, who dwelt in the region belonging to Eleutheropolis [and] Jerusalem, three miles beyond Hebron; they call the village Kapharbaricha.

**40,1,4a** This old man at first wore an astonishing garb, full of hypocrisy; for outwardly he was indeed clothed in a sheep’s fleece, but it was not known that inwardly he was a ravening wolf.

**40,1,4b** For he was reputed to be an anchorite, sitting in a certain cave; he supposedly gathered many into renunciation, and was supposedly called father because of both his old age and his habit; and he distributed his possessions to the poor and gave alms daily.

**40,1,5** In his early life he had been found among many sects; but under the bishop Aetius he was accused and convicted of following at that time the sect of the Gnostics, and was deposed from the presbyterate (for he had at one time been ordained a presbyter); and after his conviction he was driven from the place by Aetius, and went away and settled in Arabia, at Kokabe, where the roots of the Ebionites and the Nazoraeans began, as I have also indicated concerning this place in many of the sects.

**40,1,6** But later, as though brought to soberness, he returned again in his old age, carrying this venom about with him secretly and unknown to all, until at last, convicted as such on the strength of the words he had whispered into the ears of certain people, he was anathematised by us, and he has been refuted by our littleness.

**40,1,7** And thereafter he sat in the cave, abhorred by all and cut off from the brotherhood and from most of those who take care for their own life.

**40,1,8** The aforementioned Eutactus, if indeed Eutactus, passing through from Egypt, put in with this old man, and having partaken of the old man’s evil teaching, took this poison as great merchandise and carried it off to his own homeland; for he came, as I said, from Lesser Armenia, from the places near Satala.

**40,1,9a** Having returned, then, to his homeland, he defiled many in this same Lesser Armenia, corrupting in addition certain rich people, a certain lady of senatorial rank and other persons of distinction, and through these conspicuous men he destroyed many there.

**40,1,9b** But the Lord quickly wiped him out of life, except that he had sown his own tare.

**40,2,1a** These too have likewise forged for themselves certain apocryphal books, whose names are these: one book they call, supposedly, the Little Symphonia, and another the Great Symphonia.

**40,2,1b** But they also heap up certain other books for themselves [...] whatever they happen upon, to be brought in besides, so that they may seem to confirm their error through many.

**40,2,2a** And they already make use of the so-called Allogeneis too; for there are books so called.

**40,2,2b** And they take their pretexts from the Ascension of Isaiah, and further from certain other apocrypha.

**40,2,3a** But the whole of it is from the book called Symphonia [...], in which they say there is a certain Ogdoad of heavens, and a Hebdomad as well, and that in each heaven there are archons; and that some are in the seven heavens, one archon in each heaven, and that each archon has orders, and that the luminous Mother is highest, in the eighth.

**40,2,3b** Just as the other sects do.

**40,2,4** And some of them are defiled in their bodies with licentiousness, while others supposedly feign a pretended fasting and deceive the simpler people, priding themselves on a kind of renunciation in the guise of monks.

**40,2,5** And they say that in each heaven, as I said before, there is a principality and an authority and certain angelic ministries, each archon having begotten and made a ministry for himself; and that there is no resurrection of the flesh, but only of the soul.

**40,2,6a** And they anathematise the washing, even if there are some among them who were received earlier and baptised.

**40,2,6b** And they reject participation in the mysteries, and its goodness, as alien and as having come into being in the name of Sabaoth; for they hold that he, as some of the other sects hold, is in the seventh heaven, ruling as a tyrant and prevailing over the others.

**40,2,7** And they say that the soul is food for the principalities and authorities, and that without it they cannot live, because it is from the moisture above and furnishes them with power.

**40,2,8** And they say that when it has come into knowledge and has fled from the church’s baptism and from the name of Sabaoth, the giver of the law, it ascends through each heaven and makes its defence to each authority, and so passes beyond to the higher Mother and Father of all things, from where indeed it came down into this world.

**40,2,9a** I have already said that they anathematise baptism, like “dying flies that spoil a preparation of sweet-scented oil”, as the parable in Ecclesiastes was spoken concerning these and those like them.

**40,2,9b** For they are in truth flies that are deadly and cause death and spoil the perfumer’s oil of sweet scent, the holy mysteries of God bestowed on us in the washing for the forgiveness of sins.

**40,3,1** One might marvel at finding even among the naturalists certain things full of usefulness, if one likens oneself to the intelligence of the bee, which settles on all the shoots but gathers for itself what is useful; for the intelligent man suffers loss from nowhere,

**40,3,2a** but rather gains from everywhere, while the unintelligent will suffer loss, as the holy prophet too says, “Who is wise, and will understand these things?

**40,3,2b** And he to whom the word of the Lord belongs will also know them: that the Lord’s ways are straight, while the ungodly will grow weak in them?”

**40,3,3** For we have found among the so-called naturalists too, or rather we ourselves see, that the race of beetles which some call bylari has the habit of rolling about in stench and dung; and this is both their food and their work, while to others this same filthy food of theirs, for vexation and foul odour [...].

**40,3,4a** And to bees this dung and stench is death, but to the bylari it is work and food and occupation.

**40,3,4b** To the bees, on the contrary, fragrance and flowers and perfumes serve for refreshment, for acquisition and food, for work and occupation, but to the aforementioned beetles, or bylari, such things are hostile.

**40,3,5** For whoever wishes to test them, as the naturalists say, takes some perfume (I mean balsam or nard) and brings it to the beetles, and causes their death; for they die at once, unable to bear the fragrance.

**40,3,6** So too these people, reaching after lust and fornication and wickedness, have their hope set on evil things, but when they come near the holy washing and its fragrance they die, blaspheming against God and rejecting his lordship.

**40,4,1a** But we shall overturn them with one or two testimonies.

**40,4,1b** For even if there are things called principalities and authorities, these have not come into being apart from God, especially in the heavens.

**40,4,2** For Scripture knows how to speak of angels and archangels, not as set in opposition, but as “spirits that minister, sent out to serve for the sake of those who are going to inherit salvation”.

**40,4,3a** For indeed on earth too, in each kingdom, there are many principalities, but under one king.

**40,4,3b** “For the authorities that exist have been ordained by God”, as the apostle says; “so then, whoever resists the authority has resisted the ordinance of God.

**40,4,4a** For rulers are not set against the good, but are there on behalf of the good; and they are not set against the truth, but are there on behalf of the truth.

**40,4,4b** Do you wish, it says, not to fear the authority?

**40,4,4c** Do what is good, and you will have praise from it.

**40,4,4d** For it does not bear the sword in vain; for it is a minister ordained by God for this very purpose, against the one who does evil.”

**40,4,5a** And you see that this worldly authority has been ordained by God and has received the authority of the sword; and not from somewhere else, but from God, for vengeance.

**40,4,5b** And it is not open to us to say that, because the world has principalities and authorities, their king is not king; rather, the principalities and the authorities exist, and so does their king.

**40,4,6** As, then, we see on earth (for it has been shown) that the principalities are not hostile to the king, but subject to him, for the governing of the entire realm and for good order on earth, where there are murders and battles, ignorances and teachings, good laws and lawlessness, and that it is on this account that there are authorities, so that all things may be fittingly set in order and managed by God into a well-ordered system for administering the whole world,

**40,4,7** so too in heaven, and there above all and in a special degree, where there is neither envy nor jealousy nor lawlessness nor opposition nor faction nor oath nor robbery nor anything else of that kind, authorities have been ordained for another task.

**40,4,8a** And which task do I mean, if not for the rhythm of the hymn, for the pure glorification above, through which our ungrudging God and King willed to grant to each of those made by him a fitting glory, so that the brightness and incomprehensibility and wonder of his kingdom may ever be glorified?

**40,4,8b** Clearly, then, those people have gone astray, being ignorant of the grace of God.

**40,5,1a** And these people say, as has also been made clear by me above, that the devil is son to the seventh authority, namely Sabaoth.

**40,5,1b** And they say that Sabaoth is the God of the Jews, and the devil his wicked son, who, being on the earth, opposes his own father.

**40,5,2** And they say that his father is neither of that sort, nor, again, the incomprehensible God whom they name Father, but belongs to the left-hand authority.

**40,5,3** And such people tell another myth again: that, it says, the devil came to Eve and was joined to her as a man to a woman, and begot of her Cain and Abel.

**40,5,4a** Therefore the one rose up against the other, because of the jealousy they had towards one another, not because Abel had in some way pleased God, as the truth has it; but, wandering astray, they tell another account: since, it says, both were in love with their own sister, for this reason Cain rose up against Abel and killed him.

**40,5,4b** For they say that by nature they were born, as I said before, from the seed of the devil.

**40,5,5** And when they wish to deceive some, they bring testimonies from the divine books, as has also been said by me in another sect, [that] the Saviour said to the Jews, “You are of Satan”, and “When he speaks the lie, he speaks from his own, because his father too was a liar”;

**40,5,6** so that, supposedly, they may say that Cain is [of] the devil, since he said that he was a murderer from the beginning and [...],

**40,5,7** so as to show that his father is the devil, and that the devil’s father is the lying archon, whom the senseless people, blaspheming upon their own heads, say is Sabaoth,

**40,5,8** supposing that Sabaoth is the name of some god, as has already been treated by us at length in the earlier sects concerning the interpretation of Sabaoth and of other names: Eli and Eloeim, El and Saddai, Ellion, Rabboni, Ia, Adonai and Iabe,

**40,5,9** that all of them, when interpreted, are designations of glorification and not names assigned, so to speak, to the Godhead; and these will be diligently set down here too, interpreted:

**40,5,10a** El is God; Eloeim, God for ever; Eli, my God; Saddai, the Sufficient; Rabboni, the Lord; Ia, Lord; Adonai, the Lord who is; Iabe, he who was and is, the one who ever is, as he interprets it to Moses, “He who is has sent me, you shall say to them”; and Ellion is interpreted Most High, and Sabaoth, of powers.

**40,5,10b** Lord Sabaoth, then, is Lord of powers.

**40,5,11a** For [Lord] is attached wherever the Scripture names Sabaoth; for [the Scripture] does not simply pronounce the words “Sabaoth told me” or “Sabaoth has spoken”; rather, it says straightway, Lord Sabaoth.

**40,5,11b** For thus the Hebrew says, “Adonai Sabaoth”, which is interpreted Lord of powers.

**40,6,1** And in vain, among them and those like them, in accordance with the blindness of their understanding, are things well said slandered.

**40,6,2** For it was not about the devil that the Saviour spoke in his discourse to the Jews, as is found plain to anyone who follows the truth, but he spoke the saying to them on account of Judas.

**40,6,3** For they were not children of Abraham, who received him under the oak of Mamre before his coming in the flesh, but they condemned themselves to become sons of the betrayal of Judas, who was named by the Lord Satan and devil, as he says, “Did I not choose twelve of you, and one from among you is a devil?”

**40,6,4** And for this reason the Lord, interpreting what belonged to his evil disposition, said, “When he speaks the lie, he speaks from his own”, as the gospel also says elsewhere, “He was a thief, and he himself carried the money-box”.

**40,6,5** This Judas, then, who was called devil, had Cain for his father, who deceived his brother Abel by a lie and killed him, as also, when asked by the Lord, “Where is Abel your brother?”, he lied and said, “I do not know”.

**40,6,6a** Fittingly, then, this man too, having imitated in his ways the one who is by nature the devil, has deservedly been proclaimed his son by the Saviour.

**40,6,6b** For “by whatever a man is overcome, to that he is also enslaved”, and each of us, whatever he may accomplish, will have as fathers those who accomplished it before, by having imitated them.

**40,6,7** The saying spoken by the Lord, then, was clearly expressed: “You are children of the devil”, and again, “When he speaks the lie, he speaks from his own, because his father is a liar”, so as to mean Judas and Cain;

**40,6,8a** then, “for his father too was a liar”, so as to mean the devil himself, because of the things done in like manner by each of these.

**40,6,8b** For the devil, breathing into the mouth of the serpent, spoke nothing but falsehoods, and so at that time deceived Eve.

**40,6,9a** And the erring mythmaking among these people is done away with, even if the Scripture says, “as Cain killed his brother, because he was of the devil”.

**40,6,9b** For it has been shown altogether [that] it was not because Eve conceived from the seed of the devil, as in a marriage union and a joining of bodies, and bore Cain and Abel, as these people thought, but because of his likeness of ways and his imitation of the devil’s evil disposition that he was called his son.

**40,7,1a** And again the same people say that Adam, joined to Eve, his own wife, begot Seth, his own son by nature.

**40,7,1b** And then, they say, the power above, with the angels who are ministers of the good God, came down and snatched away Seth himself,

**40,7,2** whom they also call Allogenes, and carried him up somewhere above and nurtured him for a considerable time, so that he might not be killed; and after a long time brought him down again into this world and made him spiritual and bodily, so that neither the [Demiurge] nor the other authorities and principalities of the world-making God might prevail against him.

**40,7,3** And they say that he no longer worshipped the Maker and Demiurge, but recognised the unnameable power and the good God above, [and] worshipped him, and revealed many things against the maker of the world and the principalities and authorities.

**40,7,4** Hence they have also fashioned certain books written under the name of Seth himself, claiming that they were handed on by him, and others under his name and that of his seven sons.

**40,7,5** For they say that he begot seven [sons] called Allogeneis, as we have stated in the case of other sects as well: I mean those of the Gnostics and the Sethians.

**40,7,6** And these people claim that there are further prophets as well, one Martiades and Marsianus, who were caught up into the heavens and descended after three days.

**40,7,7** And there are many things which, in their mythmaking, they fashion fictitiously, inventing blasphemies against him who is God Almighty, Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, alleging him to be an archon and the inventor of wickedness, as they are caught by the very words they use.

**40,7,8a** For if he is an inventor of evils and an evildoer, how could he not at once be found good, as we have also said in the other sects, when he legislated against fornication and adultery and robbery and covetousness?

**40,7,8b** For they themselves too say that he is the God of the Jews; and it was to the Jews that he gave the law, in which he prohibited all the very things they call him the inventor of.

**40,7,8c** And how could he be called father of Satan who has pointed out so many things against him?

**40,7,9** And if he is alien to the God whom they speak of as above, and is not himself God Almighty, our King and Lord, the one [proclaimed] in the law and in the prophets and in the gospels and apostles, himself God and Lord, and moreover Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, how is it that the Lord himself plainly teaches us in the gospel, saying, “I give thanks to you, Father, Lord of heaven and earth”, in order to show his own Father as God of all things?

**40,8,1** And again the Son who is from him, indicating that there is to be a resurrection of the dead, says, “Tear down this temple, and in three days I shall raise it up”; and by the temple he meant his own body, undone by human hands, that is, put to death.

**40,8,2** But that which is not a body, but appears in semblance, as these people again say, could not have come into men’s hands and been raised on the third day, as he promised.

**40,8,3** It has been clearly shown, then, through such a dispensation, that the resurrection of the dead is beyond dispute, and [that] the soul has no need of a defence, so that it should speak to each authority (for they invent this too, as has been said), but of the beneficence of the Lord, which is established through works and faith, as the most holy Paul says,

**40,8,4** writing to Timothy in these words, “so that you may know how one must walk in the Lord’s house, which is the church of the living God, pillar and bulwark of the truth”, “which many have forsaken and turned aside to myths and to foolish talk”, “understanding neither what they say nor the things about which they make assertions”; “of whom are Phygelus and Hermogenes”.

**40,8,5** But in the second epistle too he says that Hymenaeus and Philetus have missed the mark concerning the truth, who themselves too followed this sect, proclaiming another God and endless genealogies,

**40,8,6a** and sowing error among men, and claiming that the world came into being not from God but at the hands of rulers and authorities, and that the resurrection is already past, having come about through the children begotten by each of those who beget, whereas that of the dead, they say, will not be.

**40,8,6b** And see, brothers, the stamp of the truth and the refutation of the lawlessness of these people.

**40,8,7** I suppose that what has been said about these people too is sufficient; hence, leaving this sect behind, I shall make my way to those that follow, saying only this, that through its many-named archontic nomenclature it appears very like the intricate malignity of reptiles.

**40,8,8** For in a way the venom of their error has been gathered from many reptiles mixed together, having, for instance, the boldness of the dragon, the craftiness of the physalus, the drawing-in of breath of the baeon, the arrogance of the dart-snake, the rashness of the ostracites.

**40,8,9** And having trampled on the head of all these with the foundation of the truth, let us pass on to the rest, dearly beloved, trying in God to uncover the error of each.

### Sect 41: Cerdonians

Against the Cerdonians, the twenty-first, but the forty-first in the series.

**41,1,1a** A certain Cerdo succeeds these and Heracleon, being of the same school, having taken his pretexts from Simon and Satornilus.

**41,1,1b** He becomes an emigrant from Syria, and coming to Rome he has proved a ruin to himself and to those persuaded by him, the most pitiable man.

**41,1,2** For pitiable is the nature of human beings when, having abandoned the way of God, it goes astray, and it has perished, having separated itself from the calling of God.

**41,1,3** For the proverb of the dog has befallen such people, which, intent on the shadow of what it held in its mouth [...], looking into water and thinking the shadow in the water larger than the food in its mouth, opened its mouth and lost even what it had.

**41,1,4** So too these people, having found the way and wishing to get hold of a shadow that arose in their own imagination, not only lost the food that was in their mouth, so to speak, bestowed by God, but also drew destruction upon themselves.

**41,1,5a** This Cerdo, then, lived in the times of Hyginus the bishop, who held the ninth place from the succession of the apostles James and Peter and Paul.

**41,1,5b** And his preaching, sharing in the drivel of the aforementioned heresiarchs, seems to be the same, but it has been altered by him and is of this sort:

**41,1,6** this man too proclaimed to the world two first principles and, supposedly, two gods: the one good, and unknown to everyone, to whom he has also given the name of father of Jesus; the other the Demiurge, evil and known, who spoke in the law, appeared to the prophets and frequently became visible.

**41,1,7a** And he says that Christ was not born of Mary nor appeared in the flesh, but was in semblance and appeared in semblance, and did everything in semblance.

**41,1,7b** And he too thrusts aside the resurrection of the flesh and disowns the old covenant given through Moses and the prophets, as being alien to God.

**41,1,8** And he says that Christ came from the realms above, from the unknown Father, for the setting aside, it says, of the rule and tyranny here of the world-maker and Demiurge, just as, of course, many of the sects have also declared.

**41,1,9** And having been in Rome a short time, he imparted his venom to Marcion; for which reason Marcion succeeded him.

**41,2,1a** And since the sect is likewise easily detected, our account of it will be brief.

**41,2,1b** And we shall begin the overthrow of him again from the very things he says.

**41,2,2a** For that it is impossible for two first principles to exist together is plain.

**41,2,2b** For either the two are from some one, or the one is second and the other the cause and first principle of the second.

**41,2,2c** So there will be sought either a cause of the two or whichever of them is the first principle of the other [...], as I said.

**41,2,3** And so in every way the understanding must be brought back to the one, which is found to be first, from which the second also comes, or both, as has been shown.

**41,2,4a** And it is impossible for the two first principles to exist together, but neither is it possible for the one to be at variance with the other.

**41,2,4b** For if they are at variance, they are indeed two, but by being found in the number two they have gone beyond the number one; and when the one and first number is sought, the two are subordinate to the number one, which is higher, since it is the cause of the two.

**41,2,5** For the pair, coming after the number one or the one first principle, cannot be causes of themselves, since the singular and highest number is always sought.

**41,2,6** For if the two are found to be of one accord, rejoicing together and agreeing with one another, in that the one remains alongside while the other is well pleased, and the other rejoices in its union with the first, what is the strife between both?
