**41,2,7** But if they are at strife and both are equally strong against one another at once, Christ, who came for the setting aside of the one, will no longer be able to destroy its tyranny, since it holds out and is able to contend against the invisible and unnameable one above and to hold out, and can never be destroyed.

**41,3,1** And the simpleton asserts that the law and the prophets alike are of the base and opposed first principle, but Christ of the good one.

**41,3,2a** How, then, did the prophets proclaim beforehand the things prefigured concerning Christ, if it was not one and the same power that spoke in the law and in the prophets and in the gospels?

**41,3,2b** Just as it says, “I who speak in the prophets, behold, I am present”, and what follows.

**41,3,3a** And how is it that the Lord too, in the gospel, proclaimed beforehand, saying, “If you believed Moses, you would also believe me.

**41,3,3b** For he wrote about me.”

**41,3,4** And there is much for me to say concerning testimonies, about how he gathered and made a school for himself, he who sprouted up upon the world as an evil mischance and led astray those who were led astray by him.

**41,3,5** But I shall pass by this sect too, having made an end of it as of a hornet and a wasp, those pain-bringing ones among winged creatures, which dart out and fly forth with a single sting, through the most manifest faith of God and the saving doctrine of our Lord Jesus Christ, who said, “Beware lest you be led astray;

**41,3,6** for many false prophets will come”, and of the apostle, [who] spoke about these same false Christs and false teachers and false brethren and made us secure; and proceeding in order to those that follow, I shall [also] give my account of the others.

### Sect 42: Marcionites

Against the Marcionites, the twenty-second, but the forty-second in the series.

**42,1,1** Marcion, from whom come the Marcionites, having taken his pretext from this aforementioned Cerdo, himself too came forth into the world as a great serpent, and having deceived a great multitude, he has set up a school that remains to this day, in many forms.

**42,1,2a** And the sect is still found even now in Rome and in Italy, in Egypt and in Palestine, in Arabia and in Syria, in Cyprus and the Thebaid, and indeed in Persia too and in other places.

**42,1,2b** For the evil one has greatly strengthened the deceit in him.

**42,1,3** This man was a Pontian by birth, of Helenopontus, I mean, of the city of Sinope, as the common report about him has it.

**42,1,4a** In his early life he supposedly practised virginity; for he was living as a monk, and was son to a bishop of our holy catholic church.

**42,1,4b** But as time went on he formed a corrupt attachment to a certain virgin, and having deceived the virgin, he dragged both her and himself down from hope; and having accomplished her corruption he is expelled from the church by his own father.

**42,1,5** For his father was, because of his surpassing piety, one of those distinguished men who take very great care for the church, excelling in the ministry of the episcopate.

**42,1,6a** And Marcion, supposedly, after much entreaty and asking for penance, did not obtain it from his own father.

**42,1,6b** For it grieved the worthy old man and bishop that not only had that one fallen away, but that he had also brought the disgrace upon him.

**42,1,7** So, since he did not obtain from him by his flattery what he asked for, not bearing the mockery of the many, he runs away from his own city and goes up to Rome itself, after Hyginus the bishop of Rome had died (he was the ninth from Peter and Paul the apostles), and meeting the elders who still survived and who derived from the disciples of the apostles, he asked to be received into communion, and no one granted it to him.

**42,1,8** Thereupon, carried away by jealousy, since he had not obtained the presidency and entrance into the church, he takes counsel with himself and takes refuge in the sect of Cerdo the deceiver.

**42,2,1a** And he begins, so to speak, from the very beginning and as from the doors of the questions, to put this question to the presbyters of that time, saying, “Tell me, what is this: ‘They do not put new wine into old wineskins, nor a patch of unfulled rag on an old garment; otherwise it both takes away the fullness and will not agree with the old.

**42,2,1b** For a greater rent will come about.’”

**42,2,2** And when the gentle and all-holy presbyters and teachers of the holy church of God heard this, giving the account of its sequence and harmony and explaining it to him with gentleness, they said:

**42,2,3** “Child, the old wineskins are this: the hearts of the Pharisees and scribes, grown old in sins and not having received the proclamation of the gospel;

**42,2,4** and the old garment is as Judas, who, grown old in love of money, did not receive the proclamation of hope of the new and holy and heavenly mystery, although he had been united with the eleven apostles and had received his call from the Lord himself,

**42,2,5** and he had a greater rent through himself, though no one else was the cause of it for him, since his mind was not in accord with the hope above and the heavenly calling of the good things to come, instead of the riches here and boasting and the friendship of a passing hope and pleasure.”

**42,2,6a** But he, Marcion, contradicting them, says, “Not so, but there are other things besides these.”

**42,2,6b** [And] since they were not willing to receive him, [for] this reason, then, he said to them openly, “Why were you not willing to receive me?”

**42,2,7a** And when they said, “We cannot do this without the permission of your honourable father;

**42,2,7b** for the faith is one, and the concord one, and we are unable to oppose our good fellow minister, who is your father,”

**42,2,8** this man thereupon became jealous, and being lifted up into great anger and arrogance he brings about the schism, setting up the sect for himself and saying, “I shall rend your church and cast a rent into it for ever”; and in truth he did cast no small rent, rending not the church but himself and those persuaded by him.

**42,3,1a** And he took his pretext from the aforementioned Cerdo, a sorcerer and deceiver.

**42,3,1b** For he too proclaims two first principles, but, adding again to that one, Cerdo, I mean, he puts forward something different from him, saying that there are three first principles: one, the unnameable and invisible one above, which he also wishes to call the good God, but which created none of the things in the world;

**42,3,2a** and another, the visible God, both creator and Demiurge; and the devil a third, so to speak, standing midway between this pair, the visible one and the invisible.

**42,3,2b** And he says that the creator and Demiurge, the visible God, is the Jews’ God, and that he is a judge.

**42,3,3a** And with Marcion himself virginity too is proclaimed, and he proclaims fasting on the sabbath.

**42,3,3b** And mysteries are supposedly celebrated by him with the catechumens looking on; and this man uses water in the mysteries.

**42,3,4** And he gives such a reason for fasting on the sabbath: since, he says, the rest is that of the Jews’ God, the maker of the world, who took his rest on the seventh day, let us keep that day as a fast, so that we may not do what is proper to the God of the Jews.

**42,3,5** And he rejects the resurrection of the flesh, just as many of the sects do; and he holds that the soul has a resurrection, and that life and salvation belong to it alone.

**42,3,6** And not only one washing is given by him, but among them it is permitted to give even up to three washings and beyond to whoever desires it, as many people have told me.

**42,3,7** And it came about that he permitted three or even more washings to be given because of the mockery he endured from those of his disciples who knew him, on account of his transgression and the corruption done to the virgin.

**42,3,8** For since, having corrupted the virgin in his own city and run away, he was found in great transgression, the charlatan devised a second washing for himself, saying that it is permitted for up to three washings, that is, three baptisms, to be given for the forgiveness of sins, so that if anyone fell away after the first, he may repent and receive a second, and likewise a third, if he falls into transgression after the second.

**42,3,9** And, supposedly to retrieve the mockery against himself, he brings forward, as proof that after his transgression he was cleansed again and is thenceforth among the innocent, a testimony about this, falsely asserting it as persuasive, one able to deceive but not signifying what he himself says:

**42,3,10a** that, he says, the Lord, after he had been baptised by John, said to the disciples, “I have a baptism to be baptised with, and what do I wish, if I have already accomplished it?”

**42,3,10b** And once more, “I have a cup that I must drink, and what do I wish, if I shall already fulfil it?”

**42,3,10c** And so he made it a doctrine to give more baptisms.

**42,4,1** And not only this, but he also rejects the law and all the prophets, saying that such men prophesied from the archon who made the world.

**42,4,2** And he says that Christ came down from above, from the invisible and unnameable Father, for the salvation of souls and for the refutation of the God of the Jews and of the law and the prophets and such things.

**42,4,3** And he says that the Lord came down even as far as Hades, to save Cain and Korah and Dathan and Abiram and their company, and Esau and all the nations that had no knowledge of the God of the Jews;

**42,4,4** but that he left there Abel and Enoch and Noah and Abraham and Isaac and Jacob and Moses, David and Solomon and their company, because, he says, they recognised the God of the Jews, who is maker and creator, and did what was due to him, and did not give themselves over to the invisible God.

**42,4,5a** He also gives women permission to administer baptism.

**42,4,5b** For among them everything is full of mockery and nothing else, since they even dare to celebrate the mysteries in the presence of catechumens.

**42,4,6a** And this man, as I said, speaks of a resurrection not of bodies but of souls, and assigns salvation to these, not to the bodies.

**42,4,6b** And he likewise asserts transfusions of souls and transmigrations from bodies into bodies.

**42,5,1a** But his vain empty babbling falls on every side, as I have already dealt with in other sects.

**42,5,1b** For how will the soul that has not fallen rise again?

**42,5,1c** And how will there be said to be a resurrection of it, of the soul that has not fallen?

**42,5,2a** For everything that falls needs resurrection; and it is not the soul that falls, but the body.

**42,5,2b** Hence common usage too rightly calls it a corpse, and so does the Lord himself, saying, “Where the corpse is, there the eagles will be gathered”.

**42,5,3** For indeed we do not shut up souls in the tombs, but we lay bodies in the earth and cover them over, whose resurrection holds the proclamation in hope, like the grain of wheat;

**42,5,4** as the holy apostle too bore witness concerning the grain of wheat and the other seeds, and the Lord himself in the gospel, that “unless the grain of wheat falls and dies, it remains alone”.

**42,5,5** And the holy apostle says, “Fool” (for he calls the unbeliever a fool, who doubts altogether and says, “How does the resurrection come about? And with what kind of body do they come?”, and at once he says to such people), “Fool, what you sow is not brought to life unless it dies”.

**42,5,6** And the Scripture showed in every way that the resurrection takes place for the fallen grain, that is, for the body that is buried, and not for the soul.

**42,5,7a** And how will the soul come alone?

**42,5,7b** And how will the soul reign alone, which wrought what is just or what is base together with the body?

**42,5,7c** For the judgement will be perverse and not just.

**42,6,1a** And how will the doctrine of three first principles held by Marcion himself be made to stand?

**42,6,1b** And how will [the one] be found good who accomplishes a work, whether of salvation or of other things, in the regions of the base one?

**42,6,2** For if the world is not his, but he dispatched his Only-begotten into the world in order to take from the alien world what he himself neither sowed nor made, he will be found either coveting what belongs to others, or undergoing poverty and having nothing of his own, giving himself over to what belongs to others, so that he may procure for himself what he did not have before.

**42,6,3a** And how will the Demiurge become judge between both?

**42,6,3b** Then whom has he to judge?

**42,6,3c** For if he presides as judge over the vessels that are from the one above, he is stronger than the one above, since he brings into the midst of his tribunals the possessions of the one above, as Marcion supposed.

**42,6,4a** And if he is in any sense a judge, he is just.

**42,6,4b** And we shall show from the name “just” that the good and the just are the same.

**42,6,4c** For everything that is just is also good.

**42,6,5** For from being good he bestows the good justly, with truth, on the one who has done good things, and he will no longer be opposed to the good with respect to the good, furnishing in justice the good to the good man and to the base man the penalty of punishment.

**42,6,6** Nor again would he be good who at the end renders the good reward to the wicked man who does not repent, even if at present he makes his sun rise on the good and the base and provides his rain, because of the present free will, to both wicked and good men.

**42,6,7** For the nature of one who grants the wicked man a reward of salvation in the coming age, instead of hating what is wicked and base, will no longer be good and just.

**42,6,8** And the third, who is found with him to be wicked, if he has the power to do wicked things and to prevail over those in the world, whether those of the good ones from above or of the just one in the middle, will be found stronger than the two gods spoken of by Marcion, since he wields the power to carry off what belongs to others;

**42,6,9** and the two will thereafter be condemned as feeble beside the one wicked one, since they are unable to hold out and to rescue their own from the one who seizes them and changes them into wickedness.

**42,7,1a** But let us consider it again in another way, so as to expose the mockery of the charlatan’s vain-mindedness.

**42,7,1b** For if the wicked one is altogether wicked, but seizes the good from the good one and the just from the just one, and does not seize only his own, the wicked one will be found no longer wicked, since he reaches after the good and lays claim to them as better.

**42,7,2a** But if he also judges his own and exacts punishment from those who have done wrong, he who is the judge of the wicked will no longer be wicked.

**42,7,2b** And his hypothesis will be found in every way overturning itself.

**42,7,3a** And tell me again, from where have the three first principles received their being?

**42,7,3b** And who is it that established a boundary for them?

**42,7,3c** For if each is bounded in his own place, the three will no longer be found perfect, being bounded in certain containing places, and the container of each will be found greater than what is contained, and it would not be what is contained that is called God any longer, but rather the containing boundary.

**42,7,4** But if, having come together into the same place with one another, each was allotted his own place by partition, and each, being in his own, does not intrude upon the other nor encroach on him, the first principles will no longer be found opposed to one another, nor any of them base, because they care for their own in justice and quiet and stability and do not undertake to go further.

**42,7,5** But if the wicked one is overpowered and constrained and worn down by the one above, though he is separated off and exists in his own place, a place with which the good one above has nothing to do, and none of the things here has been created by him (I mean in the place of the wicked one), the one above will rather be found more tyrannical and no longer good, since he sent his own Son, that is, Christ, to take what belongs to others.

**42,7,6a** And where is the boundary that divides the three first principles, according to the account of the charlatan’s hypothesis?

**42,7,6b** For a fourth will be sought, most equitable and wiser than the three, a surveyor of boundaries and an expert, who distributed the measures to each and made peace among the three, so that they might not be at strife with one another, nor one send into the domain of another.

**42,7,7a** And this one, having persuaded the three first principles, will be found a fourth, both wiser and more equitable.

**42,7,7b** And he too, in turn, will have to be looked for in a place of his own, from which he came into the midst of the three and wisely marked out for each his portion, so that they might not wrong one another.

**42,7,8** But if the two first principles live as citizens in the domain of the one, I mean of the Demiurge, both the wicked one in the regions and spaces of the Demiurge and the Christ of the good one, who sojourned there, then the judge will no longer be found only judge and Demiurge, but also good, allowing the two to do in his own domain what they wish; or he will be found feeble and unable to prevent the alien robbers of his own.

**42,7,9a** But if he is also inferior in power, the creation will no longer be found to subsist, but it failed long ago, being snatched away every day both by the wicked one into his own portion and by the good one into the realms above.

**42,7,9b** And how will the creation still stand?

**42,7,10** But if your claim is that in time it will cease, and that it can cease entirely through the good one’s care, then will not the good one be the cause of the harm, who did not do long ago what he later decided to accomplish as a good thing, nor did it from the very beginning, before most people were wronged and came into the custody of the judge and remained below?

**42,8,1** And again, not understanding the things of the divine Scripture rightly, he brings them forward and deceives the simple, distorting the apostle’s saying, “Christ bought us back from the curse of the law, having become a curse for us”; and he argues, “Had we been his, he would not have been buying what was his own;

**42,8,2a** but as a buyer he came into an alien world to buy us back, who were not his.

**42,8,2b** For we were the handiwork of another, and for this reason he was buying us into his own life.”

**42,8,3a** But the fool is wholly ignorant that Christ did not become a curse (God forbid), but took away the curse that was on account of our sins, having crucified himself and become death to death and himself a curse to the curse.

**42,8,3b** Therefore Christ is not a curse, but the curse’s undoing, and a blessing for everyone who has believed in him in truth.

**42,8,4a** So too the word “bought back” [...].

**42,8,4b** He did not say “bought”; for he did not come into what belonged to another to seize it or to buy it.

**42,8,4c** For if he bought, he bought because he did not possess, and like a poor man acquired what he did not have.

**42,8,5a** And if the one who possessed us sold us, he sold because he was in want.

**42,8,5b** Driven, then, by some creditor.

**42,8,5c** But it is not so.

**42,8,5d** For he did not say “bought”, but “bought back”.

**42,8,6a** Similar to this the same holy apostle says, “buying back the season, since the days are evil”.

**42,8,6b** And we do not buy days, nor do we pay a price for days; rather, he said this to signify that which comes through endurance [...] and the patient waiting of long-suffering.

**42,8,7a** So the word “bought back” has disclosed the purpose of the undertaking on our behalf, of the coming in the flesh into the world, [through] which God, who is impassible, took it upon himself to suffer for our sake while abiding in the proper impassibility of his Godhead and [...] the very thing which he undertook to suffer for us.

**42,8,7b** He was not buying us from others; rather, he took upon himself the undertaking of the cross on our behalf, by free choice and not under compulsion.

**42,8,7c** Hence Marcion’s argument has been refuted on every point.

**42,8,8** And there are many things that serve to overturn this man’s contrivance and theatrical tale, proceeding from pious reasoning and well-grounded demonstration, in the rebuttal of him.

**42,9,1a** I shall now come to the things written by him, or rather fraudulently tampered with.

**42,9,1b** For this man has only the Gospel according to Luke, cut away at the beginning on account of the Saviour’s conception and his coming in the flesh.

**42,9,2** And this man, who has mutilated himself [rather] than the gospel, not only cut off the beginning, but also cut away many of the words of the truth from the end and from the middle, and has added other things beyond what is written; and he uses this text alone, the Gospel according to Luke.

**42,9,3a** He also has with him ten epistles of the holy apostle, which alone he uses, yet not all that is written in them; rather, he cuts away some of their passages and has altered others.

**42,9,3b** These two books he uses; but he has composed other treatises of his own for those who are led astray by him.

**42,9,4** The epistles that are spoken of with him are these: first, to the Galatians; second, to the Corinthians; third, the second to the Corinthians; fourth, to the Romans; fifth, to the Thessalonians; sixth, the second to the Thessalonians; seventh, to the Ephesians; eighth, to the Colossians; ninth, to Philemon; tenth, to the Philippians; and he also has parts of the one called ‘to the Laodiceans’.

**42,9,5** From this very text preserved with him, both of the gospel and of the apostle’s epistles, we are able, with God, to show him to be a deceiver and himself deceived, and to refute him most thoroughly.

**42,9,6a** For he will be overthrown beyond all doubt from the very things he acknowledges.

**42,9,6b** For from the same remnants of the gospel and of the epistles that are still found with him it will be shown to people of understanding that Christ is no stranger to the Old Testament, and that the prophets, then, are no strangers to the Lord’s sojourn,

**42,9,7** and that the apostle proclaims the resurrection of the flesh, and gives the prophets the name of righteous, and speaks of Abraham and Isaac and Jacob and those with them as among the saved, and all that belongs to the holy church of God, saving and holy and established by God, in faith and in knowledge, in hope and in teaching.

**42,10,1** I shall also set out the treatise which I composed against him before I had made it my concern to produce this present work of mine at the urging of you, the brethren.

**42,10,2** Some years ago, while examining the falsehood invented by this Marcion and his nonsensical teaching, I took into my hands the very books which the aforesaid possesses, both what he calls the gospel and what is called by him [the] Apostolikon; and having culled and gathered in order from the two aforesaid books the things able to refute him, I made a kind of groundwork of a treatise, arranging the passages in sequence and heading each saying 1, 2, 3.

**42,10,3** And so I went through to the end; and in these he is seen foolishly keeping, against himself, these words of the Saviour and of the apostle that still remain.

**42,10,4** For some of them were tampered with by him in altered form, and not as the copy of the Gospel according to Luke had them, nor the sense of the apostolic text;

**42,10,5** but others stand in their natural form, as both the gospel and the apostle have them, unaltered by him yet able to refute him, and through them [the] Old Testament is shown to be in harmony with the New, and the New in turn with the Old Testament;

**42,10,6** and again other words of the same books, which indicate that it was in the flesh that Christ came, and that he has become fully man among us;

**42,10,7** and yet others again, which confess the resurrection of the dead, and God as being one, Lord of all, almighty, himself the one who made heaven and earth and all things that have come to be on the earth; and which neither counterfeit the calling of the gospel nor deny the maker and creator of all things, but make known the one clearly acknowledged by the text of the apostolic and of the gospel proclamation.

**42,10,8a** And the work we have done is set out in what follows, and it is this:

**42,10,8b** Preface to the account and refutation concerning the books of Marcion.

**42,11,1** Whoever cares to examine closely the spurious notions of the deceiver Marcion and to discern the fabricated contrivances of this same brute, let him not shrink from reading this compiled work.

**42,11,2** For we have taken pains to set out, from the gospel he has, the things that serve to rebut his knavish tampering, so that those wishing to read the work may have in it an exercise of sharpness for refuting the strange expressions he has devised.

**42,11,3** For the text does point to the Gospel according to Luke; but as it has been mutilated, having neither beginning nor middle nor end, it bears the likeness of a garment eaten by many moths.

**42,11,4** For right at the start, everything that Luke had set down from the beginning — that is, as he says, “Inasmuch as many have undertaken,” and what follows, and the matters concerning Elizabeth and the angel bringing the good news to Mary the virgin, concerning John and Zacharias and the birth in Bethlehem, the genealogy and the account of the baptism —

**42,11,5** all this he cut away and leapt over, and fixed as the gospel’s opening this: “In Tiberius Caesar’s fifteenth year”, and what follows.

**42,11,6a** From this point, then, he begins, and again he does not keep to the sequence; rather, as I said before, he cuts some things away and adds others this way and that, not walking straight but going round everything fraudulently; and it is as follows:

**42,11,6b** 1. “Go, show yourself to the priest and make an offering for your cleansing.”

**42,11,6c** “just as Moses ordered”; “so that this may be a testimony to you”, in place of what the Saviour said, “for a testimony to them”.

**42,11,6d** 2. “But that you may know that the Son of Man has authority to forgive sins on earth.”

**42,11,6e** 3. “The Son of Man is Lord even over the sabbath.”

**42,11,6f** 4. “Judas Iscariot, who became a traitor.”

**42,11,6g** And in place of “he went down with them” he reads “he went down among them”.

**42,11,6h** 5. “And all the crowd sought to touch him.”

**42,11,6i** “And he, lifting up his eyes,” and what follows.

**42,11,6j** 6. “In the same way your fathers did to the prophets.”

**42,11,6k** 7. “But I say to you, not even in Israel have I found such faith.”

**42,11,6l** 8. “Blessed is he who is not offended in me” has been altered; for he had it as referring to John.

**42,11,6m** 9. “It is he about whom it has been written, ‘See, I am sending my messenger before your face.’”

**42,11,6n** 10. “And he went into the house of the Pharisee and reclined at table.”

**42,11,6o** “And the woman, the sinner, standing behind at his feet, wet the feet with her tears, and anointed and kissed them.”

**42,11,6p** 11. And again: “This woman wet my feet with her tears, and anointed and kissed them.”

**42,11,6q** 12. He lacked “his mother and his brothers”, having only “your mother and your brothers”.

**42,11,6r** 13. “While they were sailing he fell asleep; and, having been roused, he reproved the wind and the sea.”

**42,11,6s** 14. “Now it happened, while they were on their way, that the crowds were pressing upon him.”

**42,11,6t** “And a woman who touched him was cured of her bleeding; and the Lord said, ‘Who was it that touched me?’”

**42,11,6u** And again: “Someone touched me.”

**42,11,6v** “For I perceived power going out from me.”

**42,11,6w** 15. “Looking up to heaven, he blessed them.”

**42,11,6x** 16. “Saying, ‘It is necessary for the Son of Man to suffer much, and to be put to death, and after three days to be raised.’”

**42,11,6y** 17. “And behold, two men were talking with him, Elijah and Moses, in glory.”

**42,11,6z** 18. “A voice out of the cloud: ‘This is my beloved Son.’”

**42,11,6aa** 19. “I begged your disciples.”

**42,11,6ab** And next to “they were not able to cast it out” he had “and to them: ‘You faithless generation, how long am I to put up with you?’”

**42,11,6ac** 20. “For the Son of Man is about to be delivered into the hands of men.”

**42,11,6ad** 21. “Have you not read even this, what David did: he entered the house of God?”

**42,11,6ae** 22. “I thank you, Lord of heaven.”

**42,11,6af** But he did not have “and of earth,” nor did he have “Father.”

**42,11,6ag** But he is refuted; for further on he had “Yes, Father.”

**42,11,6ah** 23. He said to the lawyer, “What stands written in the law?”

**42,11,6ai** And answering, after the lawyer’s answer, he said, “You have spoken rightly.”

**42,11,6aj** “Do this, and you will live.”

**42,11,6ak** 24. And he said, “Which of you will have a friend, and will go to him at midnight asking for three loaves?”

**42,11,6al** And then, “Ask, and it will be given.”

**42,11,6am** “For which father among you will a son ask for a fish, and he will give him a serpent instead of a fish, or a scorpion instead of an egg?”

**42,11,6an** “If you then, being evil, know good gifts, how much more the Father?”

**42,11,6ao** 25. The passage about Jonah the prophet has been cut away.

**42,11,6ap** For his text read, “This generation: a sign will not be given to it.”

**42,11,6aq** But he had nothing about Nineveh and the queen of the south and Solomon.

**42,11,6ar** 26. Instead of “you pass over the judgement of God” he had “you pass over the calling of God.”

**42,11,6as** 27. “Woe to you, because you build the prophets’ tombs and your fathers put them to death.”

**42,11,6at** 28. He did not have “For this reason the wisdom of God said, ‘I send prophets to them,’” nor the words about the blood of Zacharias and Abel and the prophets, that it will be required of this generation.

**42,11,6au** 29. “I say to my friends, ‘Do not be afraid of those who kill the body, but fear him who after killing has authority to cast into Gehenna.’”

**42,11,6av** But he lacked “Are five sparrows not sold for two pennies, while not one of them stands forgotten in the sight of God?”

**42,11,6aw** 30. Instead of “will confess before the angels of God” he says “before God.”

**42,11,6ax** 31. He does not have “God clothes the grass.”

**42,11,6ay** 32. “But your Father knows that you need these things,” the things of the flesh, that is.

**42,11,6az** 33. “But seek God’s kingdom, and all these things will be added to you.”

**42,11,6ba** 34. Instead of “your Father” he had “the Father.”

**42,11,6bb** 35. In place of “in the second or third watch” his text read “in the evening watch”.

**42,11,6bc** 36. “The master of that servant will come and cut him in two, and will set his portion with the unbelievers.”

**42,11,6bd** 37. “Lest he drag you to the judge, and the judge hand you over to the bailiff.”

**42,11,6be** 38. It had been cut away from “There came some who told him about the Galileans, whose blood Pilate had mixed with their sacrifices,” up to where he speaks of the eighteen in Siloam who died in the tower, and “Unless you repent,” and [what follows], up to the parable of the fig tree, about which the farmer said, “I am digging and putting on manure, and if it does not bear, cut it down.”

**42,11,6bf** 39. “But this daughter of Abraham, whom Satan bound.”

**42,11,6bg** 40. Again he cut away “Then you will behold Abraham and Isaac and Jacob and all the prophets within God’s kingdom”; and in its place he put “When you behold all the righteous within God’s kingdom, but yourselves thrown” — though he made it “held” — “outside”, “there will be the weeping and the gnashing of teeth.”

**42,11,6bh** 41. Again he cut away “They will come from east and west and will recline in the kingdom”, and “The last will be first”, and “The Pharisees came up, saying, ‘Leave and be on your way, since Herod wishes to kill you’”, and “He said, ‘Go and tell that fox’”, up to where he said, “It cannot be that a prophet should perish outside Jerusalem”, and “Jerusalem, Jerusalem, who kills the prophets and stones those who are sent”, and “How often have I wished to gather your children as a hen”, and “Your house is left to you”, and “You will not see me until you say, ‘Blessed.’”

**42,11,6bi** 42. Again he cut away the entire parable about the two sons — of him who had received his portion of the estate and squandered it in dissolute living, and of the other.

**42,11,6bj** 43. “The law and the prophets lasted until John, and everyone forces his way into it.”

**42,11,6bk** 44. Concerning the rich man and Lazarus the poor man: that he was carried away by the angels into the bosom of Abraham.

**42,11,6bl** 45. “But now he is comforted here,” the same Lazarus.

**42,11,6bm** 46. Abraham’s words were, “They have Moses and the prophets; let them listen to them, since they will not listen even to one rising from the dead.”

**42,11,6bn** 47. He cut away “Say, ‘We are useless servants; what we were bound to do we have done.’”

**42,11,6bo** 48. When the ten lepers met him.

**42,11,6bp** And he cut away much and made it “He sent them away, saying, ‘Show yourselves to the priests’”, and he put other things in place of others, saying, “There were many lepers in Elisha the prophet’s days, and not one was made clean except Naaman the Syrian.”

**42,11,6bq** 49. “Days will come when you will long to see a single one of the Son of Man’s days.”

**42,11,6br** 50. “Someone said to him, ‘Good teacher, by doing what shall I inherit eternal life?’”

**42,11,6bs** “But he said, ‘Do not call me good.’”

**42,11,6bt** “One is good, God”; he added “the Father,” and instead of “You know the commandments” he says “I know the commandments.”

**42,11,6bu** 51. “And it came to pass, as he drew near to Jericho, that a blind man cried out, ‘Jesus, son of David, have mercy on me.’”

**42,11,6bv** “And when he was healed, he says, ‘Your faith has saved you.’”

**42,11,6bw** 52. He cut away “Taking the twelve, he said, ‘See, we are going up to Jerusalem, and everything that stands written in the prophets about the Son of Man will be brought to completion.’”

**42,11,6bx** “For he will be handed over and killed, and on the third day he will rise”; all this he cut away.

**42,11,6by** 53. He cut away the section concerning the donkey and Bethphage, as well as that concerning the city and the temple, since it had been written, “My house will be called a house of prayer, and you are making it a robbers’ cave.”

**42,11,6bz** 54. “And they were seeking to put their hands on him, and they took fright.”

**42,11,6ca** 55. Again he cut away the matters concerning the vineyard let out to farmers, and “What then is this: ‘The stone which the builders rejected’?”

**42,11,6cb** 56. He cut away “But that the dead are raised, Moses made known at the bush, as he calls the Lord the God of Abraham and of Isaac and of Jacob.”

**42,11,6cc** “And he is God of the living, and not of the dead.”

**42,11,6cd** 57. He did not have these words: “But that the dead are raised Moses also made known, saying, ‘God of Abraham and God of Isaac and God of Jacob,’ God of the living.”

**42,11,6ce** 58. Again he cut away “Not a hair of your head will perish.”

**42,11,6cf** 59. Again he cut away these words, “Then let those in Judaea flee to the mountains,” and what follows, because of what is added in the passage, “until all that is written is fulfilled.”

**42,11,6cg** 60. “He conferred with the officers how he might deliver him to them.”

**42,11,6ch** 61. “And he said to Peter and the rest, ‘Go and prepare, so that we may eat the Passover.’”

**42,11,6ci** 62. “And he reclined, together with the twelve apostles, and he said, ‘I have longed with longing to eat this Passover with you before my suffering.’”

**42,11,6cj** 63. He cut away “For I tell you, I will not eat it from now on until it is fulfilled in the kingdom of God.”

**42,11,6ck** 64. He cut away “When I sent you out, did you lack anything?”

**42,11,6cl** And what follows, because of “And this which is written must be fulfilled: ‘And he was reckoned with the lawless.’”

**42,11,6cm** 65. “He withdrew from them about a stone’s throw, and kneeling down he prayed.”

**42,11,6cn** 66. “And Judas came near to kiss him, and he said.”

**42,11,6co** 67. He cut away Peter’s deed, when he struck and took off the ear of the high priest’s servant.

**42,11,6cp** 68. “Those who held him made sport of him, flogging and beating him and saying, ‘Prophesy: who is the one that hit you?’”

**42,11,6cq** 69. After “We found this man perverting the nation” he appended “and abolishing the law and the prophets”.

**42,11,6cr** 70. An addition after “forbidding to pay tribute”: “and turning away the wives and the children.”

**42,11,6cs** 71. “And when they came to a place called the place of the Skull, they crucified him and divided his garments, and the sun was darkened.”

**42,11,6ct** 72. He cut away “Today you will be with me in paradise.”

**42,11,6cu** 73. “And crying out with a loud voice he breathed his last.”

**42,11,6cv** 74. “And behold, a man named Joseph took down the body, wrapped it in a linen cloth and laid it in a tomb hewn out of rock.”

**42,11,6cw** 75. “And the women returned and rested on the sabbath according to the law.”

**42,11,6cx** 76. “Those in shining clothing said, ‘Why do you seek the living among the dead?’”

**42,11,6cy** “He has been raised; remember everything he said while he was yet among you, that it is necessary for the Son of Man to suffer and to be handed over.”

**42,11,6cz** 77. He cut away the words spoken to Cleopas and the other man when he met them, “You senseless men, slow in believing everything the prophets spoke: was it not necessary for him to suffer these things?”

**42,11,6da** And instead of “in what the prophets spoke” he made it “in what I spoke to you.”

**42,11,6db** But he is refuted, because “when he broke the loaf, their eyes were opened, and they knew him”.

**42,11,6dc** 78. “Why are you troubled?”

**42,11,6dd** “Look at my hands and my feet, because a spirit has no bones, as you see me having.”

**42,11,7** We further append these things too against the aforesaid heresiarch to this treatment of him made [by] us, things which again we found with him, as in an arbitrary selection of the epistles of the apostle Paul, not all of them but some (whose names have been set down by us at the end of the whole treatise, as the Apostolikon is carried with him)

**42,11,8a** and these too mutilated, as usual, by his tampering [...], just as in what was set out first under the name of gospel, remnants indeed of the true gospel [...], if one must speak the truth, but nonetheless, contriving it all dreadfully, he has falsified everything.

**42,11,8b** Of the Epistle to the Romans: with him the fourth, but in the Apostolikon the first.

**42,11,8c** 1 (28). “As many as sinned without law will also perish without law, and as many as sinned within the law will be judged through the law.”

**42,11,8d** “For it is not those who hear the law who are righteous in God’s sight; rather, those who do the law will be justified.”

**42,11,8e** 2 (29). “For circumcision indeed is of benefit if you practise the law; but if you are a transgressor of the law, your circumcision has become uncircumcision.”

**42,11,8f** 3 (30). “Having the form of knowledge and of truth in the law.”

**42,11,8g** 4 (31). “For while we were still weak, Christ, still at the due time, died for the ungodly.”

**42,11,8h** 5 (32). “So then the law is holy, and the commandment is holy and righteous and good.”

**42,11,8i** 6 (33). “So that the law’s just requirement might be fulfilled in us.”

**42,11,8j** 7 (34). “For Christ is the end of the law for righteousness to everyone who believes.”

**42,11,8k** 8 (35). “For he who loves his neighbour has fulfilled the law.”

**42,11,8l** First to the Thessalonians: [with him the fifth], but with us the eighth.

**42,11,8m** Second to the Thessalonians: [with him the sixth], but with us the ninth.

**42,11,8n** Of the Epistle to the Ephesians: [with him] the seventh, but with us the fifth.

**42,11,8o** 1 (36). “Remembering, you who were once the Gentiles, called uncircumcision by the so-called circumcision that is in the flesh and made by hand, that at that time you were apart from Christ, estranged from the commonwealth of Israel and strangers to the covenants of the promise, without hope and godless in the world; but now, in Christ Jesus, you who once were far off have been brought near in his blood.”

**42,11,8p** “For he is our peace, who has made both one,” and what follows.

**42,11,8q** 2 (37). “Therefore he says, ‘Awake, O sleeper, and arise from the dead, [and] Christ will shine upon you.’”

**42,11,8r** 3 (38). “For this reason a man will forsake his father and his mother and will cleave to the wife, and the pair will become one flesh”, differing at “to the wife”.

**42,11,8s** [Of the Epistle] to the Colossians: [with him] the eighth, but with us the seventh.

**42,11,8t** 1 (39). “Let no one, then, judge you in eating or in drinking or in respect of a feast or a new moon or sabbaths, which is a shadow of the things to come.”

**42,11,8u** To Philemon: [with him] the ninth, but with us the thirteenth or even the fourteenth.

**42,11,8v** To the Philippians: [with him] the tenth, but with us the sixth.

**42,11,8w** [Of the Epistle] to the Laodiceans: [with him] the eleventh.

**42,11,8x** 1 ([40]). “One Lord, one faith, one baptism, a single God and Father of all, who is over all and through all and in all.”

**42,11,8y** From the Epistle to the Galatians: [with him] the first, but with us the fourth.

**42,11,8z** 1. “Learn that the righteous shall live by faith.”

**42,11,8aa** “For all who are under law are under a curse; but the one who has done them will live by them.”

**42,11,8ab** 2. “Cursed is everyone who hangs on a tree.”

**42,11,8ac** “But the one from the promise, through the free woman.”

**42,11,8ad** 3. “And I testify again that a man who is circumcised is a debtor to fulfil the whole law.”

**42,11,8ae** [4.] Instead of “A little leaven leavens the whole lump” he made it “adulterates.”

**42,11,8af** [5.] “For the whole law has been fulfilled in you: ‘You shall love your neighbour as yourself.’”

**42,11,8ag** [6.] “Plain to see are the works of the flesh, namely fornication, impurity, licentiousness, idolatry, sorcery, enmities, strife, jealousies, outbursts of anger, rivalries, dissensions, factions, envyings, drunkenness, revellings, of which I tell you beforehand, as I also said before, that those who do such things will not inherit the kingdom of God.”

**42,11,8ah** 7. “And those who are Christ’s have crucified the flesh with its passions and desires.”

**42,11,8ai** 8. “For not even those who are circumcised keep the law themselves.”

**42,11,8aj** [Of the] First to the Corinthians: with Marcion himself the second, and with us the second.

**42,11,8ak** 1 (9). “For it has been written, ‘I will bring to ruin the wisdom of the wise, and the understanding of the intelligent I will set aside.’”

**42,11,8al** 2 (10). “That, as it is written, ‘He who boasts, let him boast in the Lord.’”

**42,11,8am** 3 (11). “Of the rulers of this age, who are being brought to nothing.”

**42,11,8an** 4 (12). “For it is written, ‘He who catches the wise in their craftiness.’”

**42,11,8ao** “And again, ‘The Lord knows the reasonings of men, that they are futile.’”

**42,11,8ap** 5 (13). “For indeed Christ our Passover was sacrificed.”

**42,11,8aq** 6 (14). “Do you not know that he who is joined to a prostitute is one body?”

**42,11,8ar** “For the two, he says, shall become one flesh.”

**42,11,8as** 7 (15). Altered: instead of “in the law” he reads “in Moses’ law”.

**42,11,8at** But before this he says, “Or does not the law also say these things?”

**42,11,8au** 8 (16). “Is it for oxen that God cares?”

**42,11,8av** 9 (17). “For I do not wish you to be ignorant, brethren, that our fathers were beneath the cloud, and all went through the sea, and all ate the same spiritual food, and all drank the same spiritual drink.”

**42,11,8aw** “For they drank from a spiritual rock that followed them; and the rock was Christ.”

**42,11,8ax** “But with most of them he was not well pleased.”

**42,11,8ay** “Now these things became types of us, so that we should not be desirers of evil things, as they also desired.”

**42,11,8az** “Do not become idolaters either, as some of them did, according to what is written: ‘The people sat down to eat and to drink, and they stood up to play.’”

**42,11,8ba** “Neither let us put Christ to the test,” up to where he says, “Now these things happened to them as types, but they were written for us,” and what follows.

**42,11,8bb** 10 (18). “What then do I say?”

**42,11,8bc** “That what is sacrificed in a temple is anything, or that what is sacrificed to idols is anything?”

**42,11,8bd** “But that the things they sacrifice are to demons and not to God.”

**42,11,8be** And Marcion added “what is sacrificed in a temple.”

**42,11,8bf** 11 (19). “A man ought not to wear his hair long, since he is God’s glory and image.”

**42,11,8bg** 12 (20). “But God has blended the body together.”

**42,11,8bh** 13 (21). Erroneously, Marcion added, [after] “But in church I wish to speak five words with my mind,” the words “on account of the law.”

**42,11,8bi** 14 (22). “In the law it is written, ‘In other tongues and with other lips I will speak to this people.’”

**42,11,8bj** 15 (23). “Let the women keep silent in church; for it is not permitted to them to speak; but let them be in subjection, as the law also says.”

**42,11,8bk** 16 (24). Concerning the resurrection of the dead: “Now I make known to you, brethren, the gospel which I preached to you.”

**42,11,8bl** And that “if Christ has not been raised, vain,” and what follows.

**42,11,8bm** “So we preach and so you came to believe”, “that Christ died and was laid in the tomb and has been raised on the third day”.

**42,11,8bn** “But when this mortal puts on immortality, then the word that stands written will come about: ‘Death has been swallowed up in victory.’”

**42,11,8bo** Of the Second to the Corinthians: both with him and with us the third.

**42,11,8bp** 1 (25). “For as many as are the promises of God, in him is the Yes; therefore through him also is the Amen to God.”

**42,11,8bq** 2 (26). “For we do not preach ourselves, but Christ Jesus as Lord, and ourselves as your servants through Jesus, because the God who said, ‘From darkness light will shine.’”

**42,11,8br** 3 (27). “And having the same spirit of faith, we too believe, and therefore we also speak.”

**42,11,8bs** But he cut out “according to what is written.”

**42,11,9** This is the falsified compilation of Marcion, which has the form and outline of the Gospel according to Luke, and of Paul the apostle

**42,11,10** not the whole, not of all his epistles, but only of that to the Romans and that to the Ephesians and [that] to the Colossians and that to the Laodiceans and that to the Galatians and of the First and Second to the Corinthians and of the First and Second to the Thessalonians and of that to Philemon and [that] to the Philippians;

**42,11,11** and of the First and Second to Timothy and [that] to Titus and that to the Hebrews [...] of those that are carried with him, as not being complete, but as in counterfeit form.

**42,11,12** And this same compilation [...] tampered with on every side, and having in certain expressions an artificially made addition, not for any benefit but for inferior and harmful strange expressions against the sound faith, out of the thunderstruck mind of this same brute.

**42,11,13** These things have been laboured over and searched out by us from the scripture already mentioned that he has, both of the apostle and of Luke’s gospel, [so that] everyone who attempts to contradict his error may be able to know that the altered words have been inserted by way of tampering,

**42,11,14** while whatever is not carried in its proper places has been stolen away by his audacity; for these alone, the brutish fellow thought, were opposed to his fabricated way of thinking.

**42,11,15** And the third [...] of our studious care is to gather all that is found, both with him and with us, to be in agreement and to bear the sense of the Saviour’s coming in the flesh, and the testimony that the New Testament is in harmony with the Old, and the confession of the Son of God in the gospel, who confesses God as maker of heaven and earth, and as having himself spoken in the law and in the prophets, and that this is his own Father.

**42,11,16** And this is the concise compilation of notes on our aforesaid subject, set out in scholia from Marcion’s copies word for word, written by us as a groundwork.

**42,11,17a** But so that the things in it that are hard to understand may not remain obscure and unknown to some, I shall once again go through the numbers of the headings in order — of the first passage, I mean, and the second and the third — and explain for what reason each saying was gathered up and has been transferred here.

**42,11,17b** And I shall begin to speak thus:

**42,11,17c** Scholion [1] from the gospel that Marcion has.

**42,11,17d** “Go, show yourself to the priest and make an offering for your cleansing, just as Moses ordered”; “so that this may be a testimony to you”, in place of what the Saviour said, “for a testimony to them”.

**42,11,17e** Refutation 1. How could the Lord, who according to you holds his teaching against the law and against the God of the law, say to those healed by him — to the leper, I mean — “Go, show yourself to the priest”?

**42,11,17f** For in saying “priest” he does not set aside the priesthood of the law; and “make an offering for your cleansing” — even if you cut away “the gift,” it will be evident from “make an offering” that he is speaking of a gift — “for your cleansing, as Moses commanded”; for if he advises that the command of Moses be carried out, he neither sets aside nor blasphemes the God of the law, but confesses that both he himself and his Father, God, gave the law to Moses.

**42,11,17g** But you have distorted the saying, Marcion: instead of saying “for a testimony to them,” you say “a testimony to you.”

**42,11,17h** And this is a plain lie you have told against your own head.

**42,11,17i** For if he said “a testimony to you”, he was making himself a witness that “I did not come to abolish the law or the prophets, but rather to fulfil”.

**42,11,17j** Scholion 2. “But that you may know that the Son of Man has authority to forgive sins on earth.”

**42,11,17k** Refutation 2. If, then, the Only-begotten names himself Son of Man, he is not denying that he became man, and your refrain that he appeared in mere semblance is sung in vain.

**42,11,17l** And if he holds authority upon the earth, then the earth is no stranger to his own works and to his own Father.

**42,11,17m** Scholion 3. “The Son of Man is Lord even over the sabbath.”

**42,11,17n** Refutation 3. At once, in one and the same place, the Saviour confesses himself in his teaching to be two things, Son of Man as well as Lord of the sabbath, lest the sabbath be thought foreign to his own making [...], even if at [the] last he is called Son of Man from his coming in the flesh.

**42,11,17o** Scholion 4. “Judas Iscariot, who became a traitor.”

**42,11,17p** And in place of “he went down with them” he reads “he went down among them”.

**42,11,17q** Refutation 4. Judas Iscariot, “who became a traitor.”

**42,11,17r** Of whom, tell me.

**42,11,17s** Surely of him who was arrested, yes, and was crucified and suffered many things.

**42,11,17t** How then is he who, by your account, Marcion, does not come under touch arrested and crucified?

**42,11,17u** For you say that he is a semblance.

**42,11,17v** But your supposition will be refuted by the fact that Judas is written down as a traitor.

**42,11,17w** For he betrayed and handed over his own Master into human hands.

**42,11,17x** And it profited you nothing to say “he went down among them” in place of “with them”.

**42,11,17y** For you cannot define as an illusion the one who, even with you and against your will, is afterwards shown coming under touch.

**42,11,17z** Scholion 5. “And all the crowd sought to touch him.”

**42,11,17aa** “And he, lifting up his eyes,” and what follows.

**42,11,17ab** Refutation 5. Again, how could the crowd touch one who was not tangible?

**42,11,17ac** And what sort of eyes did he lift up to heaven, who was not fitted together out of flesh?

**42,11,17ad** But this was so as to show that the mediator of God and men is a man, Christ Jesus, who has both: the flesh from men, and from God the Father the invisible essence.

**42,11,17ae** Scholion 6. “In the same way your fathers did to the prophets.”

**42,11,17af** Refutation 6. If he makes mention of prophets, he is not disowning prophets; if he exacts vengeance for the prophets’ slaughter and reproaches those who slew and hounded them, he is no stranger to the prophets, but is their God, the one who vouches for them.

**42,11,17ag** Scholion 7. “But I say to you, not even in Israel have I found such faith.”

**42,11,17ah** Refutation 7. If not even in Israel did he find such faith as in the centurion who came from the Gentiles, then he does not disparage the faith of Israel.

**42,11,17ai** For if it belonged to a foreign god and not to his own Father himself, he would not have made it the subject of praise.

**42,11,17aj** Scholion 8. “Blessed is he who is not offended in me” has been altered; for he had it as referring to John.

**42,11,17ak** Refutation 8. Whether he has it as referring to John or to the Saviour himself, he pronounces blessed those who are not offended, either in him or in John, so that they may not invent for themselves what they do not hear from him.

**42,11,17al** But it holds a deeper meaning, on account of which the Saviour in fact spoke: so that no one should think John, whom he had ranked as the greater among those born of women, greater even than the Saviour himself, because he too was born of a woman, he takes precautions and says, “And blessed is he who is not offended in me.”

**42,11,17am** Hence he says, “But he who is lesser in the kingdom is greater than he.”

**42,11,17an** For in time, as regards his birth from the flesh, the Saviour was lesser than he by a span of six months, but greater in the kingdom, plainly as his God.

**42,11,17ao** For it was not to speak anything in secret that the Only-begotten came, nor to falsify any part of his own proclamation.

**42,11,17ap** For he declares, “Not in secret have I spoken, but with boldness.”

**42,11,17aq** For he is truth itself, according to his words, “I am the way and the truth.”

**42,11,17ar** Neither, then, does the way contain error, nor does the truth, hiding itself, speak falsehood.

**42,11,17as** Scholion 9. “It is he about whom it has been written, ‘See, I am sending my messenger before your face.’”

**42,11,17at** Refutation 9. If the only-begotten Son of God recognises John and knows him beforehand, and in foreknowing him points out to those who wish to know the truth that this is he about whom it has been written, “I am sending my messenger before your face”, then he who wrote and said, “I am sending my messenger before your face” — the eternal God, who spoke in the prophets and in the law — was no stranger to his own Son, Jesus Christ.

**42,11,17au** For it is his own messenger that he sends ahead of his face, ahead of the countenance of the Son honoured by the Father.

**42,11,17av** For he would not have sent his own messenger to serve a stranger, to whom, according to your account, Marcion, he was in fact opposed.

**42,11,17aw** Scholion 10. “And he went into the house of the Pharisee and reclined at table.”

**42,11,17ax** “And the woman, the sinner, standing behind at his feet, wet the feet with her tears, and anointed and kissed them.”

**42,11,17ay** Refutation 10. “He went in” shows a body; for it shows a house and the measure of a body.

**42,11,17az** And reclining belongs to no one but one [having] a body of bulk that lies down; and as for the woman’s wetting the feet with her tears, these were not the feet of an illusion, nor of a semblance; for she anointed and wet and kissed them, perceiving the touch of the body.

**42,11,17ba** Scholion 11. And again: “This woman wet my feet with her tears, and anointed and kissed them.”

**42,11,17bb** Refutation 11. So that you may not think, Marcion, that it is only supposed among men that the sinful woman wet the Saviour’s feet and anointed and kissed them, the Saviour himself confirms it, teaching that these things happened not in semblance but in truth, to the refutation of the Pharisee and of you, Marcion, and of those who think as you do, insisting and saying, “This woman anointed my feet and kissed them.”

**42,11,17bc** And what sort of feet, if not those consisting of flesh and bones and the rest?

**42,11,17bd** Scholion 12. He lacked “his mother and his brothers”, having only “your mother and your brothers”.

**42,11,17be** Refutation 12. Even if you cut away the saying of the gospel further up, Marcion, so as to make the evangelist not agree with the words spoken by certain people, “Your mother and your brothers,” you cannot get past the truth.

**42,11,17bf** For why did he not name many mothers?

**42,11,17bg** Why did he not speak of many homelands?

**42,11,17bh** How many people say how many things about Homer?

**42,11,17bi** Some say he was an Egyptian, others a Chian, others a Colophonian, others a Phrygian, others a Smyrnaean, of Meles and Critheïs; Aristarchus and his school declared him an Athenian; others say he was a Lydian, of Maeon, and others a Cypriot of Propodias, a district in the territory around Salamis — and that though he was a man.

**42,11,17bj** And on account of his having been born in many homelands, it has driven many to differing accounts.

**42,11,17bk** But here, when speaking of God and of Christ, they did not suppose many mothers, but the one who truly bore him, and not many brothers, but the sons of Joseph by what was in fact another wife of his; and you are not able to arm yourself against the truth.

**42,11,17bl** And do not let the saying mislead you which the Lord spoke, “Who are my mother and my brothers?”

**42,11,17bm** For he did not say this in denial of his mother, but to rebut the untimeliness of the speaker, when so great a crowd was standing round, and his saving teaching was being poured out, and he himself was occupied with the healings and the proclamation; for it seemed a distraction that the speaker should interrupt him by saying, “Behold, your mother and your brothers.”

**42,11,17bn** And had he not received it with joy (not as though he did not know, before he heard it, that they had come, but knowing beforehand that they were standing outside), he would then have overturned the speaker’s untimely words with a rebuke, as he also once said to Peter, “Get away from me, Satan, for you do not think the things of God, but the things of men.”

**42,11,17bo** Scholion 13. “While they were sailing he fell asleep; and, having been roused, he reproved the wind and the sea.”

**42,11,17bp** Refutation 13. Who fell asleep, tell me.

**42,11,17bq** You will not dare to say it of the Godhead; and should you say it, you accursed man, you will blaspheme against your own head.

**42,11,17br** But it is plain to everyone that he who truly became man, needing sleep because of what is bodily, fell asleep.

**42,11,17bs** For it was no semblance that those who roused him beheld, but a true incarnation.

**42,11,17bt** Indeed, by moving him with their hands and calling out, they testify that they woke him.

**42,11,17bu** “For having arisen,” it says, the God who slept, bearing flesh, who came down from heaven and put on flesh for our sake, “was roused” as a man, but “reproved” the sea as God, and made [a calm].

**42,11,17bv** Scholion 14. “Now it happened, while they were on their way, that the crowds were pressing upon him.”

**42,11,17bw** “And a woman who touched him was cured of her bleeding; and the Lord said, ‘Who was it that touched me?’”

**42,11,17bx** And again: “Someone touched me.”

**42,11,17by** “For I perceived power going out from me.”

**42,11,17bz** Refutation 14. “While they were on their way”, and he did not say “while he was on his way”, so as not to portray him otherwise than in keeping with the progress of those travelling on the road.

**42,11,17ca** And as for “the crowds were pressing upon him”: the crowds could not press upon a spirit.

**42,11,17cb** And the woman who touched him and was healed did not touch air, but what was humanly tangible.

**42,11,17cc** For to show that the touching of his body by the woman had not taken place in mere semblance, he teaches, saying, “Who was it that touched me?”

**42,11,17cd** “For I perceived power going out from me.”

**42,11,17ce** Scholion 15. “Looking up to the heavens, he blessed them.”

**42,11,17cf** Refutation 15. If he looked up to the heavens and blessed them, it was not in mere semblance that he possessed the forms of his eyes and of his other members.

**42,11,17cg** Scholion 16. “Saying, ‘It is necessary for the Son of Man to suffer much, and to be put to death, and after three days to be raised.’”

**42,11,17ch** Refutation 16. If the only-begotten Son of God confesses himself Son of Man and [...] both to suffer and to be killed, here is an axe against you, cutting out your whole root, Marcion, you who were born of thorns, waterless cloud, tree fruitless and autumnal.

**42,11,17ci** For again he says, “and after three days to be raised”.

**42,11,17cj** And what was raised, if not that very thing which suffered and was laid in the tomb?

**42,11,17ck** But a semblance, or a wind, or a spirit, or an illusion would not admit of funeral rites and burial and resurrection.

**42,11,17cl** Scholion 17. “And behold, two men were talking with him, Elijah and Moses, in glory.”

**42,11,17cm** Refutation 17. Perhaps, I think, through these words the sickle in the holy Zechariah has been figured against you, Marcion, cutting out all your falsehood devised against the law and the prophets.

**42,11,17cn** For since you were going to set aside the law and the prophets, saying that they were strangers to the Saviour and to his glory and to his inspired teaching, he brought both along with himself in his own glory and displayed them to his disciples, and the disciples to us and to the world, that is, to every man who wishes to live, so that by the first, as with an axe, he might cut your roots, and by the second, as with the sickle of the word of truth, he might cut off your branches, which produce hemlock and what is deadly to men, a slimy sap of blasphemy.

**42,11,17co** For if Moses, who was entrusted with the law by Christ of old, was a stranger, and the prophets were strangers, he would not have revealed them with him in his own glory.

**42,11,17cp** For observe the marvel: it was not at the tomb that he showed these men, nor beside the cross; but when he was revealing to us a portion of his glory as a pledge, then he brought the saints — Moses, I mean, and Elijah — with him, so as to show these same men to be joint heirs of his kingdom.

**42,11,17cq** Scholion 18. “A voice out of the cloud: ‘This is my beloved Son.’”

**42,11,17cr** Refutation 18. It is plain to everyone that the cloud is not in the highest region, nor is it above heaven, but within the creation that surrounds us, whence the voice was borne to the Saviour.

**42,11,17cs** If then the Father speaks even in a cloud, pointing out his Son to the disciples, the Creator is not another, but the same, he who bore witness to his own Son even through a cloud, and not one who rules only the things above heaven, as you yourself maintain.

**42,11,17ct** Scholion 19. “I begged your disciples.”

**42,11,17cu** And next to “they were not able to cast it out” he had “and to them: ‘You faithless generation, how long am I to put up with you?’”

**42,11,17cv** Refutation 19. “How long” signifies the time of the coming in the flesh, and so does “you faithless generation”, since the prophets had worked divine signs in his name and had believed, as Elijah is found doing, and Elisha, and the others.

**42,11,17cw** Scholion 20. “For the Son of Man is about to be delivered into the hands of men.”

**42,11,17cx** Refutation 20. In one who is Son of Man and is to be delivered into the hands of men, what is indicated is not a semblance nor an illusion, but the sight of a body and of members.

**42,11,17cy** Scholion 21. “Have you not read even this, what David did?”

**42,11,17cz** “He went into the house of God.”

**42,11,17da** Refutation 21. If what he calls the house of God is the house of the tabernacle set up by Moses, he does not set aside the law, nor the God who spoke in the law.

**42,11,17db** For he calls him God who is his Father, [...] or the Only-begotten himself.

**42,11,17dc** For the Trinity, Father and Son and Holy Spirit, is accustomed to work in the law and in the prophets and in the gospels and in the apostles.

**42,11,17dd** Scholion 22. “I thank you, Lord of heaven.”

**42,11,17de** But he did not have “and of earth,” nor did he have “Father.”

**42,11,17df** But he is refuted; for further on he had “Yes, Father.”

**42,11,17dg** Refutation 22. It is to the Lord of heaven that he gives thanks, even if you remove “[and] of earth”, and even if you cut away “Father”, Marcion, so as not to show Christ calling the Creator his Father.

**42,11,17dh** For the members of the truth remain alive.

**42,11,17di** For just as you have left, as in a remnant, through forgetfulness, Marcion, the words “Yes, Father,” [...].

**42,11,17dj** It has been shown, then, beyond all doubt that Christ gives thanks to his own Father and names him Lord of heaven.

**42,11,17dk** And great is your derangement, which does not perceive the path of the truth.

**42,11,17dl** Scholion 23. He said to the lawyer, “What stands written in the law?”

**42,11,17dm** And answering, after the lawyer’s answer, he said, “You have spoken rightly.”

**42,11,17dn** “Do this, and you will live.”

**42,11,17do** Refutation 23. Being truth, the Son of God deceived none of those who asked about life; for he came for the sake of the life of men.

**42,11,17dp** Since, then, he cares for life, and shows that the law is life to the one who keeps it, and told the one who answered in accordance with the law that his words were right, and “Do so, and you will live”, who would be so thunderstruck as to obey Marcion, who blasphemes against the God who has graciously bestowed on mankind the law as well as the grace of the gospel, and to be carried off along with one who holds nothing of his teaching from the law, nor from the Holy Spirit?

**42,11,17dq** Scholion 24. And he said, “Which of you will have a friend, and will go to him at midnight asking for three loaves?”

**42,11,17dr** And then, “Ask, and it will be given.”

**42,11,17ds** “For which father among you will a son ask for a fish, and he will give him a serpent instead of a fish, or a scorpion instead of an egg?”

**42,11,17dt** “If you then, being evil, know good gifts, how much more the Father?”

**42,11,17du** Refutation 24. The self-chosen religiosity of the deceiver’s way of life has been refuted by this saying.

**42,11,17dv** For with him the way of life is not for the sake of self-control, nor for a good reward and the hope of the contest, but comes from impiety and the perversity of an evil supposition.

**42,11,17dw** For this man teaches people not to partake of things that have a soul, saying that those who partake of meats are liable to the judgement, as eating souls.

**42,11,17dx** But the whole thing is foolish.

**42,11,17dy** For the meat is not the soul, but the soul is in the meat; nor do we hold the soul present in animals to be a soul like the precious soul of men, but a soul for the living creature merely to live.

**42,11,17dz** But this pitiable man, together with those who think in the same way, supposes that the same soul exists in men and in animals.

**42,11,17ea** For this is vainly supposed among many of the heresies that have gone astray.

**42,11,17eb** For Valentinus too, and Colorbasus, and all the Gnostics, and the Manichaeans, say that there is a transmigration of souls and re-embodiments of the soul of men who are in ignorance, according, as they themselves say, to a certain mythmaking; they say that this soul returns and is re-embodied in each of the animals, until it attains knowledge, and so, cleansed and released, passes over into the heavenly places.

**42,11,17ec** And first, the whole vain construction of his myth has been refuted.

**42,11,17ed** For the precise truth of these matters can be known by none other than our Lord Jesus Christ, who came on account of the sheep that had gone astray, that is, for the sake of the souls of men; who, caring for these, healed in body and in soul, [as] being master of both body and soul, and the giver of the life here and of the life to come; raising from the dead those who had died — I mean Lazarus, and the son of the widow, and the synagogue ruler’s daughter — not bringing them into evil, as they lay it down as doctrine that the body is a prison, but doing them good, and knowing that both the sojourn here in the flesh has been appointed by him and the resurrection of flesh and soul that is to come.

**42,11,17ee** And again, if he knew the soul in animals and that in men to be one and the same, and he came to bring about the salvation of the soul, he ought not, when cleansing one man possessed by demons (the one, I mean, coming out from the tombs), to have ordered the demons to go off and kill two thousand swine, if the souls of men and of swine were equal.

**42,11,17ef** For how, caring for one, would he have brought about the destruction of two thousand?

**42,11,17eg** But if again you twist like a serpent, contriving and saying that he released them from their bodies so that they might ascend, then Lazarus, once released from the body, ought not to have returned to the body again, and rather he ought to have released the demoniac himself also from the bond of the body.

**42,11,17eh** But he did not do so; rather, he took forethought in this way, knowing what is beneficial for the body.

**42,11,17ei** Your argument about the soul has collapsed, Marcion, and that of those who start out from you and from the other heresies.

**42,11,17ej** And I shall speak again about your feigned way of life, because you say that it is evil and unlawful to partake of flesh.

**42,11,17ek** But the Saviour refutes you, knowing more than you and teaching what is better, from such a saying as this; for he says, “Whom will the son ask for a fish — will he give him a serpent, or a scorpion instead of an egg?”

**42,11,17el** And later he says, “If, then, you who are evil know how to give your children good gifts, how much more your heavenly Father?”

**42,11,17em** If then he has called fish and egg good gifts, what is given by God and partaken of with thanksgiving is not evil, and your perversity has been refuted on every side.

**42,11,17en** Scholion 25. The passage about Jonah the prophet has been cut away.

**42,11,17eo** For his text read, “This generation: a sign will not be given to it.”

**42,11,17ep** But he had nothing about Nineveh and the queen of the south and Solomon.

**42,11,17eq** Refutation 25. Even in the very things you think you are cutting away, Marcion, you cannot escape the notice of the truth.

**42,11,17er** For even if you take away [the passage] about Jonah the prophet, which signifies the Saviour’s dispensation, and remove as well the passage concerning the southern queen and Solomon, and the saving account of Nineveh and the preaching of Jonah, the Saviour’s word set before us itself refutes [you].

**42,11,17es** For he says, “This generation asks for a sign, and no sign shall be given to it,” since the generations before this one had been granted signs from God out of heaven: as Elijah performed a sign through the fire that came down from heaven and took the sacrifice, and Moses divides the sea, and strikes the rock and pours out water, and brings manna from heaven, and Joshua the son of Nun makes the sun and the moon stand still.

**42,11,17et** And in every way, even if the deceiver hides what is written, he will not harm the truth in the least; rather, he estranges himself from the truth.

**42,11,17eu** Scholion 26. Instead of “you pass over the judgement of God” he had “you pass over the calling of God.”

**42,11,17ev** Refutation 26. Where are you not refuted, and where is the testimony [not] gathered against you?

**42,11,17ew** For the first things agree with those that come after, and your tampering is refuted.

**42,11,17ex** For if he says, “You cling to the traditions of your elders, and you neglect the mercy and the judgement of God”, learn from what period he accuses them of this practice, and when the elders’ tradition came into being among them, and you will find that Adda’s came into being after the return from Babylon, that of Akiba even prior to the captivities in Babylon, and the tradition of Asamonaeus’ sons in the times of Alexander and Antiochus, a hundred and ninety years before the sojourn of Christ.

**42,11,17ey** So then, even from that time judgement was through the law and mercy through the prophets, and your charlatan’s argument collapses on every side.

**42,11,17ez** Scholion 27. “Woe to you, because you build the prophets’ tombs and your fathers put them to death.”

**42,11,17fa** Refutation 27. If he takes thought for the prophets, reproaching those who killed them, the prophets were not strangers to him, but servants, sent ahead by him and by the Father and by the Holy Spirit as those who prepared beforehand for his coming in the flesh; and they also bore witness to the New Testament: Moses saying, “The Lord God will raise up for you a prophet from among your brethren, like me”, and before him Jacob saying, “From a shoot, my son Judah, you went up; you lay down and slept; a ruler shall not fail from Judah”, and a little after, “until there comes he for whom it is laid up, and he is what the nations await, and in him the nations will hope”; and Isaiah, “Behold, the virgin shall conceive in the womb”, and Jeremiah, “He is a man as well, and who will know him?”, and Micah, “And you, Bethlehem”, and after other words, “From you shall come forth for me a ruler”, and what follows, and Malachi, “Suddenly the Lord will come to the temple”, and David, “The Lord said to my Lord, Sit at my right hand”, and what follows.

**42,11,17fb** And there is much to say, since the Saviour himself also says, “If you believed Moses, you would believe me also.”

**42,11,17fc** “For he wrote about me.”

**42,11,17fd** Scholion 28. He did not have “For this reason the wisdom of God said, ‘I send prophets to them,’” nor the words about the blood of Zacharias and Abel and the prophets, that it will be required of this generation.

**42,11,17fe** Refutation 28. In this too there is great shame for you, Marcion, since the text of the truth is preserved, and the excision of your thefts is discovered from the copy of the Gospel according to Luke, the places are found, and the things removed by you are exposed.

**42,11,17ff** Scholion 29. “I say to my friends, ‘Do not be afraid of those who kill the body, but fear him who after killing has authority to cast into Gehenna.’”

**42,11,17fg** But he lacked “Are five sparrows not sold for two pennies, while not one of them stands forgotten in the sight of God?”

**42,11,17fh** Refutation 29. The words “I say to my friends, ‘Do not be afraid of those who kill the body, but fear him who has authority, after killing the body, to cast the soul into Gehenna’” compel you, Marcion, to confess the rest of the parable as well.

**42,11,17fi** For nothing happens without God, even if you remove the passage about the sparrows.

**42,11,17fj** Make your defence, then, Marcion, concerning what you have left in the passage, and answer us what you have in mind about the one who has authority.

**42,11,17fk** For should you claim that it is Christ’s Father, the one you call the good God, then, though your distinction is a bad one, you have nevertheless granted, since he holds the authority, that he is a judge and assigns to each according to his deserts.

**42,11,17fl** But if you do not mean him, but the Creator, who is celebrated by you as by nature a judge, tell me, who gave him the authority?

**42,11,17fm** For if he has it of himself, then he is great and has authority; and if he has authority to judge, he has it also to save.

**42,11,17fn** For he who can judge can also acquit.

**42,11,17fo** And put another way: if, when the judge throws the souls into Gehenna, the good God, who cares in every way for these same souls, does not rescue them, how could he be good?

**42,11,17fp** For either it is because the other is stronger than he and he cannot rescue them from his hand, or because he can but does not wish to; and then where is the goodness?

**42,11,17fq** But if, since he created, he also has authority to judge, how does the God above, about whom you tell your myth, save some piecemeal?

**42,11,17fr** For if he saves by taking from what belongs to others, he is grasping, coveting what is another’s; and if you say that it is not grasping because the deed is done for good and for salvation, you make him a respecter of persons, who does good not equally to all, but piecemeal.

**42,11,17fs** Scholion 30. Instead of “will confess before the angels of God” he says “before God.”

**42,11,17ft** Refutation 30. He is refuted on every side as one who does not walk according to God, even if he alters the truth in the briefest word.

**42,11,17fu** For he who dares to alter anything of what was written from the beginning does not stand on the road of truth.

**42,11,17fv** Scholion 31. He does not have “God clothes the grass.”

**42,11,17fw** Refutation 31. Even if you do not leave what is written as it stood when spoken by the Saviour, the passages are nevertheless kept safe in the holy church’s gospel, though you deny the God who made all things and by his word cares for all things, even to the grass, and who is confessed by the Saviour.

**42,11,17fx** Scholion 32. “But your Father knows that you need these things,” the things of the flesh, that is.

**42,11,17fy** Refutation 32. The Father knows that the disciples have need of the necessities of the flesh, and he takes forethought for such things.
