**42,11,17fz** And he takes forethought not in another age but here, making provision for his own servants not among things belonging to others but among the things created by him.

**42,11,17ga** Scholion 33. “But seek God’s kingdom, and all these things will be added to you.”

**42,11,17gb** Refutation 33. If we are fed from what belongs to another, and the God of the kingdom of heaven is another, how will the saying still agree?

**42,11,17gc** For either the things here are his and the kingdom is his — which is why he adds all the things here, since they are his, on account of the toil of longing for his kingdom — or the kingdom and the age there are his, but the things here belong to the Creator, and the Creator consents to the kingdom of the one above, helping those who seek the righteousness of the one above and his kingdom.

**42,11,17gd** But since there is one good pleasure, and not a discordant one, there are no longer two principles, or three; for in truth God is one, who made all things, and made them well and not in opposition.

**42,11,17ge** And it is ours to sin and to go astray, in willing and in not willing.

**42,11,17gf** Scholion 34. Instead of “your Father” he had “the Father.”

**42,11,17gg** Refutation 34. In this too you will do us no harm, but will confirm it further.

**42,11,17gh** For you have acknowledged that the Saviour said that his own Father takes thought for the things here.

**42,11,17gi** Scholion 35. In place of “in the second or third watch” his text read “in the evening watch”.

**42,11,17gj** Refutation 35. The brutish fellow has been refuted, having foolishly twisted the divine words to his own supposition.

**42,11,17gk** For the watches are not kept by day but by night, and they have the first advance of their extent from evening, and not from dawn to evening, as this man is caught out in his tampering.

**42,11,17gl** Scholion 36. “The master of that servant will come and cut him in two, and will set his portion with the unbelievers.”

**42,11,17gm** Refutation 36. Who is the one who cuts the servant in two?

**42,11,17gn** Tell me.

**42,11,17go** If it is the Creator and judge, whom you call God, who is to do this, then the faithful are his; for in rebuking the servant who does not act well he sets that servant’s portion with the unbelievers.

**42,11,17gp** But if it is the Father of Christ, or Christ himself, who is to do this, you are plainly keeping by you a testimony that tells against you.

**42,11,17gq** For by admitting that it is Christ, or else his Father, who is going to do this, you have confessed beyond doubt that the same is judge and the same is good, and that he is one, [the one] who takes thought for the things here and the things there.

**42,11,17gr** Scholion 37. “Lest he drag you to the judge, and the judge hand you over to the bailiff.”

**42,11,17gs** Refutation 37. You say that the judge is the Creator, and the bailiff each of his angels, who are to exact from sinners what they have done.

**42,11,17gt** And what are these but the faults and sins, which Jesus too abhors, and which you too say that you forbid?

**42,11,17gu** If then what the good God abhors, the judge and Creator abhors as well, he is proved to be one and the same by his work and by the one good pleasure.

**42,11,17gv** Scholion 38. It had been cut away from “There came some who told him about the Galileans, whose blood Pilate had mixed with their sacrifices,” up to where he speaks of the eighteen in Siloam who died in the tower, and “Unless you repent,” and [what follows], up to the parable of the fig tree, about which the farmer said, “I am digging and putting on manure, and if it does not bear, cut it down.”

**42,11,17gw** Refutation 38. The plunderer made away with all these things, hiding the truth from himself, because the Lord had agreed with Pilate, who judged such men rightly, and because those in Siloam died deservedly, being sinners and punished thus by God.

**42,11,17gx** But when some people tamper with imperial ordinances, the copies produced from the archives, being securely kept, refute the senseless.

**42,11,17gy** So too the gospel, produced from the royal house, that is, from the holy church of God, refutes the mice that destroy fine garments.

**42,11,17gz** Scholion 39. “But this daughter of Abraham, whom Satan bound.”

**42,11,17ha** Refutation 39. If the Lord, when he came, cares for the daughter of Abraham, Abraham is not a stranger to him.

**42,11,17hb** For he confesses that Abraham was well-pleasing to him, by showing compassion to his daughter.

**42,11,17hc** Scholion 40. Again he cut away “Then you will behold Abraham and Isaac and Jacob and all the prophets within God’s kingdom”; and in its place he put “When you behold all the righteous within God’s kingdom, but yourselves thrown” — though he made it “held” — “outside”, “there will be the weeping and the gnashing of teeth.”

**42,11,17hd** Refutation 40. How the traces of the truth appear!

**42,11,17he** For no one can hide a road.

**42,11,17hf** For it is possible to lead people astray from it and to conceal it from the ignorant, but from those who have walked it, that is impossible.

**42,11,17hg** For he cannot obliterate the ground where the road was.

**42,11,17hh** And even if he obscures it, yet since the place of the road remains, the one who tampered with the road is refuted by those who know it.

**42,11,17hi** See, then, the traces of the sequence.

**42,11,17hj** For to whom did he speak this word, if not to the Jews?

**42,11,17hk** And if to the Jews, he has already shown them to be within and being cast out from the righteous.

**42,11,17hl** And who might [these] be, if not their fathers, Abraham and Isaac and Jacob and the prophets?

**42,11,17hm** For he did not say, “You will see righteous men going in, while you do not go in”; rather, “You will see the righteous in the kingdom, but yourselves cast out.”

**42,11,17hn** And concerning “cast out” he declared it as something still to come, but he showed that the righteous already exist, not strangers to these either according to the flesh or according to the calling, but called together with them, and already justified before his coming in the flesh.

**42,11,17ho** And even if those remaining outside [...], as the fathers are within — yet whence comes the gnashing of teeth at the judgement, you fool, unless a resurrection of bodies takes place?

**42,11,17hp** Scholion 41. Again he cut away “They will come from east and west and will recline in the kingdom”, and “The last will be first”, and “The Pharisees came up, saying, ‘Leave and be on your way, since Herod wishes to kill you’”, and “He said, ‘Go and tell that fox’”, up to where he said, “It cannot be that a prophet should perish outside Jerusalem”, and “Jerusalem, Jerusalem, who kills the prophets and stones those who are sent”, and “How often have I wished to gather your children as a hen”, and “Your house is left to you”, and “You will not see me until you say, ‘Blessed.’”

**42,11,17hq** Refutation 41. See this great audacity: how great a removal does he make from the gospel?

**42,11,17hr** It is as if someone were to take a living creature and cut away half of its body, and with the half try to persuade those who do not know, saying that the creature is like this and that nothing has been taken from it.

**42,11,17hs** Scholion 42. Again he cut away the entire parable about the two sons — of him who had received his portion of the estate and squandered it in dissolute living, and of the other.

**42,11,17ht** Refutation 42. What follows in his tampering will differ in nothing from what he dared before; but he brings the loss upon himself, while the truth that is according to God abides.

**42,11,17hu** Scholion 43. “The law and the prophets lasted until John, and everyone forces his way into it.”

**42,11,17hv** Refutation 43. If he sets the law in its place and names the prophets, and does not declare the law to be lawlessness nor give the prophets the name of false prophets, it is openly admitted that the Saviour has borne witness to the prophets, and it has been shown that they prophesied about him.

**42,11,17hw** Scholion 44. Concerning the rich man and Lazarus the poor man: that he was carried away by the angels into the bosom of Abraham.

**42,11,17hx** Refutation 44. Behold, Abraham has been enrolled by the Lord among the living and the blessed and in the inheritance of rest, and Lazarus has been deemed worthy of his bosom.

**42,11,17hy** No longer, then, Marcion, blaspheme Abraham, who recognised his own Master and said to him, “Lord, who judges all the earth.”

**42,11,17hz** For behold, he was attested by the Lord himself to be righteous, and no stranger to the life praised by the Saviour.

**42,11,17ia** Scholion 45. “But now he is comforted here,” the same Lazarus.

**42,11,17ib** Refutation 45. If Lazarus finds comfort in Abraham’s bosom, then Abraham is not outside the comfort of life.

**42,11,17ic** Scholion 46. Abraham’s words were, “They have Moses and the prophets; let them listen to them, since they will not listen even to one rising from the dead.”

**42,11,17id** Refutation 46. It is not as one still in the world, nor as one deceived, that Abraham bears witness to the law of Moses and to the prophets, nor as one who did not know what would come of them, but after he has come to experience the rest that is there.

**42,11,17ie** For after his death he is attested by the Saviour in the parable as having obtained salvation by the doctrines of the law and of the prophets, and that though he did these things before the law.

**42,11,17if** And likewise it is attested that those who afterwards kept the law and heeded the prophets are within his bosom and go away with him into life; of whom Lazarus was one, who through the law and the prophets was deemed worthy of the life-giving bosom of Abraham’s blessedness.

**42,11,17ig** Scholion 47. He cut away “Say, ‘We are useless servants; what we were bound to do we have done.’”

**42,11,17ih** Refutation 47. Even the safeguard contained in the Lord’s teaching he refuses to receive.

**42,11,17ii** For in safeguarding his own disciples, so that they might not lose the reward of their work through pride, he admonished them to be humble-minded.

**42,11,17ij** But this man does not accept it; for he has been lifted up in everything by conceit, and not by truth.

**42,11,17ik** Scholion 48. When the ten lepers met him.

**42,11,17il** And he cut away much and made it “He sent them away, saying, ‘Show yourselves to the priests’”, and he put other things in place of others, saying, “There were many lepers in Elisha the prophet’s days, and not one was made clean except Naaman the Syrian.”

**42,11,17im** Refutation 48. In this place too the Lord gives Elisha the name of prophet, and calls himself the one who fulfils what had taken place beforehand through him in like figure, so as to refute Marcion and all who set aside the prophets of God.

**42,11,17in** Scholion 49. “Days will come when you will long to see a single one of the Son of Man’s days.”

**42,11,17io** Refutation 49. If he numbers days and signifies time and calls himself Son of Man, then he has indicated both the measure of his age and a pattern of the days of his preaching.

**42,11,17ip** So the Word is not without flesh; rather, the good pleasure is in a body.

**42,11,17iq** Scholion 50. “Someone said to him, ‘Good teacher, by doing what shall I inherit eternal life?’”

**42,11,17ir** “But he said, ‘Do not call me good; one is good, God.’”

**42,11,17is** That man added “the Father,” and instead of “You know the commandments” he says “I know the commandments.”

**42,11,17it** Refutation 50. So as not to show that the commandments had already been written beforehand, he says, “I know the commandments.”

**42,11,17iu** But the whole passage is plain from the sequence.

**42,11,17iv** And if he calls the Father good and names him God, he rightly teaches from the law of his Father the one who wishes to inherit life, and does not set it aside nor reject it, but rather bears further witness that those who lived their lives in the law, Moses as well as the other prophets, have become heirs of eternal life.

**42,11,17iw** Scholion 51. “And it came to pass, as he drew near to Jericho, that a blind man cried out, ‘Jesus, son of David, have mercy on me.’”

**42,11,17ix** “And when he was healed, he says, ‘Your faith has saved you.’”

**42,11,17iy** Refutation 51. In faith there is no falsehood; for if it lies, it is not faith.

**42,11,17iz** He says, at any rate, “Son of David.”

**42,11,17ja** And the one who confessed the name both makes his request and receives it, and he was not rebuked as a liar, but was pronounced blessed as faithful.

**42,11,17jb** So he who granted sight to the blind man because of the invocation of the name was not without flesh.

**42,11,17jc** For he was truly, and not in semblance, born from David’s seed after the flesh, from Mary the holy virgin and through the Holy Spirit.

**42,11,17jd** Scholion 52. He cut away “Taking the twelve, he said, ‘See, we are going up to Jerusalem, and everything that stands written in the prophets about the Son of Man will be brought to completion.’”

**42,11,17je** “For he will be handed over and killed, and on the third day he will rise.”

**42,11,17jf** All this he cut away.

**42,11,17jg** Refutation 52. This was so that he might walk straight in nothing, and, by not walking straight, might be refuted as tampering in every way.

**42,11,17jh** For he hid the sayings, so as, supposedly, to deny what concerns the passion.

**42,11,17ji** But since later he confesses that he was crucified, the labour of his tampering will be in vain for him.

**42,11,17jj** Scholion 53. He cut away the section concerning the donkey and Bethphage, as well as that concerning the city and the temple, since it had been written, “My house will be called a house of prayer, and you are making it a robbers’ cave.”

**42,11,17jk** Refutation 53. Its own refutation wickedness does not see; for it is blind.

**42,11,17jl** And it thinks it can hide the road of the truth, which is impossible.

**42,11,17jm** For he leapt straight on, passing over all the passages mentioned before because of the place of the temple, which was attested to be his own and built for his name, and leaving out, from Jericho onward, the whole sequence of the journey, and how he came to Bethphage; for it was in fact an ancient highway that led through the Mount of Olives to Jerusalem, a road well known to those who also give an account of the place.

**42,11,17jn** But so that he may be refuted out of his own mouth, his text reads, “It happened on one of the days, while he was teaching in the temple, that they were seeking to put their hands on him, and they took fright”, as the next passage, 54, has it.

**42,11,17jo** How then he came from Jericho to be in the temple will be known from that same journey and from the distance of the road; and this, so that the tamperer may be seen to have kept hidden both the events on the road and the words spoken in the temple by the Saviour himself before this saying — I mean “My house will be called a house of prayer”, and what follows, as the prophecy has it.

**42,11,17jp** Scholion 54. “And they were seeking to put their hands on him, and they took fright.”

**42,11,17jq** Refutation 54. This has been considered and explained in the refutation before this, together with the wording, with fitting brevity.

**42,11,17jr** Scholion 55. Again he cut away the matters concerning the vineyard let out to farmers, and “What then is this: ‘The stone which the builders rejected’?”

**42,11,17js** Refutation 55. This will do us no harm.

**42,11,17jt** For even if he cuts it away, he has not cut it away from us, but has done damage to himself and his own; for the refutation against him is sufficient through many testimonies.

**42,11,17ju** Scholion 56. He cut away “But that the dead are raised, Moses made known at the bush, as he calls the Lord the God of Abraham and of Isaac and of Jacob.”

**42,11,17jv** “And he is God of the living, and not of the dead.”

**42,11,17jw** Refutation 56. One may well marvel at the folly of this vain-minded man.

**42,11,17jx** How does he not perceive that this testimony is equal to that of Lazarus the poor man and to the parable of the people who are not allowed to go into the kingdom, the remnants of which parables he let stand and did not cut away, but left, to his own shame, “There will be the weeping and the gnashing of teeth”?

**42,11,17jy** And a finger dipped in water after the departure from here, and a tongue cooled with water, according to the rich man’s words to Abraham, because of Lazarus, and gnashing of teeth and weeping taking place — this is a sign of the resurrection of bodies.

**42,11,17jz** Let the brutish fellow cut away, if he will, what was truly said by the Lord about the resurrection of the dead.

**42,11,17ka** Scholion 57. He did not have these words: “But that the dead are raised Moses also made known, saying, ‘God of Abraham and God of Isaac and God of Jacob,’ God of the living.”

**42,11,17kb** Refutation 57. Because the Saviour repeated the parable, it has been entered twice by us, so that we may not, by imitating the charlatan Marcion, leave out anything of what is written.

**42,11,17kc** [And] the rebuttal of his tampering has already been made in the refutation above.

**42,11,17kd** Scholion 58. Again he cut away “Not a hair of your head will perish.”

**42,11,17ke** [Refutation 58.] [...]

**42,11,17kf** Scholion 59. Again he cut away these words, “Then let those in Judaea flee to the mountains,” and what follows, because of what is added in the passage, “until all that is written is fulfilled.”

**42,11,17kg** Refutation 59. Being given over to forgetfulness, he thinks that everyone is as senseless as he is, and does not know that even if a small saying is left by him, it makes a refutation of each of the many sayings he has cut away.

**42,11,17kh** Nothing, then, will prevent anyone who wishes from setting what he has acknowledged against these testimonies that have been cut away.

**42,11,17ki** For it will be shown to be in agreement with these passages cut away by him [...], in which Abraham said after his death, “They have Moses and the prophets; let them listen to them.”

**42,11,17kj** For the words of the prophets and of Moses were from God the Father, and from the very Lord, God’s Son, and from the Holy Spirit — things which, being written, had to be fulfilled.

**42,11,17kk** Scholion 60. “He conferred with the officers how he might deliver him to them.”

**42,11,17kl** Refutation 60. O the derangement of Marcion!

**42,11,17km** “He conferred”: who, if not Judas?

**42,11,17kn** To do what, if not to betray the Saviour?

**42,11,17ko** But when the Saviour is betrayed, the one betrayed is no longer a semblance, but truth.

**42,11,17kp** For if he were spirit only, he would not have been betrayed to fleshly men; but being man he came under touch, [and], having put on flesh, he willingly delivered himself into the hands of human nature.

**42,11,17kq** But in their folly they utter things contrary to themselves.

**42,11,17kr** For once, when I was conversing with some of his disciples, with a certain Marcionite, and saying that, as it stands in the gospel, the Spirit took him into the desert to be tempted by the devil, I heard from him: How could Satan tempt him who is God and is greater than he and his Lord (as you say), Jesus, his Master?

**42,11,17ks** And I, by the help of God taking understanding on the spur of the moment, answered him, saying: Do you not believe that Christ was crucified?

**42,11,17kt** And he said, Yes; and he did not deny it.

**42,11,17ku** Who then crucified him?

**42,11,17kv** And he said: Men.

**42,11,17kw** Then I said to him: Who is stronger, men or the devil?

**42,11,17kx** And he said: The devil.

**42,11,17ky** When he had said this, I answered: If the devil is stronger than men, and men, who are weaker, crucified Christ, it is no wonder if he was also tempted by the devil.

**42,11,17kz** For Christ gave himself for us wholly of his own will and not under compulsion, suffering in truth, not through weakness but by free choice, as a pattern for us, and the things concerning the devil for our salvation in the passion of the cross [...], to condemn sin and to set death aside.

**42,11,17la** Scholion 61. “And he said to Peter and the rest, ‘Go and prepare, so that we may eat the Passover.’”

**42,11,17lb** Refutation 61. In a single testimony the saying holds a cloud of arrows against you, Marcion.

**42,11,17lc** For if he orders that preparation be made so that he may eat the Passover, and the Passover used to be kept before Christ’s suffering, then it certainly had its institution from the law.

**42,11,17ld** And since Christ lived according to the law, it was clear that he came not to abolish the law but to fulfil it.

**42,11,17le** And if a king does not abolish a law, the ordinance in the law is not unhallowed, nor forbidden by the king.

**42,11,17lf** And since the law is duly hallowed and the ordinance is acknowledged, if a king should add something to the ordinance for a greater gift, the splendour of the addition comes about by his authority.

**42,11,17lg** And since the legislation and the gift by way of addition belong to one and the same, it is plain and clear to everyone that the one who made the addition does not set himself against the law.

**42,11,17lh** It has been demonstrated, then, that the old covenant is in no respect hostile to the gospel, nor to the sequence of the prophets.

**42,11,17li** But you, Marcion, have brought the refutation upon yourself in many ways — or rather, under compulsion from the truth itself.

**42,11,17lj** For the old Passover was nothing but the sacrifice of a sheep and the eating of meat, and the partaking of a creature with a soul, with unleavened bread.

**42,11,17lk** And who compelled you not to obliterate entirely the refutation against you, if not (as I said) the truth itself?

**42,11,17ll** For the Lord Jesus, with his own disciples, partook of the eating of flesh which you abhor, celebrating the Passover according to the law.

**42,11,17lm** And do not say that it was the mystery he was going to celebrate that he named in advance when he said, “I wish to eat the Passover with you”.

**42,11,17ln** For so that the truth might put you to shame in every way, he does not perform the mystery at the beginning, so that you may not deny it, but it says, After supper, taking such and such, he said, This is such and such; and it left [no] room for tampering.

**42,11,17lo** For it showed that after eating the Passover according to the Jews, that is, after supper, he came to the mystery.

**42,11,17lp** Scholion 62. “And he reclined, together with the twelve apostles, and he said, ‘I have longed with longing to eat this Passover with you before my suffering.’”

**42,11,17lq** Refutation 62. The Saviour reclined, Marcion, and the twelve apostles with him.

**42,11,17lr** If he reclined and they reclined with him, a single word cannot have one meaning in the one case and another in the other, even if it carries a difference in rank and in manner.

**42,11,17ls** For either you will grant that the twelve too reclined in semblance, or that he too, having flesh in truth, truly reclined.

**42,11,17lt** And “I have longed with longing to eat this Passover with you before my suffering”, so as to show that the Passover before his passion was prefigured in the law, and became the confirmation of his passion, and called it forth more perfectly; and showing, as the holy apostle also says, [that] “the law has become our tutor to Christ.”

**42,11,17lu** And if the law is a tutor to Christ, the law is not a stranger to Christ.

**42,11,17lv** Scholion 63. He cut away “For I tell you, I will not eat it from now on until it is fulfilled in the kingdom of God.”

**42,11,17lw** Refutation 63. He removed this and tampered with it so that, supposedly, he might not put eating or drinking into God’s kingdom — the brutish fellow not knowing that there can be spiritual and heavenly counterparts of earthly things, partaken of in a way we do not know; for again the Saviour bears witness and says, “You will be seated at my table, eating and drinking within the kingdom of the heavens.”

**42,11,17lx** Or again he cut these words away, so as, supposedly, to make the things in the law have no place in the kingdom of heaven.

**42,11,17ly** How then were Elijah and Moses seen with him on the mountain in glory?

**42,11,17lz** But no one will be able to do anything against the truth.

**42,11,17ma** Scholion 64. He cut away “When I sent you out, did you lack anything?”

**42,11,17mb** And what follows, because of “And this which is written must be fulfilled: ‘And he was reckoned with the lawless.’”

**42,11,17mc** Refutation 64. Even if you cut away the words, their places are evident from the event, the law going before, the prophets proclaiming beforehand, and the Lord fulfilling.

**42,11,17md** Scholion 65. “He withdrew from them about a stone’s throw, and kneeling down he prayed.”

**42,11,17me** Refutation 65. Kneeling down, he bent his knees visibly and performed it perceptibly.

**42,11,17mf** And if perceptibly, he did the act of kneeling in accordance with the form [...]; so the Only-begotten did not sojourn here without flesh.

**42,11,17mg** For to him “every knee will bend, of beings in heaven and on earth and under the earth”: of things in heaven spiritually, of things on earth perceptibly, of things under the earth in their own fashion.

**42,11,17mh** And here he accomplished all things in truth, being seen and found within reach of touch by the disciples, and not presenting an illusion.

**42,11,17mi** Scholion 66. “And Judas came near to kiss him, and he said.”

**42,11,17mj** Refutation 66. He drew near to a Master who was in flesh, to a God who had taken a body, in order to kiss true lips, and not lips that existed in semblance and illusion.

**42,11,17mk** Scholion 67. He cut away Peter’s deed, when he struck and took off the ear of the high priest’s servant.

**42,11,17ml** Refutation 67. Thinking, out of regard for Peter, to hide what truly happened, the deceiver cut out the saying that tells to the Saviour’s glory, and hid it.

**42,11,17mm** But it will do him no good; for even if he himself cuts it away, we know the divine signs.

**42,11,17mn** For when the ear had been cut off, the Lord took it back and healed it, so that it might be shown that he is God and accomplished a work of God.

**42,11,17mo** Scholion 68. “Those who held him made sport of him, flogging and beating him and saying, ‘Prophesy: who is the one that hit you?’”

**42,11,17mp** Refutation 68. That the holding and the mocking and the flogging and the beating and the “Prophesy: who is the one that hit you?” — this was no semblance, but is indicative of bodily touch and of a substance in the flesh; and it is plain to everyone, even if you, Marcion, blinded, should not wish to confess the manifest truth of God.

**42,11,17mq** Scholion 69. After “We found this man perverting the nation” he appended “and abolishing the law and the prophets”.

**42,11,17mr** Refutation 69. Where will you not be caught, where will you not be refuted as perverting the way of the Lord?

**42,11,17ms** For when you add here what is not written, slandering yourself — for I would not say the Lord — [and] saying, We found this man abolishing the law and the prophets, its counterpart refutes you, you who labour in vain, since the Saviour himself says, “I did not come to abolish the law and the prophets, but to fulfil.”

**42,11,17mt** The same one, then, who says, “I did not come to abolish,” cannot be accused of abolishing.

**42,11,17mu** For the saying did not run so, but: “We found this man perverting the people, claiming that he is himself Christ, a king.”

**42,11,17mv** Scholion 70. An addition after “forbidding to pay tribute”: “and turning away the wives and the children.”

**42,11,17mw** Refutation 70. Who is it that procures a precipice for himself, fulfilling what is written, “The one who is wicked towards himself — towards whom will he be good?”

**42,11,17mx** For it is an example of every audacity and wickedness, and of a perilous course, to cut away what is written and to add what is not written, above all in the gospel, which is indestructible for ever.

**42,11,17my** And the matter of the addition itself has neither a proper place nor any hidden sense.

**42,11,17mz** For Jesus did not turn away wives or children; for he himself said, “Honour your father and your mother,” and “What God has joined together, let not man separate.”

**42,11,17na** But even if he says, “If anyone does not leave father and mother and brothers and wife and children and what follows, he is not my disciple,” it is not so that we should hate our fathers, but so that we should not be subjected to the command of fathers and mothers [...] of another faith, or to a way contrary to the Saviour’s teaching.

**42,11,17nb** Scholion 71. “And when they came to a place called the place of the Skull, they crucified him and divided his garments, and the sun was darkened.”

**42,11,17nc** Refutation 71. Glory to the merciful God, who has bound up your chariots, Marcion, you Pharaoh, and, when you wished to escape, sank them in the sea.

**42,11,17nd** For though you make excuses for everything, here you will have no excuse.

**42,11,17ne** For one who does not have flesh cannot be crucified either.

**42,11,17nf** How did you not escape this great saying?

**42,11,17ng** How did you not attempt to hide this great matter, which has undone all your perversity contrived from the beginning?

**42,11,17nh** For if he was crucified at all, how do you not see that the crucified one was tangible and was fastened with nails through hands and feet?

**42,11,17ni** This could not have been a semblance or a phantom, as you say, but truly a body, which the Lord took from Mary (flesh of our own nature, and bones, and the rest), since even by you it is acknowledged that the Lord was fixed to a cross.

**42,11,17nj** Scholion 72. He cut away “Today you will be with me in paradise.”

**42,11,17nk** Refutation 72. Well and fittingly did you cut this away, Marcion; for you have taken away from yourself the entrance into paradise.

**42,11,17nl** For neither will you enter, nor will you let those with you go in.

**42,11,17nm** For in very truth those who lead astray and those who are led astray hate the good.

**42,11,17nn** Scholion 73. “And crying out with a loud voice he breathed his last.”

**42,11,17no** Refutation 73. If he breathed his last, Marcion, and uttered a loud cry, from where did he breathe out, or what was it that breathed out?

**42,11,17np** But it is plain, even if you do not say it: the soul, together with the Godhead, went forth from the body, the body remaining without breath, as the truth has it.

**42,11,17nq** Scholion 74. “And behold, a man named Joseph took down the body, wrapped it in a linen cloth and laid it in a tomb hewn out of rock.”

**42,11,17nr** Refutation 74. If the taking down and the wrapping and the laying in a hewn tomb do not persuade you, Marcion, who is more foolish than you?

**42,11,17ns** And what else could scripture have shown more clearly, when it showed the tomb and the place and the manner, and the laying of the body for three days, and the wrapping of the linen cloth, so as to show the whole truth?

**42,11,17nt** Scholion 75. “And the women returned and rested on the sabbath according to the law.”

**42,11,17nu** Refutation 75. From where did the women return?

**42,11,17nv** And why is “they rested” also written, if not so that scripture might show their testimony, which refutes your folly, Marcion?

**42,11,17nw** For behold, women bear witness, and apostles, and Jews, and angels, and Joseph, who took down a body that could truly be handled and wrapped it.

**42,11,17nx** And who will set right the one who, self-condemned, perverts himself, as it is written.

**42,11,17ny** Scholion 76. “Those in shining clothing said, ‘Why do you seek the living among the dead?’”

**42,11,17nz** “He has been raised; remember everything he said while he was yet among you, that it is necessary for the Son of Man to suffer and to be handed over.”

**42,11,17oa** Refutation 76. Not even these holy angels persuade you, Marcion, though they confess that for three days he was among the dead, but thereafter living and no longer dead — always living in his Godhead and never put to death at all, but put to death in the flesh for three days and living again.

**42,11,17ob** For they say to them, “He has risen; he is not here.”

**42,11,17oc** And what does “he has risen” mean, if not that he had also fallen asleep?

**42,11,17od** For they tell it more clearly: “For remember,” it says, “that while he was still living he was telling you this, that it is necessary for the Son of Man to suffer.”

**42,11,17oe** Scholion 77. He cut away the words spoken to Cleopas and the other man when he met them, “You senseless men, slow in believing everything the prophets spoke: was it not necessary for him to suffer these things?”

**42,11,17of** And instead of “in what the prophets spoke” he made it “in what I spoke to you.”

**42,11,17og** But he is refuted, because “when he broke the loaf, their eyes were opened, and they knew him”.

**42,11,17oh** Refutation 77. From what did the breaking of the loaf come about?

**42,11,17oi** Tell me, Marcion.

**42,11,17oj** Was it from an illusion, or from a solid body acting in truth?

**42,11,17ok** For when he rose from the dead, he rose in truth in that very holy body.

**42,11,17ol** But you, Marcion, made it, instead of “Are not these the things which the prophets spoke?”

**42,11,17om** “Are not these the things which I spoke to you?”

**42,11,17on** But if he had said to them “I spoke to you,” they would certainly have recognised him from the words “I spoke to you.”

**42,11,17oo** How then does it say, at the breaking of the loaf, “Their eyes were opened, and they knew him, and he became invisible”?

**42,11,17op** For it befitted him, being God and changing his body into a spiritual body, to display it as a true body, yet to vanish when he wished, since all things are possible to him.

**42,11,17oq** For Elisha too, being a prophet and having received grace from God, asked God that those seeking him be struck with blindness; and they were struck and did not see him as he was.

**42,11,17or** And in Sodom too the angels hid Lot’s door, and the Sodomites did not see it.

**42,11,17os** Was Lot’s door also a semblance, Marcion?

**42,11,17ot** No counter-argument at all is left to you.

**42,11,17ou** For he plainly broke the loaf and shared it out among his disciples.

**42,11,17ov** Scholion 78. “Why are you troubled?”

**42,11,17ow** “Look at my hands and my feet, because a spirit has no bones, as you see me having.”

**42,11,17ox** Refutation 78. Who would not laugh at the nonsense of one who in his folly has dragged down to Hades both himself and the souls of others?

**42,11,17oy** For if these words had not been acknowledged by him, his error would have been plausible, and those deceived by him would have had some excuse; but now, since he acknowledged them and did not remove these sayings, and his followers too read them, his sin and theirs abides, and inescapable is the fire, both his and theirs, since they are without defence, the Saviour having plainly taught [that] even after rising again he has bones and flesh, as he himself testified, saying, “as you see me having”.

**42,12,1** This is the case against him, drawn from the remnants of the gospel preserved by him, set out in the treatise we have composed on his account, a treatise sufficient, as I at least think, for the rebuttal of his deceit.

**42,12,2** I shall go on also to what follows, the apostolic sayings still preserved by him and collected again by us in the same way, [those] from the Epistle to the Galatians being placed by us at the very first, and so in sequence throughout; for with him this epistle stands first.

**42,12,3a** At that time we made the collection not as [it stands] with him but as the apostolic collection has it, placing the Epistle to the Romans first; but here we set them out in this way, according to his own arrangement.

**42,12,3b** [From the Epistle to the Galatians.]

**42,12,3c** Scholion 1. “Learn that the righteous shall live by faith.

**42,12,3d** For as many as are under law are under a curse; but he who does them shall live in them”.

**42,12,3e** Refutation 1. The words “Learn that the righteous shall live by faith”, according to what was spoken by the apostle, point to ancient scripture; they are things which by the apostle, for our life, [...] were spoken from the law and the prophets concerning a new covenant, and which are yoked together with our hope.

**42,12,3f** And by “they are under a curse” he means that in the law there was a threat against the disobedience of Adam, until he who came from above came and, clothing himself with a body from the lump of Adam, changed the curse into blessing.

**42,12,3g** Scholion 2. “Cursed is everyone who hangs on a tree”.

**42,12,3h** “But he who is of the promise, through the free woman”.

**42,12,3i** Refutation 2. In showing once more that the dispensation of the incarnate coming and of the cross came about for the undoing of the curse, and that this was recorded in advance in the law and foretold [as] what would be, and afterwards fulfilled in the Saviour, the holy apostle has made it plain that the law is no stranger to the Saviour.

**42,12,3j** For it uttered prophecy, and gave its witness, concerning what was destined to be brought about through him.

**42,12,3k** Scholion 3. “And I testify again that a man who is circumcised is a debtor to fulfil the whole law”.

**42,12,3l** Refutation 3. He says “is a debtor” no longer as of something forbidden, but as of a heavier burden that can be lightened, there being one Master, who is able both to make it heavy and to lighten it according to the choice of those who have not refused to receive the salvation that comes through his grace in his incarnate coming.

**42,12,3m** Scholion 4. In place of “A little leaven leavens the whole lump” he has put “adulterates”.

**42,12,3n** Refutation 4. So that nothing true might be found with him, there is hardly a place where he has handled the scriptures without knavery.

**42,12,3o** But the meaning of the saying is made plain from the very sequence of the words; for leaven is, in its form, a product of the lump, and the leavening comes from the lump, and he who with understanding framed the comparison of the riddle would not have done away with the form in the originals.

**42,12,3p** Scholion 5. “For the whole law has been fulfilled for you: you shall love your neighbour as yourself”.

**42,12,3q** Refutation 5. What need has the holy apostle to make use of the law, if the new covenant had been estranged from the old legislation?

**42,12,3r** But in order to show that the two covenants belong to the one God, and that the agreement [in respect of] the fulfilment of the law through love of the neighbour is made known in equal measure in the two covenants, since love works the perfect good, he said that love is the perfecting of the law.

**42,12,3s** Scholion 6. “Plain to see are the works of the flesh, and they are these: fornication, impurity, wantonness, idol-worship, sorcery, hostilities, quarrels, jealousies, fits of anger, selfish rivalries, divisions, factions, envyings, bouts of drunkenness, carousings; of these I warn you beforehand, as I warned you before, that people who do such things will not inherit God’s kingdom”.

**42,12,3t** Refutation 6. O wondrous mysteries and contrary [...] examples of God!

**42,12,3u** For he has fastened all the dreadful things upon the flesh.

**42,12,3v** But the flesh did not always exist; rather, on the sixth day of the making of the world, when Adam was formed, the flesh [came into being]; from then it had its beginning, I mean [from] the forming, so that those who say that evil exists always and from the beginning may be refuted.

**42,12,3w** For neither did the flesh sin from the time it was formed, lest the Maker be thought the cause of sin for having formed a sinful flesh, nor did evil exist before the thing that was formed.

**42,12,3x** But after a time Adam, having fallen into disobedience, [as] one possessing free will, conceived sin in himself by his own choice and of his own accord, and committed it; I mean that through his disobedience he set aside his own Master.

**42,12,3y** Where, then, was evil before the flesh existed?

**42,12,3z** And how was it that, as soon as it was formed, it did not work evil, but only at a later time?

**42,12,3aa** So then the argument about a beginning of evil has been done away with.

**42,12,3ab** For it cannot be primordial, since it is in the flesh, which came later, that evil has its coming to be or its not coming to be; and neither, again, is the flesh shut out from the inheritance of the heavenly things.

**42,12,3ac** And let no one seize upon the holy apostle’s saying, “Flesh and blood will have no inheritance in God’s kingdom”.

**42,12,3ad** For he does not accuse all flesh.

**42,12,3ae** For how shall flesh be accused that has not done the things mentioned before?

**42,12,3af** And I shall establish the point in question from other proofs as well.

**42,12,3ag** “For who,” he says, “shall bring a charge against the elect of God?”

**42,12,3ah** How will the holy Mary, together with the flesh, fail to inherit the kingdom of the heavens, she who did not commit fornication, did not give herself to licentiousness, was not led into adultery, and wrought none of the incurable works of the flesh, but remained undefiled?

**42,12,3ai** It is not, then, of flesh that he says that it does not inherit the kingdom of heaven, but of carnal men, who do base things through the flesh, which are fornication, idolatry and things like these.

**42,12,3aj** And on every count your contrivance has been refuted, O deluded Marcion, since the truth has forestalled it at every point and the firmness of the preaching of life is made secure.

**42,12,3ak** Scholion 7. “And those of Christ have crucified the flesh with its passions and desires”.

**42,12,3al** Refutation 7. If those of Christ have crucified even the flesh, O Marcion, it is plain, then, that the servants of Christ, together with the desires and the passions, rendered the flesh pure and imitated Christ, showing that he had crucified flesh.

**42,12,3am** Therefore they too, being of like mind with their Master, crucified the flesh.

**42,12,3an** And if they crucified the flesh, it cannot be that the flesh which suffered on Christ’s behalf should fail to reign together with Christ, just as in another saying too the holy apostle shows it, when he says, “Just as you have a share in Christ’s sufferings, so too in the glory”.

**42,12,3ao** Scholion 8. “For not even those who are circumcised keep the law themselves”.

**42,12,3ap** Refutation 8. The former circumcision, then, was not forbidden in its own time, if it kept the law.

**42,12,3aq** But the law proclaimed that Christ was coming to grant a law of freedom, and that in Christ’s time circumcision in the flesh would no longer be of service; for the true one has come through Christ, and that one was its type.

**42,12,3ar** But those who are still sealed in that one, even if they keep the whole law, will no longer have the keeping of the law reckoned to them; for indeed the law said concerning Christ, “The Lord God will raise up for you a prophet from among my brothers, like me; him you shall hear”.

**42,12,3as** And since they have not heeded Christ, “circumcision becomes uncircumcision for them”, and the keeping of the law is no longer a keeping.

**42,12,3at** Good, then, is the law, and good is circumcision, from which we have come to know Christ, and the more complete law and more complete circumcision that are his.

**42,12,3au** From the [First] Epistle to the Corinthians, since with them, as with us, this one stands second.

**42,12,3av** Scholion 1 and 9. “For it is written: ‘The wisdom of the wise I will destroy, and the understanding of the prudent I will set aside’”.

**42,12,3aw** Refutation 1 and 9. If the apostle gathers testimonies from what is written in the prophets to establish truth and good teaching, the prophets are not alien to the truth, nor to the good God and his good teaching.

**42,12,3ax** Scholion 2 and 10. “That, as it is written, he who boasts, let him boast in the Lord”.

**42,12,3ay** Refutation 2 and 10. If he who boasts in the Lord is praiseworthy in the prophet, and the prophet knows as Lord the God of the law, the one who with you, O Marcion, is called both judge and creator and just, then this God is not someone other than Christ’s Father, of whom Paul is a disciple; since Paul, appointed by Christ the teacher of the Gentiles, draws from the teaching of the prophet, from like and identical teachings, as it were pure water, and with it waters the church with which he has been entrusted.

**42,12,3az** Scholion 3 and 11. “Of the rulers of this age, who are being brought to nothing”.

**42,12,3ba** Refutation 3 and 11. If the rulers of this age are many, and such as these are being brought to nothing, you will be compelled, O Marcion, no longer to seek the roots of three principles, but to search out for yourself another myth of many principles and many roots and much tragic fable.

**42,12,3bb** And when you fabricate it with your slanders (for you would not find it), the word “who are being brought to nothing” will confront you; and by the saying that says “who are being brought to nothing” the root of the principles, which you imagine to be without beginning, will be destroyed.

**42,12,3bc** For everything that comes to an end is not eternal; rather, if it had a beginning of being, it will also undergo an end.

**42,12,3bd** For it is impossible for what has had a beginning to endure everlastingly for ever, unless that which is should so will, having become the cause of what once was not but began to be.

**42,12,3be** Now that which is, is Father and Son and Holy Spirit; but that which is not is the whole of what has been created, things that have a beginning of their being, among which is also what is called evil and is evil, having begun from men, who came into being and once were not; and since evil has had a beginning, from the time when man too, who once was not, [...] evil will no longer exist.

**42,12,3bf** For it will without doubt be done away with, because he who is does not consent with him who has had a beginning and has set himself among base things; for it will cease after the resurrection, and not only then, but also from the proclamation of the law, and before the law from the many who lived according to the natural law, and still more abundantly from the incarnate coming of Christ, and most perfectly from the raising of the dead, because “they are sown in perishability, they are raised in imperishability”, no longer working evil, no longer dying.

**42,12,3bg** And that it will cease, the same saying of the apostle will bear witness, when it says, “Of the rulers of this age, who are being brought to nothing”.

**42,12,3bh** And your hypothesis, O Marcion, has been overthrown on every side, being a thing of fancy, false, unstable and irrational.

**42,12,3bi** Scholion 4 and 12. “For it is written: ‘He who catches the wise in their craftiness.’

**42,12,3bj** And again: ‘The Lord knows the reasonings of men, that they are vain’”.

**42,12,3bk** Refutation 4 and 12. Since “it is written” is brought forward as a citation, and “the Lord knows” is acknowledged, the wording of the saying is not alien to him who gathered it, I mean to the holy apostle, in whom the citation occurs.

**42,12,3bl** From this citation the stamp of the old covenant, from which his testimony was established, will be seen to be not alien to the preaching of the apostle.

**42,12,3bm** Scholion 5 and 13. “For indeed our Passover, Christ, was sacrificed”.

**42,12,3bn** Refutation 5 and 13. If the apostle confesses a Passover and does not deny the sacrifice of Christ, then the Passover is no stranger to Christ, who according to the law sacrifices the Passover sheep truly and not in semblance, of which sheep the type was Christ, who was sacrificed not in semblance and did not suffer without flesh.

**42,12,3bo** For how could a spirit be sacrificed?

**42,12,3bp** It is plain [that] it could not.

**42,12,3bq** And since he could not be sacrificed without flesh, but was sacrificed in truth, as is acknowledged through the indisputable testimony of the apostle, [...].

**42,12,3br** Therefore it has been clearly shown on every count that the law is not alien, being carried along in type for a time until the more perfect and manifest sheep, Christ, was sacrificed in truth, before whom the perceptible sheep went in the ancient times, being sacrificed.

**42,12,3bs** “But our Passover, Christ, was sacrificed”.

**42,12,3bt** Scholion 6 and 14. “Do you not know that he who is joined to a harlot is one body?

**42,12,3bu** For the two, he says, shall become one flesh”.

**42,12,3bv** Refutation 6 and 14. If the law is not true, for what reason do the truthful take their testimonies from the law?

**42,12,3bw** Of these one is the holy apostle of God, Paul, who took this testimony, along with many others too, for the clear establishment of the truth and of the preaching of the good God.

**42,12,3bx** Scholion 7 and 15. In altered form: for he says “in the law of Moses” in place of “in the law”.

**42,12,3by** But before this he says, “Or does not the law also say these things?”

**42,12,3bz** Refutation 7 and 15. Even if in the second statement you alter the form, O Marcion, and think that by having made it “in the law of Moses” you estrange the law from God through the words “of Moses”, the connecting phrase above convicts your folly: “Or does not the law also say these things? For in the law it is written: ‘You shall not muzzle an ox while it threshes’”.

**42,12,3ca** But you have done us no wrong, even if you add the name of Moses; rather, you have helped us, and have bound the testimony tight against yourself, and on every side you have unwittingly confessed that the law of Moses is God’s, from the phrase “in the law of Moses” and from “the law says”.

**42,12,3cb** For the apostle follows on in agreement, when he says afterwards, “Is it for the oxen that God cares? Or is it altogether for our sake that he has spoken?”

**42,12,3cc** And if it is for the apostles’ sake that the law has spoken, then the God who spoke in the law also cares for the apostles of Christ, in commanding that they be not muzzled, either from speaking the teaching of his own Christ, or from having their daily food from the peoples without want.

**42,12,3cd** He does not, then, regard the apostles as strangers to his Godhead, nor do the apostles consider him a strange god.

**42,12,3ce** On the strength of which the holy apostle has rendered testimony on behalf of all, saying, “Is it about the oxen that God cares? No, he has said it for the sake of the apostles”.

**42,12,3cf** And if he has said it for the sake of the apostles, and he is the creator of men and of beasts, among which are oxen and sparrows, creeping things and wild creatures, creatures of the sea and the rest, then he cares about all things according to the proportion of each.

**42,12,3cg** And he does care about all things, as is said: “Men and beasts you will save, O Lord”, and as it has been said: “Who has given the raven its food?” and “The nestlings of ravens have cried out to the Lord, looking for food”, and “You will give food in due season to all”.

**42,12,3ch** But it was not for the sake of the oxen’s threshing that he commanded not to muzzle an ox while it threshes, since he would then have shown that God is unable to feed his creation otherwise than through the food supplied to the beasts [by] men.

**42,12,3ci** But the holy apostle showed that it is not for lack of food that God takes care of oxen by means of the threshing, in commanding that such be not muzzled, but that by the riddle his regard for the apostles is signified.

**42,12,3cj** For of all things in the perceptible realm he exercises providence, and he cares alike about all.

**42,12,3ck** For the apostle did not write in opposition to the Saviour, so that the many might make this their pretext.

**42,12,3cl** For sparrows are smaller than oxen, and of these the Saviour said, “Five sparrows are sold for two pennies”, and again, “Are two sparrows not sold for a single penny?”

**42,12,3cm** If, then, two sparrows are sold for one penny, and not one of them shall fall into a snare without your Father who is in heaven, he exercises providence over all things together, but takes care of the greater according to the greater form of the spiritual order.

**42,12,3cn** So then it is acknowledged on every count that the same is maker and creator and lawgiver of the old and the new covenant, God good and just, and Lord of all things.

**42,12,3co** Scholion 8 and 16. “Is it for the oxen that God cares?”

**42,12,3cp** Refutation 8 and 16. It has already been dealt with, and interpreted at length, in the saying before it.

**42,12,3cq** Therefore, thinking it superfluous to speak of it again, I have been content with what was said before.

**42,12,3cr** Scholion 9 and 17. “For I would not have you be unaware, brothers, that our fathers were beneath the cloud, and all went through the sea, and all ate the same spiritual food, and all drank the same spiritual drink.

**42,12,3cs** For they drank from a spiritual rock that followed them; and the rock was Christ.

**42,12,3ct** But with most of them he was not well pleased.

**42,12,3cu** Now these things became types of us, so that we should not be desirers of evil things, as they too desired.

**42,12,3cv** Do not become idolaters either, as some of them were, as it is written: ‘The people sat to eat and to drink, and stood up to play.’

**42,12,3cw** Neither let us tempt Christ”, as far as where he says, “Now these things happened to them by way of type, but were written for us”, and what follows.

**42,12,3cx** Refutation 9 and 17. O what great derangement!

**42,12,3cy** Who that has eyes will lead himself astray from the light when the sun is rising?

**42,12,3cz** For if the holy apostle calls the men of that time his own fathers, and says that they passed under the cloud and through the sea, and that there was spiritual food and drink, and that they ate and drank from a spiritual rock that followed them, and declares that the rock was Christ, who will be persuaded by the folly of Marcion, who darkens the understanding of himself and of those who listen to him, to say that Christ is alien to the things that took place in the law, when they are acknowledged by the apostle in truth and not in semblance?

**42,12,3da** And the apostle declares that Christ took no delight in most of them, no doubt because of the lawless conduct of those same men.

**42,12,3db** But if he took no delight in those who under the law had done lawless things, then he is indignant with such men, as being himself the giver of the law, and he teaches that the law is his own, given for a season and rightly made to serve until his incarnate coming.

**42,12,3dc** For it is fitting for a master of a house to enjoin on his own household servants what is proper, in a manner suited to each season.

**42,12,3dd** And straightway he adds, saying, “Now these things became types of us, so that we should not be desirers of evil things, as they too desired. Neither be idolaters, as some of them were”; and he does not carry the sentence through against all.

**42,12,3de** And how do you know these things, O apostle?

**42,12,3df** He goes on, with the words “as it is written”: “The people sat to eat and to drink, and stood up to play”.

**42,12,3dg** So then the scripture is true, from which the apostle also takes his refutation of the lawless.

**42,12,3dh** Then, once more: “Nor let us put the Lord to the test”.

**42,12,3di** But Marcion has made it “Christ” in place of “the Lord”.

**42,12,3dj** But Lord and Christ are one and the same, though it may not seem so to Marcion, since the name of Christ has been set down beforehand in the words, “The rock was Christ; and with most of them he was not well pleased”.

**42,12,3dk** And the holy apostle, going through the whole subject of the passage once more, says, “Now these things happened to them by way of type, but were written for our admonition”.

**42,12,3dl** If, then, the things that happened to them by way of type were written for our admonition, he who wrote down the things that took place then for our admonition was caring for those whom he was also admonishing, namely that we should not become desirers of evil things.

**42,12,3dm** And he who does not wish us to be desirers of evil things is, then, good and not evil.

**42,12,3dn** For in the things in which he himself is, in these he urges us to be, he who is the same God, good and just, the God of those who were written of beforehand and of those who are admonished afterwards, being the creator of all things and their fashioner and lawgiver, and the giver of the gospels and the guide of the apostles.

**42,12,3do** Scholion 10 and 18. “What then do I say?

**42,12,3dp** That what is sacrificed in a temple is anything, or that what is sacrificed to idols is anything?

**42,12,3dq** No, but that what they sacrifice is to demons and not to God”.

**42,12,3dr** Marcion added “sacrificed in a temple”.

**42,12,3ds** Refutation 10 and 18. “What then do I say?

**42,12,3dt** That what is sacrificed to idols is anything?

**42,12,3du** No, but that the things they sacrifice, they offer to demons and not to God”.

**42,12,3dv** In declaring that those who offer sacrifice to the idols are sacrificing to demons and not to God, the apostle did not repudiate the ancient time of the fathers, down to his own day and as long as Jerusalem stood.

**42,12,3dw** He did not, then, condemn those who sacrificed to God when there was need of sacrifices.

**42,12,3dx** But he condemns those who sacrifice to idols, not because they sacrifice, but because they sacrifice to idols instead of to God.

**42,12,3dy** For they do not sacrifice in order to prepare for themselves, for their partaking, food granted by God, but, sacrificing to demons and to vanity of mind, [...].

**42,12,3dz** But you, O Marcion, added “sacrificed in a temple”, thinking that from the mixing of the two names, of temple and of idol, the two ways would be bound together in one relation.

**42,12,3ea** And this is not so.

**42,12,3eb** For if, after the coming of the new covenant, sacrifice were offered to Christ separately and animals were offered up in his name, the falsehood of your slander would be found plausible, on the ground that those who now sacrifice to God sacrifice to Christ, while those who sacrificed then in the temple of Jerusalem are joined together with those who sacrifice to idols, and sacrifice to demons and not to God.

**42,12,3ec** But since no one has sacrificed animals to Christ from the time of Christ’s sojourn and of the new covenant, your addition is manifest.

**42,12,3ed** And even if the expression about what is sacrificed in a temple and what is sacrificed to idols did in fact stand in the apostle, it would nevertheless be judged one and the same thing by those who possess sound reasoning, the expression being used loosely by the apostle because of the custom of men, who always call the idol a temple.

**42,12,3ee** And on every side your lying intent has collapsed, since the truth has been shown, that those who sacrifice to idols (whether, by their own account, what they made or make is a temple sacrifice [...]) work falsehood and make their offerings to demons and not to God, while those who once sacrificed most rightly according to the law did not.

**42,12,3ef** But now, since this no longer takes place, by his own will, as he said even from of old through Jeremiah the prophet, “Why do you bring me frankincense from Sheba, and cinnamon from a distant land?” and again, “Your sacrifices have given me no pleasure”, and elsewhere, “Take away your sacrifices, Israel, and eat meat. For I laid no command on your fathers about sacrifices on the day when I took hold of their hand to lead them forth from the land of Egypt; rather, this is what I commanded them, that each should do righteousness towards his neighbour”; and since he says “I did not command”, while he himself says in the law to Moses, “If any of the sons of Israel brings a sacrifice of the oxen or of the sheep, he is to bring a male without blemish”, and again, “If anyone sins and falls into some offence, he is to bring forward a sheep”, and further, “If the people sin, they are to bring a calf”, he showed that he accepted with favour the sacrifices once performed for the salvation of the people, not because he needed them or wanted them, but because he stooped to their weakness and to the preconception of mankind, so as to carry the mind of men across from the belief in many gods to knowledge of the one God.

**42,12,3eg** For since their mind had been firmly grounded in sacrifice as in piety, the sacrifice offered to idols for their own propitiation and salvation, and so that they should not take it hard all at once because of custom, in order to turn [...] to do this for a time in his name, away from the fancy of many gods, he willed to change over to himself the things customarily done by them, so that thereafter, knowing the One and believing securely in the One, they might hear from him, “Shall I eat the flesh of bulls, or shall I drink the blood of goats?” and “For forty years in the wilderness, O house of Israel, did you offer me sacrifice?”

**42,12,3eh** And yet many sacrifices were offered at that time – that he might show that it was not to him they offered them, although he did accept them and they offered them in his name, but because of their custom, which had been carried far in such things, until he should extract them, drawing them away from the custom of the many to the One, and they should thereafter learn from the One that he neither needed nor needs them, and he should thereafter cut off, through the incarnate coming of his Christ, the whole matter of the sacrifices, the one sacrifice having brought to completion all those that existed before, that is, the sacrifice of Christ, since, as it is written, “Our Passover, Christ, was sacrificed”; and the law became the tutor leading to this sacrifice, this Passover and this teaching, guiding by means of the type and restoring to the more perfect teaching.

**42,12,3ei** Scholion 11 and 19. “A man ought not to wear long hair, since he is God’s glory and image”.

**42,12,3ej** Refutation 11 and 19. The apostle defines man not only as the image of God, but also as his glory.

**42,12,3ek** And in supposing the hair to be bodily, as being properly in the body and not in the soul, he defines the thing made as not alien, then, to the good God, by his acknowledging that the things from the old are fulfilled in the new covenant.

**42,12,3el** Scholion 12 and 20. “But God tempered the body together”.

**42,12,3em** Refutation 12 and 20. If God tempered the body together, and the apostle preaches no other God but him who is, and acknowledges that God has tempered the body together with its members, then he knows of no God except the Creator, himself good and creator and just, maker of all things, of all of which man is one work, he who has been well tempered together with his members by him.

**42,12,3en** Scholion 13 and 21. Erroneously, Marcion, [after] “But in church I wish to speak five words with my mind”, added “because of the law”.

**42,12,3eo** Refutation 13 and 21. So then the tongues too are of the gift of the Spirit.

**42,12,3ep** But what sort of tongues does the apostle mean?

**42,12,3eq** [...] so that [those who] prided themselves on the Hebrew sounds, finely varied with wisdom in diverse and manifold ways in each word, and also on the pompous tongue of the Greeks, on speaking Attic and Aeolic and Doric, might know [...], certain of those among the Corinthians who had caused the alarms and factions, to whom the epistle was being sent.

**42,12,3er** And he acknowledged that to use Hebrew words and to teach the law is a spiritual gift; nevertheless, having also pulled down the other pompous things of the Greek tongue, he said that he himself spoke in tongues more than they, because he was a Hebrew of the Hebrews, reared at Gamaliel’s feet; and these Hebrews’ letters he places among things praiseworthy, and [...] that they are gifts of the bounty of the Spirit; that is why, writing about the same things to Timothy, he said, “that from youth you have learned the sacred letters”.

**42,12,3es** And further, adding [to] those who set out from Greek poets and orators, and saying the same things, he likewise said, “I speak in tongues more than you all”, to show that he too had become exceedingly experienced in Greek learning.

**42,12,3et** For his style too indicates that he was versed in preparatory learning, whom, [...], the Epicureans and Stoics were not able to withstand, being overthrown by the inscription on the altar, learnedly read by him, the inscription which was written “To the unknown God”, read by him word for word and at once rendered by way of paraphrase, “Him whom you worship without knowing, him I proclaim to you”, and again when he said, “One of them, their own prophet, said: ‘Cretans are always liars, evil beasts, idle bellies’”, in order to point to Epimenides, who was an ancient philosopher and the founder of the idol among the Cretans; from whom Callimachus the Libyan also took up the testimony for himself, speaking falsely about Zeus: “Cretans are always liars; for indeed, O lord, the Cretans built a tomb for you; but you did not die, for you are for ever.”

**42,12,3eu** And you see how the holy apostle explains concerning tongues: “But I wish in church five words with my mind”, that is, through the interpretation, “to utter”.

**42,12,3ev** As the prophet in the Holy Spirit, bringing to light through prophecy what has been supplied beforehand to his mind, benefits his hearers, so I too, he says, [wish] to speak for the hearing and edification of the church, and not, through the boast of the Greek and the Hebrew, that he who knows should edify himself and not the church, through whatever tongue he knows.

**42,12,3ew** But you, O Marcion, have added “because of the law”, as though the apostle were saying, “I wish [to speak] five words in church because of the law”.

**42,12,3ex** Be ashamed, you second Babylon and new promiscuous mingling of Sodom!

**42,12,3ey** How long will you confound the tongues?

**42,12,3ez** How long will you make bold against those who suffer no wrong at all from you?

**42,12,3fa** For you seek to do violence to angelic powers, casting the words of the truth out of the church, saying to holy Lot, “Bring out the men”.

**42,12,3fb** And what you attempt, you attempt against yourself.

**42,12,3fc** But you will not cast out the words of the truth; rather, you strike yourself with blindness and pass your time in a darkened night, groping for the door and not finding it, until the sun comes up and you behold the day of judgement, on which the fire too will meet your lying speech.

**42,12,3fd** For this awaits you, as you see.

**42,12,3fe** For [the words] “because of the law” do not stand in the apostle, and this has been invented by you.

**42,12,3ff** And even if the apostle had said “because of the law”, he would have been speaking in agreement with his own Lord — not so as to abolish the law, but in order to fulfil it.

**42,12,3fg** Scholion 14 and 22. “It is written in the law that ‘By men of other tongues and by other lips I will speak to this people’”.

**42,12,3fh** Refutation 14 and 22. If the Lord did not fulfil the things foretold in the law, what need was there for the apostle to call to mind the things from the law being fulfilled in the new covenant?

**42,12,3fi** Just as the Saviour too made it plain that he was the very one who at that time also spoke in the law and decreed against them by way of threat, saying, “Therefore I was provoked with this generation, and I said, ‘They always go astray in their heart’, and I swore, ‘They shall not enter into my rest’”; and therefore he also promised to speak to them in other tongues, as he did speak, and they did not enter.

**42,12,3fj** For he is found saying this to his disciples: “To you have been given the mysteries of the kingdom, but to them in parables, so that seeing they may not see”, and what follows.

**42,12,3fk** Everywhere, then, the things of the old are being fulfilled in the new; to anyone at all it is clear that the two covenants stand as belonging not to one god and another god, but to the same.

**42,12,3fl** Scholion 15 and 23. “Let the women be silent in church; for it is not permitted to them to speak, but let them be subject, as the law also says”.

**42,12,3fm** Refutation 15 and 23. If the holy apostle of God appoints good order for the holy church of God according to the law, the law is not disorderly, since it is from it that he carries over the good order; nor does the law belong to a strange god, since it subjected the woman to the man.

**42,12,3fn** For this is pleasing to the apostle too, in the legislation of the church, as he says: “as the law also says”.

**42,12,3fo** And where did the law say it, if not when God said to Eve straightway, “Your turning shall be to your husband, and he shall rule over you”?

**42,12,3fp** For even if in other places too, yet the beginning is from here.

**42,12,3fq** If, then, from that time the woman was subjected to the man by the command of God, and the apostle consistently subjects her, he himself too, giving the command not in opposition to the God who made the man and the woman, shows on every count that the apostle is lawgiver of the same God, whose was the law and the whole old covenant, and of the same God is the new covenant, that is, the two covenants, which both then and now subjected the woman to the man for the sake of a pious and well-balanced good order.

**42,12,3fr** Scholion 16 and 24. Concerning the resurrection of the dead: “Now I make known to you, brothers, the gospel which I preached to you”.

**42,12,3fs** And that “if Christ has not been raised, it is vain”, and what follows.

**42,12,3ft** “So we preach, and so you came to believe”, “that Christ died and was entombed, and has been raised on the third day”.

**42,12,3fu** “But when this mortal puts on immortality, then shall come to pass the word that is written: ‘Death has been swallowed up in victory’”.

**42,12,3fv** Refutation 16 and 24. “Now I make known to you, brothers, the gospel which I preached to you”.

**42,12,3fw** If he preached it, and makes the same known again, there is no other gospel nor any other knowledge after the single knowledge and the single gospel, which in fact is one through the four gospels and the apostles, so as to refute Marcion, who came so many years later, after the times of the one called Hyginus, the bishop of the Romans, who was ninth in succession from the consummation of the apostles Peter and Paul.

**42,12,3fx** Therefore the same apostle, making it doubly secure, said, “Even if we or an angel should preach to you contrary to what you received, let him be anathema”, knowing by the Holy Spirit that Marcion and those of his kind were going to pervert the way that had been well grounded.

**42,12,3fy** Hence he no longer said, “I preach the gospel to you”, but “I make known to you the gospel”, which is no other, but “which I have already preached to you”, of which I am putting you in remembrance.

**42,12,3fz** “I make known to you in what words I preached the gospel to you, if you hold it fast, unless you believed in vain”.

**42,12,3ga** For if you do not keep hold of it, as I proclaimed it to you, then without it you believed to no purpose.

**42,12,3gb** “For I proclaimed the gospel to you, that Christ died on behalf of our sins in accordance with the scriptures, and that on the third day he has been raised, in accordance with the scriptures”, and not according to a myth, nor according to the teaching of those who were going to speak of their own accord what is not written; for the Jews say of their own accord that he has not been raised, and Marcion and the others say of their own accord that he suffered and was buried in semblance, but I make you secure in accordance with the scriptures.

**42,12,3gc** For straightway he adds, saying, “So we preach, and so you came to believe, that Christ died and was entombed, and has been raised on the third day”, and straightway, “If the dead are not raised, neither has Christ been raised; and if Christ has not been raised, our preaching is vain”.

**42,12,3gd** And after all this: “For it is necessary that this mortal put on immortality, and that this corruptible put on incorruption”.

**42,12,3ge** And he did not speak of this mortal containing immortality, or of this corruptible containing incorruption, but of the mortal and corruptible putting on immortality and incorruption.

**42,12,3gf** And what is the mortal thing but the body, which has not only contained immortality in itself, but has also made it its own and is to put on immortality, not with the body cast away and the soul, which does not die, putting on immortality, but with immortality being put on by the mortal, and incorruption by the corruptible, that is, by the body?

**42,12,3gg** For to it belong both death and a temporary corruption, because of the dissolution brought upon it through the disobedience of Adam.

**42,12,3gh** And that he is speaking of the benefits which are to be perfected in it, he indicates through the promise that the fulfilment is [yet to come], saying, “Then shall come to pass the word that is written: ‘Death has been swallowed up in victory’”, in order to signify the resurrection of the dead which is to take place then; since death was swallowed up in part in the resurrection of Christ and of those who rose along with him (“for many of the saints’ bodies rose up,” the gospel says, “and entered the holy city together with him”), but then it is swallowed up in victory, when it is made to vanish from all universally.

**42,12,3gi** Of the Second Epistle to the Corinthians; this one stands third with Marcion, but in an altered position, because with him the Epistle to the Galatians has been placed first.

**42,12,3gj** Scholion 1 and 25. “For as many as are the promises of God, in him is the Yes; therefore also through him the Amen to God”.

**42,12,3gk** Refutation 1 and 25. Open your eyes and be saved, O Marcion.

**42,12,3gl** But if you can no longer do so (for you are dead), let those who have been deceived by you open their eyes and flee from you as from a dreadful serpent that does harm to those who come near.

**42,12,3gm** For if “as many as are the promises of God, in him is the Yes”, and the apostle knows promises of God through the law and the prophets, then in Christ the Yes of the promises of the fulfilment was confirmed.

**42,12,3gn** Christ, then, is not alien to the old law and the prophets, nor to the God who once spoke in the law and in Christ has fulfilled his own promises.

**42,12,3go** Nor indeed is Christ opposed to the God who gave the law and the prophets.

**42,12,3gp** For because the promised pledges become Yes in him, for this reason, he says, through him too is the Amen to God; God the Father having promised, and Christ both confirming and, as Lord, securing the Amen through himself among those who through him have been confirmed in the promise and have come to know his Father, who spoke in the law and in the gospel has bestowed salvation on those who have believed, among those who say it through him, since Christ says, “Yes, Father, for so it was well-pleasing in your sight”.

**42,12,3gq** Scholion 2 and 26. “For we do not preach ourselves, but Christ Jesus as Lord, and ourselves your servants for Jesus’ sake, because the God who said, ‘Light will shine from darkness’”.

**42,12,3gr** Refutation 2 and 26. It is not themselves that the apostles preach, but Christ Jesus as Lord.

**42,12,3gs** Therefore there is no sect, nor church, proclaimed in the name of apostles.

**42,12,3gt** For we have never heard of Petrians or Paulians or Bartholomaeans or Thaddaeans, but from the beginning there has been one preaching of all the apostles, which preaches not themselves but Christ Jesus as Lord.

**42,12,3gu** Therefore all of them also gave the church one name, not their own but that of their Lord Jesus Christ, since they began at Antioch to be called Christians; and this is the only catholic church, which has no other [name] but that of Christ, being the Christians’ church – not of Christs, rather of Christians – since he is one, while those who come from the one are called Christians.

**42,12,3gv** But after it, and after its heralds, all [...], no longer of the same character [...], appearing through the added names of Manichaeans and Simonians and Valentinians and Ebionites; of whom you too, Marcion, are one, and those who have been deceived by you have taken your name upon themselves, as of one who preached himself and not Christ.

**42,12,3gw** Then he says, “because the God who said, ‘Light will shine from darkness’”.

**42,12,3gx** And what God is this, but the One who in the prophet showed light out of darkness, that is, light and knowledge shining in Christ, out of the unbelief and ignorance of men, within our hearts, who were formerly Gentiles worshipping idols but now [have come to know] God, who then in the prophet promised beforehand that his own light would blaze forth in the world, since he is not alien to the old and the new covenant; as it has become our custom to persuade you, O Marcion, from the remnants of the gospel that you have in writing, and not to be deceived by you.

**42,12,3gy** Scholion 3 and 27. “And having the same spirit of faith, we too believe, and therefore we also speak”.

**42,12,3gz** But he has cut out “according to what is written”.

**42,12,3ha** Refutation 3 and 27. No room will be given you in anything you may dare.

**42,12,3hb** For even if you cut out “according to what is written”, the sequence of the expression written beforehand is apparent.

**42,12,3hc** For from “I believed, and therefore I spoke”, the apostle, straightway bringing forward the like, said, “Having the same spirit of faith, we too believe, and therefore we also speak”.

**42,12,3hd** And this is plain to everyone, that in the hundred and fifteenth psalm, the one that bears the title Alleluia and belongs to the book of David and was prophesied by him, the saying “I believed” is written.
