**42,12,3he** From there the apostle too took the saying, and likewise said, “Therefore we too believe and speak”, being one of the number of the apostles; hence he does not say, “Therefore I too believed and spoke”, but “we believe, and therefore we also speak”, in order to join himself with the other apostles.

**42,12,3hf** And he says, “And having the same spirit”, in order to make plain that the Spirit who spoke in David is the same Spirit in the apostles too, from whom he then, prophesying, believed, and being in which Spirit they too believe and speak.

**42,12,3hg** But great, so to speak, is the injustice and greed of [...] thoughts.

**42,12,3hh** For when the apostle declares one and the same Spirit, how does the folly of Marcion, which admits that the holy apostle spoke this saying, dare to say that the Spirit then was one, and the Spirit in the apostles another?

**42,12,3hi** Of the fourth epistle, that to the Romans; for so it stands with Marcion, so that nothing with him may be right.

**42,12,3hj** Scholion 1 and 28. “All who sinned without the law will also perish without the law, and all who sinned in the law will be judged through the law.

**42,12,3hk** For it is not those who hear the law who are righteous in God’s sight, but those who do the law will be justified”.

**42,12,3hl** Refutation 1 and 28. If all who sinned without the law will also perish without the law, the law, when it is kept, is a contributing cause of salvation and does not let those who keep it perish.

**42,12,3hm** And if those who have sinned through the law will be judged through the law, then the law is the judge of transgressions, being a matter not of destruction but of righteous judgement, since it judges the transgressors in holiness.

**42,12,3hn** “For it is not those who hear the law who are righteous in God’s sight, but those who do the law will be justified”.

**42,12,3ho** But if the law, when it is done, justifies him who has done it, the law is neither unjust nor base, through which those who fulfil the law are made righteous.

**42,12,3hp** And from the law comes also the faith prophesied as directed to Christ, without whom no one will be justified, and having believed in whom contrary to the testimony prophesied [by] the law, no one can be justified either, since Christ is the fulfilment of the law, according to what was said by the apostle, “Christ is the fulfilment of the law for righteousness”, who shows that without law and Christ there is no righteousness.

**42,12,3hq** For neither will the Jews be justified without Christ, if they have not received Christ, nor will you, Marcion, be justified while you deny the law.

**42,12,3hr** Scholion 2 and 29. “For circumcision indeed profits, if you practise the law; but if you are a transgressor of the law, your circumcision has become uncircumcision”.

**42,12,3hs** Refutation 2 and 29. If the holy apostle pronounces that circumcision will bring benefit, who would find fault with things that bring benefit, unless indeed he is to become like the serpent?

**42,12,3ht** For you resemble him, O Marcion; for he too, turning round the things spoken by God, led Eve astray, saying, “You shall not die the death”.

**42,12,3hu** For he bound the law together with circumcision, and showed circumcision to be in keeping with the law, and made it plain that the command belongs to the same God, who once gave circumcision and gave the law for help, from whom Christ, believed in, grants believers both to speak and to do perfect things.

**42,12,3hv** Scholion 3 and 30. “Having the form of knowledge and of truth in the law”.

**42,12,3hw** Refutation 3 and 30. If knowledge has a form, and from the form the likeness appears, and the apostles who have the knowledge and the truth, and their disciples, know that they have acquired from the form of the law its likeness, that is, the knowledge and the truth, then the law is not alien to knowledge and truth.

**42,12,3hx** For through the form present in it the truth’s heralds recognised the knowledge and the truth.

**42,12,3hy** Scholion 4 and 31. “For while we were yet weak, Christ yet died at the right time for the ungodly”.

**42,12,3hz** Refutation 4 and 31. The word “yet” and [the word] “died” signify not semblance but truth.

**42,12,3ia** For if it were semblance, why would “yet” need saying, since Christ could always, both then and now, appear in semblance, and “while we were yet weak” need not be said?

**42,12,3ib** For from “yet”, his dying at that time [...] he paid and justified by his death, so that he who once died for sinners, and does not die on his own account, should no longer have need to die.

**42,12,3ic** Scholion 5 and 32. “So that the law, for its part, is holy, and the commandment is holy and just and good”.

**42,12,3id** Refutation 5 and 32. He agrees with the law, in its being holy, and with the commandment given in it, securing this with three testimonies, calling it holy and just and good, in order to refute you, Marcion, and to teach us that the law belongs to the Holy One, whose commandment too is holy, and that he himself is the Holy One and good.

**42,12,3ie** Therefore, being the commandment of one who is good, it is called good, and being of one who is holy, it is holy, and being of one who is just, it is called a just commandment, since he who [...] then and now is one, he who is holy and just and good.

**42,12,3if** Therefore his commandment too, which from then and now is in the law and in the new covenant, is holy and just and good.

**42,12,3ig** Scholion 6 and 33. “So that the law’s just requirement might be fulfilled in us”.

**42,12,3ih** Refutation 6 and 33. If in the apostles and in us the law’s just requirement is fulfilled, how do you, O Marcion, dare to say that the law is alien to the apostles of God, who are justified according to the fulfilment of the law?

**42,12,3ii** Scholion 7 and 34. “For Christ is the end of the law for righteousness to everyone who believes”.

**42,12,3ij** Refutation 7 and 34. If Christ has come for righteousness to everyone who believes, since the law is not perfected unless Christ perfects it by his own coming, then you will not be perfected, O Jews, if you stay within the law, unless you put your faith in Christ who has sojourned among you and receive him.

**42,12,3ik** But neither will you, Marcion, be able to be saved in Christ, since you cast away the beginning and the root of the preaching, which is the law, from which Christ, being known, perfects him who does not abhor the law as being alien to Christ.

**42,12,3il** Scholion 8 and 35. “For he who loves his neighbour has fulfilled the law”.

**42,12,3im** Refutation 8 and 35. If the law is fulfilled through loving one’s neighbour, the law that commands us to love the neighbour is no stranger to Christ, nor to the God who is Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, nor to God; because God is love, and all the things proclaimed by him are always proclaimed alike, both then and now, both in the old and in the new covenant.

**42,12,3in** Of the [First] to the Thessalonians, the fifth epistle (for so it stands in Marcion), but the eighth in the apostle: since Marcion has everything from it in perverted form, we have set out nothing from it.

**42,12,3io** Of the Second to the Thessalonians, which stands sixth with Marcion but is ninth in the apostle: since it has likewise been perverted by Marcion himself, again we have set out nothing.

**42,12,3ip** Of the Epistle to the Ephesians, which is seventh with Marcion but stands fifth in the apostle, the following:

**42,12,3iq** Scholion 1 and 36. “Remembering, you who were once the Gentiles, who are called uncircumcision by what is called circumcision, done in the flesh by hand, that in that season you were apart from Christ, estranged from the commonwealth of Israel and strangers to the covenants of the promise, having no hope and godless within the world; yet now in Christ Jesus you who once were far off have become near in his blood.

**42,12,3ir** For he is our peace, who has made both one”, and what follows.

**42,12,3is** Refutation 1 and 36. The word “remembering” signifies the character of a time, and [the words] “who are called by what is called” signify the types of the realities, and so does “in the flesh”, in order to show the type in the flesh awaiting the time of the Spirit, so that he may show the more perfect things from the type.

**42,12,3it** For without Christ the uncircumcision had been estranged from the commonwealth of Israel, and being estranged it was a stranger to the promise and the covenant, and those who came from it had no hope, but were godless in the world, as has been shown from the words of the apostle.

**42,12,3iu** But you, Marcion, neither see nor hear; otherwise you would understand of how many good things the holy apostle declares the law to have been a cause for those who lived in the law in that former time: “For in Christ Jesus you who were once far off [have become] near now in his blood. For he himself is our peace, the one who made both one.”

**42,12,3iv** And if he made both one, and did not abolish the one and establish the other, then neither is the former alien to him, nor did he divide the second from the first, but he gathered both into one, not loosely nor in semblance, but manifestly in his blood, as the sure teaching of the apostle shows.

**42,12,3iw** Scholion 2 and 37. “Therefore it says: ‘Awake, you who sleep, and arise from the dead, and Christ will shine upon you’”.

**42,12,3ix** Refutation 2 and 37. Whence did the apostle get “therefore it says”, if not plainly from the old covenant?

**42,12,3iy** And this occurs in Elijah.

**42,12,3iz** And from where did Elijah set out?

**42,12,3ja** Surely he was one among the prophets who conducted their lives according to the law, setting out from the law and the prophets.

**42,12,3jb** And if he prophesied in Christ the words, “Awake, you who sleep, and arise from the dead, and Christ will shine upon you”, then the original pattern had been fulfilled through Lazarus and the others, about whom those very persons were in doubt, Martha and Mary saying, “He already stinks, he is four days dead”, and the people of the ruler of the synagogue saying, “Trouble the Teacher no further”, and he himself saying, “Do not be afraid; for she has not died, but sleeps”.

**42,12,3jc** For clearly from that time the preaching proclaimed that there would be a resurrection through Christ, and showed the ease that belongs to authority, in order to show the power of Christ: that just as for a man it is an easy thing to rouse with his voice, not one who has died, but one who sleeps, so too for Christ it was the readiest thing to say, “Lazarus, come out”, and “Koumi koumi talitha”, that is, arise, little girl.

**42,12,3jd** Through these [...] and manifest proofs the word signified our calling from dead works and from the heavy sleep of us who once slept, [and] Christ arousing and, through the calling, enlightening us; and this has become the second state.

**42,12,3je** But the perfect and universal one is awaited, when he himself who says, “I am the resurrection”, shall call all and raise them in body and soul and enlighten them at his coming that is to be.

**42,12,3jf** Scholion 3 and 38. “On this account a man will forsake his father and his mother and will cleave to his wife, and the two will become one flesh”, without “to his wife”.

**42,12,3jg** Refutation 3 and 38. Often has it been demonstrated that the things in the law are not alien to the things taught by the apostle, even if you, O Marcion, cut out “to [his] wife”.

**42,12,3jh** For from “they shall become one flesh” your whole knavery will be plain.

**42,12,3ji** Of the Epistle to the Colossians, which stands eighth with Marcion, but stands seventh in the apostle:

**42,12,3jj** Scholion 1 and 39. “Let no one, then, judge you in eating or in drinking or in respect of a feast or new moon and sabbaths, which is a shadow of the things to come”.

**42,12,3jk** Refutation 1 and 39. A shadow falls from nowhere, O Marcion, except from a body, and a body cannot exist without a shadow appearing from it.

**42,12,3jl** Hence those who have been deceived by you ought to be persuaded, through the remnants of the truth of the divine scriptures still preserved by you, that the ordinances of that time, enjoined for a season concerning eating and drinking and in respect of feasts and new moons and sabbaths, were not alien to the good things that were to be revealed; of which good things the things mentioned before were shadows, and through these shadows we have laid hold of the body of the present good things, which were sketched in shadow in the law but perfected in Christ.

**42,12,3jm** To Philemon, the ninth: for that is its place with Marcion, whereas in the apostle it is placed last; and in some copies it is put thirteenth, before the Epistle to the Hebrews, the fourteenth, while in other copies the Epistle to the Hebrews comes tenth, ahead of the two to Timothy, and Titus and Philemon.

**42,12,3jn** But all the sound and true copies have the Epistle to the Romans first, not as you, Marcion, placed the Epistle to the Galatians first.

**42,12,3jo** Nevertheless, from this Epistle to Philemon we have set out nothing, because with him it stands entirely in perverted form.

**42,12,3jp** To the Philippians, the tenth: for so it stands with Marcion, last and tenth, but in the apostle sixth.

**42,12,3jq** Likewise from it too, because it stands with him in perverted form, we have selected nothing.

**42,12,3jr** Here is completed the compilation of Marcion, [that of the] remnants of sayings preserved from the Gospel according to Luke and from the Apostle, and of the argument; from which, having gathered the things against him from what is preserved by him, we have set out the refutations.

**42,12,3js** And in what is called his own Apostolicon he also added from the epistle known as the one to the Laodiceans:

**42,12,3jt** Scholion 1 and 40. “One is the Lord, one the faith, one the baptism, one the God and Father of all, the one over all and through all and in all”.

**42,12,3ju** Refutation 1 and 40. In harmony with the epistle to the Ephesians, O Marcion, you have gathered these testimonies against yourself as well from the one called To the Laodiceans, so that at the end of the treatise we may learn, by reading what is in your text, and, knowing what concerns you, may condemn the three beginningless principles strangely devised by you, which are at variance with one another.

**42,12,3jv** For the argument of the holy apostle and his secure preaching are not like this, but otherwise than your fabrication.

**42,12,3jw** For he clearly said, “one is the Lord, one the faith, one the baptism, one the God, the very same Father of all, the same over all, the same through all and in all”, both by means of the law and prophets and among all the apostles, and in those who followed them in order.

**42,13,1** This, then, is our [compilation], surveyed beforehand in what has been set out above, from the passages gathered out of the scripture still preserved with Marcion himself; and anyone who considered the selection made from it would be struck with amazement at the dispensations of the bountiful God.

**42,13,2** For since every matter is confirmed and established through three testimonies, how is it that God has here simply granted us, by his dispensation, to join together (as I said) seventy-eight testimonies from the gospel and forty from the Apostle?

**42,13,3** And these are still preserved with him even to this day and are [not] disputed, so that they come to a hundred and eighteen in all, and all of them contradict the opinion of Marcion himself, as though from the person of the name of the Lord through the eighteen, and of his blessing at the right hand through the hundred.

**42,13,4** But also from another, a further one over and above these, [...] outside the gospel and the Apostle; for the most pitiable Marcion did not see fit to cite this testimony from the Epistle to the Ephesians, but from the one to the Laodiceans, which is not in the Apostle.

**42,13,5a** Foolishly failing to read these with care, the brutish wretch, who has fallen in many things, does not see the overthrow of himself that is acknowledged day after day.

**42,13,5b** And let no one wonder at this.

**42,13,6** For how could this man, professing to have something of the gospel and of the Apostle, fail to keep even a handful of the scripture’s words?

**42,13,7** For since the whole body of the divine scripture, so to speak, is alive, what member did he find dead, according to his own opinion, so as to bring in falsehood against the truth?

**42,13,8a** But, so to speak, he maimed it, and, mutilating it, cut away many of its members, while he kept some few of them with himself.

**42,13,8b** And the very parts that he kept, being still alive, cannot be made dead, but they retain the living force of their meaning, even if he should cut them away piece by piece ten thousand times over.

**42,14,1** Further, after all this, we have recalled that some of these same Marcionites, having fallen into an abyss of blasphemy and become altogether thunderstruck by their own demonic teaching, and, with unbridled tongue, perverting in yet another way the divinity of the very Lord whom they seemed at least to mention by name alone, are not ashamed to disparage the name of his generation from above.

**42,14,2** For some of them, as I said, have had the audacity to say without shame that the Lord himself is the son of the evil one, and others not so, but of the judge and creator;

**42,14,3** [and] that, having become more compassionate and being good, he left his own father below (some of them saying the creator, others the evil one), and ran up on high to the good God in places that cannot be named, and attached himself to him;

**42,14,4a** and that Christ, sent by him into the world and having come to contend against his own father, destroyed all that his natural father had legislated, whether the one who spoke in the law or the god of wickedness, who is ranked by them as the third principle.

**42,14,4b** For they set it out in different ways, as I said, one saying the creator, another the evil one.

**42,15,1** These things are plain to everyone possessed of understanding: that the thinking and the teaching are those of an unclean spirit, and that there was no need either to make a defence on this subject or to bring forward a refutation against Marcion, who in every respect has brought upon himself forgetfulness of life.

**42,15,2** For his blasphemous empty prating and his reckless work of perdition will be detected by all who have understanding.

**42,15,3** But since we are accustomed not to give thorns room, but to cut them out with the sword of God, “which is [sharper] than any two-edged sword and passes through to the division of soul and spirit, of both joints and marrow,” as it is written, we shall not shrink from saying a little on this too.

**42,16,1a** For, first, if a good one has been born of an evil one, the doctrine of the principles will no longer stand.

**42,16,1b** For his father too, if he is evil (may it never be so; rather, he is good), can change his mind, just as the one begotten by him has changed.

**42,16,2** And the second point: if the Only-begotten came at all to save men, and to bring a robber into paradise, and to call a tax-collector from the tax office to repentance, and to heal of her fornication a harlot who anointed his feet, and further does [other] good things besides, because he is good and compassionate and merciful,

**42,16,3** much more, then, ought he to have extended his pity to his own father and to have brought him first over to a saving state, so that, by first changing his father, he might fully display his perfect goodness in having pity on his father; as it is also written, “to do good first to those who are members of the faith’s household”, by how much the more to have mercy on their own fathers.

**42,16,4** And I have further added the other point to my statement and to the refutation against Marcion: that if he is the son of the one and has fled for refuge to the other, he will not be received as trustworthy by the other.

**42,16,5** For he who did not keep good conscience towards his own father will not be trusted by the other either, because of the things he had done before.

**42,16,6** But again in yet another way: will you once more accuse the good God, Marcion, of having been unable, for want of someone to send, to save those who are being saved by him and have been shown mercy by him through Christ?

**42,16,7** For if, according to Marcion’s account, Christ had not fled to the God above, the good God would have been at a loss for anyone to send, unless the father of Christ, as Marcion says, had come into conflict with his own son.

**42,16,8** And in another way too: if he is the son of the creator, yet sets himself against the creation and his father’s work, then on coming into the world he ought first to have destroyed men, so as to do away with the work fashioned by his own father, as one who had become an adversary of his father’s ordering;

**42,16,9** or else, having received power of healing and cure and salvation, he ought before all things to have shown the work of his loving-kindness towards his own father, and first to have persuaded his father to become like him, so that, he being good both to himself and to his father, one and the same goodness might become [the cause] of salvation for men.

**42,16,10a** But the matter does not stand as this man’s idly prattled, fabulous account [...].

**42,16,10b** For there are neither three principles, nor is there another father of Christ, nor is he the offspring of wickedness;

**42,16,11a** may it never be.

**42,16,11b** For he says, “I am within the Father, and the Father within me.”

**42,16,11c** And if he says that he has a father in a spurious sense, the one called his spurious father cannot be in him, nor he himself in the father.

**42,16,12** But teaching, and speaking truly, that his Father by nature is ever good and ever God and the maker of all, who is within him and exists together with him, he sets out the threat against Marcion, saying, “He who does not honour the Son as he honours the Father, the wrath of God abides upon him.”

**42,16,13a** And by many testimonies we have already shown that the one God, the Father of Jesus Christ our Lord, being good, begot one who is good, being love, begot love, being a fountain of life, begot a fountain of life (“For with you is the fountain of life,” says the scripture), being truth, begot truth, being light, begot light, being life, begot life, without beginning, eternally and timelessly.

**42,16,13b** And Marcion’s error has been refuted in every respect.

**42,16,14** Since these things are so, then, and the overthrow of the deceiver has been accomplished by us in God through a full and true demonstration, now that this one has been trampled down like a great asp by the unlying word of the Saviour, who said, “I have given you to tread upon serpents and scorpions and upon all the power of the enemy,” let us go on again to the next, and set ourselves to examine and refute the follies of the others as well, calling upon God as our helper in all things.

### Sect 43: Lucianists

Against the Lucianists, the twenty-third, but the forty-third in the series.

**43,1,1** There was a certain Lucian of old — not the one who lived recently, in the times of Constantine the elder, whom the Arians, if you please, vote in among the martyrs; for this Lucian too, the recent one I mean, adhered to the heresy of the Arians, and we shall tell of him afterwards, in our overthrow of them; but now our account is directed at the ancient Lucian mentioned above.

**43,1,2** For this man, being a follower of the aforementioned Marcion and splitting away from him, himself also made a gathering for himself and became the head of a sect, from whom come those called the ancient Lucianists.

**43,1,3a** In every respect, then, he holds the doctrines of Marcion, but he makes use, as the report that has reached us and the account concerning him [...].

**43,1,3b** For since these men are ancient and were quickly extinguished, the investigation concerning them has become difficult for us, as one may truly say.

**43,1,3c** And what we have learned in part of his teachings is as follows:

**43,1,4** after supposedly establishing that the creator, the judge and just one, is one, and likewise the good one another, and the evil one another, he wishes, like Marcion, to make use also of certain testimonies from the scripture of the prophets in accordance with his own notion — I mean the saying “Vain is he who serves the Lord,” and the one that says, “They opposed God and were saved.”

**43,1,5** But this man, going beyond the teaching of his master, rejects marriage altogether and practises chastity, not for chastity’s sake but in order to set aside the works of the creator, teaching that one should not join in marriage — “for from marriage,” he says, “there comes abundance in the world for the creator through the begetting of children” — in opposition to the abundance of the creator and maker in the world through the begetting of children.

**43,1,6** But such a man will be detected and refuted, first, by the rebuttal already made against his master, since we have at length made our reply to him and his overthrow, as to the kind and the number of points in which the gospel agrees with the Old Testament,

**43,1,7** and how our Lord himself confesses that the workmanship of the world belongs to him also, and that the creation is the Father’s, as is shown above all, to put it most briefly, by a most pre-eminent saying spoken in the holy John: “In the beginning the Word was, and the Word was in the presence of God, and the Word was God. Through him all things came to be, and apart from him not even one thing came to be that has come to be,” and so on.

**43,2,1a** But such a man will be overthrown at once.

**43,2,1b** For even if he should wish to say that it is for the sake of opposing and pulling down the things made here by the creator that the union of marriage is rejected by him, so that he may not work together with the creator, and so as to abstain altogether from the creator’s work, how is his notion not irrational, and easily detected, and readily refuted?

**43,2,2** For see, from the creation and the creator’s handiwork, from foods and clothing, the sorcerer and charlatan seeks the use of drinks and foods, and in no way will he be able to escape making use of these things.

**43,2,3** For God, the master of all and creator, who cares for all, makes his sun rise on the evil and the good, and sends his rain on those who blaspheme him and on those who glorify him, and feeds them all,

**43,2,4** not by some irrational and insensible decree, but showing long-suffering, with the retribution appointed by him at the judgment to come, and administering all things with decrees of his own and with wisdom, as befits his benefaction towards all, so that those who repent, obtaining the amnesty that comes from him, may gain salvation;

**43,2,5** but should they continue in their blasphemous notions and follies, which were not given them by God, then, after their departure from here, when their free will no longer remains with them, [...] but bringing upon them neither out of anger the judgment that will then take place, nor the things to come as one acting in wrath [...], —

**43,2,6** for he foretold all things, because of his Godhead, which is not contained within passions —, but rather that through which each one of [...], by doing something to his own hurt, has become the cause of it to himself [...], God being blameless for our turning aside and for the condemnation that will come upon us from it.

**43,2,7** In all things, then, this man too will be detected, intermingled with the earlier and the later sects, being of the number of “the children of perdition,” as the truth and the light of the gospel make plain, which shines brightly over the whole world and saves the sons of the true faith in truth.

**43,2,8** So then, having killed with a quick aim, by means of some small implement, a snake that had peeped out, [and] having left it lifeless, let us go on to what follows, according to our promise, taking God’s help along with us for setting forth his truth.

### Sect 44: Apelleans

Against the Apelleans, the twenty-fourth, but the forty-fourth in the series.

**44,1,1** This aforementioned Lucian is succeeded by Apelles — not that holy one commended by the holy apostle, but another, from whom come the Apelleans — he too being a fellow student of Lucian himself and a disciple of the aforementioned Marcion, like the offshoots of many thorns grown wild from a single root.

**44,1,2** But this man wishes to teach other doctrines than the rest; and, taking up arms against the truth as well as against his own teacher, so that he too might gather to himself a school of people led astray, he wishes to teach such doctrines as these, saying that things did not

**44,1,3** so come about, he says, but Marcion has gone astray — so that folly and lawlessness, refuting themselves on every side, may be seen to be crushed within themselves and to raise up the overthrow against themselves, while the truth is ever firm and has no need of help, but is self-established and is ever commended with God, who truly [is].

**44,1,4** This aforementioned Apelles, at any rate, and those who derive from him, say that there are not three principles, nor two, as seemed good to the followers of Lucian and Marcion; rather, he says, there is a single good God and a single principle and a single power that is unnameable; and this one God, that is, the one principle, has no concern for anything that has come into being here in this world.

**44,1,5** But the same holy God above, the good one, made one other god; and this other god who came into being created all things, heaven and earth and all that is in the world.

**44,1,6** But he turned out not good, and the things that came into being through him, he says, were not wrought well, but it is according to his base intent that the things [...] by him have been created.

**44,1,7a** But who will put up with such talk, and not rather laugh at such futile labour?

**44,1,7b** For in two ways he will be found to be acting unfittingly in using such a notion.

**44,1,8a** And for this reason I will speak to him as though he were present: tell me, you there.

**44,1,8b** For you will grant, Apelles, either that God, not knowing the future, made the god who you say fashioned his works badly, or that, while foreknowing that the god created by him would turn out such, he made him for this very purpose, so that he himself might not be the cause of the things badly fashioned by that god.

**44,1,9** And in any case the God above will himself be the creator, having made the one who made all things; and the one who made the creatures will no longer be the cause, but the God above, who made the maker and is himself the creator of all things.

**44,2,1** And he says that Christ came at the end of the times, being the Son of the good God above, and in the same way his Holy Spirit, for the saving of those who arrive at knowledge of him,

**44,2,2** and that when he came he did not appear in semblance, but took flesh in truth — not from Mary the virgin, but he had true flesh and a body, [yet] neither from the seed of a man nor from a virgin woman.

**44,2,3** Rather, he had true flesh indeed, [but] in this way: and, he says, as he was coming from the heavenly places he came to the earth and gathered to himself a body from the four elements.

**44,2,4a** And how is this man too not hastening to have his lawlessness detected, following as it does the opinions of the ancient Greek poets about this empty prattle?

**44,2,4b** For he too says, as they did, and speaking still more frigidly than they, that the Saviour formed the body for himself.

**44,2,5** For, [taking] the dry from the dry, and the hot from the hot, and the moist from the moist, and the cold from the cold, and so moulding a body for himself, he appeared truly in the world and taught us the knowledge from above,

**44,2,6a** and to despise the creator and to renounce his works, having shown us in what sort of scripture which sayings are by nature spoken from him, and which are those from the creator.

**44,2,6b** “For thus,” he says, “he said in the gospel, ‘Become approved money-changers’; for I make use,” he says, “of every scripture, gathering from it what is useful.”

**44,2,7** Then, he says, Christ gave himself to suffer in that very body, and he was crucified in truth, and was buried in truth, and rose in truth, and he let his own disciples see the flesh itself.

**44,2,8** And having dissolved, he says, his very incarnation, he distributed it again, rendering to each of the elements its own, the hot to the hot, the cold to the cold, the dry to the dry, the moist to the moist; and having thus again dissolved the fleshly body from himself, he flew up to heaven, whence he had also come.

**44,3,1** And oh, the great play-acting of those who say such things, so that it would be plain to everyone that they possess a workshop of mimes rather than the character of a promise of life or of understanding!

**44,3,2** For if he dissolved at all the very body which he had taken, for what reason did he prepare it for himself from the beginning?

**44,3,3** And if he prepared it for some use, and completed the work of that use, he ought to have left it on the earth, especially since, according to you, the form of the hope in the resurrection of the flesh has no need to be fulfilled.

**44,3,4** But he raised it again, in order to throw himself into greater toil, so as to gain nothing by it, preparing it, and laying it in a tomb, and dissolving it, and distributing to each of the elements what he had received from it, like a grateful debtor.

**44,3,5** And if he did indeed render to each [its own], that is, the cold to the cold and the hot to the hot, these things could have been unseen by his disciples, but certainly not the dry body.

**44,3,6** For the dry is certainly body, flesh and bones, and the moist is certainly serum and flesh dissolving into moisture; and these he would surely have made known most plainly to the apostles in laying them aside,

**44,3,7** just as at first, at the burial of his body, Joseph of Arimathea was counted worthy to wrap it in a linen cloth and lay it in a tomb.

**44,3,8** And at the same time the women too would have been able to see where the remains were left, so as to honour them with ointments and spices, as at first.

**44,3,9a** But nowhere has this lie of yours been revealed, you Apelleans, by a single one among the holy apostles; for there is no such thing.

**44,3,9b** But they saw, visibly, the two men who are invisible, and him going up into heaven and taken up by a shining cloud, but no remains of him left anywhere; for it was neither needful nor possible.

**44,3,9c** And Apelles lies, and the Apelleans who derive from him.

**44,4,1** And concerning the rest of flesh and the other matters he taught likewise the same things as his master Marcion, saying that there is no rising of the dead, and the other things that [...] on the earth, he saw fit to teach likewise.

**44,4,2a** But his reasoning will be overthrown, being a kind of nonsense and astray in every way.

**44,4,2b** For neither will darkness prevail where the light shines forth, nor will falsehood stand [...] when the truth is present.

**44,4,3** For if you make use of the scriptures at all, Apelles, and you Apelleans who derive from you, you will be found to be refuted by the scriptures themselves:

**44,4,4** first, that God made man after the image of God, and he who made him said, “Let us make man after our image and likeness”; so that if anyone should turn back from your erring sect towards the truth, like one who has escaped from darkness and risen up out of night, he would discover that the light of God’s knowledge had dawned upon him like the sun, and above the sun.

**44,4,5** For it will be plain to everyone possessed of sound reasoning that the one who says “Let us make man” is the God who is Father of the universe; and he calls together with himself God the Word, who is ever with him, the only-begotten Son, begotten of him without beginning and timelessly, and with him also his Holy Spirit, who is not alien to him nor to his own Son.

**44,4,6** For if he who moulded man – that is, who also made the world – had been one being, and the good God above, from whom Christ came down, another, Christ would not have been taking a body to himself and moulding it, stamping the creator’s image upon himself anew.

**44,4,7** But it is plain that he himself is the creator of man and of the world, he to whom the Father spoke the words, “Let us make man after our image and likeness”;

**44,4,8** and from the one work the craftsman, made manifest, will be shown to be the one who then made man, both moulding the body of Adam from the earth and making it a living soul.

**44,4,9** Therefore the holy John too bore witness in the holy gospel, when he said, “In the beginning the Word was, and the Word was in the presence of God, and the Word was God. This one was in the presence of God in the beginning; through him all things came to be, and apart from him not even one thing came to be,” and so on.

**44,4,10** And if [all things] came into being in him and came into being by him, he himself then moulded Adam, and he himself again re-moulded into himself the body from Mary the virgin, and perfectly united his whole incarnation, which was then moulded by him and is now united in himself.

**44,4,11a** But if he took into himself the alien workmanship of one who moulded his moulding badly and who is evil, according to your teaching, and if he made use at all of the evil works which, according to you, the evil maker wrought, then he became mingled with the maker’s wickedness, through use and benefaction, and through his own image.

**44,4,11b** But that is not possible.

**44,4,12a** For if he became man, he took not only flesh but also a soul.

**44,4,12b** For this will be plain; since otherwise, on what ground did he say, “I have authority to take my soul and to lay it down”?

**44,4,13** Having taken on, then, the whole reality, the image spoken of by the creator, the Word took on the whole reality, coming in body and soul and in all the things of which man consists.

**44,4,14** And these things having thus been accomplished, your poison has been utterly dimmed, and your unfounded position has fallen, since it has no fixity of a foundation of truth.

**44,5,1** And if you take what you please from the divine scripture and leave what you please, then you have set yourself up beforehand as a judge, not an interpreter of the laws but a selector of things that were not written according to your mind, but which are true, though falsely altered by you according to the mind of your deceit and of those deceived by you.

**44,5,2a** And if an evil maker wrought the things here at all — I mean the world — for what reason did the one from the good Father come into this age?

**44,5,2b** And if it was to save men, then he cared for his own, and the creator would no longer be another;

**44,5,3** but if it was not his own that he was caring for, and he wishes to intrude upon what belongs to others and to save what is not his, then either he is a flatterer, fawning upon what belongs to others, or vainglorious, so as to appear superior to the maker in the things belonging to others that he tries to save, coveting what is not his own, and he will no longer be true;

**44,5,4** or else it is because, according to you, charlatan, he is of slender means, and, having no creation of his own, he desires what belongs to others and tries to plunder it, acquiring for himself from what belongs to others souls that are not his nor his Father’s.

**44,5,5** But if the souls are his and are seen to be arrivals from above, they were therefore sent into a good world by the God above, who according to you is good, and not into an evil work.

**44,5,6** But if they were sent for some one purpose, about which perhaps you are making up a fable, but on entering fell away into another — that is, they were sent to do something just, but did evil — then the one who sent them will be seen to have no foreknowledge; since he sent them for one thing, and they were found to have done something else.

**44,5,7** Or, again, should you claim that it was not by his will that they came, but through the tyranny of the one who snatches them away, then the evil creator, created by the good God, is stronger than the good God, since he snatched away what was his and made use of it.

**44,5,8** And how will you escape refutation, when the Saviour himself says, “I have authority to lay down my soul and take it up”, since it was he who took a soul and laid it down and took it back again, which shows that the soul is not alien nor the work of another creator?

**44,5,9a** And again, he will be found to be wearing a good body.

**44,5,9b** For a good being would not be persuaded to make use of an evil work, lest, through sharing in a part of the evil, he too should be defiled by the wickedness of the mixture.

**44,5,10** And if he raised it too, tell me, to do what after the resurrection has he also left it again, distributing it to the four elements, the hot to the hot, and the cold to the cold, and the dry to the dry, and the moist to the moist?

**44,5,11a** For if he raised it in order to dissolve it again, then this would be a work of play-acting and not of truth.

**44,5,11b** But our Lord Jesus Christ raised it, and took along with him what he had re-moulded into himself, body with soul and the whole incarnation, most perfectly;

**44,5,12** for since he was seated together, according to the apostolic word, “God raised up and seated together in the heavenly places,” as the two who appeared to the apostles in shining raiment bear witness, as they say, “Men of Galilee, why do you stand gazing into heaven? This Jesus, who was taken up from you into heaven, will come in the same manner as you beheld him being taken up.”

**44,6,1** And so that no pretext for wickedness against the truth may be given you again, a long time after the taking up of our Saviour, Stephen, the holy martyr of God, when he was being stoned by the Jews, answered, saying, “See, I behold heaven opened wide, and the Son of Man standing on the Father’s right hand”,

**44,6,2** so as to show that the very body had in truth risen, with the Godhead of the Only-begotten, into what is spiritual, made wholly one into what is spiritual and joined into Godhead.

**44,6,3a** And above is the holy body itself, with the Godhead, wholly God, a single Son, God’s holy one, sitting on the Father’s right hand, as the Gospel of Mark too has it, and that of the other evangelists: “And he went up into the heavens and sat at the right hand of the Father.”

**44,6,3b** And on every side the charlatan account of yours, and of those led astray by you, will collapse.

**44,6,4** And on the rising of the dead, listen to the apostle when he says, “It is necessary that this corruptible put on incorruption, and that this mortal put on immortality.”

**44,6,5** For if the mortal were not to put on immortality and the corruptible put on incorruption, the immortal one would never have come for the purpose of dying, that he might suffer in it and, having fallen asleep, rise on the third day and bring it up along with him, united in himself with his Godhead and his glory, so that from his good sojourn among us we might hold fast all hope in truth, he showing himself to us as a pattern and a pledge for the hope of the whole reality of life.

**44,7,1a** These things being so, and having been said, what further need is there for me to wear myself out over this zopyrus wasp, a thing of no account, for the sake of overthrow or of any other treatment?

**44,7,1b** For of itself it has lost its sting, and its counterfeited doctrine of error has been shown to be a mere copy and the stuff of charlatans.

**44,7,2** For they say that the painful wasp, which some have called the zopyrus, has a short venomous sting, not possessing much power to cause pain, but venomous in proportion to its strength.

**44,7,3** But when someone passing by knocks down its hole or nest — for in thickets of plants it makes for itself certain hives like a comb, and in these hives it lays its young and produces its offspring — when, I say, someone passing by prods the comb with a rod or a stick, as I said, and knocks it down, out comes, enraged, this very wasp, fearsome indeed but feeble;

**44,7,4** and should it come upon a rock or a piece of wood close by, from the rage that fills it, it halts with a whirring and rushes on and strikes the rock; and it does no harm to the rock, nor yet to the wood, nor indeed to the man, even if it should sting him, except to cause him a little pain;

**44,7,5** and it does the rock no harm whatever, but loses its sting and so perishes, while the rock will suffer no harm from such a creature.

**44,7,6** So too this waspish sect, which like the zopyrus has but a small power to cause pain, will be overthrown, meeting with the rock, that is, the truth, and losing its sting.

**44,7,7** And having gone through this one, I will go on again to the others, trusting, in accordance with the hope of God, that the promise will be fulfilled in God.

### Sect 45: Severians

Against the Severians, the twenty-fifth, but the forty-fifth in the series.

**45,1,1a** After these in order follows [Severus], either a contemporary or about the time [...]; for I cannot speak with certainty about his time, except that they were close to one another.

**45,1,1b** Nevertheless I will relate what has come down to us.

**45,1,2a** A certain Severus arose, from whom come those called Severians, following next in order after the aforementioned Apelles.

**45,1,2b** And the fables found with him are these, which I am about to tell.

**45,1,3** He too wishes to connect our creation with principalities and authorities, and would have it that there is a certain good God in some unnameable and highest heaven and aeon.

**45,1,4** And he says the devil is son to the great ruler of the order of the authorities, to whom he gives the name sometimes Ialdabaoth, sometimes Sabaoth; and that this one, begotten of him, is a serpent.

**45,1,5** And that he was cast down from the power above to the earth, and, having come down, being serpent-like, was driven into frenzy and coupled with the earth just as with a woman, and that from him, when he emitted the seed of his generation, the vine sprang up.

**45,1,6** For this reason, telling fables to represent their nonsense, he [wishes] to portray the serpent-like coiling of the vine, saying that because of the crookedness of the plant it is like a serpent; and that the white kind is like a serpent, and the black like a dragon.

**45,1,7** And that the grapes of the vine are like drops, that is, droplets, of venom, because of the circular, or tapering and translucent, roundness of each grape.

**45,1,8a** And that wine, being of this origin, clouds the mind of mankind, and at one time leads it to the charms of sexual pleasure, at another rouses it to frenzy, and again stirs up anger, because the body is made giddy by the power that comes from the wine and by the venom of the aforementioned dragon.

**45,1,8b** Hence such people abstain from wine altogether.

**45,2,1a** And they say that woman too is the work of Satan, just as the Archontics also said this.

**45,2,1b** Therefore they say that those who come together in marriage fulfil the work of Satan.

**45,2,2a** And further, that of man the one half is God’s, and the other half the devil’s.

**45,2,2b** For he says that the moulding from the navel upwards is of the power of God, and that the moulding from the navel downwards is of the evil authority.

**45,2,3a** Therefore he says that everything having to do with pleasure and frenzy and desire arises from the navel and below.

**45,2,3b** But the other sects too have said this to be so.

**45,3,1a** Hence this man too is caught in every way, following the other charlatans who have prepared poisons for human life.

**45,3,1b** For he will be overthrown easily; for the overthrow of him will not need very much labour.

**45,3,2** For the whole body is blended with the things that have come into being well in it from God — I mean the appetites, which came from God not for any absurdity but for good use and for the ordering of necessary need (I mean the craving for sleep, for food, for drink, for clothing, and for everything else that befalls us for our own needs and is well-pleasing to God) — so that I could show that even the very appetite of bodily desire is not absurd.

**45,3,3** For it was given for the begetting of children in dignity, and for the glory of the one who made all things, just as seeds were given to the earth for the outpouring of a multitude of the good produce created by God, [...] I mean also of the fruits of trees, so also to mankind for the fulfilling of the saying, “Increase and multiply, and fill the earth.”

**45,4,1** And such people make use also of certain apocryphal writings, as we have heard, but also in part of the canonical books, hunting out phrases — only those which they twist according to their own mind and handle in a different sense.

**45,4,2a** For neither was the vine planted by the devil, nor did it get its seed from a serpent, as is plain to everyone.

**45,4,2b** For how might this be so, when the Lord himself bears witness and says, “I shall not drink from the produce of this vine until the day I drink it new in your company in the heavens’ kingdom”?

**45,4,3** And since the truth, flashing forth its rays in full, prefigured its words, in accordance with God’s foreknowledge of the evils that would be brought in, for their refutation, the divine writing everywhere foretold the overthrow of those who were going to rise in revolt against the truth;

**45,4,4a** as the Lord himself somewhere expressly says, for the refutation of the pitiable and deluded Severus, calling himself the vine: “I am the true vine.”

**45,4,4b** And if the name of the vine were at all blameworthy, he would not have applied the likeness of the name to himself.

**45,4,5** But the apostles too say in what is called the Ordinance, “The catholic church is God’s planting and vineyard”;

**45,4,6** and the Lord himself too, again, when in the gospel he tells the parable of the vineyard: “A certain man, a householder, had a vineyard, and let it out to farmers, and sent seeking fruit, and they did not give it,”

**45,4,7** and again: “A certain man, a householder, having a vineyard, went out seeking labourers for the vineyard, around the third hour and the sixth, the ninth and the eleventh”;

**45,4,8a** so that in every way the contrivance of this deceiver’s design too lies open on every side to being wounded by the word of truth.

**45,4,8b** For even if darkness appears when light is not present, through a small spark its vanishing by overthrow will come about.

**45,4,9** And I have dealt with the sect briefly, because, as I have already said, being easy to capture it needs no great labour for establishing the truth against it; and above all because I think that perhaps there are no longer any who derive from it, or else only a few in the uppermost regions.

**45,4,10** And passing on from this one, having trampled it down at a stroke like a fearsome scorpion, let us examine the next, calling upon the power of God for help, both to speak the truth and that I myself may not be harmed, especially since I am about to bring to light such fearsome and destructive depravity of doctrines.

### Sect 46: Tatianists

Against the Tatianists, the twenty-sixth, but the forty-sixth in the series.

**46,1,1** A certain Tatian arose, succeeding these, living either in their times or after them, again setting up his own teaching of empty prattle.

**46,1,2** And at first, inasmuch as he came from the Greeks and was of Greek education, he flourished at the same time as Justin the philosopher, a holy man and a friend of God, who had come to believe in Christ from among the Samaritans.

**46,1,3** For this Justin was a Samaritan by race, [but] having believed in Christ, and having been greatly trained, and having shown a life of virtue, at the end, bearing witness for Christ, he was deemed worthy of the perfect crown at Rome, under Rusticus the governor and Hadrian the emperor, being thirty years old, at a settled age.

**46,1,4** The aforementioned Tatian, flourishing together with him, was at first of good conduct and strong in the faith, for as long as he remained with the holy Justin the martyr.

**46,1,5** But when the holy Justin died, then, like a blind man led by the hand [who] should be abandoned by his guide and, being abandoned, gives himself over to a precipice because of the blindness that is upon him, and is borne down without check until he is carried down to death — so he too.

**46,1,6** And he was a Syrian by race, as the information that has reached us contains; and he set up his school at first in the land between the rivers, around the twelfth year of the Caesar Antoninus, who was surnamed Pius.

**46,1,7** For after the perfecting of the holy Justin he passed from Rome to the regions of the east, and while staying there, falling into an evil frame of mind, he too introduced certain aeons after the fables of Valentinus, and certain principles and emanations.

**46,1,8** And he established the greater part of his preaching from Antioch-by-Daphne and on into the regions of the Cilicians, but more especially in Pisidia; for from him, by succession, come those called Encratites, who have partaken of his venom.

**46,1,9** And the gospel through Four is said to have been made by him, which some call the Gospel according to the Hebrews.

**46,2,1a** This man too teaches the same things as the ancient sects.

**46,2,1b** And first, he says that Adam is not saved.

**46,2,1c** He preaches continence, and regards marriage as fornication and corruption, saying that marriage differs in no way from fornication, but is the same thing.

**46,2,2** Hence, under the pretence of continence and of an abstinent character, his way of life was that of a cheat at dice, like a ravening wolf putting on a sheep’s fleece and deceiving with a passing pretence those whom he leads astray.

**46,2,3** And he likewise makes use of mysteries, copying the holy church, though he uses water alone in those same mysteries.

**46,2,4a** But this man too will fall in every way, since his preaching is incoherent (and I think that by now he too has been extinguished, and his school has come to an end).

**46,2,4b** For on what ground will such a man not be refuted?

**46,2,5** For, first, in accordance with what we have shown above, against the sects too that speak in this way [...], that it is impossible for there to be many principles that generate the things begotten by succession.

**46,2,6a** For those that are many will be found to be also one, [inasmuch as] they derive from the one that is the cause of them all; and there are no longer many principles, but one, which has become their cause, and all things will be brought back to the one monarchy, and the plausible talk of such a man has collapsed [...] and is not truth, nor even able to keep its plausibility.

**46,2,6b** For the whole preaching is foolish.

**46,2,7a** And if Adam, the lump, is not saved, neither is anything [of what comes from] the lump saved.

**46,2,7b** For if the first-formed, who came into being from untouched earth, will not share in salvation, how will those begotten of him have salvation?

**46,3,1a** For in two ways he will be found to be speaking against himself.

**46,3,1b** For he says that marriage is not from God, but that it is fornication and defilement; and yet he thinks that he himself, who derives from marriage and was born of a woman and from the seed of a man, is saved.

**46,3,2a** So then he has again undone his own blasphemy against marriage.

**46,3,2b** For if he, who derives from marriage, will have salvation, then marriage, which begets those who share in salvation, is not an abomination, in whatever way he might wish to speak of it.

**46,3,3** But if his account should stand, that marriage is unlawful, then Adam will all the more be saved, since he did not originate from marriage, but was moulded in the hand of the Father and Son and Holy Spirit, according to what was said by the holy Father himself to the Son, “Let us make man after our image and likeness.”

**46,3,4** And how is Adam, whom you despair of, not saved, when our Lord Jesus Christ himself, having come into the world, raises the dead after their death in that very body, as he did Lazarus and the son of the widow, and the synagogue ruler’s daughter?

**46,3,5** And if it was not he who moulded Adam from the dust in the beginning, how did he spit on the ground and make clay and put it on the eyes of the man blind from birth and make him see,

**46,3,6a** so as to show that he himself is the moulder together with the Father and the Holy Spirit, supplying through the clay the deficient part in the man born blind from birth, in the place that was lacking?

**46,3,6b** For it is plain that he did this for the correction of the part that was lacking.

**46,3,7** But if, again, it is the Lord himself who moulded Adam, and he himself destroys the first-formed while he saves the others — oh, how great is your folly, Tatian!

**46,3,8** For you attach to the Lord a want of power, as regards his might, as one unable to save, through the repentance that leads to remembrance, his first-formed, who for one disobedience was cast out of paradise and partook of no ordinary chastening, who lived out his days in sweat and toil and dwelt opposite paradise, so that he might remember the good [...];

**46,3,9a** or [...] as one able indeed, but not having mercy.

**46,3,9b** For what reason, then, did Christ go down even to the regions beneath the earth, and to what purpose, having suffered, did he sleep for three days and rise?

**46,3,9c** And how is the saying fulfilled, “that he might be Lord of both the dead and the living”?

**46,3,9d** And of what living and dead, but those who need his help above and below?

**46,3,10a** And how will the things from the lump be holy if the lump itself has no holiness, as is also set forth in the holy apostle?

**46,3,10b** For the same apostle also says about Eve, “She will be saved through childbearing, if they remain in faith and righteousness.”

**46,4,1** And there is much to say about these things, so that to everyone possessed of understanding the ready blasphemy and the darkened mind of Tatian, and of those called Tatianists who derive from him, will be plain.

**46,4,2** Having gone briefly through what concerns them – the harmful things and the bites like those of gnats – we have healed them through the truth and power of the Lord, applying the moisture of the oil of the Lord’s teaching to those bitten by Tatian’s words, since he says, “I came only for the sheep that has gone astray, of the house of Israel.”

**46,4,3** Therefore he also said in parables that a certain man was going down from Jerusalem to Jericho, and he related that he fell among robbers, so as to show the sheep, and the one who had gone down from Jerusalem, the one brought down from the greater glory into diminution, and the one torn away from the one commandment of his own shepherd and gone astray.

**46,4,4** Hence it has also become our belief that [our] father, the holy Adam, is numbered among the living; for it was on his account, and on account of all of us who come from him, that Christ came, to grant amnesty to those who knew him of old and did not stray from his Godhead, but were held fast in Hades because of their faults — to those still in the world through repentance, and to those in Hades through mercy and salvation.

**46,5,1** Hence the one who knows may also marvel, as we have also found in books, that it was on Golgotha that our Lord Jesus Christ was crucified.

**46,5,2a** Nowhere else but where the body of Adam lay.

**46,5,2b** For having gone out from paradise and having dwelt opposite it for a long time, and having passed through many days, he later came also to this place — I mean Jerusalem — and, having paid his debt, was buried there, in the place of Golgotha.

**46,5,3** Hence the place fittingly received its name, being interpreted Place of a Skull, though the shape of the place shows no resemblance to this naming.

**46,5,4** For neither does it lie on some height, so that it might be interpreted as a skull, as the place of the head on a body is so called, nor [on] a look-out.

**46,5,5** For indeed it does not lie high in comparison with the other places; for opposite it the Mount of Olives is higher, and Gibeon, eight miles away, is very high, and even the height that once stood on Zion, but has now been cut down, was itself higher than the place.

**46,5,6** Whence, then, the name of the Skull, except that, since the skull of the first-formed man was found there and his remains lay there, for this reason it had been called the Place of a Skull;

**46,5,7** on which our Lord Jesus Christ, being crucified, showed our salvation in a figure through the water and the blood which streamed from him by way of his side when it was pierced, beginning from the beginning of the lump to sprinkle the remains of our forefather,

**46,5,8** so that to us as well he might point out the sprinkling of his own blood, the water poured out upon the one who lay buried beneath the place having become a cleansing of our defilement and of the repentant soul, and a pattern of the kneading and of the purging of the filth of our sins, for the hope both of him and of us who are begotten of him.

**46,5,9a** Therefore here too was fulfilled the saying, “Awake, you who sleep, and arise from the dead, and Christ will shine upon you.”

**46,5,9b** For even if it speaks about us who have been made dead by our works and who sleep in the depth of the sleep of ignorance, yet at any rate the beginning of the allusion [...] holds its manner from there.

**46,5,10** And not simply nor idly; for “there arose,” it says, “many bodies of the saints,” as the gospel has it, “and in company with him they went into the holy city”; and its words are not that the saints’ souls arose, but the saints’ bodies by nature, and they entered with him into the holy city, and so on.

**46,5,11** Having, then, beaten off on every side the bites of this gnat with the oil of God’s loving-kindness, and of the sojourn of our Lord among us, and of the light that is the gospel of the truth, let us make haste, as is our custom, to go on again to the next in the power of God.
